# Rationale Log

Every non-obvious call in the extraction, per article and per adverse event.
Split out of the `rationale` half of each archived response at merge time.

## Bogenschutz 2015

*Alcohol · 2015 · eligible=Y · config=pending*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Statistical analysis section names the primary drinking outcome as percent heavy drinking days with primary contrast baseline vs. weeks 5-12. Results, 'Clinical outcomes': 'Percent heavy drinking days decreased during weeks 5-12 relative to baseline (mean difference (SD) = 26.0 (22.4), 95% CI 8.7-43.2, t(8) = 3.477, p = 0.008).' Used this combined-window mean difference rather than the per-sub-window Cohen's d values in Figure 3 (which split the same span into weeks 5-8 and weeks 9-12 separately and are embedded in an OCR-garbled image-derived table), because the text explicitly designates the combined baseline-vs-weeks-5-12 contrast as primary and no single Figure-3 value corresponds to exactly that window.
- **SD**: t(8) = 3.477, the paired-samples t-statistic reported in the same sentence as Outcome, for the identical baseline-vs-weeks-5-12 contrast (Results, 'Clinical outcomes'). The raw SD of the mean difference (22.4) is given in the same sentence and is used in Retention/SampleSize reasoning but the t-statistic (with df) was chosen for this field since it is the inferential statistic paired with the effect and carries the degrees of freedom.
- **PVal**: p = 0.008, same sentence as Outcome/SD (Results, 'Clinical outcomes').
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: df = 8 on the reported t(8) = 3.477 implies n = 9. Matches the article's statement that 9 of 10 participants 'completed all follow-up assessments and are included in outcome analyses' (Results, 'Treatment exposure and follow-up') and the Figure 3 legend ('n = 9 at all other time points' after baseline).
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 14/14 met. Unmet: none. All 14 elements are explicitly described. Comparator, randomization and blinding are each described as absent rather than present ('single-group, within-subjects design'; open-label psilocybin sessions; Discussion cites 'lack of a control group or blinding' as a limitation), which satisfies the described-including-as-absent rule. Setting is described via institutional affiliation (UNM Health Sciences Center) plus the detailed dosing-room description. Power is reported explicitly (n=10, power=0.803 to detect d=1.0). Harms and funding/COI each have dedicated sections.
- **RandomizationYN**: Single-arm, open-label, within-subjects design (Methods, 'Study design'); no randomization was performed. Per the single-arm boundary rule, coded 0 (the element was absent), not 3.
- **BlindingYN**: Open-label study; Discussion lists 'lack of a control group or blinding' as a limitation. Coded 0 per the single-arm boundary rule, not 3.
- **Double+Blind**: Consistent with BlindingYN = 0; the study is open-label, not double-blind.
- **Comparator**: No control or comparison arm; Discussion states 'lack of a control group' as a limitation. The two psilocybin sessions used different per-participant doses (0.3 mg/kg then up to 0.4 mg/kg) and subjective effects were compared between them (Table 1, paired t-tests df=5), but this is a within-subject dose-escalation across sequential sessions in one arm, not a between-groups or high-vs-low-dose comparator design, so Comparator remains 0.
- **PrimaryCare**: No mention of primary-care settings or providers; conducted in a psychiatry department research clinic. The concept applies to a clinical trial in general, so coded 0 (absent) rather than 3.
- **Retention (%)**: Study is not randomized, so denominator is the enrolled/included sample. Figure 1 / Results 'Participants': 10 were 'included in the study' (received psilocybin); Results 'Treatment exposure and follow-up': 9 'completed all follow-up assessments.' Retention = 9/10 x 100 = 90.
- **ConfoundYN**: Coded 1 on substantive-discussion grounds rather than statistical adjustment: no formal confound adjustment was performed (no control group), but the Discussion explicitly names candidate confounds -- 'it is not possible to separate unequivocally the effects of attention, psychosocial treatment, and time; expectancy effects related to knowledge of receiving psilocybin; and the specific effects of psilocybin.'
- **BaseStatus**: No DSM-5-style severity specifier is given (study used DSM-IV, which has no built-in severity grading for dependence). Inferred 'Moderate' from an informal DSM-5 criterion-count crosswalk (2-3 mild, 4-5 moderate, 6+ severe) applied to the reported mean of 5.0 (SD 1.2, range 3-7) endorsed dependence-type criteria (Results, 'Participants'); this sits at the moderate end of that crosswalk. 8/10 participants also showed physical dependence (tolerance or withdrawal). Flagged as an inference, not an author-assigned label.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Results, 'Participants': 'Two participants were Native American/Alaska Native, one was African American, four were Hispanic, and three were white non-Hispanic.' Non-white/BIPOC = 2 + 1 + 4 = 7 of 10 = 70%. Hispanic is reported here as a category distinct from white non-Hispanic in the article's own breakdown, so no double-counting ambiguity.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: Coded 1 on reported income data alone (mean household income $47,023, SD $35,262; employment status also reported), per the field's 'income reported' example. Not an explicit low-income eligibility criterion -- this is descriptive demographic reporting, not a design feature targeting low-SES participants.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 0. Figure 1 shows a highly selective funnel: 70 screened, 51 excluded before consent (11 exclusionary psychiatric/drug-use disorder, 9 lifetime hallucinogen use >10 occasions, 5 exclusionary medications, 4 exclusionary medical conditions, 3 age>65, etc.), plus 9 more excluded after consent, leaving 10 of 70 (14%) enrolled. A narrowly screened volunteer sample typical of an early-phase proof-of-concept trial, not reflective of a broader real-world treatment-seeking population.
- **PhysicalChangeMeasured**: Breath Alcohol Concentration was measured at each visit but the article explicitly states it 'was used to ensure safety of treatment and validity of assessments rather than as an outcome measure' (Methods, 'Substance use and consequences'). Labs/ECG/vitals were collected for screening and session-safety monitoring, not analyzed longitudinally as a physical/biological treatment outcome. Coded 0; flagged as a judgment call given the overlap between safety monitoring and outcome measurement.
- **Followup in months**: Article reports duration only in weeks, structured throughout in 4-week blocks (weeks 1-4, 5-8, 9-12, 13-24, 25-36) built on the '12-week...intervention'; total follow-up is 'a total of 36 weeks' (Methods, 'Study design'; longest window is 'Weeks 25-36'). Converted using the article's own 4-week unit: 36 / 4 = 9 months. No separate end-of-treatment-only horizon is reported apart from this overall window.
- **CessationMeasured**: Coded 1 based on the Abstract's explicit abstinence framing: 'Abstinence did not increase significantly in the first 4 weeks of treatment... but increased significantly following psilocybin administration.' The underlying instrument (TLFB) yields percent drinking/abstinent days rather than a discrete binary cessation rate, so this is a judgment call that the article's own abstinence language qualifies as a cessation-type outcome, distinct from the partial-reduction outcome (percent heavy drinking days) captured under ReductionMeasured.
- **RelapseMeasured**: Coded 0. TLFB tracks daily drinking continuously, which could in principle reveal a return to use, but the article never names or reports a distinct relapse rate or relapse-tracking outcome.
- **Implementation Outcome**: Coded 1 on two grounds: (1) MET session fidelity was formally coded with the Motivational Interviewing Treatment Integrity system (MITI 3.1) and benchmarked against a proficiency threshold (Results, 'Treatment exposure and follow-up'); (2) the Discussion states the study 'clearly demonstrat[es] feasibility' as one of its own framings of the findings.
- **NumDistinctAEs**: Counted the 4 events in the dedicated 'Treatment-related adverse events' results subsection (headache, nausea/emesis, diarrhea, insomnia). A separate finding -- modest, transient, statistically significant blood-pressure elevation during dosing (Results, 'Acute effects') -- was not added as a 5th event: it is reported as an expected monitored physiologic parameter in a different subsection, not adjudicated by the authors as an adverse event, and the AE subsection itself confirms 'No participant required medication or other intervention for blood pressure.' Flagged as a borderline inclusion call.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: The article reports per-AE-type counts (5 headache, 1 nausea/emesis, 1 diarrhea, 1 insomnia) but never states a total number of distinct participants with >=1 AE, and overlap between these groups (e.g., whether the insomnia participant also had headache) is not addressed. A summed or de-duplicated total would require an unsupported assumption, so coded '.'.
- **AE_denominator**: AEs were 'collected on an AE case report form at the end of the psilocybin sessions and at all subsequent visits' (Methods, 'Safety assessment') for all participants who received psilocybin; 10 completed the first psilocybin session (Figure 1) and were subject to this monitoring.

### Adverse events
- **Bogenschutz 2015_AE01**: Graded Mild via rubric (not author CTCAE/SAE): transient, resolved within 24 hours, consistent with the cited Johnson et al. (2012) report of dose-dependent transient headache -- hence Onset 'post-session-acute'. InterventionRequired coded '.' rather than inferred 'N': the article's blanket no-intervention sentence ('No participant required medication or other intervention for blood pressure, anxiety, or other psychiatric symptoms') does not name headache specifically.
- **Bogenschutz 2015_AE02**: Single transient episode of nausea/emesis during dosing, no reported escalation or complication; graded Mild via rubric. InterventionRequired coded '.' because the article's blanket no-intervention statement covers only blood pressure/anxiety/psychiatric symptoms, not GI events, and no antiemetic use is mentioned either way.
- **Bogenschutz 2015_AE03**: Diarrhea in a participant with pre-existing irritable bowel syndrome, occurring during one session only; graded Mild via rubric given no reported complication, hospitalization, or persistence. InterventionRequired coded '.' for the same reason as AE02 (not covered by the article's blanket statement).
- **Bogenschutz 2015_AE04**: Single-night insomnia following a session, no reported persistence beyond that night; graded Mild via rubric (persistence of days-to-weeks would move it to Moderate). Onset coded 'post-session-acute'. InterventionRequired coded '.' as not addressed by the article's blanket no-intervention statement, which names only blood pressure/anxiety/psychiatric symptoms.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: pending
- **metric / method / confidence**: "Cohen's d (derived from paired t-test)" / 'd' / 'approx'
- **note**: d derived as t/sqrt(n) = 3.477/sqrt(9) = 1.159 from the primary designated contrast (baseline vs. weeks 5-12 percent heavy drinking days, paired t(8)=3.477, n=9). This treats a within-subjects paired statistic as an input to a between-groups conversion pipeline, which is the textbook 'approx' scenario named in this prompt's Step 4. It does not exactly reproduce the authors' own Morris-Deshon-corrected Cohen's d values in Figure 3, which are reported for different sub-window splits (weeks 5-8 and weeks 9-12 separately, not the combined 5-12 window used in the text's primary analysis) -- those range 1.109 and 0.869 respectively, bracketing this derived value and supporting its plausibility without matching it exactly.

## Bogenschutz 2022

*Alcohol · 2022 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Primary outcome per abstract/Methods is percentage of heavy drinking days (PHDD), weeks 5-36, between-group. Efficacy section reports the between-group contrast directly as Hedges g = 0.52 (95% CI 0.11-0.94), mean difference 13.86 (95% CI 3.00-24.72), P=.01 (also Table 2, 'Between-group follow-up' row for %Heavy drinking days). Used the authors' own Hedges g rather than the mean-difference or the 3-dimensional MANOVA omnibus statistic, since g is the designated effect-size metric and requires no conversion.
- **SD**: Paired F-test for the same PHDD weeks-5-36 between-group contrast is given in the Abstract as F(1,86)=6.43, P=.01, matching the P-value attached to the Hedges g in Table 2/Efficacy text. Note: the Results/Efficacy section separately reports F(1,86)=6.18, P=.02 for 'a main effect of treatment on the 3-dimensional drinking outcome vector' -- this is the multivariate MANOVA omnibus test across PHDD+PDD+DPD jointly, not the PHDD-specific univariate contrast, so it is a different statistic and was not used here. Flagging the two F-values as belonging to different analyses rather than treating them as inconsistent duplicates of the same number.
- **PVal**: P=.01 printed exactly, attached to the same PHDD weeks-5-36 between-group Hedges g/mean-difference contrast (Abstract and Table 2).
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: Table 2 gives the analyzed n for the between/within-group effect-size table as Diphenhydramine (n=45), Psilocybin (n=48); 45+48=93, matching 'Of the 95 randomized participants, 93 received at least 1 dose of study medication and were included in the primary outcome analysis.'
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 14/14 met. Unmet: none. Fully reported CONSORT-style RCT: explicit objective (Abstract/Intro), double-blind RCT design and 2-site setting named, detailed inclusion/exclusion criteria, psilocybin vs diphenhydramine dosing/session structure, active comparator described, primary+secondary outcomes listed, a priori interim-analysis power plan (f2=0.16, power 0.8) plus post hoc power limitation discussed, randomization procedure (stratified blocks) and blinding procedure (with blinding-integrity data) both described, detailed statistical analysis plan, full Results section, dedicated Safety section with MedDRA-coded AE tabulation, and an extensive Funding/COI disclosure section.
- **Retention (%)**: Denominator = randomized N (95), since both randomized (95) and a broader 'qualified' pre-randomization pool (110) are reported, and attrition after randomization is what the criterion measures. Completers = participants who completed the full 8-month (32-week) double-blind follow-up per the CONSORT diagram: 42 (diphenhydramine) + 46 (psilocybin) = 88. 88/95 x 100 = 92.6%.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Article reports non-Hispanic White = 78.9%; no single combined non-white figure is given. Computed as complement: 100 - 78.9 = 21.1. Note the article states race categories sum to >100% because 2 participants selected multiple categories, and Hispanic ethnicity (14.7%) is reported as a category alongside racial categories rather than crossed with them, so this 21.1% figure is an approximation using non-Hispanic-White as the reference 'majority' group, not a clean non-white sum.
- **BaseStatus**: Judgment call. All participants met DSM-IV criteria for alcohol dependence (the more severe of DSM-IV's two AUD diagnoses), mean 5.3/7 dependence criteria, mean 14.2 years dependent, and WHO risk levels were Very High 31.6% / High 33.7% / Moderate 22.1% / Low 12.6% (majority high/very-high risk). Coded 'Severe' on this basis, despite the Discussion's own caveat that 'the study population was lower in drinking intensity at screening than in most AUD medication trials' -- that comment is a relative statement about comparability to other AUD pharmacotherapy trials, not an absolute severity description, and the dependence diagnosis plus risk-level distribution still indicate a severe target population.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Both sites are in the United States: New York University Grossman School of Medicine (New York, NY) and University of New Mexico Health Sciences Center (Albuquerque, NM).
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 0. Although recruited from the community via advertisements, the funnel was highly selective (569 screened by phone -> 172 screened in person -> 110 qualified -> 95 randomized) with extensive psychiatric/medical exclusions (major psychiatric and drug use disorders, hallucinogen use, contraindicating medical conditions), consistent with a volunteer, highly-screened research sample rather than a real-world clinical/community population.
- **ConfoundYN**: Coded 1. The primary MANOVA model included fixed effects for site and site-by-treatment interaction plus baseline covariates for each drinking measure; Discussion also substantively addresses inability to evaluate effects in subgroups (women, racial/ethnic minorities, psychiatric comorbidity) due to insufficient power, which is a substantive discussion of potential moderating factors.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: Coded 1. Table 1 reports household income as median (range): $100,000 ($3,700-$4,000,000). Although the median is not low, the reported range's floor ($3,700/year) documents that participants with very low income were part of the sample, and the field's own parenthetical ('income reported') treats explicit income reporting as sufficient evidence of SES description. This is a closer call than a study reporting only a low mean/median income (e.g., the field could instead be read as requiring evidence that low-SES participants specifically were included/targeted, which this article does not state as an eligibility criterion) -- flagged as a genuine judgment call.
- **Followup in months**: Article defines its primary follow-up window as the '32-week double-blind period following the first dose of study medication' (weeks 5-36), consistent throughout Abstract, Methods, and Limitations ('does not provide information on the duration of effects beyond the 32-week double-blind observation period'). Converted using the stated weeks/4 convention: 32/4 = 8 months. A separate open-label extension (week 38 dosing + 18 more weeks) is described but is outside the double-blind results this article reports, so it was not used as the follow-up horizon.
- **Implementation Outcome**: Coded 0. The article states that 'training, supervision, and fidelity monitoring procedures are described in the protocol' but no feasibility/acceptability/fidelity results are reported as a study outcome in this article (fidelity monitoring is a study-conduct procedure, not a reported outcome measure).
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: Coded 0. The MEQ (States of Consciousness Questionnaire/Mystical Experience Questionnaire) was used as a subjective-effects/process measure of the acute drug session, not as a mental-health outcome (depression, anxiety, PTSD, well-being); no such mental-health outcome scale is reported.
- **ArmScope**: Coded 'pooled'. The headline safety figures (204 total AEs: 119 psilocybin, 85 diphenhydramine) and the 3 serious adverse events are reported across both treatment arms together, not psilocybin-arm-only, even though some specific AE types (e.g., headache) are also broken out by arm.
- **AE_denominator**: Coded 93 (participants who received >=1 dose of study medication: 48 psilocybin + 45 diphenhydramine), matching the 32-week AE-reporting period 'following the first administration of study medication.' Note: the Methods state AEs were coded 'occurring after informed consent,' which could imply a denominator of 95 (all randomized/consented) rather than 93 (as-treated); flagging this as an alternative reading not resolved by the text provided.
- **NumDistinctAEs**: Coded 'Not numbered'. The main text names 204 total AE events and describes several specific AE types in prose (headache, anxiety, nausea, passive suicidal ideation, psychiatric hospitalization, Mallory-Weiss tear), but the full MedDRA-coded distinct-type breakdown is in eTable 2a/2b of Supplement 2, which is not included in the converted text provided for this extraction, so the true total distinct-type count cannot be verified.
- **AE_scope_cardiovascular_exclusion**: The 'Cardiovascular Effects' subsection (under 'Acute Effects', not 'Safety') reports psilocybin was associated with transient increases in systolic/diastolic blood pressure and heart rate during dosing, resolving by ~300-360 minutes, but states explicitly 'no participant reported symptoms or was treated for hypertension.' This is analyzed elsewhere as a formal continuous safety outcome ('Safety Outcomes' subsection, mixed models for BP/HR) rather than adjudicated as one of the 204 MedDRA-coded adverse events in the 'Safety' section. Because it is asymptomatic, untreated, and not counted among the tabulated AEs, it was not added as a distinct ae_events row (captured instead under PhysicalChangeMeasured=1); flagged here as a non-obvious inclusion/exclusion judgment.

### Adverse events
- **Bogenschutz 2022_AE01**: Graded Severe with SeveritySource='author': this is one of the article's explicitly enumerated 'three serious adverse events'; inpatient psychiatric hospitalization independently meets the FDA hospitalization seriousness criterion. Onset coded 'delayed' since it occurred during the 32-week follow-up in association with binge-drinking episodes, not during or immediately after a dosing session (this participant was in the diphenhydramine arm).
- **Bogenschutz 2022_AE02**: Graded Severe with SeveritySource='author' as one of the article's three named SAEs; hospitalization meets FDA seriousness criterion. Onset coded 'delayed' for the same reason as AE01 (diphenhydramine-arm, follow-up-period event, not a dosing-session event).
- **Bogenschutz 2022_AE03**: NAffected reported as 21 (psilocybin-arm count, the intervention of interest for this review); the diphenhydramine-arm rate (2/45, 4.4%) is captured in Description rather than as a second NAffected value, since the field takes a single integer. Onset 'post-session-acute' per the article's own framing that this is drawn from 'treatment-emergent adverse events occurring within 48 hours of study drug administration' (eTable 2b). Defaulted to Mild consistent with the Discussion's group-level characterization of psilocybin AEs as 'mostly mild and self-limiting' and the absence of any reported intervention or hospitalization for headache specifically, but SeverityIndeterminate set to 'Y' because no headache-specific duration, resolution, or intervention detail is given -- only the group-level statement, which is a general signal rather than event-specific evidence.
- **Bogenschutz 2022_AE04**: AE_type 'both': anxiety during psilocybin dosing is expected challenging-experience phenomenology but was also coded as a MedDRA AE. Graded Moderate (not Mild) because the article documents that a subset (2 participants) required active pharmacological intervention (oral diazepam 10 mg) per rubric guidance that expected in-session distress is graded by intervention need, not intensity; the rubric's Moderate band explicitly includes 'benzodiazepine rescue.' No total anxiety case count is given (only 'more frequently' and the 2-participant diazepam subset), so NAffected='.'.
- **Bogenschutz 2022_AE05**: SeverityIndeterminate=Y: nausea is named only as 'reported more frequently' with no case-level detail (no count, no intervention/duration specific to nausea). Defaulted to Mild per rubric's under-specified-AE rule, informed by the Discussion's general statement that psilocybin AEs were 'mostly mild and self-limiting,' but flagged indeterminate because that statement is a group-level characterization, not nausea-specific evidence.
- **Bogenschutz 2022_AE06**: SeverityIndeterminate=Y: this is a genuinely close call. The severity rubric lists 'suicidal behavior/attempt' among PAT-specific events that are Severe by default, but this event is passive ideation (not behavior/attempt), lasted 15 minutes, required no intervention, resolved without sequelae, and was NOT included among the article's own list of three named serious adverse events (all of which were in the diphenhydramine arm) -- an implicit author judgment that this event did not meet their seriousness bar. Graded Moderate as a middle position that neither silently downgrades a suicidal-ideation event to Mild (risking understated harm) nor overrides the authors' own non-SAE classification by declaring it Severe; flagged for human review given the safety-sensitive nature of any suicidal ideation signal.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: "Hedges' g" / 'g' / 'clean'
- **note**: Authors report Hedges' g = 0.52 (95% CI 0.11-0.94) directly for the primary PHDD between-group contrast at weeks 5-36 (Table 2/Efficacy text); this is a direct pass-through of an author-computed Hedges g, no conversion required. Article's own convention already orients positive g toward the favored (psilocybin) direction, so direction=1.

## Bowen 1970

*Alcohol · 1970 · eligible=Y · config=pending*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Primary comparison selected as Table 4 (p.117), which combines Experiments I and II into the article's final, most comprehensive comparison: LSD-500μg-treated patients (n=71, pooled across both experiments) vs all non-500μg comparators (HRTL-only + HRTL+placebo-LSD, n=87). Value taken verbatim from prose immediately following Table 4: 'The percentages of those rated as making a good adjustment, 24 per cent for the LSD group and 27 per cent for the HRTL group.' This prose statement is unambiguous, unlike Table 4's own Poor/Fair cell counts, which show OCR digit-merging artifacts (e.g. '22. 18 | 25; 36 | 54) 17 | 24) 71') that could not be reliably reconstructed and were therefore not used. Table 1 (Experiment I only, N=81, chi2(2)=0.661, first-reported result) and Table 3 (Experiment II only, dose-comparison, chi2=5.738) were considered as alternative primaries -- Table 1 is the first comparison named in the Purpose paragraph and Results, while Table 4 is the paper's summary bottom-line figure the Discussion returns to ('approximately equal in adjustment status after 1 year'). Table 4 was judged the closest to a single stated-primary result because it pools the full study sample rather than a sub-experiment; this is a non-obvious judgment call and a reviewer could reasonably prefer Table 1 instead.
- **SD**: Printed directly beneath Table 4 (OCR): 'x2 = 1.468, x2.05 = 5.991.' Degrees of freedom are not explicitly labeled by the authors; df=2 is inferred because the printed critical value 5.991 matches the standard chi-square table's alpha=.05 critical value for df=2, consistent with the 3-category (Poor/Fair/Good) x 2-group table design. This inference is about df labeling only -- the numeric value 1.468 is reported exactly as printed, not altered.
- **PVal**: Text states 'The differences in outcome are not statistically significant' in the paragraph introducing Table 4. No exact p-value or explicit threshold symbol (e.g. '<0.05') is printed for this comparison, so per the anti-conversion rule for narrative non-significance language this is coded 'NS' verbatim rather than inferred as a numeric threshold.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: 158 = 71 + 87, the Table 4 totals underlying the 24%/27% good-adjustment percentages in Outcome. Cross-checked by addition: 71 = Table 2's LSD-all-patients total (49, completers+followed-up dropouts) + Table 3's LSD-500μg total (22, Experiment II); 87 = Table 2's HRTL-only total (50) + Table 3's placebo-LSD-25μg total (22) + Table 3's no-LSD group (15). Both sums are internally consistent with the printed Table 4 column totals (71 and 87).
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 10/14 met. Unmet: eligibility, sample_size_power, harms, funding_coi. objective, design, setting, intervention, comparator, outcomes, results, and statistical methods (chi-square with critical values) are all clearly described. Randomization and blinding are described for Experiment II ('through random selection...a double blind experimental procedure'), so both criteria are met for the article even though Experiment I itself was not randomized/blinded. Not met: eligibility (only 'voluntarily applied,' 'male veterans,' and Experiment-II-only contraindication exclusions are given -- no formal inclusion/exclusion list); sample_size_power (no power calculation or sample-size justification anywhere); harms (no adverse-event/safety section, table, or statement exists at all -- not even described as absent); funding_coi (no funding source or conflict-of-interest statement found anywhere in the text). n_met=10 < 13, so MethodologyDescribedYN=0.
- **Pos Result Y/N**: Coded 0 (null/negative). Table 4's good-adjustment rate is numerically lower for the LSD group (24%) than the comparator group (27%), and the chi-square is non-significant. The article's own conclusion states 'the long term gains through use of LSD are negligible.'
- **Clincial Trial YN**: 1, consistent with the eligibility screen: prospectively planned LSD administration under a defined 60-day inpatient protocol, with assigned regimen (500μg vs 25μg vs none) and prospective 1-year outcome assessment via structured interview.
- **RandomizationYN**: Coded 0 for the study underlying the chosen primary Outcome (Table 4). The article's design is genuinely mixed: Experiment II (n=44 of the pooled 158) used explicit random assignment to 500μg vs 25μg dose ('through random selection they would receive either a large or small dose of LSD'), but Experiment I (the larger component of both pooled arms, n=81 of 158) used sequential, non-randomized cohorts -- the LSD group entered Sept 1965-Apr 1966 and the HRTL-only group entered Oct 1966-Apr 1967, after LSD was administratively discontinued from the program, not by random allocation. Because the majority of the pooled Table-4 sample derives from this non-randomized Experiment I comparison, RandomizationYN is coded 0 with this caveat flagged for reviewer override if the field is intended to characterize the article as a whole rather than the specific primary comparison.
- **BlindingYN**: Coded 0 for the same reason as RandomizationYN: Experiment II's placebo-dose arm was double-blind, but Experiment I (majority of the pooled Table-4 sample) was open/unblinded, with no placebo control and both groups aware of program assignment. Flagged as a judgment call; a reviewer characterizing the article as a whole rather than the primary comparison might instead prefer 1.
- **Double+Blind**: Coded 0 to remain consistent with BlindingYN=0 (must not be 1 when BlindingYN=0). The article does describe a double-blind sub-experiment (Experiment II, 'a double blind experimental procedure was employed'), but this does not characterize the non-blinded Experiment I majority of the pooled primary comparison.
- **Comparator**: 1. Two comparator types exist across the pooled Table-4 group: an active no-LSD comparator (HRTL program alone, Experiment I) and a placebo-dose comparator (25μg LSD, Experiment II) -- both are pooled into the single 'HRTL only or HRTL+LSD 25μg' comparator arm (n=87) in Table 4.
- **Power**: 0. No a priori or post hoc power calculation, or any discussion of statistical power, appears anywhere in the article.
- **Retention (%)**: 73.1 = 87 completers / 119 enrolled, calculated for Experiment I only (the only sub-cohort with a disclosed enrolled-vs-completed breakdown). LSD arm: 46 completers (41 analyzed + 5 completed-but-lost-to-follow-up) / 61 enrolled (46 completers + 15 who 'dropped out of their programs prematurely') = 75.4%. HRTL arm: 41 completers (40 analyzed + 1 lost-to-follow-up) / 58 enrolled (41 + 17 dropouts) = 70.7%. Combined: (46+41)/(61+58) = 87/119 = 73.1%. Experiment II is excluded from this calculation because the article states only that '59 alcoholic patients entered and completed the alcoholic program' -- completers are given but the original enrolled/entering count before completion is not disclosed for Experiment II, so a single study-wide retention percentage spanning both experiments is not calculable; the reported 73.1% reflects Experiment I only.
- **ConfoundYN**: 1. The article explicitly analyzes several confounding/moderating factors against outcome: pretreatment employment level (Table 5, Hollingshead classification), marital status (Table 6), education, age (dichotomized at the sample median of 44.5 years), timing of LSD administration within the program (Table 8), and program completion status (Table 7).
- **InclusionExclusionCriteria**: 0. Only minimal, implicit criteria are given -- 'male veterans who had voluntarily applied for the alcoholic treatment program' -- and, for Experiment II only, that 6 of 65 candidates were 'denied the drug on the basis of physical or psychiatric contraindications.' No formal inclusion/exclusion criteria list (diagnostic criteria, age limits, comorbidity exclusions) is stated for study entry generally.
- **ValidatedScaleMeasYN**: 0. The primary outcome measure is an 'empirically derived 8-point scale' devised by the authors themselves, validated only informally ('excellent agreement was obtained between scale ratings and the social worker's subjective impressions'), not an externally validated psychometric instrument. The Chwelos Scale (cited to Chwelos et al. 1959) is used to rate depth of LSD response as a secondary correlate, not as the study's SUD outcome measure, so it does not satisfy this field either.
- **Funding/COI YN**: 0. No funding source, grant support, or conflict-of-interest disclosure appears anywhere in the article text as OCR'd.
- **BaseStatus**: Coded '.' -- the article never characterizes baseline alcoholism severity (e.g., years of drinking, DSM-equivalent severity, prior treatment failures) with language mapping to Mild/Moderate/Severe. Patients are described only as veterans admitted to an inpatient VA alcoholism treatment program.
- **Age_Median**: Bracket '30-50' derived from two convergent figures: Experiment I mean ages of 43.8 (LSD group) and 46.1 (HRTL group), and the combined-sample median age of 44.5 years used for dichotomization in the age-outcome analysis ('either above or below 44.5 years'). Both fall within the 30-50 bracket.
- **PercentFem**: 0. 'All subjects were male veterans' (Experiment I Subjects paragraph); no female participants are described anywhere in either experiment.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Coded '.' -- race/ethnicity of the sample is never reported anywhere in the article.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: 1. The article reports occupational level using the Hollingshead classification, explicitly including lower-SES categories ('Skilled, semiskilled, unskilled -- Hollingshead positions 5-7', n=89) alongside higher-SES categories (Table 5), which describes inclusion of low-occupational-status participants, though the field's example criteria (income, low-income eligibility, public-insurance enrollment) are not stated verbatim. Judgment call.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: US. Study conducted at the Topeka Veterans Administration Hospital, Topeka, Kansas (footnote 1: '2200 Gage Boulevard, Topeka, Kansas 66622').
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: 1. Patients were routine VA hospital admissions who 'voluntarily applied for the alcoholic treatment program,' reflecting a real-world clinical population of hospitalized alcoholic veterans rather than a healthy-volunteer or highly research-screened sample. Caveat: Experiment II adds a further layer of self-selection within that population -- only 44 of 59 program completers 'volunteered' to be randomized to LSD dose, with 9 refusing and 6 excluded for contraindications -- which somewhat narrows representativeness for that sub-experiment.
- **InterventionDescription**: Summarized from the LSD program Methods section (500μg oral dose, single session, 60-day inpatient program, HRTL component) and the Experiment II placebo dose (25μg).
- **HarmsAssessed**: 0, consistent with SafetyReported='N'. No section, table, or statement addressing adverse events, side effects, or safety of the LSD sessions appears anywhere in the article. The 8-point outcome scale's 'No DTs or convulsions' item is a post-treatment adjustment criterion scored at 1-year follow-up, not an in-study harms/AE assessment of the LSD dosing sessions themselves.
- **Followup in months**: 12. 'All patients in the study were followed up by a research social worker 1 year after discharge from the program.' The Discussion mentions a separate, currently-ongoing 'revised LSD program' with 4-month and 1-year return visits, but this describes a different, later program under development, not additional follow-up of this study's cohort, so it is not used to extend the Followup value.
- **BehaviorChangeMeasured**: 1. Abstinence (a use-frequency/behavioral outcome) is directly scored within the 8-point adjustment scale ('Abstinent 6 months or more,' 'Abstinent 11 or 12 months').
- **PhysicalChangeMeasured**: 1, judgment call. 'No DTs or convulsions' is one of the 8 scale points and reflects a physical/medical withdrawal-related complication rather than a laboratory biomarker or vital sign; coded 1 as the closest fit to 'physical or biological outcomes measured.'
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: 0. The interview covered 'personal adjustment' broadly, but no depression/anxiety/well-being scale or distinct mental-health outcome is scored or reported as part of this study's own measured outcomes (the Discussion cites other studies' mental-health findings, not this study's).
- **RelapseMeasured**: 0, judgment call. Abstinence status at the single 1-year follow-up point is scored, but no explicit tracking of relapse/return-to-use as a distinct longitudinal construct (e.g., time-to-relapse, relapse after a period of confirmed sobriety) is described.
- **SafetyReported**: N. No Adverse Events, Safety, or Side-Effects section, table, or explicit statement exists anywhere in the article; the text is entirely silent on harms of the LSD sessions.
- **AnyAEReported**: NA because SafetyReported='N'.
- **NumDistinctAEs**: NA because SafetyReported='N'.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: NA because SafetyReported='N'.
- **AE_denominator**: '.' (never 'NA' per convention) because no safety assessment was reported.
- **ArmScope**: NA because SafetyReported='N'; no AE values exist to scope to an arm.
- **WorstSeverity**: NA because SafetyReported='N'.
- **EligibleYN**: Y. (a) Clinical trial: prospectively planned LSD dosing under a defined hospital protocol with assigned regimen and prospective 1-year outcome assessment -- passes. (b) SUD outcome: abstinence is directly scored within the primary 8-point adjustment scale ('Abstinent 6 months or more') -- passes.
- **EligibilityNote**: NA because EligibleYN='Y'.
- **EligibilityFailedCriterion**: NA because EligibleYN='Y'.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: pending
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Two-group proportions (%)' / 'OR' / 'approx'
- **note**: Table 4 (combined Experiments I & II) reports good-adjustment rates of 17/71 (24%) for LSD-500μg vs 23/87 (27%) for the pooled non-500μg comparator. The printed inferential statistic (chi2(2)=1.468, Table 4) is a 3-category (Poor/Fair/Good) statistic with df=2, inferred from the printed critical value 5.991 matching the standard df=2 table -- this does not meet the 1-df chi-square requirement in CONVERSION_RULES for a clean/approx chi2 conversion. Instead, collapsing the 3-category outcome to binary good/not-good yields OR = (17/54)/(23/64) = 0.876 (rounded 0.88), offered as an approx-confidence proportion-to-OR conversion per the framework's stated allowance for this transformation. A methodologist should confirm the good/not-good collapse is an appropriate representation of the outcome before ratifying; Table 1 (Experiment I only, chi2(2)=0.661, N=81) is a defensible alternative primary if reviewers prefer the first-reported sub-experiment over the pooled analysis.

## Costello 1969

*Alcohol · 1969 · eligible=N · config=pending*

### Article
- **Outcome**: This article is a narrative review with no original trial and no single stated primary outcome of its own; it synthesizes improvement/abstinence rates from six LSD studies (Table II, mean 75% improvement, range 50-93%) and multiple aversion-therapy studies, but designates no single primary comparison of its own. Coded '.' rather than selecting one reviewed study's statistic, which would misattribute another trial's effect size to this record.
- **SD**: No dispersion or test statistic is paired with a primary outcome of this article's own; individual reviewed studies' statistics (e.g., Table I's chi-squared values for Madill et al.'s craving-reduction comparison) belong to those separate trials, not to this review. Coded '.'.
- **PVal**: No p-value for a primary outcome of this article's own. Table I reports chi-squared = 5.123 (non-significant) for the Madill et al. craving-reduction comparison, but that is a cited study's statistic, not this review's finding. Coded '.'.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: No original analyzed sample exists; the article synthesizes studies with heterogeneous Ns (145 in the unpublished Saskatchewan report, 10/10/10 in Smart et al. 1966, 68 in O'Reilly & Funk 1964, etc.). Coded '.'.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 1/14 met. Unmet: design, setting, eligibility, intervention, comparator, outcomes, sample_size_power, randomization, blinding, statistical_methods, results, harms, funding_coi. The article is a narrative critical review, not an original trial. Only the objective (evaluate the aversion-therapy and LSD-therapy literature for alcoholism) is stated. There is no original design, setting, eligibility criteria, intervention protocol, comparator, outcome measures, sample-size/power statement, randomization, blinding, statistical methods, results, harms assessment, or funding/COI disclosure for an original study, because no such study was conducted by the author; the review's synthesis of others' results and harms is not the same as this article describing its own trial's results/harms.
- **Pos Result Y/N**: Coded 3 (cannot be determined) because no single trial result belongs to this article. The author is skeptical about aversion therapy ('no good evidence that aversion procedures are particularly effective') and cautious/mixed about LSD (75% mean improvement across largely uncontrolled comparisons, but the one adequately controlled study, Smart et al. 1966, found no LSD advantage over ephedrine or time). No single directionality is attributable to 'this study'.
- **Clincial Trial YN**: Coded 0 to match the eligibility screen: the article is a narrative review of others' trials, not a trial the author conducted himself.
- **RandomizationYN**: Coded 3 (does not apply): no original participants were enrolled or randomized by the author; this presupposes an original trial that does not exist here (distinct from the single-arm-trial boundary case, where a trial exists but lacks randomization).
- **BlindingYN**: Coded 3 (does not apply): no original study procedure exists to blind.
- **Double+Blind**: Coded 3 for the same reason as BlindingYN; no contradiction since BlindingYN is also 3, not 0.
- **Comparator**: Coded 3 (does not apply): the author ran no comparison of his own; comparator arms discussed (e.g., ephedrine, placebo, no-treatment) belong to the individual reviewed trials.
- **PrimaryCare**: Coded 0: unlike randomization/blinding/comparator, this asks a straightforward question answerable directly from the text regardless of whether an original trial exists -- nothing in the article ties any aspect to primary-care settings or providers.
- **Power**: Coded 0: statistical power is a topic a review could discuss even without an original trial (e.g., as a critique of the reviewed studies), but this article never raises it anywhere.
- **Retention (%)**: Coded '.': no original enrolled/randomized sample exists to compute a completion percentage for.
- **ConfoundYN**: Coded 1: the article substantively discusses confounding/moderating variables that should be controlled in future aversion/LSD studies (employment status, personality traits such as extraversion and anxiety level, brain damage) and explicitly flags a confound in Smart et al.'s own design (employment imbalance across randomized groups: 8/10 unemployed in the LSD arm vs 4/10 and 5/10 in the others).
- **InclusionExclusionCriteria**: Coded 3 (does not apply): the author enrolled no participants of his own, so there are no inclusion/exclusion criteria to state.
- **ValidatedScaleMeasYN**: Coded 3 (does not apply): no original outcome measurement was performed by the author.
- **Funding/COI YN**: Coded 0: the only provenance note is that the paper was based on a 1967 conference presentation; no funding source or conflict-of-interest statement appears anywhere in the text.
- **BaseStatus**: Coded '.', never a number: this is a review with no participant sample, so the severity-of-condition concept does not apply and the closed-enum field takes '.'.
- **Age_Median**: Coded '.', never a number, for the same reason as BaseStatus: no participant sample exists to have a median/mean age.
- **PercentFem**: Coded '.': no original sample exists to report a sex/gender composition for.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Coded '.': no original sample exists to report race/ethnicity composition for.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: Coded 3 (does not apply): no original participants were enrolled or described by the author.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Coded '.': no original study population exists. (The author's affiliation is the University of Calgary, Canada, but that is the author's institution, not a study population, and this field is reserved for the actual study sample's country.)
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 3 (does not apply): no original recruitment occurred.
- **InterventionDescription**: Coded '.': the article discusses several distinct interventions (emetic-drug aversion therapy, succinylcholine aversion therapy, electric-shock aversion therapy, and single-dose LSD ranging roughly 200-800 microgram across cited studies) rather than one protocol of the author's own; no single description applies.
- **InterventionTherapy**: Coded 3 (does not apply): no intervention of the author's own was administered.
- **HarmsAssessed**: Coded 1, consistent with SafetyReported='Y'. Although the author conducted no original trial, the text explicitly relays adverse/side-effect information from the aversion-therapy literature it reviews (succinylcholine-induced apnea and post-conditioning physiological disturbance; emetic-drug side effects: diarrhea, sinusitis, cystitis, prostatitis, neuromuscular disturbances). Per the prompt's cardinal rule against understating harm, this content is coded as harms reporting rather than discarded as 'safety never assessed', even though it is second-hand (drawn from cited studies) and concerns the non-psychedelic aversion-therapy agents rather than LSD specifically -- the LSD-therapy section of the article contains no comparable AE detail.
- **Followup in months**: Coded '.': the article cites many different follow-up windows across different reviewed studies (e.g., Madill et al. 3 weeks/3 months, Clancy et al. 1 year, O'Reilly & Funk 2-34 months, Jensen & Ramsay various) with no single follow-up duration belonging to this article itself.
- **BehaviorChangeMeasured**: Coded 1: the article extensively reports on drinking-behavior and craving outcomes measured in the reviewed literature (Table I's 'Reduction in Craving'; avoidance/aversive-response behaviors). Judgment call: reflects outcome domains covered in the surveyed literature, not an outcome measured by Costello's own (nonexistent) study.
- **PhysicalChangeMeasured**: Coded 1: physiological measures (heart rate, GSR, apnea duration) from the succinylcholine studies are reported. Same judgment call as BehaviorChangeMeasured.
- **ReductionMeasured**: Coded 1: Table I reports a distinct 'Reduction in Craving' outcome (partial behavioral reduction, not full cessation) drawn from Madill et al.
- **CessationMeasured**: Coded 1: complete abstinence ('completely abstinent', 'totally dry') is extensively discussed as an outcome across both the aversion-therapy and LSD-therapy sections.
- **RelapseMeasured**: Coded 1: relapse/return-to-drinking is explicitly tracked in the reviewed follow-up data (e.g., the Saskatchewan report's 'occasional relapses ... becoming fewer and of shorter duration').
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: Coded 0: no depression/anxiety/well-being scale outcome is discussed anywhere as a measured domain; anxiety and personality traits are discussed only as hypothesized predictors of conditionability, not as a measured mental-health outcome.
- **QualityOfLifeMeasured**: Coded 0: no quality-of-life instrument or outcome is discussed anywhere in the text.
- **Implementation Outcome**: Coded 0: no feasibility/acceptability/fidelity/reach/adoption/sustainability outcome is discussed anywhere in the text.
- **MortalityMeasured**: Coded 0: mortality is never addressed as an outcome or otherwise anywhere in the article; treated as 'not covered' consistent with the other undiscussed outcome domains (MentalHealthChangeMeasured, QualityOfLifeMeasured, Implementation Outcome), rather than '.' since its absence from the text is itself informative for this review's scope.
- **LSD**: Coded 0, not 1, despite LSD being the article's central topic: the substance flags are intervention flags for the study being coded (the author's own study), and Costello administered no LSD himself -- LSD was administered only in the separate trials he reviews (Table II).
- **Mescaline**: Coded 0 for the same reason as LSD: mescaline appears only as an alternative arm in one reviewed trial (Smith 1958, '200 or 300 microgram LSD or 0.5 gr. mescaline'), not as something the author administered.
- **SafetyReported**: Coded 'Y': the article explicitly reports AE-type content relayed from the aversion-therapy literature it synthesizes (see HarmsAssessed rationale), so it is not silent on safety, even though the reporting is second-hand and concentrated on non-psychedelic aversion agents rather than LSD.
- **AnyAEReported**: Coded 'Y', consistent with SafetyReported='Y' and the non-empty ae_events array.
- **NumDistinctAEs**: Counted 7 distinct AE types named in the text: succinylcholine-induced apnea, post-conditioning physiological disturbance (heart rate/muscle twitch/GSR), and four emetic-drug side effects (diarrhea, sinusitis, cystitis, prostatitis) plus neuromuscular disturbances.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: Coded '.': no article-wide count of participants experiencing at least one AE is given; the only count present is n=15 for the apnea-duration statistic, not for AE incidence overall.
- **AE_denominator**: Coded 'Not numbered': the AEs are drawn from a narrative synthesis of several different reviewed sources with different sample sizes (e.g., the succinylcholine physiological data come from a 15-patient pilot study, while the emetic-drug side-effect list cites no denominator at all); no single assessed population size applies to the set as a whole.
- **ArmScope**: Coded 'NA': the AE data are pooled narratively across multiple distinct non-trial sources (a 15-patient succinylcholine pilot study and an unspecified emetic-drug literature) rather than drawn from one trial's exposed arm(s), so neither 'psychedelic_arm' nor 'pooled' cleanly applies; this is a genuine case where arm scope does not apply rather than merely a single-arm study.
- **WorstSeverity**: Coded 'Moderate': the maximum Severity among the seven ae_events is Moderate (succinylcholine apnea/paralysis, rubric-graded; and author-graded 'light to moderate' neuromuscular disturbances).

### Adverse events
- **Costello 1969_AE01**: Apnea duration (mean 90.4s, SD 24s, range 63-150s) is reported for Sanderson, Campbell & Laverty's 15-patient succinylcholine pilot study (ref 27), relayed narratively by Costello rather than measured by him. Graded Moderate/rubric because respiratory paralysis is the deliberate, monitored unconditioned stimulus of the method (saline-drip access, three therapists present) and self-resolves within the reported duration, but the article gives no detail on clinical management or complications beyond duration, so SeverityIndeterminate=Y; a stricter reading of 'life-threatening' under FDA seriousness criteria could argue for Severe instead. NAffected=15 because the duration statistics are reported across the full pilot sample.
- **Costello 1969_AE02**: Irregular heart rate, muscle twitches and GSR-baseline fluctuation after the succinylcholine conditioning session are described qualitatively (ref 27) with no participant count or explicit severity grading; defaulted to Mild/rubric with SeverityIndeterminate=Y per the under-specified-AE rule.
- **Costello 1969_AE03**: The author's own text states emetic-drug diarrhea is 'mostly mild', a direct severity descriptor, so SeveritySource=author and SeverityIndeterminate=N despite no participant count being given.
- **Costello 1969_AE04**: Sinusitis is named in a summary list of emetic-drug side effects with no severity language or count anywhere in the text; defaulted to Mild/rubric with SeverityIndeterminate=Y.
- **Costello 1969_AE05**: Cystitis is named in the same summary list with no severity language or count; defaulted to Mild/rubric with SeverityIndeterminate=Y.
- **Costello 1969_AE06**: Prostatitis is named in the same summary list with no severity language or count; defaulted to Mild/rubric with SeverityIndeterminate=Y.
- **Costello 1969_AE07**: The author explicitly characterizes neuromuscular disturbances from emetic drugs as 'light to moderate'; the worst end of that stated range (Moderate) is used per the WorstSeverity/max-severity convention, so SeveritySource=author and SeverityIndeterminate=N.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: pending
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'None (narrative review; no original effect size reported)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: Review article with no original trial: the author conducted no prospective enrollment or intervention of his own. The six LSD studies and multiple aversion-therapy studies discussed each carry their own separately-extractable statistics rather than a single value belonging to this record, so no defensible conversion exists for this article itself.

## D.C. Mash 2000

*Opioid · 2000 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: No single statistic is designated 'primary' by the authors. The Abstract's first substantive finding sentence is 'ibogaine significantly decreased craving for cocaine and heroin during inpatient detoxification,' with depressive-symptom improvement introduced afterward as 'also' significant -- so per the precedence rule (author designation > abstract-led SUD outcome > largest of co-equal), craving is the SUD-behavioral outcome the abstract leads with, ranking it above the Results section's BDI-first ordering and above depression (not an SUD outcome). Craving itself is reported as 5 subscales x 2 drug groups (10 F-tests, Tables 2-3) with none singled out as primary. Restricting to the opiate-craving subscales (HCQN-29, Table 2) because this record's Condition is 'Opioid', the largest/most significant of the 5 co-equal subscales is Intention to Use, F(3,24)=81.38, p=.0001. Outcome = 3.26 (pre-ibogaine) - 1.57 (post-ibogaine) = 1.69, the pre/post reduction on the 0-6 HCQN-29 scale (Table 2), computed directly from the two reported means (arithmetic only, no assumed model). The parallel cocaine-craving subscales (Table 3) were not selected given the Condition='Opioid' framing; the largest of those is Relief of Negative States, F(2,10)=15.26, p=.0005, noted here in case this choice is revisited.
- **SD**: F(3,24) = 81.38, the omnibus repeated-measures ANOVA statistic printed in the same row/table (TABLE 2) as the Intention-to-Use means used for Outcome. Numerator df=3 implies a 4-level within-subjects time factor (matching the Methods' 'three different time points...and at one month following discharge'), with the 1-month point excluded from the table ('data not shown' per Results) but still contributing to the omnibus test.
- **PVal**: p = .0001 as printed in TABLE 2, same row as the Intention-to-Use F-statistic and means used for Outcome/SD.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: Not reported. The article gives only the total enrolled N (27: 23 men, 4 women) and separate mean ages for the 'opiate group' and 'cocaine group' without stating either subgroup's size. TABLE 2's F(3,24) implies an error df of 24, which is consistent with several different repeated-measures error structures (one-way within-subjects on the opiate subgroup alone vs. a mixed model with drug-of-abuse as a between-subjects factor) that would each imply a different N -- back-calculating N from df would require assuming an ANOVA design the Methods paragraph does not confirm, which is exactly the kind of unstated decoding rule the extraction spec prohibits. Coded '.' rather than inferred.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 12/14 met. Unmet: comparator, randomization. 12 of 14 criteria are explicitly described. Comparator and randomization are never mentioned in any form anywhere in the article -- no 'control,' 'randomiz-', or comparable language occurs -- so neither is described as present or as absent, and both are coded N (a genuine gap, not an inferred absence). Setting is inferred only from co-author affiliation (e) and the acknowledgments naming the Healing Visions Institute, St. Kitts, W.I., rather than an explicit statement of the treatment site in Methods. Blinding is described via the explicit 'open-label conditions' phrase. Harms is coded Y on the single explicit tolerability sentence ('Ibogaine was well tolerated...') even though it is brief and cites a companion paper for detail. All remaining elements (objective, design, eligibility, intervention, outcomes, sample size, statistics, results, funding) are explicitly and specifically described.
- **RandomizationYN**: Coded 0, not '.' or 3. Methods states participants were 'assigned to one of three fixed-dose (500, 600, or 800 mg) ibogaine HCl treatments under open-label conditions' with no mention of a randomization procedure and no other arm to randomize against (every participant received ibogaine). Treated as a single-arm design per the boundary rule (single-arm/open-label studies code 0, not 3), since nothing in the text suggests dose assignment was a randomized comparison rather than a non-comparative dosing schedule.
- **BlindingYN**: 0. Methods explicitly states treatment was administered 'under open-label conditions'.
- **Comparator**: 0. No control, placebo, or comparison group is mentioned anywhere in the article. The three fixed doses (500/600/800 mg) are not analyzed as a dose-comparison design -- no results are stratified or contrasted by dose; the reported RM-ANOVAs use 'primary drug of abuse (opiates versus cocaine)' as the between-subjects factor, not dose -- so the 'high vs low dose counts as a comparator' provision does not apply here.
- **PrimaryCare**: 0. Treatment occurred in an inpatient detoxification/research setting; no interface with primary-care providers or settings is mentioned.
- **Power**: 1, a close call. No formal a priori or post hoc power calculation is reported. The Discussion states: 'A limitation of the study is that it is based on findings in only 27 subjects; thus, replication is needed in future studies to determine the stability of the findings.' Treated as functionally equivalent to the 'bare statement that the study was underpowered' provision, since it explicitly frames small sample size as a limitation on the reliability of the findings.
- **Retention (%)**: Not calculable. The study was not randomized, so the denominator would be the enrolled N (27), but the article never reports how many of the 27 completed the 14-day inpatient program or the 1-month follow-up assessment (the 1-month craving data are described only as 'data not shown'). No completer count is given anywhere in the text.
- **ConfoundYN**: 1, on substantive-discussion grounds rather than formal statistical adjustment. Results explicitly checks a candidate confound: CYP2D6 extensive-metabolizer proportions were compared between opioid- and cocaine-dependent groups and found 'not significantly different... (data not shown).' Discussion also attributes the null cocaine Desire-to-Use/Intention-to-Use findings 'in part because of floor effects at pre-ibogaine assessments and because of the small sample size,' a substantive discussion of a moderating factor.
- **InclusionExclusionCriteria**: 1. Methods states subjects 'met inclusion/exclusion criteria' and specifies several: DSM-IV criteria for cocaine or opioid dependence, positive urine screens at entry, no history of stroke/epilepsy/axis I psychotic disorders, and ECG/labs within predetermined limits.
- **BaseStatus**: '.' No DSM-5-style severity specifier or count of dependence criteria is reported (study used DSM-IV, which lacks a built-in severity grade), and no ASI composite/severity score is reported in Results despite the ASI having been administered at admission. Inpatient detoxification and clinically significant baseline BDI scores (>=11) suggest substantial severity but do not support a specific Mild/Moderate/Severe assignment without fabricating a threshold not given in the article.
- **Age_Median**: Methods reports two subgroup means, not one overall figure: 'The mean age was 34.6 +/- 1.9 years old for the opiate group and 37.5 +/- 2.9 years old for the cocaine group.' Both fall within the 30-50 bracket, so '30-50' is used despite no single combined mean being stated.
- **PercentFem**: Methods: 'Participants included 27 treatment-seeking opioid- and cocaine-dependent men (n = 23) and women (n = 4).' 4/27 x 100 = 14.8%.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: '.' No race or ethnicity data are reported anywhere in the article.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: 'NonUS', inferred rather than explicitly stated in Methods. Co-author affiliation (e) is 'Healing Visions Institute for Addiction Recovery, Ltd., St. Kitts, West Indies,' and the Acknowledgments separately thank 'the staff of the Healing Visions Institute for Addiction Recovery, Ltd., St. Kitts, W.I.' -- the institution most plausibly hosting the 14-day inpatient treatment described in Methods. 'Holistic Counseling, Inc., Miami, Florida' is also thanked, but the university/home-institution affiliations (Miami, Toronto, Montreal, Baltimore) are separate from the treatment site. Flagged because the Methods paragraph itself never names a country.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: 0. Subjects were 'self-referred for inpatient detoxification' for an experimental, non-standard-of-care treatment, with exclusion of anyone with stroke, epilepsy, or axis I psychotic disorder histories -- a self-selected, screened volunteer sample rather than a recruitment strategy reflective of a general treatment-seeking population.
- **InterventionTherapy**: 0. Beyond drug administration, the study describes structured psychiatric evaluations (SCID I/II), the Addiction Severity Index, and a Psychosocial Assessment -- these are assessment/measurement instruments, not a described therapy, counseling, integration, or preparation component structured around the ibogaine session itself. 'Holistic Counseling, Inc., Miami, Florida' is thanked in the Acknowledgments but its role in the study protocol is never described, so it was not counted as evidence of an intervention-embedded therapy component.
- **HarmsAssessed**: 1, required to agree with SafetyReported = 'Y' (see AE rationale below).
- **IndigenousFramework**: 0. The Abstract/Introduction describe ibogaine's traditional use by 'indigenous peoples' of western Africa 'to combat fatigue, hunger and thirst, and... as a sacrament in religious rituals' -- this is background on the plant medicine's traditional origin, not integration of an Indigenous framework, knowledge system, or practice into this study's protocol. Matches the codebook's explicit 'traditional origin in passing is not integration' example.
- **PhysicalChangeMeasured**: 1. Whole-blood pharmacokinetic levels of ibogaine and its metabolite noribogaine (FIGURE 1, GC/MS assay) are a central reported physiological/biological measure of this study, distinct from the baseline-only ECG/lab screening used for eligibility.
- **ReductionMeasured**: 0. The study measures craving intensity (captured under BehaviorChangeMeasured) via the HCQN-29/CCQN-45, not a distinct partial-use-reduction or harm-reduction endpoint; no drug-use quantity/frequency outcome is reported (participants were inpatient throughout the assessed period).
- **CessationMeasured**: 0. No abstinence or cessation rate is reported; the 1-month follow-up assessed self-reported craving only ('subjects' responses indicated that they still experienced diminished craving... (data not shown)'), not abstinence status.
- **RelapseMeasured**: 0. Discussion explicitly places relapse tracking in future work, not this study: 'Future research efforts will be aimed at determining an association between CYP2D6 metabolizer status, depressive symptoms, drug craving, and relapse rates.'
- **Followup in months**: 1. Longest reported follow-up is the self-report battery administered 'at one month following discharge from the treatment program' (Methods), i.e., measured from end of treatment.
- **SafetyReported**: 'Y'. Methods states an aim was 'to determine the safety and effectiveness of ibogaine,' and Results states explicitly: 'Ibogaine was well tolerated by both opiate- and cocaine-dependent subjects, in agreement with our previous findings.' This is a brief but explicit safety statement, judged sufficient to clear the SafetyReported gate even though it is far less detailed than a dedicated AE table (the cited companion paper, ref 4, appears to carry the fuller safety analysis).
- **AnyAEReported**: 'N'. The single safety statement in the article ('well tolerated... in agreement with our previous findings') is a positive tolerability claim with no adverse event of any kind named, counted, or described anywhere in the text. Treated as equivalent to an explicit no-AEs-occurred statement per the three-state framework, rather than 'AEs reported but not counted', since no specific AE is ever named.
- **NumDistinctAEs**: 0, required by AnyAEReported = 'N'.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: 0, consistent with AnyAEReported = 'N' (no AE of any kind was named).
- **AE_denominator**: 27. All enrolled participants received ibogaine under the single-arm open-label protocol described in Methods; no separate 'safety population' distinct from the 27 enrolled is defined.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'F (multi-df, within-subjects repeated-measures ANOVA)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: The selected primary craving statistic (HCQN-29 Intention-to-Use, opiate group) is an omnibus repeated-measures F with 3 numerator df across a 4-level within-subjects time factor, from a single-arm design with no comparator. Per the codebook, multi-df F statistics and within-subjects designs without a between-groups comparison are not convertible to Hedges' g and are marked excluded, to be routed to Tier 2 instead.

## Dakwar 2020

*Alcohol · 2020 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Year**: Article states 'published online Dec. 2, 2019' but the version-of-record issue header reads 'Am J Psychiatry 2020; 177:125-133' / 'Am J Psychiatry 177:2, February 2020'. Per the request's Year=2020 and the issue-year rule, 2020 (issue) is used; 2019 (online-first) is the noted discrepancy.
- **Outcome**: Primary outcome is self-reported alcohol abstinence (dichotomized abstinent/nonabstinent days) over 21 days postinfusion, per the longitudinal logistic mixed-effects model (Methods 'Statistical Analysis'; Results 'Primary Outcome: Alcohol Abstinence'). The time-by-treatment interaction is the test of ketamine vs. midazolam on this primary outcome: F=25.1, df=1,797. Outcome is coded as the bare F value (25.1); the labelled statistic with df is carried in SD.
- **SD**: Same sentence as Outcome ('Primary Outcome: Alcohol Abstinence' paragraph): 'the time-by-treatment interaction (F=25.1, df=1, 797, p,0.001)'. Reported as 'F(1,797) = 25.1' with df in parentheses as required.
- **PVal**: Printed in the text as 'p,0.001' (Primary Outcome paragraph). The comma is a clear OCR/typographic substitution for '<' — the identical 'p,0.001' pattern recurs verbatim later in the Secondary Outcomes section for a different F-test, while a nearby correctly-rendered value in the same paragraph reads 'p=0.004'. Read through as '<0.001' per the instruction to read through unambiguous punctuation artifacts; this is a punctuation correction, not a fabricated number.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: N=40, the full randomized/intent-to-treat sample ('40 were randomly assigned in an intent-to-treat analysis', Results/Participants), consistent with the primary model using all randomized participants with missing days imputed as nonabstinent.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 14/14 met. Unmet: none. All 14 elements are explicitly described: objective (Abstract), design/setting (RCT at NY State Psychiatric Institute), eligibility (DSM-IV alcohol dependence + inclusion/exclusion list), intervention/comparator (ketamine vs. midazolam dosing detailed), outcomes (primary abstinence, secondary heavy-drinking/relapse), sample_size_power (target N=60 for adequate power to detect a large effect), randomization (1:1 statistician-generated random blocks), blinding (staff blinded; participant deception procedure), statistical_methods (detailed mixed-effects modeling section), results (fully reported with test statistics), harms (adverse-effects paragraph), funding_coi (NIAAA/NIDA grants listed; no commercial COI).
- **BaseStatus**: No formal DSM-5 severity subtype or validated severity score (e.g., AUDIT) is reported. Coded 'Severe' by inference from the DSM-IV 'alcohol dependence' diagnosis (the more severe of the two DSM-IV categories) combined with the quantitative eligibility threshold (>=4 heavy drinking days/week or >=35/28 drinks/week) and observed baseline consumption (~5-6.6 drinks/day, Table 1). This is an inferential judgment rather than an author-assigned severity label.
- **Retention (%)**: Denominator = randomized N=40 (both randomized N=40 and enrolled N=50 are reported, so randomized is used). Completers: midazolam 23-6 dropouts=17; ketamine 17-0 dropouts=17 (CONSORT diagram, Figure 1; confirmed in Results/Participants: 'six participants dropped out of the midazolam group... No participants dropped out of the ketamine group'). Total completers=34/40=85%.
- **Double+Blind**: Coded 1 from two passages: 'All staff involved in this trial were blinded to treatment condition' (Study Setting) and the deception procedure in which participants 'were informed that they might receive any of several medications, in addition to ketamine or midazolam... intended to disguise what drug was specifically given' (Infusion Procedures) — i.e., both participants and staff were nominally blinded by design. The Discussion explicitly notes blind integrity was never formally tested ('we did not test the integrity of the blind'), and ketamine's dissociative effects plausibly compromised it in practice; the code reflects design intent, not verified effectiveness.
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: Coded 1 because perceived stress and mindfulness were tracked longitudinally and modeled as secondary outcomes over time ('When modeling the secondary outcomes (craving, withdrawal, mindfulness, impulsivity, stress sensitivity, and self-efficacy)...', Secondary Outcomes). The Hamilton Depression Rating Scale, by contrast, appears only as a Table 1 baseline/exclusion measure, not a repeated outcome. Borderline call resting on the stress/mindfulness constructs rather than a canonical depression/anxiety endpoint.
- **Implementation Outcome**: Coded 1 because treatment fidelity (an implementation outcome) was explicitly monitored and reported: 'fidelity to the motivational enhancement therapy manual was monitored through supervision and review of audiotaped sessions only' (Limitations).
- **Followup in months**: Longest reported follow-up is the 6-month post-trial telephone interview (partial response: data collected for 19/40 = 47.5%; Results). This differs materially from the 21-day (~0.7-month) primary-outcome assessment window; both are reported, and the field captures the longer duration per the 'longest follow-up' rule.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Table 1: Asian 5.4% + Black or African American 13.5% + Multiracial 10.8% = 29.7% (consistent with White=70.3%; 100-70.3=29.7).
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: No income, low-income eligibility, or public-insurance criteria are described anywhere in the article; only employment status is reported (71.8% employed), which does not meet the SES-description definition. Coded 0.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Sample was treatment-seeking and heavily screened: 95 screened, 45 excluded pre-enrollment (31 did not meet criteria, 14 not interested/lost contact), plus exclusions for psychiatric/medical comorbidity, current depressive symptoms, and severe withdrawal history. Coded 0 as not representative of real-world community/clinical populations.
- **AE_denominator**: 40 = sum of both randomized arms (ketamine N=17 + midazolam N=23), both of which received an infusion and were monitored for adverse effects (Results/Participants; CONSORT diagram).

### Adverse events
- **Dakwar 2020_AE01**: Results/Participants paragraph reports sedation as the most common adverse effect, given per-arm (midazolam N=12; ketamine N=8). Ketamine-arm N=8 used for NAffected (ArmScope=psychedelic_arm); midazolam's N=12 is retained in Description for completeness rather than summed. Graded Mild: transient, no intervention or persistence noted, resolved ~12h postinfusion.
- **Dakwar 2020_AE02**: Same sentence as sedation. Ketamine-arm N=6 used for NAffected; midazolam N=4 noted in Description. Graded Mild: transient, resolved ~12h postinfusion, no intervention reported.
- **Dakwar 2020_AE03**: CADSS dissociative-state score comparison (median=19, IQR 9-30.75, ketamine vs. median=2, IQR 0.25-9.25, midazolam; chi2=7.87, p=0.005) is a measured/expected ketamine effect rather than an author-labeled adverse event, so it is coded AE_type=challenging_experience per the CEQ-phenomenology guidance rather than a clinical AE. NAffected is coded '.' because the article reports group medians/IQR, not a participant count meeting a threshold. Graded Mild per the rubric's core adaptation principle (a high dissociation score alone is Mild absent intervention need or persistence); the article separately states there were 'no incidents of persistent psychoactive effects' in either group.
- **Dakwar 2020_AE04**: Authors explicitly label the event 'mild agitation' (SeveritySource=author) in 2 ketamine-group participants, lasting about 1 hour postinfusion, with no intervention reported.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'F' / 'F' / 'clean'
- **note**: Primary outcome (21-day alcohol abstinence) tested via the time-by-treatment interaction, F(1,797)=25.1, p<.001 (single-numerator-df F, directly convertible to a Hedges' g equivalent); direction=1 because the effect favors ketamine, the psychedelic arm.

## Das 2019

*Alcohol · 2019 · eligible=Y · config=pending*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Methods, 'Statistical approach' explicitly names 'Primary drinking-related dependent variables (cue reactivity, alcohol consumption)', assessed with 2(Time)x3(Group) mixed ANOVA/mixed models -- these two domains are co-designated primary, with no single outcome named primary above the other. Chose alcohol consumption (not cue reactivity) as the SUD-behavioral outcome matching this review's eligibility criterion (b) (consumption quantity/frequency) and the paper's own headline claim (Abstract: 'reduced ... long-term drinking levels'; title: 'reduce harmful drinking'). Within alcohol consumption, used the RET+KET group's own reduction from baseline to post-manipulation (Results, 'Drinking behavior': 'highly significant reductions in general alcohol consumption ... F(1,89.17) = 19.55, p < 0.001, np2 = 0.14'), i.e. the active-treatment arm's effect on the primary drinking DV, rather than: (1) the larger np2 values reported for cue-reactivity simple effects (up to np2=0.219 for post-consumption urge to drink more beer), which were not chosen per the 'primary, not largest' rule; or (2) the 3-group Group x Time omnibus interaction on alcohol consumption itself, which was only marginally significant and reported without an eta-squared [F(2,89.432) = 3.123, p = 0.049] -- noted here for transparency since it is arguably the more literal 'primary comparison' test in this 3-arm design, but it supplies no partial eta squared to record as Outcome. A covariate-adjusted version of this same interaction, explicitly labelled 'the primary mixed-model analysis of unit alcohol consumption' by the authors, is reported later with np2 [F(2,85.42)=3.719, p=0.037, np2=0.078] (Results, 'Predictive blood biomarkers of response'), but this includes achieved plasma ketamine level as a post hoc covariate not part of the original 2x3 design, so it was not used as Outcome either; it is recorded here as an alternative candidate.
- **SD**: F(1,89.17) = 19.55, the test statistic (with Satterthwaite-approximated df) printed in the same sentence as the np2 = 0.14 value used for Outcome (Results, 'Drinking behavior').
- **PVal**: Printed as 'p < 0.001' in the same sentence as Outcome/SD (Results, 'Drinking behavior'); recorded as the threshold string exactly as printed, per instructions to never convert a printed threshold to an exact value.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: Coded as 30, the number randomized to RET+KET (Methods, 'Design and procedure': 'Random allocation to the active group (RET+KET) and two control conditions (N = 30 per group)'). Flagged as an inference: the reported df_error of 89.17 for this F-test reflects the shared Satterthwaite denominator df of the joint linear mixed model fit across all three groups' longitudinal data (the article states 'linear mixed models were used to analyze follow-up data'), not the RET+KET arm's own N specifically, so the true per-arm analyzed N for this simple effect cannot be independently verified from the text; the one participant whose TLFB data were lost to technical error was in RET+PBO, not RET+KET, so 30 is used as the best-supported approximation for this arm's analyzed subgroup.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 13/14 met. Unmet: harms. Thirteen of fourteen elements are described. Objective (Introduction/Abstract hypotheses), design (3-arm, single-blind, placebo-controlled reconsolidation-interference RCT), setting (UCL and UCLH), eligibility (AUDIT>8, non-SCID-AUD, >40/>30 units/week, beer-preferring, non-treatment-seeking), intervention (ketamine infusion protocol, dosing, timing), comparator (RET+PBO and No RET+KET arms), outcomes (cue reactivity, TLFB drinking measures), sample_size_power (G*Power a priori calculation, N=78 required vs N=90 randomized), randomization ('Random allocation ...
