N=30 per group'), blinding ('single-blind and placebo controlled'), statistical_methods (detailed 'Statistical approach' section), and results (extensive) are all explicitly described. Funding/COI is described (MRC grant in Acknowledgements; 'The authors declare no competing interests'). Harms is not met: the article never describes a harms-assessment method (no AE case-report form, no safety-monitoring protocol stated) nor reports any harms results (no AE counts, no statement that no AEs occurred) for this study's own participants -- Discussion only makes general, literature-level reassurances about ketamine's safety profile ('we argue are minimal', 'widely used and safe') without describing what was actually monitored or observed in this trial.
- **RandomizationYN**: Explicit random allocation to three arms, N=30/group (Methods, 'Design and procedure').
- **BlindingYN**: Methods, 'Design and procedure': 'Drug manipulations were single-blind and placebo controlled.'
- **Double+Blind**: Explicitly single-blind, not double-blind, per the same sentence; coded 0, consistent with BlindingYN=1 (blinding was present but not double-blind, so no BlindingYN=0/Double+Blind=1 contradiction).
- **Comparator**: Two control arms: RET+PBO (memory retrieval + placebo infusion, controlling for retrieval per se) and No RET+KET (ketamine after control/orange-juice memory retrieval, controlling for ketamine's non-reconsolidation effects). Both are legitimate comparison arms against the active RET+KET condition.
- **PrimaryCare**: No aspect of recruitment, delivery, or follow-up interfaces with primary-care settings or providers; conducted entirely at a specialist psychopharmacology unit and hospital (UCL/UCLH). Coded 0 (absent), not 3, since the concept is straightforwardly answerable and simply did not occur.
- **Retention (%)**: Denominator = randomized N (90; Methods: 'we randomized N=30/group'). No separate 'enrolled' N distinct from randomized is reported. The article does not state a single overall study-completion percentage; the only explicit post-randomization completion counts given are the 9-month follow-up respondent Ns (Methods, 'Design and procedure': 'Attrition during remote follow-up left 9 month respondent Ns at: Ret+PBO=20/RET+KET=17/No RET+KET=19'). Completers = 20+17+19 = 56. Retention = 56/90 x 100 = 62.2%. Note this differs from completion of the immediate Day-1/Day-3/Day-10 in-person protocol, which the article frames separately and implies was materially more complete (the only reported deviation there is one participant's Day-10 TLFB data lost to 'technical error', not withdrawal); the Methods explicitly attach the word 'attrition' to remote follow-up specifically ('Attrition during remote follow-up'), supporting that in-person completion and follow-up completion are distinct, but only the follow-up N is directly stated, so it is used here as the best-supported study-wide retention figure.
- **ConfoundYN**: Coded 1: the follow-up linear mixed model explicitly included 'baseline alcohol unit consumption as a covariate' (Results, 'Long-term maintenance'), and the Discussion substantively addresses a candidate confound of its own design -- baseline alcohol consumption trending higher in RET+KET -- explicitly considering and arguing against regression to the mean as an alternative explanation ('we cannot rule out regression to the mean as a contributing factor ... this explanation is highly unlikely').
- **InclusionExclusionCriteria**: Methods, 'Participants': AUDIT>8, not meeting SCID criteria for AUD at screening, consuming >40 (men) or >30 (women) UK units/week, primarily drinking beer, non-treatment seeking.
- **ValidatedScaleMeasYN**: Multiple validated instruments used, including AUDIT, OCDS, SOCRATES, BAS/BIS, BDI, CEOA, DTS, PANAS, and SCID (Table 1; Methods).
- **BaseStatus**: Judgment call. The sample scored very highly on drinking quantity/frequency (74.09+/-37.92 UK units/week) and AUDIT (22.13+/-4.93, described in Results as 'denoting physically harmful drinking and moderate-high risk of developing AUD'), and Discussion calls the pattern 'clearly harmful and problematic ... equivalent to that seen in clinical AUD.' However, participants explicitly 'did not meet SCID criteria for [AUD or] severe alcohol dependence at screening' and were not formally diagnosed or treatment-seeking. Coded 'Moderate' to reflect a harmful/hazardous-drinking population that is sub-diagnostic for severe dependence by the study's own SCID criterion, rather than 'Severe' (no formal severe-dependence diagnosis) or 'Mild' (drinking levels and AUDIT scores are well above typical mild/hazardous cutoffs).
- **Age_Median**: Table 1 / Results, 'Sample characteristics': overall mean age 27.48 +/- 8.11 years (text: '27.5 +/- 8.1 yrs'), which falls in the <30 bracket.
- **PercentFem**: Methods, 'Participants': 90 participants, 55 men and 35 women. 35/90 x 100 = 38.9%.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: No race or ethnicity data are reported anywhere in the text (Table 1's baseline variables and the Participants section report only age, sex, smoking status, and drinking/psychometric measures). Coded '.' -- the concept applies to a human sample but was simply not reported.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: Coded 0: no income, employment, insurance status, or other SES indicator is reported or discussed anywhere in the article.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: University College London (UCL) and University College Hospital (UCLH), London, United Kingdom -- coded 'NonUS'.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 0: participants were recruited via open internet advertisements but explicitly excluded if formally diagnosed with or seeking treatment for AUD, and were required to be beer-preferring with a specific hazardous-drinking profile (AUDIT>8, non-SCID-AUD, quantity thresholds). This is a screened, non-treatment-seeking volunteer research sample rather than a real-world clinical/treatment-seeking population; the Discussion itself notes assessment in a treatment-seeking sample 'would have been premature' at this stage.
- **InterventionTherapy**: Coded 0: the retrieval procedure (rating/consuming a sample beer or orange juice, then unexpected withholding) is an experimental memory-destabilization manipulation, not therapy, counseling, or a preparation/integration process in the psychedelic-assisted-therapy sense; no counseling or psychotherapeutic component accompanies the ketamine infusion.
- **HarmsAssessed**: Coded 0, consistent with SafetyReported='N'. No AE monitoring protocol, AE form, or safety results are described for this study's participants; Discussion's safety comments ('ketamine is widely used and safe ... its dissociative and psychotogenic properties ... may be contraindicated for certain individuals') are general statements about ketamine's known profile drawn from the wider literature, not a report of what was assessed or observed in this trial's participants.
- **Followup in months**: Results, 'Long-term maintenance': drinking levels were compared 'post manipulation (Day 10) across follow-up to 9 months', with explicit 2-week, 3-, 6-, and 9-month assessment points and 9-month respondent Ns reported. Longest reported follow-up = 9 months, measured from Day 10 (post-manipulation/end of the single-session intervention on Day 3), which is the article's own baseline for the follow-up window.
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: Coded 0: the Beck Depression Inventory (BDI) appears only in Table 1 as a baseline characteristic with a between-group inferential test at baseline (no significant group difference, p=0.197); no mental-health scale is reported as a pre-post or follow-up outcome anywhere in Results.
- **ReductionMeasured**: Coded 1: the primary alcohol-consumption results are framed as a partial, graded reduction in ongoing drinking (e.g., 'a reduction of 23.5 UK units/188g ethanol over a week' in RET+KET; 'halved their average weekly consumption from ~84 to ~41 UK units' by 9 months), not a binary cessation/abstinence outcome.
- **CessationMeasured**: Coded 0: no discrete abstinence/complete-cessation rate is reported anywhere; all drinking outcomes (TLFB units, drinking days, binge days) are continuous reduction measures, and participants were explicitly non-treatment-seeking and not pursuing abstinence as a goal.
- **RelapseMeasured**: Coded 1: Results, 'Long-term maintenance' explicitly frames the 9-month follow-up as assessing reversion to heavy drinking/rebound -- 'Reversion to heavy drinking typifies drinking interventions. We assessed this by comparing drinking levels post manipulation (Day 10) across follow-up to 9 months' -- and reports 'no evidence of rebound to baseline levels.'
- **PhysicalChangeMeasured**: Coded 1: plasma levels of ketamine and its metabolites (norketamine, dehydroxynorketamine) were measured via gas chromatography as biological/biomarker outcomes (Methods, 'Tasks and apparatus'; Results, 'Predictive blood biomarkers of response').
- **SafetyReported**: Coded 'N': the article never describes an adverse-event assessment method (no AE form, no monitoring protocol referenced) and reports no harms results (no AE counts, no statement that no AEs occurred) for the trial's participants. Discussion's remarks on ketamine's tolerability are general, literature-level statements, not a report of what was assessed or found in this study -- per the cardinal rule distinguishing 'no AEs occurred' from 'the authors never looked/reported', this is coded as the latter.
- **AnyAEReported**: Coded 'NA' because SafetyReported='N', per the required NA-only-when-SafetyReported=N rule.
- **NumDistinctAEs**: Coded 'NA' for the same reason as AnyAEReported.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: Coded 'NA' for the same reason as AnyAEReported (the field is Integer/'.'/'NA'; 'NA' applies here since safety was not reported at all, not merely uncounted).
- **AE_denominator**: Coded '.', never 'NA' per the field's explicit rule, since no safety assessment was reported for this study.
- **ArmScope**: Coded 'NA': with no AE data reported at all, arm scope for AE values genuinely does not apply.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: pending
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Partial eta squared' / 'eta_p' / 'approx'
- **note**: np2=0.14 (F(1,89.17)=19.55, p<0.001) is RET+KET's own reduction in alcohol consumption from baseline to post-manipulation, drawn from a linear mixed model's within-group Time simple effect (Satterthwaite-approximated df), not a classical between-subjects ANOVA or a direct 2-group comparison -- treating this repeated-measures/mixed-model statistic as an input to a between-groups effect-size conversion is the kind of assumption that warrants 'approx' rather than 'clean'. The parallel 3-group Group x Time omnibus interaction on this same outcome was only marginally significant and reported without an eta-squared (F(2,89.432)=3.123, p=0.049), so it could not be used instead; a covariate-adjusted version of that interaction, which the authors explicitly label 'the primary mixed-model analysis of unit alcohol consumption', reports np2=0.078 (F(2,85.42)=3.719, p=0.037) but rests on a post hoc plasma-ketamine covariate outside the original 2x3 design and was not used as the primary value.

## Denson 1970

*Alcohol · 1970 · eligible=N · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: No single primary outcome is designated. The study ran nine comparisons (three timepoint-pairs x Treatment/Control, plus cross-group comparisons at each timepoint) across five instruments (MMPI, Lorr Scale, IPAT, Eysenck/Maudsley Personality Inventory, and a Background/Follow-up Questionnaire) and reports only counts of statistically significant differences per instrument against the number expected by chance at p<.05 (e.g. 'six statistically significant differences... out of a total of 126 comparisons, five per cent of 126 or six... could be expected on the basis of chance'). No instrument or comparison is identified as primary, no SUD-specific outcome is reported anywhere (see EligibilityNote), and the one comparison flagged as 'the only area in which positive results were obtained' (a general-health question) is explicitly deemed 'insufficient to justify rejection of the null hypothesis' by the authors themselves. Selecting any one instrument's count as 'the' effect size would misrepresent a multi-comparison chance-rate analysis as a single primary result, so this is coded '.' per the anti-fabrication rule rather than guessing among five co-equal, non-designated measures.
- **SD**: No statistic is paired with a primary Outcome because none was identified (see Outcome rationale). Coded '.'.
- **PVal**: No p-value belongs to a single primary comparison; the article reports only significant-vs-chance-expected counts per instrument, not one p-value for one designated outcome. Coded '.'.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: No single analysis produces 'the' Outcome, so no single denominator applies. For context: 51 patients were accepted overall (25 Treatment / 26 Control, derived from 'the twenty-five members of the Treatment group' and 51 total); 17 patients per group (34/51) completed 6-month ratings; 13 Treatment and 16 Control (29/51) completed 12-month ratings, with partial data from about half of the remainder. Coded '.'.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 11/14 met. Unmet: eligibility, sample_size_power, harms. 11/14 criteria met. Not described: eligibility (patients were 'referred by Saskatoon psychiatrists for LSD treatment' and 'accepted for the study'; no formal inclusion/exclusion criteria -- diagnostic thresholds, age limits, medical exclusions -- are stated); sample_size_power (no a priori or post hoc power/sample-size discussion anywhere); harms (no Adverse Events/Safety/Side-effects section, table, or explicit statement anywhere -- the promazine/chlorpromazine session-termination detail is procedural intervention description, not a harms assessment). n_met=11 < 13, so MethodologyDescribedYN=0.
- **Pos Result Y/N**: Coded 0 (null/negative). The article's own summary states results were 'interpreted as negative' and that 'the supposed therapeutic benefits of LSD treatment in alcoholism and neurosis were not demonstrated by this experiment.' Significant-difference rates across all five instruments approximated the ~5% expected by chance, and the one exception (a general-health question) was explicitly judged insufficient to reject the null hypothesis.
- **Clincial Trial YN**: Coded 1, consistent with the eligibility screen: criterion (a) is met (prospectively planned LSD administration, random allocation, defined enrollment of 51 patients over 2 years, an assigned regimen of up to five LSD sessions, and prospective outcome assessment at baseline/6/12 months). Only criterion (b) fails, which does not contradict Clincial Trial YN=1.
- **RandomizationYN**: 1. 'Patients... were allocated at random to Treatment and Control groups after undergoing a series of psychological tests.'
- **BlindingYN**: 0. 'No attempt was made to use a double or single blind procedure' -- stated explicitly.
- **Double+Blind**: 0, consistent with BlindingYN=0 per the field's must-not-contradict rule.
- **Comparator**: 1. Waitlist/deferred-treatment control: Control-group patients 'were informed that this type of therapy would be made available to them after a twelve-month waiting period,' while referring psychiatrists continued 'standard treatment' for both groups in the interim.
- **PrimaryCare**: 0. Treatment was delivered in a general hospital setting by referring psychiatrists (specialist, not primary-care, providers); no aspect of the study is described as interfacing with primary-care settings or providers.
- **Power**: 0. No a priori or post hoc statistical power calculation or discussion appears anywhere; the article discusses only the expected chance rate of significant findings at p<.05 across many comparisons, which is a multiplicity/chance argument, not a power discussion.
- **Retention (%)**: 56.9 = 29/51 x 100, using the randomized N (51; enrolled and randomized are the same cohort here) as denominator and the longest-follow-up (12-month) completers as the numerator: 13 Treatment + 16 Control = 29 of 51 patients completed 12-month ratings ('this had fallen to 13 in the Treatment group and 16 from the Controls'). Not calculated from the 6-month figure (17+17=34/51=66.7%) because Followup in months (12) reflects the longest reported horizon. Not within 2 points of 80, so no additional near-threshold flag applies.
- **ConfoundYN**: 1. The article substantively discusses a confound: baseline (time-1) differences between Treatment and Control on the Questionnaire data (Control patients more frequently reported seeing their doctor about physical complaints and that their feelings interfered with work), explicitly interpreted as evidence 'that the matching of Treatment and Control samples was not perfect,' though judged to have 'no bearing on the effects of treatment.'
- **InclusionExclusionCriteria**: 0. No explicit inclusion/exclusion criteria are stated beyond psychiatrist referral 'for LSD treatment' and subsequent 'acceptance' into the study; no diagnostic thresholds, age limits, or medical/psychiatric exclusions are given.
- **ValidatedScaleMeasYN**: 1. The test battery comprised the Eysenck Personality Inventory, the IPAT Objective Anxiety Scale, the MMPI, and the Lorr Multidimensional Rating Scale (Out-patient Form) -- all validated psychometric instruments.
- **Funding/COI YN**: 1. 'This project was supported by National Health Research Grant 607-7-68.' No separate conflict-of-interest statement appears, but funding source disclosure alone satisfies this field.
- **BaseStatus**: Coded '.': no severity descriptor (Mild/Moderate/Severe) or severity-mapping language for the alcoholism/neurosis diagnoses is given anywhere; only diagnostic category counts, which sit in the scrambled Table I and are not usable (see PercentFem).
- **Age_Median**: Bracket '30-50': 'The age range extended from 17 to 54 years with a median of 33 and a mean of 33.'
- **PercentFem**: Coded '.'. Table I (classification of subjects by group, sex and diagnosis) was flattened by the text conversion into an unattributable column of loose digits and dot-leaders near the top of the file (per the fidelity note flagging a garbled table). No digit in that column can be confidently mapped to a Male/Female/Total cell for either group, so per the anti-fabrication instruction this was left uncoded rather than reconstructed with a guessed decoding rule.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Coded '.': race/ethnicity of the sample is never reported anywhere in the article.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: 0. No income, occupational, insurance, or other SES information is reported for the sample anywhere in the text.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: NonUS. The study was conducted in Saskatoon, Saskatchewan, Canada (patients referred by 'Saskatoon psychiatrists,' data analyzed at the 'Computer Centre of the Saskatoon Campus, University of Saskatchewan'; author affiliations list the 'Saskatchewan Department of Public Health' and 'University of Saskatchewan, Saskatoon').
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: 0. The sample was psychiatrist-referred specifically 'for LSD treatment' rather than drawn from a general or consecutive clinical population, and the article gives no indication of broader community/clinical representativeness; this constitutes a selected referral sample rather than a real-world representative one. Judgment call given limited detail.
- **InterventionDescription**: Summarized from the Treatment section: oral lysergide 50-300mcg (modal 100mcg, mean 163mcg) preceded by 5mg dextroamphetamine to reduce induction-phase anxiety; up to five sessions at two-week intervals in single hospital rooms; state terminated with promazine or chlorpromazine when necessary.
- **InterventionTherapy**: 0. No preparation, integration, or psychotherapy component tied to the LSD sessions themselves is described; assessments during sessions were performed by uninvolved staff. 'Standard treatment' continuing via referring psychiatrists is described as ongoing usual care applied identically to both Treatment and Control groups, not as a therapy component added to the LSD intervention.
- **HarmsAssessed**: 0, consistent with SafetyReported='N'. No Adverse Events/Safety/Side-effects section, table, or explicit statement exists. The detail that the psychedelic state 'was terminated by an injection of promazine or chlorpromazine when necessary' is procedural intervention-delivery description (ending a session so a patient could be assessed fit to leave), not a reported assessment of harms or adverse events across the sample -- there is no count, no description of what necessitated termination, and no statement characterizing it as an adverse reaction. This is a genuine judgment call, flagged here per the instruction to avoid understating harm; it was resolved toward SafetyReported='N' because no explicit AE/safety framing accompanies the detail anywhere in the article.
- **Followup in months**: 12. Testing was planned 'at six months and at twelve months'; twelve months is the longest reported follow-up horizon, measured from treatment (baseline testing occurred 'before allocation to the groups').
- **BehaviorChangeMeasured**: 0. No use-frequency, craving, or compulsive-behavior measure is reported for either group; the five instruments used (MMPI, Eysenck, IPAT, Lorr, general questionnaire) are personality/psychiatric-symptom measures, not substance-use-behavior measures. Consistent with the eligibility failure on criterion (b).
- **PhysicalChangeMeasured**: 0. No biomarker, physiological measure, or vital-sign outcome is reported as a study outcome (the LSD/dextroamphetamine/promazine dosing details are intervention description, not outcome measurement).
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: 1. The MMPI, Eysenck Personality Inventory, IPAT Objective Anxiety Scale, and Lorr Multidimensional Rating Scale are all psychiatric-symptom/personality outcome measures.
- **QualityOfLifeMeasured**: 0. No dedicated quality-of-life instrument is used; the general-health question within the follow-up questionnaire is a single item, not a QoL instrument.
- **Implementation Outcome**: 0. No feasibility, acceptability, fidelity, reach, adoption, or sustainability outcome is discussed anywhere.
- **ReductionMeasured**: 0. No partial-reduction-in-use outcome is reported anywhere in the text.
- **CessationMeasured**: 0. No abstinence/cessation outcome is reported anywhere in the text.
- **RelapseMeasured**: 0. No relapse or return-to-use tracking is reported anywhere in the text.
- **MortalityMeasured**: 0. Mortality is never addressed as an outcome or otherwise anywhere in the article.
- **LSD**: 1. Lysergide (LSD) is the sole study intervention administered to the Treatment group.
- **OtherPsychedelic**: 0. Dextroamphetamine (anxiolytic premedication) and promazine/chlorpromazine (used to terminate the drug state) are adjunct pharmacological agents around the LSD sessions, not separate psychedelic interventions, so neither sets OtherPsychedelic.
- **SafetyReported**: N. No Adverse Events, Safety, or Side-effects section, table, or explicit statement exists anywhere in the article. See HarmsAssessed rationale for discussion of the borderline promazine/chlorpromazine termination detail, which was judged to be intervention-delivery description rather than a safety report.
- **AnyAEReported**: NA because SafetyReported='N'.
- **NumDistinctAEs**: NA because SafetyReported='N'.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: NA because SafetyReported='N'.
- **AE_denominator**: '.' (never 'NA' per convention) because no safety assessment was reported.
- **ArmScope**: NA because SafetyReported='N'; no AE values exist to scope to an arm.
- **WorstSeverity**: NA because SafetyReported='N'.
- **EligibleYN**: N. Criterion (a) is met (a genuine randomized controlled trial with defined enrollment, assigned regimen, and prospective outcome assessment). Criterion (b) fails: the five outcome instruments (MMPI, Eysenck, IPAT, Lorr, general follow-up questionnaire) are personality/psychiatric-symptom measures; no consumption, abstinence, cessation, relapse, craving, withdrawal, dependence-severity, or validated-SUD-instrument outcome is reported anywhere in the text, despite the sample including alcoholics.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Non-significant (no statistic)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: No single primary outcome or effect size is designated among the battery of measures (MMPI, Lorr, IPAT, Eysenck/Maudsley, general questionnaire); the article reports only counts of statistically significant comparisons approximating the ~5% rate expected by chance for each instrument, rather than one primary comparison's statistic, and explicitly concludes the therapeutic benefit was 'not demonstrated.' No defensible single conversion exists, and the study reports no SUD-specific outcome at all.

## Dusen 1967

*Alcohol · 1967 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Primary comparison selected as the 18-month row of Table 2 (the longest follow-up horizon reported for all groups), comparing the PRELYSERGIDE historical-control group (N=37 header) to the LYSERGIDE EXPERIMENT group (N=56 header, the pooled 1-, 2- and 3-session subjects). Value taken verbatim from the Abstract, which restates Table 2's numbers in prose: 'the average score of the control group was 4.03... and of the lysergide group, 3.97' (on the 2=worse/3=same/4=improved/5=much-improved scale). The 'Pilot Lysergide' column (3.70, N=15) was not used as the primary arm because the pilot phase was explicitly an uncontrolled, exploratory dose-finding period ('In this period we experimented with dose levels and setting to obtain maximum positive results'), whereas the 'Lysergide Experiment' group (1-,2-,3-session subjects equated on age and Mindlin Index) is the article's actual controlled comparison and the one the Results section's chi-square/Duncan's-range-test statement addresses ('there was no significant difference between any of these groups'). This is a non-obvious judgment call; a reviewer could reasonably argue for pooling all 71 lysergide-treated women (pilot + experiment) against the 37 controls instead.
- **SD**: No dispersion or test-statistic value is printed for this specific two-group (prelysergide vs. lysergide-experiment) comparison. The article states only that 'the groups were compared by chi-square test and Duncan's range test (14), a variant of the analysis of variance. At the 5% level there was no significant difference between any of these groups' -- no chi-square value, F value, or degrees of freedom are given anywhere for this comparison. Coded '.'.
- **PVal**: No exact p-value or printed threshold symbol (e.g. '<.05') accompanies this comparison. The article states directly 'there was no significant difference between any of these groups-indeed the differences did not even approach significance,' which is narrative non-significance language. Per the anti-conversion rule, this is recorded as 'NS' verbatim rather than converted to a numeric threshold.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: Coded '.' because the exact number of subjects whose scores produced the 18-month means (4.03 and 3.97) is not stated as an integer -- Table 2 gives only '% Followed' (76% for prelysergide, 63% for lysergide-experiment) alongside the group header N's (37 and 56), and back-calculating (0.76x37=28.1; 0.63x56=35.3) would fabricate false integer precision from rounded percentages. There is also an unresolved internal inconsistency in the source text: the Method section states 'We could then compare results on 28 prelysergide controls, 15 pilot lysergide patients, 29 given 1 lysergide session, 18 given 2 sessions, and 9 given 3 sessions, a total of 99 subjects' (28+15+29+18+9=99), whereas both the Abstract ('a control group of 37 women') and Table 2's own header ('PRELYSERGIDE (N=37)') give 37, not 28, for the same group (37+71=108, not 99). Per the instruction to report inconsistent numbers as printed rather than construct a decoding theory, this discrepancy is flagged rather than resolved, and no single confident integer is asserted for SampleSize_NFinal.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 12/14 met. Unmet: eligibility, sample_size_power. 12/14 criteria met. Not described: eligibility (only a brief screening statement is given -- 'the grossly brain damaged, prepsychotic, and emotionally severely disturbed were screened out' -- with no formal inclusion/exclusion list covering diagnostic thresholds, age limits, or medical comorbidities; Table 1's age range of 23-64 is descriptive sample data, not a stated entry criterion); sample_size_power (no a priori or post hoc power calculation or sample-size justification anywhere). Met: objective and design (Method opens with the study's purpose and describes the pilot/control/experimental phases); setting (Mendocino State Hospital, 1,600 alcoholics/year, the day-hall session room described in detail); intervention and comparator (dose, session structure, and the prelysergide historical-baseline comparator are all described, including the abandoned double-blind scopolamine attempt); outcomes (the 4-point Much improved/Improved/Unchanged/Worse rating scale is defined); randomization and blinding (both are described, including as abandoned -- 'we abandoned the double-blind control after running five subjects' -- which satisfies 'described, including when described as absent'); statistical_methods and results (chi-square, Duncan's range test, and Pearson correlations are named and results reported in Table 2 and text); harms ('three sessions were terminated early because of the subject's reaction-an asthmatic attack, an epileptic seizure, and a disturbed emotional reaction' is an explicit statement, even though not in a dedicated section); funding_coi ('This study was supported by a research grant from the California Department of Mental Hygiene'). n_met=12 < 13, so MethodologyDescribedYN=0.
- **Pos Result Y/N**: Coded 0 (null/negative). The article states directly 'the lysergide subjects were not noticeably more sober than those treated before lysergide,' and the 18-month mean for the lysergide-experiment group (3.97) was numerically lower than the prelysergide control group (4.03). No comparison across groups approached significance ('did not even approach significance'). The Conclusion reiterates 'we did not demonstrate increased sobriety.'
- **Clincial Trial YN**: 1, consistent with the eligibility screen: a prospectively planned lysergide intervention with defined enrollment (71 women across pilot/1-/2-/3-session groups), an assigned regimen (number of lysergide sessions), and prospective outcome assessment at 6, 12 and 18 months post-hospitalization.
- **RandomizationYN**: 0. The design that produced the reported Outcome data was not randomized: 1-, 2- and 3-session subjects were 'equated on the basis of age and the Mindlin Prognostic Index' (matching, not random allocation), and the prelysergide comparison group is a non-concurrent historical cohort. The article does describe an earlier, abandoned attempt at true random assignment (scopolamine vs. lysergide, 'subjects were to be assigned by a person outside the study'), but this was stopped after only 5 subjects due to unblinding and is not the source of any reported outcome data. Coded 0, not 3, per the boundary-case rule that an absent design element is 0.
- **BlindingYN**: 0. The planned double-blind procedure was explicitly abandoned: 'we abandoned the double-blind control after running five subjects... too small for reliable conclusions.' No blinding was in place for the data reported in Table 2.
- **Double+Blind**: 0, consistent with BlindingYN=0 per the field's must-not-contradict rule.
- **Comparator**: 1. A non-concurrent (historical) control group of 37 women 'who had been in the same program just prior to the addition of lysergide to the treatment' serves as the comparator, explicitly described as substituting for the abandoned randomized/blinded control ('To substitute for the missing control group we began following up 37 women...'). This is judged an active-comparator/historical-control design, not a design where the concept does not apply, so it is coded 1 rather than 3 or 0.
- **PrimaryCare**: 0. All treatment occurred on an inpatient state-hospital alcoholism unit (Mendocino State Hospital); no aspect of the study interfaces with primary-care settings or providers.
- **Power**: 0. No a priori or post hoc statistical power calculation or discussion appears anywhere in the article.
- **Retention (%)**: 68. Directly stated in the Follow-Up section: 'Social service field workers and our own staff attempted to trace the outcome in all subjects at 6, 12 and 18 months after hospitalization... They were able to find 68% of all the subjects.' Denominator is the enrolled cohort (the study was not randomized, so per the codebook rule enrolled N is the denominator). Used directly rather than recalculated because it is the article's own aggregate figure; the per-group, per-timepoint '% Followed' cells in Table 2 range from 61% to 80% (the 80% cell is 12-month Pilot Lysergide), so this overall 68% figure sits within that range. Not within 2 points of 80 itself, so no additional near-threshold flag applies to the value used.
- **ConfoundYN**: 1. The article substantively examines potential confounding/prognostic factors against outcome: Pearson correlations of follow-up results with age (.21), history of delirium tremens (.05), history of arrests (-.02), length of time drinking had interfered with life pattern (.17), the Mindlin Prognostic Index (+.22), and diagnosis ('Diagnosis showed no relationship to outcome'). None were found significant, but the assessment itself satisfies the field.
- **InclusionExclusionCriteria**: 0. Only a brief screening statement is given -- 'the grossly brain damaged, prepsychotic, and emotionally severely disturbed were screened out to remove some not likely to benefit or those who might be disturbed further' -- with no accompanying formal inclusion criteria (diagnostic thresholds, age limits) or a stated exclusion list beyond this one sentence. Judged not to constitute 'clearly stated' inclusion/exclusion criteria, consistent with how comparably brief screening mentions were treated in other pre-1980 articles in this corpus.
- **ValidatedScaleMeasYN**: 0. The primary alcohol-outcome measure is a 4-point scale (Much improved/Improved/Unchanged/Worse) devised by the investigators themselves with no validation citation. The Blewett Scale of Psychedelic Reactions (used) and the Mindlin Prognostic Index (used as a covariate, cited to ref. 13) are both validated instruments, but neither is used to measure the alcohol/SUD outcome itself -- the Blewett Scale characterizes the drug experience, and the Mindlin Index is a prognostic/matching covariate, not an outcome measure.
- **Funding/COI YN**: 1. 'This study was supported by a research grant from the California Department of Mental Hygiene' (footnote 1). No separate conflict-of-interest statement is given, but funding-source disclosure alone satisfies this field.
- **BaseStatus**: Coded '.'. No severity descriptor (Mild/Moderate/Severe) or severity-mapping language for the alcoholism diagnosis is given anywhere; Table 1 gives only descriptive statistics (mean years of excessive drinking, number of DTs, number of arrests) without a stated severity classification.
- **Age_Median**: Bracket '30-50', from Table 1's Total column: 'Mean years 41.4' (range 23-64), corroborated by the Abstract's 'Their mean age was 41 years.' Note that Table 1 (headed Pilot/1-Dose/2-Dose/3-Dose/Total/Range) reports baseline characteristics only for the 71 lysergide-treated women; no age data is reported anywhere for the 37-woman prelysergide historical-control group. Age_Median therefore reflects the lysergide-exposed cohort, the group with reported baseline data.
- **PercentFem**: 100. 'Our patients were all women' (opening paragraph, contrasting with the mostly-male Canadian studies), and the Abstract separately confirms 'a control group of 37 women' -- both the lysergide-treated and prelysergide-control cohorts were entirely female.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Coded '.': race/ethnicity of the sample is never reported anywhere in the article.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: 0. No income, occupational, insurance, or other SES information is reported for the sample. 'Economic resources' and 'occupational level' are named only as component factors of the Mindlin Prognostic Index scale (a prognosis-matching covariate), not as reported descriptive characteristics of the study sample.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: US. Mendocino State Hospital, Talmage, California (author affiliations; 'a hospital that admits 1,600 alcoholics in a year').
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: 1, judgment call. Subjects were routine admissions to a state-hospital alcoholism unit that 'admits 1,600 alcoholics in a year' and 'learned of the experiment from each other and from the investigators' -- a real-world clinical inpatient population rather than an externally recruited research sample. Caveat: participation was voluntary ('when they volunteered for the experiment') and the 'grossly brain damaged, prepsychotic, and emotionally severely disturbed' were screened out, which narrows representativeness somewhat.
- **InterventionDescription**: Summarized from the Sessions/Method sections: oral lysergide, mean dose 400ug (range 100-800), given to 1-4 women at a time (average 2) in a private day-hall setting with music, art, and privacy screens; 1, 2 or 3 sessions per subject, each with an assigned staff therapist present before, during and after.
- **InterventionTherapy**: 1. Each subject was 'assigned a staff member who worked with them before, during and after the experience,' subjects were 'vigorously counseled to cooperate with the experience,' and 'much that emerged in the session was worked through with staff in the days following' -- integration/preparation support beyond drug administration alone.
- **HarmsAssessed**: 1, consistent with SafetyReported='Y'. The article explicitly reports that 'only three sessions were terminated early because of the subject's reaction-an asthmatic attack, an epileptic seizure, and a disturbed emotional reaction,' and states the investigators' approach to managing such reactions -- an explicit statement about adverse reactions during the intervention, judged sufficient per the instruction that the distinction between 'no AEs occurred' and 'authors never looked' is the one thing to get right, and to avoid understating harm.
- **Followup in months**: 18. Follow-up was conducted 'at 6, 12 and 18 months after hospitalization' (Table 2); 18 months is the longest reported horizon and is reported for all three groups (prelysergide, pilot, lysergide-experiment).
- **BehaviorChangeMeasured**: 1. The core outcome scale directly measures drinking behavior: 'Much improved: Complete abstinence or only drinking very small quantities. Improved: Definite reduction of alcohol intake.'
- **PhysicalChangeMeasured**: 0. No biomarker, physiological measure, or vital-sign outcome is reported as a study outcome for the alcohol-treatment analysis (the psychiatrist 'was prepared to adjust the dose' during sessions is procedural, not an outcome measure).
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: 0, judgment call. The four written-account rating scales (A: personal-life engagement, B: naturalness of expression, C: emotional intensity, D: degree of transcendence) characterize the phenomenology of the lysergide experience itself, not a depression/anxiety/PTSD/well-being outcome instrument; no such mental-health outcome scale is applied to the participants as a treatment-outcome measure.
- **QualityOfLifeMeasured**: 0. No dedicated quality-of-life instrument is used. The informal notes on being 'more productive at home or in social relationships' and 'more stable and productive at work' are part of the qualitative follow-up rating, not a validated QoL measure.
- **Implementation Outcome**: 0. No feasibility, acceptability, fidelity, reach, adoption, or sustainability outcome is discussed; the pilot phase's dose-titration purpose is not framed or measured as an implementation outcome.
- **ReductionMeasured**: 1. 'Improved: Definite reduction of alcohol intake' is a distinct rating-scale category from complete abstinence, satisfying the partial-reduction outcome definition.
- **CessationMeasured**: 1. 'Much improved: Complete abstinence or only drinking very small quantities' directly measures cessation.
- **RelapseMeasured**: 1, judgment call. Relapse is embedded in the outcome scale's definition ('Unchanged: No fundamental change or temporary improvement and relapse') and is explicitly discussed in the Results: 'alcoholics could look very good one week and be quite drunk the next.' Coded 1 because relapse/return-to-use is an explicit part of what the follow-up rating tracked, though it is not a separately reported standalone metric.
- **MortalityMeasured**: 0. Mortality is never addressed as an outcome or otherwise anywhere in the article.
- **LSD**: 1. Lysergide (LSD) is the sole psychedelic intervention administered.
- **OtherPsychedelic**: 0. Scopolamine, used only in the brief abandoned double-blind control arm (5 subjects, no outcome data reported from it), is a non-psychedelic anticholinergic control agent, not a psychedelic, and sets no substance flag.
- **SafetyReported**: Y. The article contains an explicit statement about adverse reactions during dosing sessions: 'Only three sessions were terminated early because of the subject's reaction-an asthmatic attack, an epileptic seizure, and a disturbed emotional reaction. We learned to deal with disturbed reactions and would later have been willing to work through seizures or any other kind of reaction.' This is judged an explicit safety-relevant statement even though it is not set off in a dedicated Adverse Events section.
- **AnyAEReported**: Y, per the three session-terminating reactions described above.
- **NumDistinctAEs**: 3 distinct AE types: asthmatic attack, epileptic seizure, disturbed emotional reaction, each named once in 'an asthmatic attack, an epileptic seizure, and a disturbed emotional reaction.'
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: 3, judgment call. The text describes 'three sessions... terminated' for three named reactions, most naturally read as three distinct patients (one event type per terminated session); the article does not state whether any single patient experienced more than one of the three events, so 3 is the most parsimonious reading and is flagged here as an assumption.
- **AE_denominator**: 71, the full lysergide-exposed cohort (15 pilot + 29 one-session + 18 two-session + 9 three-session). The three terminated-session events are described in the general Sessions section covering the whole treated cohort's protocol, not restricted to a named subgroup, and no more specific AE-assessment population is stated, so the full exposed cohort is used as the denominator.
- **ArmScope**: psychedelic_arm. All three reported AE-like events occurred during lysergide dosing sessions; the historical prelysergide comparison group received no lysergide and reports no AE data.
- **WorstSeverity**: Severe, the maximum across the three ae_events (epileptic seizure graded Severe per the rubric's explicit PAT-specific severe-event list).
- **EligibleYN**: Y. (a) Clinical trial: a prospectively planned lysergide intervention with defined enrollment, an assigned session-count regimen, and prospective 6/12/18-month outcome assessment -- passes. (b) SUD outcome: the primary outcome scale directly measures abstinence/reduction of alcohol intake -- passes.
- **EligibilityNote**: NA because EligibleYN='Y'.
- **EligibilityFailedCriterion**: NA because EligibleYN='Y'.

### Adverse events
- **Dusen 1967_AE01**: Severity graded Moderate under the assumption that ending the session constituted active intervention (satisfying the rubric's 'requires active intervention... but meets no FDA seriousness criterion' Moderate band), but the article gives no detail on medical management, duration, or outcome of the asthmatic attack beyond 'terminated early because of the subject's reaction.' SeverityIndeterminate='Y' because this is a genuine under-specified-AE case per the rubric's default-Mild-unless-signal-present rule; here the session-termination signal itself supports at least Moderate, but insufficient detail exists to rule out a more serious reading, so it is flagged for human review rather than silently absorbed.
- **Dusen 1967_AE02**: Graded Severe per the severity rubric's explicit statement that seizure is a PAT-specific event that is Severe by default ('suicidal behavior/attempt, psychosis..., seizure, serious cardiovascular event, hospitalization, HPPD... or any event the authors themselves label an SAE'). SeveritySource='rubric' because this is a 1967 article -- CTCAE did not exist, and the authors used no formal grading language, only narrative description ('an epileptic seizure'). SeverityIndeterminate='N' because the rubric provides an unambiguous categorical default for this event type regardless of the article's sparse descriptive detail.
- **Dusen 1967_AE03**: The 'disturbed emotional reaction' is coded AE_type='both' because it plausibly reflects challenging-experience phenomenology (per the rubric's core adaptation principle, expected in-session distress is graded by consequence/intervention need, not intensity) that in this case had a clinical consequence (session termination). Graded Moderate on the signal that the session was actively terminated, but SeverityIndeterminate='Y' because no detail is given on the reaction's specific content, duration, or resolution beyond termination and the authors' general statement that they 'learned to deal with disturbed reactions' in later cases -- insufficient detail to confirm whether this met any FDA seriousness criterion or resolved without further intervention.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Non-significant (no statistic)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: The primary comparison (prelysergide control vs. lysergide-experiment group at 18 months) is reported only as two group means on an ordinal 4-point scale, with no paired dispersion statistic, chi-square/F value, or exact p-value printed for this specific comparison (only the narrative 'did not even approach significance' across all groups). None of the seven supported conversion methods (d, g, eta_p, F, chi2, OR, RR) applies without a printed test statistic or a group-level proportion breakdown, so no defensible conversion exists.

## Garcia-Romeu 2015

*Smoking · 2015 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: This is a secondary analysis of the parent open-label pilot trial (Johnson MW, Garcia-Romeu A, Cosimano MP, et al., 'Pilot study of 5-HT2AR agonist psilocybin in the treatment of tobacco addiction,' J Psychopharmacol, e-pub ahead of print, cited as ref [22]). The abstract of THIS article leads with the SUD outcome: 'Twelve of 15 participants (80%) demonstrated biologically verified smoking abstinence at 6-month follow-up' (Abstract; also Introduction). No test statistic or comparator accompanies that rate in this article — it is a single-arm descriptive proportion inherited from the parent trial. This article's own inferential 'headline' analysis (Results, Table 1) is instead a two-tailed unpaired t-test comparing States of Consciousness Questionnaire (SOCQ) mystical-experience scores between the n=12 quitters and n=3 still-smokers subgroups (t(40)=2.02, p=0.049; abstract: 'Abstainers scored significantly higher on a measure of psilocybin-occasioned mystical experience'). That is a within-sample predictive/mediational subgroup contrast on a non-SUD psychological measure (mystical experience), not a treatment-vs-control effect on smoking behavior, and per task instructions it is NOT coded as the study's Outcome/effect size to avoid presenting a subgroup contrast as a treatment effect. Only the descriptive abstinence rate is used for Outcome; the distinction is carried into the config proposal (method=null, confidence=excluded, tier2_category=single_arm_positive).
- **SD**: No dispersion or test statistic is paired with the 80% abstinence rate in this article; it is reported only as a raw proportion (12/15, Abstract/Results). The dispersion values in Table 1 (SEM, t, df) belong to the quitters-vs-nonquitters subgroup comparison on subjective-effects measures (SOCQ, Mysticism Scale, HRS Intensity, Personal Meaning, Spiritual Significance, Impact on Wellbeing), not to the SUD abstinence outcome, so they are not used here.
- **PVal**: No p-value is reported for the abstinence rate itself, consistent with a single-arm design with no comparator or hypothesis test on that rate. The p=0.049 in Table 1 belongs to the SOCQ quitters-vs-nonquitters t-test described above, a subgroup contrast on a non-SUD measure, and is deliberately not repurposed as the PVal for the SUD Outcome.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: 'Fifteen participants (10 males) were enrolled and completed the study' (Participants section). All 15 were included in the 6-month abstinence assessment that produced the 80% figure (12/15 abstinent, 3/15 still smoking), so N=15 is the correct denominator for Outcome.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 12/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power, randomization. Sample size (N=15, with per-group n=12/3) is clearly stated but no power calculation or 'underpowered' language is discussed anywhere, so sample_size_power is N. Randomization is never explicitly addressed for this study's own design (the text states 'no control group was employed' and that assessors/participants were 'not blind,' but never uses 'randomiz-' about this study), so randomization is coded N rather than inferred from the open-label/single-arm framing; this is a close call that determines whether n_met crosses the >=13 threshold (12 with this reading). Comparator and blinding are coded Y because their absence is explicitly stated ('no control group was employed'; 'researchers and participants were not blind to drug treatment conditions'), which per the prompt counts as the element being described. Harms is Y because challenging experiences and the absence of clinically significant AEs are described in this article's own Results, even though full AE detail is deferred to ref [22].

### Adverse events
- **Garcia-Romeu 2015_AE01**: Results: 'six participants (40%) reported at least one challenging psilocybin experience characterized by feelings of fear, fear of insanity, or feeling trapped. However these acute effects were readily managed by study staff, and had resolved by the end of the sessions. Aside from these short-lived dysphoric reactions, no clinically significant adverse events occurred during the study treatment [22].' Coded AE_type='challenging_experience' per the prompt's guidance that CEQ-sense in-session phenomenology should be flagged rather than counted as a clinical AE. Onset='in-session' because the text states the effects 'had resolved by the end of the sessions.' Severity='Mild' and SeverityIndeterminate='N' because the rubric's Mild band (transient, expected, self-limiting, resolved with staff support/reassurance, no persistence beyond the dosing period) is directly matched by the description; SeveritySource='rubric' since the authors assign no CTCAE grade or SAE label. InterventionRequired='N' is an inference: 'readily managed by study staff' is consistent with the standard psilocybin-session model of verbal/interpersonal support rather than pharmacological rescue, but the article does not specify the management method, so this call is somewhat uncertain. MeetsFDASerious='N' because no FDA seriousness criterion (death, life-threatening, hospitalization, persistent disability, congenital anomaly) is met or implied. Explicitly stated as separate from the six challenging-experience cases: 'no clinically significant adverse events occurred' — i.e., SafetyReported='Y' and AnyAEReported='Y' but only this one AE type is reported (NumDistinctAEs=1, NParticipantsWithAE=6, AE_denominator=15, all from the single psilocybin-exposed arm).

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Abstinence rate (%)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: Single-arm, open-label pilot with no control group; the only SUD effect size available is a raw abstinence proportion (12/15, 80% at 6 months) with no reported p-value or test statistic, so none of the seven supported conversions (d, g, eta_p, F, chi2, OR, RR) apply. The article's own inferential statistic (SOCQ mystical-experience t-test, quitters vs still-smokers, t(40)=2.02, p=0.049) is a within-sample subgroup/mediator contrast on a non-SUD measure, not a treatment-vs-control effect, and is deliberately excluded from the config proposal to avoid presenting a subgroup contrast as a treatment effect.

## Gent 2024

*Alcohol · 2024 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: The article headers its Results 'Primary outcome: Engagement (psychological and physical) in MBI' and the Aims state the study set out to test 'whether esketamine enhances engagement in MBI... and whether enhanced engagement has any impact on alcohol-related outcomes' -- so engagement, not the alcohol craving/consumption findings, is the stated primary outcome. Alcohol craving and consumption are explicitly analysed as secondary outcomes ('Alcohol-related secondary outcomes...were assessed using the ACQ-SF-R... and the TLFB'), so per the boundary-case rule for trials whose only SUD outcome is secondary, Outcome is coded from the primary (engagement) result and the discrepancy with the SUD-outcome-eligibility basis is flagged here. Of the three psychological-engagement statistics reported for the same analysis (group main effect F(1,26)=6.88, p=.014; time main effect F(2,56)=5.47, p=.007; time-by-group interaction F(2,52)=6.45, p=.003), the group main effect is used as Outcome/SD/PVal because it is the single-numerator-df test of the trial's primary condition comparison (esketamine vs placebo) and is cleanly convertible; the 2-df interaction and the esketamine-arm-only simple-effects t-test (t(52)=-3.29, p=.002) are alternative candidates for the same underlying finding but were not chosen. This selection among three co-equal-looking statistics is a genuine judgment call.
- **SD**: 'F(1, 26) = 6.88' is printed in the same sentence as the chosen group main-effect statistic (Results, 'Primary outcome: Engagement' paragraph: 'a significant main effect of group (F(1, 26) = 6.88, p = 0.014)'); degrees of freedom included in parentheses as required.
- **PVal**: 0.014, printed immediately after the F-statistic in the same sentence (Results, 'Primary outcome' paragraph).
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: N=28, the full randomized and analysed sample. The CONSORT flow diagram (Figure 2) shows Randomized (n=28) -> Esketamine (n=14)/Placebo (n=14) -> Received allocated drug (n=14 each) -> Completed 7-day follow-up (n=14 each) -> Analysed (n=14 each), i.e., zero attrition after randomization. This matches the mixed-model group-effect error df of 26 (28 participants - 2 groups = 26).
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 12/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power, harms. No a priori power calculation or sample-size justification appears anywhere in the article (described only as a 'pilot study' in the Abstract, with no target-N or power discussion). No adverse-event/safety/side-effect section, table, or explicit statement appears anywhere (Methods, Results, Discussion, Limitations all searched); CADSS/BSS/MEQ-30 are reported as subjective drug-effect/mystical-experience outcomes, not as a harms assessment, so 'harms' is not described. The remaining 12 elements are all explicitly described: objective (Aims), design (double-blind placebo-controlled mixed within/between-measures RCT), setting (Psychopharmacology and Addiction Research Centre, University of Exeter), eligibility (AUDIT 8-40, age>=18, fluent English, exclusion list), intervention/comparator (esketamine 115.1mg vs vitamin-C oral thin film, dosing and administration detailed), outcomes (engagement, mindfulness, craving, consumption, mood/well-being, subjective-effects measures all named with instruments), randomization (randomised 1:1, stated), blinding (double-blind design stated throughout), statistical_methods (a dedicated Statistical analysis section), results (fully reported with test statistics throughout), funding_coi (separate Funding and Declaration of conflicting interests sections).
- **BaseStatus**: No DSM severity subtype or single validated severity label is assigned. Coded 'Moderate' as an inferential judgment: the sample is a near-even split between 15 participants meeting only the study's 'hazardous drinking' threshold (AUDIT 8-14) and 13 meeting its 'moderate to severe AUD' threshold (AUDIT 15-40) (Results/Participants), and group-mean AUDIT scores of 15.3 (esketamine) and 14.3 (placebo) straddle that 15-point cutoff (Table 1). Genuinely ambiguous between Mild and Moderate; Moderate reflects that roughly half the sample meets the AUD (not merely hazardous-drinking) threshold and the esketamine-arm mean sits at/above 15.
- **PercentFem**: Table 1, Gender M:F = 8:6 in each 14-person arm; 12 female of 28 total = 42.9%.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Table 1, Ethnicity mixed:White:Other = esketamine 1:13:0, placebo 0:13:1. Non-white (mixed+other) = 1+1 = 2 of 28 total = 7.1%.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: No income, low-income eligibility, or public-insurance criterion is described anywhere in the article; the eligibility list (Participants) covers only AUDIT score, age, language fluency, BMI, blood pressure, and medication/psychiatric exclusions. Coded 0.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Study conducted at the Psychopharmacology and Addiction Research Centre, University of Exeter, UK (author affiliation and corresponding-author address). Coded 'NonUS'; actual country is the United Kingdom.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Recruitment was via social-media advertisement from the community (Participants), but the sample was narrowed by extensive exclusion criteria (psychosis/schizophrenia history, current SUD treatment, hypertension, BMI <16 or >35, ketamine-contraindicated medication, positive ketamine urine screen, withdrawal symptoms) and, per the Discussion/Limitations, 'largely consisted of hazardous drinking students' rather than treatment-seeking AUD patients. Coded 0 as a screened, non-representative volunteer sample.
- **Comparator**: Comparator is vitamin C in an oral thin film matched in form to the esketamine strips (Methods, 'Drug administration'), i.e., an inert placebo rather than a dose- or active-comparator design.
- **Power**: No power calculation, target-sample-size justification, or explicit under/adequately-powered statement appears anywhere in the article; the study is described only as a 'pilot study' (Abstract). Coded 0 (not discussed) rather than '.' because this absence is directly observable across the full text rather than merely unclear.
- **HarmsAssessed**: No adverse-event, safety, side-effect, or tolerability section, table, or explicit statement appears anywhere in the article (Methods, Results, Discussion, and Limitations all reviewed). The only safety-adjacent content is (1) generic background on ketamine's safety profile from the prior literature in the Introduction, which describes the drug class generally and not this trial's participants, and (2) a procedural note that a 'medical doctor assessed participants' street readiness before they were discharged.' CADSS (dissociation) and BSS (bodily symptoms) scores are reported as subjective drug-effect/mystical-experience outcomes tied to the study's mechanistic hypothesis, not framed as a harms assessment. Because the article never states whether AEs were monitored or occurred, this is coded as the article being silent on safety (SafetyReported='N', HarmsAssessed=0) rather than 'no AEs occurred' -- per the cardinal AE-gate distinction, this is flagged as a notable reporting gap given a psychoactive drug (esketamine) was administered to hazardous drinkers with no accompanying harms disclosure.
- **AnyAEReported**: Coded 'NA' because SafetyReported='N' -- the article is silent on AEs/safety entirely (see HarmsAssessed rationale).
- **NumDistinctAEs**: Coded 'NA' because SafetyReported='N'.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: Coded 'NA' because SafetyReported='N'.
- **AE_denominator**: Coded '.' (never 'NA' per field rule) because no safety-assessment population is defined; SafetyReported='N'.
- **ArmScope**: Coded 'NA' because no AE data of any kind is reported, so there is no arm scope to characterize.
- **WorstSeverity**: Coded 'NA' because SafetyReported='N'.
- **PhysicalChangeMeasured**: Coded 0: BMI and blood pressure appear only as screening/eligibility criteria (Participants), and the pre-dose breathalyser check is a procedural safety gate rather than a reported outcome. CADSS and BSS are self-report subjective-experience scales (dissociation, bodily symptoms), not objective biomarkers, vital signs, or CO-level measures, and are captured instead under the mental-health/subjective-effects constructs; no physical or biological outcome is reported.
- **QualityOfLifeMeasured**: Coded 0: the WEMWBS is a general mental well-being scale (captured under MentalHealthChangeMeasured), not a dedicated quality-of-life instrument (e.g., WHOQOL, SF-36); no such instrument is used in the study. Judgment call given the conceptual overlap between well-being and quality-of-life constructs.
- **Implementation Outcome**: Coded 1: 'physical engagement' (self-reported frequency/dose of mindfulness practice) is one of the trial's two co-primary engagement outcomes, an adherence/feasibility-type measure explicitly adapted from Banerjee et al. (2018) (Measures, 'Engagement').
- **ConfoundYN**: Coded 1: a formal mediation analysis (Results, 'Mediation') tested psychological engagement as a mediator of the esketamine-to-alcohol-craving relationship (paths a, b, c'), directly assessing a mediating factor.
- **Followup in months**: Two horizons are reported: from baseline (day 1) to final follow-up (day 14) = ~2 weeks (0.5 months), and from end-of-treatment/drug administration (day 8) to final follow-up (day 14), which the authors themselves repeatedly label 'seven days post-drug' (e.g., 'a significant difference between pre-drug and seven days post-drug'; Figure 2's 'Completed 7-day follow-up'). Per the end-of-treatment measurement rule, the 7-days-post-drug (1-week) horizon is used: 0.25 months. This differs materially (roughly 2x) from the ~2-week from-baseline horizon.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'F' / 'F' / 'clean'
- **note**: Primary outcome (psychological engagement in MBI) tested via the group main effect, F(1,26)=6.88, p=.014, a single-numerator-df F cleanly convertible to a Hedges' g equivalent; direction=1 because the effect favors esketamine (the psychedelic arm) over placebo.

## Grabski 2022

*Alcohol · 2022 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Year**: Received March 2021, published online January 11, 2022, and the version-of-record issue is 'Am J Psychiatry 2022; 179:152-162' / 'Am J Psychiatry 179:2, February 2022' — online-first and issue year agree, so no discrepancy to flag; Year=2022 per request.
- **Outcome**: Co-primary outcomes were percentage days abstinent and confirmed relapse at 6 months (Methods/Primary Outcomes). No single outcome is explicitly labeled 'the' primary in a hierarchy, so precedence rule (2) applies: the abstract Results paragraph leads with, and gives the most quantitative and narrative weight to, percentage days abstinent ('there were a significantly greater number of days abstinent... in the ketamine group compared with the placebo group at 6-month follow-up'), with relapse mentioned second and reported as null. The Results section similarly opens 'Primary Outcomes' with this comparison. Value taken is the ketamine-vs-placebo (pooled across therapy conditions) intention-to-treat mean difference at 6 months: 'mean difference=10.1, 95% CI=1.1, 19.0' (Results/Primary Outcomes; also Abstract). Note: the numeral-for-equals-sign artifact ('difference510.1') recurs throughout this article (e.g., 'SD510.59', 'N581') and is read through as '=' per the instruction to resolve unambiguous punctuation/OCR artifacts; the digits themselves (10.1, 1.1, 19.0) are unaffected and reported as printed.
- **SD**: No SD, SE, or test statistic is printed for this specific comparison in the main text — only the 95% CI accompanying the mean difference in the same sentence (Results/Primary Outcomes: 'mean difference=10.1, 95% CI=1.1, 19.0'). Reported as '95% CI = 1.1, 19.0' since this is the only dispersion measure given; the underlying per-arm SDs and the exact regression model output are in online supplement Table S1, which is not part of the converted text provided.
- **PVal**: No numeric p-value or explicit threshold (e.g. '<0.05') is printed anywhere in the main text for the 6-month ketamine-vs-placebo percentage-days-abstinent comparison. Significance is asserted only narratively ('significantly greater') and inferable from the 95% CI excluding zero, per the article's own stated convention (Statistical Analysis: 'If confidence intervals cross zero, this can be interpreted as a nonsignificant effect'). Per the instruction not to convert a threshold or narrative significance claim into a fabricated number, coded '.'.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: Denominator for the 6-month (24-week) percentage-days-abstinent analysis is not stated as a single number alongside the mean difference in the main text (it is in online supplement Table S1). It is derived from Figure 3's caption, which reports this same analysis's per-arm N at week 24: ketamine+therapy N=21, ketamine+psychoeducation N=22, placebo+therapy N=22, placebo+psychoeducation N=24, summing to 89 (43 ketamine-arm + 46 placebo-arm). Figure 3 is cited in the same sentence as the mean-difference statistic ('(Figure 3; see also Table S1...)'), supporting this as the correct analysis population. This differs from the N=96 randomized and N=81 who completed the trial, reflecting attrition in observed-data-only ITT analysis at the 24-week assessment.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 13/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power. 13/14 described. sample_size_power coded N: the article states only that 'the study was not powered to assess an interaction between the drug and the therapy condition' (Statistical Analysis) — a caveat about one secondary/exploratory analysis, not an a priori sample-size or power justification for the primary hypotheses, and no sample-size calculation is given anywhere in the provided text (it may appear in the separately published protocol, ref. 15, not part of this converted text). All other 13 elements are explicitly described: objective (Abstract), design/setting (double-blind 4-arm phase 2 RCT at Exeter/UCL NIHR facilities), eligibility (detailed inclusion/exclusion list), intervention/comparator (ketamine/saline dosing and therapy/education protocols detailed), outcomes (co-primary and secondary outcomes listed), randomization (1:1:1:1 block design stratified by site), blinding (staff/therapist/statistician blinding described, including its limits), statistical_methods (detailed Statistical Analysis section), results (fully reported), harms (dedicated Adverse Events section), funding_coi (MRC grant and individual author COI disclosures listed).
- **BaseStatus**: Coded 'Severe' from the Abstract's explicit author label ('96 patients with severe alcohol use disorder') and corroborated by the mean 7.3 (SD=2.1) DSM-5 AUD criteria endorsed (DSM-5 severe threshold is >=6 criteria).
- **Retention (%)**: Denominator = randomized N=96 (both randomized N=96 and screened/enrolled N=166 are reported, so randomized is used per rule). Numerator = 81, taken from the explicit statement 'Length of completed participation in the trial (from randomization to visit 10) averaged 190 days... N=81' (Results/Participant Characteristics), which is independently corroborated by summing the Figure 2 CONSORT diagram's 'completed all treatment visits' counts across arms (18+20+20+23=81). Retention = 81/96*100 = 84.4%.
- **PrimaryCare**: Coded 1 because recruitment explicitly interfaced with primary care: participants were recruited 'from primary care and secondary care drug and alcohol services' (Methods/Participants, ~5% of the sample), and some were 'encouraged to undergo a supervised detoxification in primary care' before screening. This is a recruitment/referral-pathway interface only — the trial's clinical procedures themselves were delivered at dedicated NIHR research facilities, not in primary-care settings.
- **Power**: Coded 1 on the bare underpowered statement: 'The study was not powered to assess an interaction between the drug and the therapy condition' (Statistical Analysis), which per the field rule counts as power having been discussed.
- **PercentFem**: 35 women of 96 randomized (Results/Participant Characteristics: '96 participants (35 women...)'), corroborated by Table 1 Male N=61/96=64%. 35/96*100=36.5%.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: No race or ethnicity breakdown is reported anywhere in the provided main text (Table 1 covers age, sex, site, psychiatric history, substance-use history, and DSM-5 criteria only, with no race/ethnicity row). The concept applies to this population but the information was not reported in the converted text (it may be in the online supplement, not provided). Coded '.'.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: UK trial: NIHR Exeter Clinical Research Facility and NIHR University College London Hospitals Clinical Research Facility (Methods/Participants); Discussion also references UK national ketamine-use prevalence data. Coded 'NonUS'.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 0: although 95% of the sample was recruited from the general community via social media/newspaper/radio ads (i.e., not solely from specialist clinical services), the funnel involved heavy screening and exclusion (166 screened, 70 excluded — for uncontrolled hypertension, abnormal liver tests, psychiatric comorbidity, suicidal ideation, prior substance dependence, seizure history, etc.; Figure 2/Methods), and volunteer, help-seeking, abstinence-motivated participants are not representative of the broader real-world AUD treatment population.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: No income, low-income eligibility, or public-insurance criterion is described; only end-of-study remuneration 'at a level correspondent to the national living wage' is mentioned, which is compensation, not an SES-inclusion criterion. Coded 0.
- **ConfoundYN**: Coded 1: 'All inferential analyses... included adjustment for treatment site' (Statistical Analysis), and select outcomes were additionally adjusted for baseline alcohol use or baseline depression score (Table 2 footnotes e, f).
- **QualityOfLifeMeasured**: Coded 1 for the SF-12 (12-item Short Form Survey), an explicit health-related quality-of-life instrument administered as a secondary outcome (Methods/Secondary Outcomes; results noted in Results/Secondary Outcomes as 'no differences in SF-12 mental and physical health subscale scores').
- **Implementation Outcome**: Coded 1: therapy-session fidelity was explicitly monitored ('an independent consultant clinical psychologist reviewed recordings on a weekly or biweekly basis to check adherence to the treatment protocol,' Methods/Therapy), which is a fidelity/implementation outcome.
- **AE_denominator**: Coded 96 (all randomized participants). The severe-AE subset is explicitly stated as occurring only in the active-drug arms ('Four adverse events in three participants were rated as severe... all in the active drug condition'), which implies AE relatedness/severity assessment was applied trial-wide across both ketamine and placebo arms (otherwise this active-vs-placebo contrast for severity would not be a meaningful statement) — supporting ArmScope='pooled' and a full-sample denominator rather than a ketamine-arm-only one.

### Adverse events
- **Grabski 2022_AE01**: Text states '53 adverse events in 20 participants were rated... as either definitely (N=7), probably (N=3), or possibly (N=43) related to the study drug... None... were rated as serious... and the majority were rated as mild,' with only the 4 severe events individually named. This is an aggregate, unitemized bucket for the remaining (up to 49) non-severe events; exact per-type counts and full mild-vs-any-moderate split are not given. Defaulted to Mild per the rubric's under-specified-AE rule (no signal of intervention need beyond the named severe cases) and flagged SeverityIndeterminate=Y because 'majority' does not confirm all non-severe events were mild.
- **Grabski 2022_AE02**: Named individually in 'Four adverse events in three participants were rated as severe (i.e., significant symptoms that prevent normal daily activity), all in the active drug condition (low mood, hypertension, tachycardia, and euphoria)' (Results/Adverse Events). No per-symptom participant count is given (4 events across 3 participants implies one overlap, not attributable to a specific symptom), so NAffected='.'. Severity taken directly from the authors' own three-tier rating scale (mild/severe used in-text) rather than the rubric, per severity-source precedence. MeetsFDASerious='N' because the same passage explicitly states 'None of these were rated as serious adverse events' — the study's 'severe' label (defined as functional impairment, roughly CTCAE Grade 2-3) is explicitly distinguished from FDA/SAE seriousness in this article's own usage.
- **Grabski 2022_AE03**: See AE02 rationale — same source sentence; hypertension is plausibly an acute infusion-related vital-sign change (vitals were monitored during/after each infusion) but no explicit timing is given, so Onset='unknown' rather than assumed 'in-session'.
- **Grabski 2022_AE04**: See AE02 rationale — same source sentence; tachycardia is plausibly acute/infusion-related but timing is not stated, so Onset='unknown'.
- **Grabski 2022_AE05**: See AE02 rationale — same source sentence. Flagged as a non-obvious severity call: 'euphoria' being rated 'severe' is atypical for a positively-valenced subjective effect, but the article applies its own functional-impairment-based severity definition uniformly across all four named events and explicitly confirms none met SAE criteria, so the author-assigned label is retained rather than overridden by the rubric's PAT-adaptation principle (which would otherwise tend toward Mild for an expected subjective drug effect).

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Mean difference (percentage days abstinent, %), site-adjusted intention-to-treat, ketamine vs. placebo pooled across therapy conditions at 6 months' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: Only a raw (non-standardized) percentage-point mean difference with a 95% CI is reported for the primary comparison (10.1, 95% CI 1.1-19.0); no per-arm SD, SE, or supported test statistic (d/g/eta_p/F/chi2/OR/RR) is given in the main text for this specific 6-month analysis (per-arm SDs are in online supplement Table S1, not provided), so no defensible conversion to Hedges' g is possible without fabricating a dispersion value. Excluded from Tier 1; routed to Tier 2 as a genuine two-arm RCT positive/significant finding of modest-to-moderate magnitude (CI excludes but is fairly close to the null on a 0-100 percentage-point scale, consistent with the authors' own 'proof-of-concept' framing).

## Heinzerling 2023

*Alcohol · 2023 · eligible=Y · config=pending*

### Article
- **Outcome**: The article's Study endpoints section (2.3) lists FOUR co-equal primary endpoints -- recruitment/retention, minutes of Visual Healing viewed, adverse events, and vital signs -- assessing feasibility/safety/tolerability, with alcohol-use outcomes explicitly designated secondary and 'psychedelic effects, anxiety and stress' exploratory. Per the boundary case for trials whose SUD outcome is secondary, Outcome is coded to the stated PRIMARY domain rather than the alcohol-use secondary outcome. Among the four co-primary domains, 'vital signs' produced the only statistically significant between-group primary-endpoint result and is the finding the title, abstract, and Discussion foreground: peak post-psilocybin increase in blood pressure was significantly lower for Visual Healing vs Standard (Results 3.2.2, Figure 4A). Systolic BP (chi2(1)=4.98, p=0.02) is selected over diastolic BP (chi2(1)=4.03, p=0.04) per the 'largest among co-equal primaries' tie-break rule, since both are the same primary domain (vital signs) and SBP has the larger test statistic; this tie is flagged as a judgment call. No numeric group means/SDs for the BP change itself are printed in the body text -- the only quantitative values given are the Kruskal-Wallis chi-square and p; the underlying means are shown only in Figure 4A, whose OCR/markdown conversion rendered as unusable garbled 'picture text' glyphs, so no numeric magnitude is fabricated for Outcome. IMPORTANT DISCREPANCY: this is a cardiovascular safety comparison between two psilocybin-exposed arms (Visual Healing vs Standard set-and-setting), not a substance-use outcome and not a psychedelic-vs-non-psychedelic comparison -- flagged per task instructions for human review before this is treated as an AUD treatment-effect size on any forest plot.
- **SD**: chi2(1) = 4.98 is the Kruskal-Wallis test statistic explicitly paired with the Outcome direction/finding in Results 3.2.2 ('systolic BP: Kruskal-Wallis test chi2(1) = 4.98, p = 0.02'); df=1 is printed explicitly by the authors (not inferred). The diastolic BP statistic (chi2(1)=4.03, p=0.04) and heart rate statistic (chi2(1)=2.78, p=0.10, non-significant) are alternative vital-sign parameters from the same analysis, not used here because SBP was selected as primary per the Outcome rationale.
- **PVal**: 0.02, printed exactly alongside the chi2(1)=4.98 statistic for systolic BP in Results 3.2.2 (Figure 4A).
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: 20 = all randomized participants (10 Visual Healing + 10 Standard) who underwent the Week 3 psilocybin dosing session, which is the specific comparison analyzed for this primary vital-signs endpoint ('analyses are focused on the Week 3 psilocybin session in which participants were randomly assigned to the Visual Healing versus Standard procedures,' Section 2.3). No attrition before the Week 3 session is reported (the one dropout occurred later, in Week 6), so N=20 is used rather than the 19-completer final sample.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 14/14 met. Unmet: none.
