All 14 elements are explicitly described. objective/outcomes: Section 2.3 lists primary/secondary/exploratory objectives and instruments. design: pilot RCT of a video intervention within open-label psilocybin therapy (Methods, para 1). setting: Pacific Treatment & Research in Psychedelics Program clinic, Santa Monica, CA. eligibility: detailed inclusion/exclusion criteria (Section 2.1). intervention: Visual Healing video protocol (Section 2.2.2). comparator: Standard eyeshades/music procedures (Section 2.2.4). sample_size_power: N=20 justified by feasibility/budget rationale (Methods para 1), and the study's underpowered nature for detecting clinical differences is explicitly acknowledged in Limitations ('adequately powered to detect potential differences ... are needed'), which satisfies the criterion even without a formal a priori power calculation. randomization: randomization list generated in advance and held by a staff member without participant contact (Section 2.2.1). blinding: explicitly described as absent -- 'lack of blinding with the video intervention' (Limitations) -- satisfies the criterion since description-of-absence counts. statistical_methods: Kruskal-Wallis, Fisher's exact, and mixed-effects repeated-measures models specified (Section 2.4). results: full Results section with tables and figures. harms: AE assessment protocol and Table 3 (Section 3.1.2). funding_coi: separate Funding and Conflict of interest sections both present and substantive. n_met=14 >= 13, so MethodologyDescribedYN=1.
- **Pos Result Y/N**: 1. The trial's stated primary endpoints (feasibility, safety, tolerability) were all met positively: 95% retention, no video-related AEs, and Visual Healing additionally showed a favorable safety signal (significantly smaller peak BP increase vs Standard). The secondary AUD outcome also improved: drinking days and heavy drinking days decreased significantly and were sustained through 14 weeks in both arms, and PROMIS Alcohol Use scores decreased significantly (F(4,18)=12.9, p<0.0001). No true non-psilocybin control exists, so this reflects the intervention's own pre/post direction rather than a placebo-controlled AUD efficacy claim.
- **RandomizationYN**: 1. Randomization applied to the Visual Healing vs Standard set-and-setting assignment for the Week 3 session (not to psilocybin vs placebo, since all participants received open-label psilocybin). Coded 1 because a genuine randomization procedure was used for the trial's controlled comparison, per Section 2.2.1.
- **BlindingYN**: 0. The video intervention could not be blinded to participants or the two-clinician team present in the dosing room; the article states this explicitly as a limitation ('lack of blinding with the video intervention').
- **ConfoundYN**: 0. Table 1 confirms baseline balance between arms (age, sex, race, AUD severity, drinking days, PROMIS, anxiety all p>0.05), and mixed-effects models include group, time, and group-by-time, but no covariate adjustment, stratification, or substantive discussion of confounding/moderating/mediating factors beyond the two-arm group variable itself is performed. Judgment call.
- **Power**: 1. No a priori power calculation is reported, but the Limitations section explicitly states additional 'adequately powered' studies are needed, which is a bare acknowledgment that the current pilot was underpowered -- this satisfies the field per the rule that a bare underpowered statement counts as 'discussed'.
- **Retention (%)**: 95 = 19/20, stated directly in the Abstract and Results ('Nineteen of the 20 (95%) randomized participants ... completed the 14-week study'). Denominator is the randomized N (20), which equals the enrolled/eligible N here since eligibility screening preceded randomization with no separate enrolled cohort reported.
- **BaseStatus**: 'Severe' -- Table 1 reports AUD severity as Moderate 2/10 (VH) + 5/10 (Standard) = 7/20 (35%) and Severe 8/10 (VH) + 5/10 (Standard) = 13/20 (65%); Severe is the plurality/majority category for the pooled sample. Judgment call to summarize a mixed distribution into a single closed-enum value.
- **Age_Median**: '30-50'. Abstract reports overall mean age 49 +/- 11 years for study completers, which falls in the 30-50 bracket (consistent with the rule that a mean of 48.5 maps to 30-50). Table 1 per-arm means (46.9 and 51.0) straddle the boundary but the reported overall figure (49) is used.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: 5 = 1/20, from Table 1's 'Asian & Native Hawaiian or Pacific Islander' row (1/10 Visual Healing, 0/10 Standard), the only non-White racial category reported. Hispanic ethnicity is reported separately and inconsistently crosses race (1/10 Hispanic in each arm = 2/20 = 10%), but Table 1 does not indicate whether these 2 Hispanic participants fall within the White or Asian/NHPI racial rows, so they are not added to the 5% figure -- flagged as an ambiguity per the codebook's guidance on Hispanic ethnicity crossing racial categories.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: 0, judgment call. Recruitment channels were broad (fliers, public radio, clinician/treatment-program referral, website), but the eligibility funnel was highly restrictive: only 20/55 (36%) of consented candidates were eligible, with exclusions for any other current psychiatric disorder (including nicotine/drug use disorder), most psychiatric medications, significant cardiac/hepatic/neurological/endocrine disease, and psychosis/bipolar history -- comorbidities common in real-world AUD populations. This narrows the sample well below a representative clinical AUD population.
- **Followup in months**: 1. Counseling/treatment concluded at Week 10; the dedicated follow-up assessment occurred at Week 14 (Section 2.2.5), i.e., 4 weeks (~1 month) after end of treatment. Per the rule to measure from end-of-treatment rather than from baseline/randomization (which would be ~14 weeks, ~3.2 months) when both horizons are reported, the shorter post-treatment interval is used.
- **QualityOfLifeMeasured**: 0, judgment call. No dedicated quality-of-life instrument was administered. The Persisting Effects Questionnaire includes one item on 'personal well-being and life satisfaction' (Table 5), but this is a single retrospective item within a psychedelic-effects questionnaire, not a validated QoL measure, so it is not counted as QoL being explicitly measured.
- **RelapseMeasured**: 1, judgment call. Relapse/return-to-use was not tracked via a dedicated instrument, but one participant's 'severe alcohol relapse' was explicitly identified, named, and tracked as the cause of a Serious Adverse Event and study discontinuation (Section 3.1.2), which constitutes relapse being explicitly tracked as an outcome of clinical significance.
- **ArmScope**: 'psychedelic_arm'. Unlike a typical psychedelic-vs-placebo trial, both study arms (Visual Healing and Standard) received identical open-label 25 mg psilocybin dosing -- the randomized factor is the video/set-and-setting intervention, not psilocybin exposure itself. All 20 participants in Table 3's AE counts are therefore psilocybin-exposed, so 'pooled' (which requires mixing exposed and unexposed participants) does not apply. Caveat: the Week 2 Serious Adverse Event (severe alcohol intoxication, AE23) occurred before this participant's own first psilocybin dose, but this does not change the article-level classification, which reflects the overwhelmingly psilocybin-exposed AE dataset in Table 3.
- **NumDistinctAEs**: 23 = 21 distinct AE types listed in Table 3 ('Frequency of adverse events rated as possibly, probably, or definitely related to study interventions') plus 2 additional distinct Serious Adverse Events described separately in prose (Section 3.1.2: severe alcohol intoxication requiring ED treatment; severe alcohol relapse requiring inpatient detoxification), which are alcohol-related medical events not attributed to study intervention and therefore not included in Table 3's intervention-related AE list.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: '.' -- the article reports per-AE-type counts (Table 3) and a total AE count (63) but never states the number of unique participants with >=1 AE; this cannot be derived from per-type counts without risking double-counting participants who had multiple AE types.
- **EligibilityNote**: NA because EligibleYN='Y'.
- **EligibilityFailedCriterion**: NA because EligibleYN='Y'.
- **EligibleYN**: Y. (a) Clinical trial: prospectively planned, IRB-approved, FDA IND-covered pilot RCT with defined enrollment, randomized assignment (video intervention), and prospective outcome assessment through Week 14 -- passes. (b) SUD outcome: alcohol use (drinking days, heavy drinking days per week via Timeline Followback, and PROMIS Alcohol Use Short Form T-scores) is reported as a secondary outcome with explicit statistical results (Section 3.2.1) -- passes, per the rule that trials whose only SUD outcome is secondary remain eligible. Note per task instructions: the article's STATED PRIMARY outcome domain is feasibility/safety/tolerability (a cardiovascular-safety finding drives the headline result), not the substance-use outcome -- this discrepancy is recorded here and reflected in the Outcome field per the boundary-case rule.

### Adverse events
- **Heinzerling 2023_AE02**: Elevated blood pressure is a vital-sign AE captured via the described 30/60/90/120min,4/6/7h monitoring protocol during the 7-hour dosing session; the article states BP/HR 'had returned to normal levels by the end of the seven-hour post-dosing observation period,' supporting in-session onset and Mild severity (no rescue medication given per 'No participant received rescue medications during the trial').
- **Heinzerling 2023_AE05**: Anxiety is listed as a study-intervention AE in Table 3, but psychedelic-session anxiety is also expected challenging-experience phenomenology (CEQ-sense); coded AE_type='both' per the rubric's instruction to flag rather than silently absorb such events into a purely clinical AE count. No escalation, rescue medication, or persistence beyond the session is reported, so Mild.
- **Heinzerling 2023_AE11**: Depressed mood's onset timing relative to the dosing session is not specified in the article; unlike the vital-sign AEs, this is plausibly a post-session/delayed phenomenon rather than an in-session one, so Onset is coded 'unknown' rather than assumed.
- **Heinzerling 2023_AE13**: Insomnia plausibly manifests after discharge (participants were monitored in-clinic for ~7 hours, typically 9am-4pm) rather than during the described in-session monitoring window; no explicit timing is given, so Onset is coded 'unknown' rather than assumed in-session.
- **Heinzerling 2023_AE19**: Vivid dreams plausibly occur during post-session sleep rather than the in-clinic monitoring window; no explicit timing given, so Onset is coded 'unknown'.
- **Heinzerling 2023_AE20**: Vomiting has specific narrative detail (Section 3.1.2): occurred 'at the conclusion of the opening video,' i.e., during the dosing session, supporting in-session onset; the participant was 'unsure if the video or the medication had contributed more' and chose not to view videos in Week 7, after which no vomiting recurred -- included for context though not required by any field.
- **Heinzerling 2023_AE22**: Authors explicitly label this a 'Serious Adverse Event' (SeveritySource='author'), so Severity='Severe' per rubric precedence (author-assigned seriousness overrides rubric grading). Onset is coded 'unknown' rather than a dosing-relative category because this event (Week 2, ED treatment for severe alcohol intoxication) occurred BEFORE this participant's first psilocybin dose (Week 3) -- none of in-session/post-session-acute/delayed cleanly describes a pre-exposure event. MeetsFDASerious='Y' (ED/inpatient-level treatment); InterventionRequired='Y' (ED treatment). Article notes 'no indication the relapse was due to study participation' for the participant's overall course.
- **Heinzerling 2023_AE23**: Author-labeled Serious Adverse Event (SeveritySource='author'), Severity='Severe'. Onset coded 'delayed' (weeks+) because this event occurred in Week 6, approximately 3 weeks after the participant's Week 3 psilocybin dosing session. MeetsFDASerious='Y' and InterventionRequired='Y' (inpatient alcohol detoxification admission, resulting in study discontinuation). Article states the participant 'has a history of multiple prior inpatient alcohol treatment episodes and there was no indication the relapse was due to study participation.'

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: pending
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Chi-squared' / 'chi2' / 'approx'
- **note**: chi2(1)=4.98, N=20 meets the 1-df chi-square conversion requirement and is mathematically convertible via phi=sqrt(chi2/N). Downgraded from 'clean' to 'approx' because this is a cardiovascular-safety comparison (peak systolic BP increase) between two arms that BOTH received active open-label psilocybin -- it is not a psychedelic-vs-non-psychedelic AUD treatment-effect comparison, and its direction/sign relative to 'favors treatment for AUD' is not well-defined. A human reviewer should decide whether this study's stated-primary safety endpoint belongs on an AUD-focused Tier-1 forest plot at all, versus using the secondary alcohol-use finding (no significant between-group difference; both arms improved similarly over time with no placebo control) instead.

## Hollister 1969

*Alcohol · 1969 · eligible=Y · config=pending*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Discussion states 'the choice of a six-month follow-up period appeared justified as the least biasing and most feasible evaluation period' -- the authors' own designation of a primary evaluation timepoint (precedence rule 1). Table 3 ('Six-Month Follow-Up Results in 45 Alcoholic Patients') footnotes give the mean DBS change scores directly: 'Mean change score on the DBS=48' (LSD) and 'Mean change score on the DBS=49' (dextroamphetamine), footnoted '(Not significant, analysis of variance.)'. The two-month result (Table 2, F=8.85, 1&50 df, p<.01) is NOT used as Outcome because the authors explicitly frame it as an early/transient effect superseded by the six-month analysis ('after six months the results were alike for both treatment groups' per the abstract). Table 3's categorical breakdown (Abstinent/Social/Same/Worse by arm) was not used instead because the printed dextroamphetamine-column counts (7+2+5+1=15) do not sum to the stated N=19 for that arm -- an internal inconsistency flagged here per the anti-fabrication rule rather than silently corrected or used.
- **SD**: Table 3's footnote reports only the qualitative result 'Not significant, analysis of variance' for the six-month comparison, with no F value, t value, or df printed (unlike the two-month comparison, which prints 'F=8.85; 1 and 50 df; p<.01'). Because no statistic value is paired with the six-month Outcome, this is coded '.' rather than substituting the two-month F, which belongs to a different (non-primary) analysis.
- **PVal**: The six-month table footnote states 'Not significant, analysis of variance' with no numeric p-value or threshold printed. Per the instruction to never convert 'not significant' language into a number or threshold, this is recorded verbatim as 'NS'.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: 45. Table 3 title: 'Six-Month Follow-Up Results in 45 Alcoholic Patients', with N=26 (LSD) and N=19 (dextroamphetamine) by arm (26+19=45), consistent with the text: 'Only 45 patients were followed for six months, 27 dropping out' (72-45=27).
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 13/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power. 13/14 criteria met. Not described: sample_size_power -- no a priori or post hoc statistical power calculation or discussion appears anywhere in the article. All other elements are explicitly described: objective (Discussion states the study's purpose), design and setting (Method: VA Hospital, Palo Alto, blind-controlled single-dose comparison), eligibility (Patients section: age 21-50, VA admission, recent acute alcoholic episode, residency/job stability, disqualifying conditions listed), intervention and comparator (Treatment section: LSD-25 600mcg vs dextroamphetamine 60mg, session structure), outcomes (Evaluations section: Drinking Behavior Scale and its categories), randomization ('assigned in a random fashion'), blinding (patient- and interviewer-blind procedures described, including the explicit caveat that staff would likely become aware of differences), statistical methods (ANOVA, chi-square named), results (detailed tables), harms (dedicated paragraph on agitation, seizure, vomiting, and prolonged hospitalization), and funding/COI (PHS grant and drug-supplier acknowledgment). n_met=13 >= 13, so MethodologyDescribedYN=1.
- **Pos Result Y/N**: 0 (null). At the primary (six-month) timepoint the two arms are essentially indistinguishable (mean DBS change 48 vs 49, NS by ANOVA); the early two-month advantage for LSD explicitly 'vanished' by six months per the authors. Judged on the primary comparison (LSD vs active comparator), not on within-arm pre/post improvement, because this is a two-arm comparator trial rather than a single-arm design.
- **Clincial Trial YN**: 1, consistent with the eligibility screen: a prospectively planned, randomized, blind-controlled single-dose trial of LSD vs dextroamphetamine with defined enrollment (72 patients), an assigned regimen, and prospective outcome assessment (DBS at 2, 6, and 12 months).
- **RandomizationYN**: 1. 'These medications were administered orally in a similar form, being assigned in a random fashion.'
- **BlindingYN**: 1. Patients were blinded ('patients were given as little concrete information as possible about the drugs to be tested... the actual drugs were not named nor any mention made of a control'), and the outcome interviewer was blinded ('The interviewer was completely divorced from the treatment program and had no knowledge of the treatment received by any individual patient').
- **Double+Blind**: 0, not 1, despite BlindingYN=1. The article explicitly states 'it was expected that patients might ultimately become aware that two treatments were being used and that most certainly staff members would become aware of differences in patients treated' -- the administering/attending staff during the 8-hour dosing session were not blinded, only the patient and the follow-up interviewer were. This falls short of a true double-blind design (patient + treating staff both blinded), so Double+Blind=0 while BlindingYN=1 reflects the patient- and rater-blinding that was achieved. Judgment call, flagged per the field's must-not-contradict rule (satisfied: 0 does not exceed BlindingYN).
- **Comparator**: 1. Active comparator design: dextroamphetamine 60mg oral vs LSD-25 600mcg oral, randomly assigned, administered and evaluated identically -- not a placebo but a dose-matched active-drug comparator.
- **PrimaryCare**: 0. Treatment was delivered as an inpatient psychiatric/medical protocol at a Veterans Administration Hospital by psychiatry service staff; no aspect interfaces with primary-care settings or providers.
- **Power**: 0. No a priori or post hoc power calculation, sample-size justification, or discussion of statistical power appears anywhere in the Method, Results, or Discussion sections.
- **Retention (%)**: 62.5 = 45/72 x 100. Denominator is the randomized N (72; all 72 patients admitted were randomized/treated, so enrolled=randomized here -- 'Of the original 72 patients treated...'). Numerator is the 45 patients followed at the six-month timepoint tied to the primary Outcome and SampleSize_NFinal ('Only 45 patients were followed for six months, 27 dropping out'). The one-year retention (29/72=40.3%, from 17 LSD + 12 dextroamphetamine) was not used because the authors themselves judged the one-year sample 'too small and too selected to consider in detail.' Not within 2 points of 80, so no additional near-threshold flag applies.
- **ConfoundYN**: 1. The article substantively discusses potential confounding via differential attrition: it compares background variables (Table 1) between studied cases and dropouts, noting dropouts 'differed from the former in being more often unmarried and having lower educational attainments,' and separately compares initial DBS severity between two-month completers and dropouts ('did not differ significantly... 78.5 for LSD patients; 82.2 for dextroamphetamine patients') to rule out selective attrition as an explanation for the two-month treatment difference.
- **InclusionExclusionCriteria**: 1. Patients section states explicit inclusion criteria (men aged 21-50, VA-hospital-admission-eligible, acute alcoholic episode within 2 weeks, continuous area residency for 1 year, same job for >=6 months) and explicit disqualifying conditions (history of schizophrenic reaction or severe affective disorder, CNS disorder complicating alcoholism, serious medical illness such as severe hypertension, MI, or decompensated cirrhosis).
- **ValidatedScaleMeasYN**: 0. The sole outcome instrument is the Drinking Behavior Scale (DBS), a measure the authors developed themselves for this line of research; its item selection and scoring were 'based on discussion among a number of colleagues' and later 'applied to known alcoholics for verification of the items and the scoring key,' but no reliability or validity statistics (e.g., internal consistency, test-retest, discriminant validity coefficients) are reported in this article, and the scale's full development/validation is deferred to a separate, explicitly 'unpublished' companion paper (Reference 1). Judgment call: coded 0 because no established, externally validated instrument or reported validation statistics appear in the article itself, as distinct from an ad hoc study-specific rating scale.
- **Funding/COI YN**: 1. 'This work was supported in part by Public Health Service grant MH-05144 from the National Institute of Mental Health,' and the drug supplier is disclosed ('The drugs used in this study were furnished by Mr. Harry Althouse, Sandoz Pharmaceuticals, Inc.'). Funding-source disclosure alone satisfies this field; no separate COI statement is given.
- **BaseStatus**: Coded '.'. The article explicitly states baseline severity spans the full range rather than clustering at one level: 'By any reckoning, all were problem drinkers, varying from the mildest to the most severe' (mean initial DBS 74, SD 29, range 20-174). No severity cut-points for the DBS are given anywhere in the article that would let a single bracket (Mild/Moderate/Severe) be assigned to the sample as a whole.
- **Age_Median**: Bracket '30-50'. Table 1 (Background Variables): 'Age: median 45' for both the LSD and dextroamphetamine studied-case groups (range 31-51 / 33-51), squarely within the 30-50 bracket.
- **PercentFem**: 0. Eligibility was restricted to 'Men between the ages of 21 and 50 years of age' -- an explicitly all-male sample.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Coded '.'. No race or ethnicity data for the sample is reported anywhere in the article.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: 0. Table 1 reports employment status ('Unemployed') and educational attainment as general demographic descriptors, but no income figures, explicit low-income eligibility criterion, or public-insurance-enrollment information are reported anywhere. Judgment call: employment/education alone were not treated as an explicit SES description absent an income or insurance-status statement.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: US. 'The authors are with the Veterans Administration Hospital, Palo Alto, Calif. 94504.'
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: 0. Patients were VA-hospital admissions but subject to substantial exclusion criteria narrowing the sample away from a typical treatment-seeking alcoholic population: excluded were those with psychiatric comorbidity (schizophrenia, severe affective disorder), CNS disorder, or serious medical illness, and inclusion required demonstrated residential and occupational stability (continuous area residency for 1 year, same job for >=6 months) -- criteria selected specifically 'for assuring some degree of stability for future follow-up efforts' rather than to reflect the general clinical population of alcoholics presenting for care. Judgment call.
- **InterventionDescription**: Summarized from the Treatment section: single oral dose of LSD-25 600mcg or dextroamphetamine 60mg, randomly assigned and blinded to the patient, administered in a dedicated furnished hospital room with music, under 8-hour constant attendance with two brief psychiatrist visits (30 minutes and 5 hours post-dose) for reassurance; inpatient VA hospital setting.
- **InterventionTherapy**: 1. A structured preparatory discussion preceded drug administration, aimed at reframing the patient's problem drinking ('designed to minimize guilt... he had... been hooked by an addicting drug') and encouraging use of the drug state 'as a generally introspective experience,' plus two in-session psychiatrist visits explicitly intended 'to reinforce the introspective goals of therapy.' Judgment call, flagged because the authors themselves place 'psychotherapeutic' in scare quotes and state 'the patient was not assigned to any type of psychotherapy' and that 'no attempt at psychotherapy was made' during the visits -- the study's design intentionally minimized formal psychotherapy (per the abstract, 'little associated psychotherapeutic intervention') to isolate the drug's effect. Coded 1 because a preparatory/integration-oriented component beyond bare drug administration is nonetheless described, but this is explicitly a minimal, non-formal version of it.
- **HarmsAssessed**: 1, consistent with SafetyReported='Y'. A dedicated paragraph in Results describes adverse events (agitation, chlorpromazine rescue, grand mal seizure, vomiting) and a further paragraph addresses the 'prolonged LSD reaction' question directly, including one patient's extended hospitalization for confusion. Two other patients' extended stays are explicitly attributed to arranging 'housing and other social considerations' rather than a medical/drug effect and were therefore not coded as adverse events in the ae_events array.
- **Followup in months**: 12. The article reports outcome data at 2 months, 6 months, and 1 year (Table 4; 17 LSD and 12 dextroamphetamine patients followed to 1 year), making 12 months the longest reported follow-up horizon, even though the authors judge the 1-year subsample 'too small and too selected to consider in detail.' Measured from the single treatment day (no separate baseline-vs-end-of-treatment distinction applies, since the intervention was a single-day dose).
- **BehaviorChangeMeasured**: 1. The Drinking Behavior Scale directly assesses drinking habits (use frequency/severity), social behavior, and occupational adjustment.
- **PhysicalChangeMeasured**: 0. No biomarker, physiological measure, or vital-sign outcome is reported as a study outcome; vital-sign/medical details (chlorpromazine administration, seizure) are adverse-event/intervention-delivery information, not outcome measures.
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: 0. No depression, anxiety, PTSD, or general well-being scale is used as an outcome; the DBS assesses drinking habits, social behavior, and occupational adjustment specifically, not general mental-health symptomatology.
- **QualityOfLifeMeasured**: 0. No dedicated quality-of-life instrument is used anywhere in the study.
- **Implementation Outcome**: 0. No feasibility, acceptability, fidelity, reach, adoption, or sustainability outcome is discussed.
- **ReductionMeasured**: 1. The DBS classification includes a 'social' category (DBS score of 10 or less, distinct from full abstinence) representing a partial reduction in drinking-related impairment as a distinct outcome category from 'abstinent.'
- **CessationMeasured**: 1. The DBS classification includes an 'abstinent' category (a zero DBS score), directly measuring complete cessation.
- **RelapseMeasured**: 1. Follow-up interviews explicitly tracked worsening/return-to-use ('worse' DBS category at each follow-up), and the Discussion explicitly names relapse: 'those observed at two months were somewhat better, with more tendency toward relapse at the six-month period.'
- **MortalityMeasured**: 1. 'Five of the original 72 patients died within a year of entering the study... from some complications of alcohol (accidents, suicide, or decompensated cirrhosis).' Deaths are reported as a study-period finding; per the field rule this counts as 1 and generates a corresponding ae_events row (Hollister 1969_AE01).
- **LSD**: 1. Lysergic acid diethylamide (LSD-25), 600mcg, is one of the two randomized study interventions.
- **OtherPsychedelic**: 0. Dextroamphetamine is the trial's other randomized arm, but per the task instructions it is not one of the nine keyed psychedelic substances and is not itself a psychedelic comparator (it is a stimulant), so it sets no substance flag. It is not routed to OtherPsychedelic because that column is reserved for psychedelic compounds outside the eight named ones, and dextroamphetamine is not a psychedelic at all.
- **MultiplePsychedelic**: 0. Only one of the nine psychedelic substance flags (LSD) is set; dextroamphetamine, the comparator, is not a psychedelic and does not count toward this field.
- **SafetyReported**: Y. Results contains a dedicated paragraph on drug-experience tolerability (agitation, chlorpromazine use, seizure, vomiting) and a further paragraph specifically addressing post-discharge safety ('prolonged LSD reaction', extended hospitalizations).
- **AnyAEReported**: Y. Multiple distinct adverse events are described (agitation, seizure, vomiting, prolonged confusion/hospitalization, deaths).
- **NumDistinctAEs**: 5 distinct AE types identified: agitation (incl. chlorpromazine-requiring subset), grand mal seizure, vomiting, prolonged confusion/extended hospitalization, and death. The two patients whose extended stay was attributed to housing/social arrangements (not a symptom or drug reaction) were not counted as a distinct AE type.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: Coded '.' rather than a summed total. The article does not provide a single unduplicated count of patients with >=1 AE: 'occasionally' (agitation) is not numbered, the seizure patient is very likely one of the two chlorpromazine-treated agitation patients (event described as occurring 'during one of these periods of agitation'), and death, vomiting, and hospitalization counts are separate and possibly overlapping subsets. Summing the printed per-event counts would double-count and fabricate a total the article never states.
- **AE_denominator**: 72. All 72 randomized patients received their assigned single-dose treatment ('Of the original 72 patients treated...'); the safety narrative is not restricted to a smaller assessed subgroup.
- **ArmScope**: pooled. The agitation/seizure/vomiting paragraph describes 'these patients' generically without disaggregating by LSD vs dextroamphetamine arm, and the treatment-day protocol (dosing, attendance, psychiatrist visits) was identical for both arms. The 'prolonged LSD reaction' paragraph is framed around addressing public concern specifically associated with LSD, but does not explicitly restrict the discharge/hospitalization figures to the LSD arm alone, and the deaths are likewise not attributed to a single arm. Judgment call, resolved toward 'pooled' because no AE value in the article is explicitly restricted to only the psychedelic-exposed subgroup.
- **WorstSeverity**: Severe. Maximum severity across ae_events (death, AE01; seizure, AE03; hospitalization for confusion, AE05).
- **EligibleYN**: Y. Criterion (a): a prospectively planned, randomized, blind single-dose trial with defined enrollment (72 patients), an assigned regimen (LSD-25 or dextroamphetamine), and prospective outcome assessment at 2, 6, and 12 months -- clearly a clinical trial. Criterion (b): the Drinking Behavior Scale directly measures drinking-related outcomes (abstinence, social/problem drinking categories), satisfying the substance-use-outcome requirement. Both criteria met.

### Adverse events
- **Hollister 1969_AE01**: Attribution to the study drugs is not established by the authors -- they note 'such high mortality rates are common in long-term observations of alcoholics' and attribute 4 of 5 deaths to alcohol-related causes (accidents, suicide, decompensated cirrhosis) rather than to LSD or dextroamphetamine specifically, and the arm distribution of the 5 deaths is not reported. Included as an AE per the field rule that deaths reported during a trial's follow-up period generate an ae_events row and set MortalityMeasured=1, regardless of causal attribution, per the instruction that understating harm is the cardinal AE error. Severity=Severe reflects the rubric's death=Grade-5-equivalent classification (SeveritySource='rubric'), not an author-assigned CTCAE grade, since CTCAE did not exist in 1969 and the authors did not apply formal seriousness language to these deaths.
- **Hollister 1969_AE02**: Merged the 'occasionally, moderate agitation' description and the 'two patients... intramuscular chlorpromazine' detail into one 'agitation' AE type rather than splitting into two rows, since both describe the same underlying event (agitation during the dosing session) at varying severity/count granularity, and the article does not provide a total count separate from the two-patient chlorpromazine subset. Severity graded Moderate because a subset required active pharmacological intervention (chlorpromazine), per the rubric's 'requires active intervention... but meets no FDA seriousness criterion' band.
- **Hollister 1969_AE03**: Graded Severe per the rubric's explicit listing of seizure as a PAT-specific severe event, regardless of the article's own note that it occurred 'without injury.' SeveritySource='rubric' because the authors describe the event factually without assigning it a seriousness label themselves.
- **Hollister 1969_AE05**: The authors' own words are 'Moderate confusion was present in the third patient, requiring hospitalization for some further 48 hours' -- author-supplied severity language ('moderate') that would normally support SeveritySource='author' per the pre-1980 provision. However, the rubric's FDA-seriousness tiebreak ('Inpatient hospitalization or prolongation of existing hospitalization... overrides the grade-based band upward, never downward') requires this event be coded Severe despite the authors' 'moderate' descriptor, because the confusion caused a prolongation of hospitalization. SeveritySource is therefore coded 'rubric' to reflect that the final band was set by applying the seriousness override rather than adopting the author's descriptor directly -- flagged here as a non-obvious severity call. The two other patients whose stay was extended for housing/social reasons were excluded from this event and from the ae_events array entirely, since the article attributes their delay explicitly to non-medical logistics, not to a drug effect or symptom.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: pending
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Non-significant (no statistic)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: The primary (six-month) outcome comparison is reported only as a non-significant ANOVA result without a printed F value ('Not significant, analysis of variance'), so no numeric statistic exists to convert. The two-month result (F=8.85, 1&50 df, p<.01) is a real, convertible F statistic, but it is not the study's designated primary outcome -- the authors explicitly identify six months as 'the least biasing and most feasible evaluation period' and report that the two-month difference had vanished by then -- so substituting it would misrepresent a superseded early/transient finding as the primary effect. Table 3's categorical (Abstinent/Social/Same/Worse) breakdown was also not used because the printed dextroamphetamine-arm counts (7+2+5+1=15) do not sum to the stated N=19, an internal inconsistency in the OCR'd table that precludes a defensible computed_or.

## Jensen 1963

*Alcohol · 1963 · eligible=Y · config=pending*

### Article
- **Outcome**: The article's Results section reports a single explicit inferential test: 'A chi-square test showed that significantly more of the alcoholics treated with LSD were dry or improved at the time of follow-up, than patients receiving group therapy alone or of the controls.' The dichotomization actually tested is dry-or-improved vs. unimproved. Counting: LSD-treated dry-or-improved = 39+7=46 of 62 followed up = 74.2% (rounds to 74%); controls dry-or-improved = 8+4=12 of 29 followed up = 41.4% (rounds to 41%). The article also separately breaks out a dry-only rate (39/62=63% LSD vs 8/29=27% control), but this narrower category is a descriptive component, not itself the category the printed chi-square test evaluated; the dry-or-improved dichotomy was chosen as Outcome because it is the one the authors' own stated inferential test addresses. This is a non-obvious judgment call — a reviewer could reasonably prefer the dry-only breakdown (63% vs 27%) instead.
- **SD**: No chi-square statistic value or degrees of freedom is printed anywhere in the article; the authors state only that 'a chi-square test showed' the group difference, followed by a parenthetical p-value notation (captured in PVal). With no numeric test statistic to report, SD is coded '.'.
- **PVal**: Printed exactly as '(P> .01)' immediately after the chi-square sentence. Transcribed verbatim as '>.01' per the non-fabrication rule against constructing a decoding theory for a number that looks internally inconsistent: the prose claims the LSD group was 'significantly' better, which conventionally implies P<.01, yet the printed inequality reads P>.01 (greater than, not less than). This is flagged as a likely typographical/OCR inversion of '<' vs '>', but the value is reported exactly as printed rather than silently corrected.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: 91 = 62 (LSD-treated patients followed up 6-18 months, of the 70 who received the full LSD programme, 8 lost to follow-up) + 29 (controls followed up, of 55 total, 26 lost to follow-up). This is the combined denominator underlying the 74%-vs-41% dry-or-improved comparison used in Outcome.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 10/14 met. Unmet: eligibility, sample_size_power, blinding, funding_coi. Met: objective (report of LSD-25 as adjuvant to psychotherapy for chronic alcoholics), design (2-month inpatient programme culminating in a single LSD session, compared against a non-LSD control group), setting (Saskatchewan Hospital, Weyburn, Alcoholic Unit), intervention (dosage, timing, therapist staffing described in detail), comparator (controls were patients admitted when no beds were available for the LSD group, receiving individual treatment by other psychiatrists), outcomes (dry/improved/unimproved categories with percentages), randomization (the text explains how the non-random control group arose — bed availability, not deliberate allocation — which is judged to satisfy 'described' even though the mechanism was not randomization), statistical_methods (chi-square test named), results (percentages and outcome counts given for both groups), and harms (drug effects — anxiety, nausea, tension, headache, confusion/amnesia — are explicitly named and discussed, even though reframed as therapeutic rather than tallied as formal AEs). Not met: eligibility (only a referral-source policy is described, 'their most severe or chronic cases,' with no formal inclusion/exclusion list), sample_size_power (no power calculation or sample-size justification anywhere), blinding (no statement about blinding, presence or absence, anywhere in the article), funding_coi (no funding source or conflict-of-interest statement found). n_met=10 < 13, so MethodologyDescribedYN=0.
- **Pos Result Y/N**: Coded 1. The article states directly that 'significantly more of the alcoholics treated with LSD were dry or improved at the time of follow-up, than patients receiving group therapy alone or of the controls' — both the direction (74% vs 41% dry-or-improved) and the authors' own characterization support a positive result, independent of the printed P-value's ambiguous inequality direction (see PVal).
- **Clincial Trial YN**: 1, consistent with the eligibility screen: a prospectively planned single LSD-25 administration ('part of a research programme carried out in Saskatchewan') delivered under a defined 2-month inpatient protocol with an assigned regimen (200 gamma, last week of stay) and prospective 6-18 month outcome assessment.
- **RandomizationYN**: 0. The control group was formed by administrative circumstance ('patients admitted to the hospital at a time when no beds were available to the group'), not randomization. This is a genuine absence of randomization, not an inapplicable design element, so 0 rather than 3.
- **BlindingYN**: 0. No blinding of any kind (patient, therapist, or rater) is described or implied anywhere in the article; treatment was open-label by nature (a therapist remains with the patient throughout the LSD session).
- **Comparator**: 1. A non-randomized comparator group of 55 patients exists — those admitted when no beds were available for the LSD programme, who instead 'received individual treatment by other psychiatrists.' This is a naturalistic/administrative comparator, not a randomized or matched control, which is noted here as a design limitation.
- **Power**: 0. No a priori or post hoc power discussion appears anywhere in the article.
- **Retention (%)**: 72.8 = (62 LSD followed-up + 29 control followed-up) / (70 LSD enrolled + 55 control enrolled) x 100 = 91/125 = 72.8%. The study is not randomized, so the enrolled N (not a randomized N) is used as the denominator per convention. Component counts ('eight were lost to follow-up' of 70; '26 lost to follow-up' of 55) are given directly in the Results section.
- **ConfoundYN**: 0. The article notes that referred patients were generally severe cases ('their policy has been to refer their most severe or chronic cases to the Saskatchewan Hospital'), which is background/prognostic framing for the treated population as a whole, but no statistical adjustment, stratification, or explicit comparison of baseline severity between the LSD and control groups is performed or discussed. Judgment call — a reviewer could argue this framing constitutes a substantive discussion of a potential confound.
- **InclusionExclusionCriteria**: 0. Only a referral-source policy is described (severity-based triage from the Alcoholic Counselling Centre in Regina); no formal inclusion or exclusion criteria (diagnostic criteria, comorbidity exclusions, age limits) for study entry are stated.
- **ValidatedScaleMeasYN**: 0. Outcome is classified by follow-up interview into dry/improved/unimproved categories devised by the authors; no named validated scale or psychometric instrument (e.g., AUDIT, ASI) is used.
- **Funding/COI YN**: 0. No funding source, grant support, or conflict-of-interest disclosure appears anywhere in the article text; only the presentation venue (Third World Congress of Psychiatry, 1961) and author affiliation are given.
- **BaseStatus**: Coded 'Severe'. The Results section states the treated sample 'would be considered to have a very serious prognosis by all ordinary means of evaluation': 87% had failed Alcoholics Anonymous at least once, 24% were separated or divorced, and 57% had received prior psychiatric treatment, with patients specifically referred as the hospital's 'most severe or chronic cases.'
- **Age_Median**: Bracket '30-50'. 'Their age varied from 24 to 65, most being between 30 and 45 and the mean age being 39.3' — the mean of 39.3 falls within 30-50.
- **PercentFem**: Coded '.'. No aggregate percent-female figure is reported anywhere. All five illustrative case histories describe male patients ('he'/'him' throughout), which is suggestive but not a substitute for a reported sample-wide percentage; not inferred as 0 to avoid fabricating an unreported number.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Coded '.'. Race/ethnicity of the sample is never reported in aggregate; individual case histories mention ancestry incidentally ('English background', 'Irish sign painter') but no BIPOC percentage is given.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: NonUS. Treatment delivered at the Saskatchewan Hospital, Weyburn, Saskatchewan, Canada.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: 1, judgment call. Patients were routine referrals from the Alcoholic Counselling Centre in Regina and direct hospital admissions rather than research-recruited volunteers, reflecting a real clinical referral pathway. However, the stated referral policy specifically channels 'their most severe or chronic cases' to this hospital, which is itself a form of severity-based selection rather than a representative cross-section of all alcoholics in care; flagged for reviewer consideration.
- **HarmsAssessed**: 1, required to agree with SafetyReported='Y' (see that entry for the underlying judgment call).
- **SafetyReported**: Y, non-obvious judgment call. There is no dedicated Adverse-Events/Safety section, table, or systematic per-patient AE tally. However, the article explicitly names and discusses a category of drug effects as 'side effects of the drug — anxiety, nausea, tension, headache' (Abreaction section), and separately states that doses above 200 gamma produce 'confusion or amnesia' that is 'undesirable and sometimes even disturbing to the patient' (Treatment Programme section). Per the cardinal rule to distinguish 'no AEs occurred' and 'the authors never looked' from cases where effects were in fact observed and discussed, this narrative discussion is judged sufficient to satisfy the SafetyReported gate, even though the effects are reframed as therapeutic/expected rather than formally tallied as adverse events. A stricter reviewer could instead code 'N' given the absence of any formal harms-assessment methodology.
- **Followup in months**: 18. The systematic cohort follow-up window used for the quantitative Outcome is stated as 'followed up between six and 18 months after discharge' for both the LSD-treated (N=70) and control (N=55) groups; 18 (the upper bound) is used as the longest systematic follow-up. Individual case histories separately report longer anecdotal single-patient follow-ups (e.g., Case 3: 'remained sober for two years'), but these fall outside the systematic 6-18-month cohort window used for the reported percentages, so they are not used to extend this field.
- **PhysicalChangeMeasured**: 0. No biomarkers, physiological measures, or vital signs are reported as a measured outcome domain. The drug-effect discussion (confusion, amnesia, nausea, headache) describes intervention side effects captured under HarmsAssessed/AE fields, not a physical outcome measure administered as part of the study's outcome assessment.
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: 0, non-obvious judgment call. The article extensively narrates psychological and spiritual transformation (the 'transcendental experience,' surrender, humility, self-acceptance) as the therapeutic mechanism of the LSD session, but this is qualitative/narrative description of process, not a formally scored mental-health outcome instrument (e.g., a depression or anxiety scale) administered and reported as a study outcome.
- **ReductionMeasured**: 1. The 'improved' category — 'were drinking, but definitely less than before' (7 LSD-treated, 4 control) — is a distinct partial-reduction outcome, separate from complete cessation.
- **CessationMeasured**: 1. 'Remained dry continuously since discharge' is the complete-cessation outcome category (39 LSD-treated, 8 control).
- **RelapseMeasured**: 1, judgment call. The dry classification explicitly accounts for 'a short testing bout of drinking immediately after discharge,' and the unimproved category tracks continued/returned drinking, indicating that return-to-use was tracked as part of follow-up classification, even though no distinct time-to-relapse metric is reported.
- **EligibleYN**: Y. (a) Clinical trial: a prospectively planned single LSD-25 administration under a defined 2-month inpatient research programme, with an assigned regimen and prospective 6-18 month outcome assessment against a comparator group — passes. (b) SUD outcome: drinking status (dry/improved/unimproved) is the article's central reported outcome — passes.
- **EligibilityNote**: NA because EligibleYN='Y'.
- **EligibilityFailedCriterion**: NA because EligibleYN='Y'.

### Adverse events
- **Jensen 1963_AE01**: SeverityIndeterminate='Y'. The article states only that higher-than-routine doses (>200 gamma) produce 'confusion or amnesia' that is 'undesirable and sometimes even disturbing,' with no information on duration, whether it resolved within the session or persisted, or whether any intervention was needed. Absent such signals, defaulted to Mild per the under-specified-AE rule, flagged indeterminate rather than silently absorbed into a confident grade. Onset coded 'post-session-acute' because the text frames the difficulty as arising 'later, when he is trying to establish for himself the reality of the experience,' i.e. after the acute drug effect, though this too is imprecise.
- **Jensen 1963_AE02**: AE_type coded 'challenging_experience' rather than plain 'AE' because the anxiety described is explicitly framed by the authors as expected, in-session phenomenology tied to the therapeutic abreaction and 'surrender' process, consistent with the rubric's guidance that expected in-session anxiety/fear during dosing is CEQ-sense phenomenology graded by consequences rather than intensity. No intervention or persistence is described, and the text explicitly notes a 'release of emotional tension' following the abreaction, supporting a confident Mild grade (not indeterminate).
- **Jensen 1963_AE03**: Grouped by the authors with anxiety/tension/headache as 'side effects of the drug' occurring around the abreaction phase; no count or duration given beyond the implied in-session, transient nature of abreaction-related symptoms, supporting Mild with no intervention.
- **Jensen 1963_AE04**: Same grouped 'side effects' passage as AE02/AE03/AE05; the text explicitly states this resolves with 'a definite release of emotional tension' after abreaction, supporting a confident Mild, in-session grade.
- **Jensen 1963_AE05**: Same grouped 'side effects' passage; headache is listed alongside anxiety, nausea, and tension with no further detail, but the shared context (transient, early-session, resolves with abreaction) supports Mild without needing an indeterminate flag.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: pending
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Two-group proportions (%)' / 'OR' / 'approx'
- **note**: Article reports dry-or-improved status at 6-18-month follow-up for 46/62 (74%) LSD-treated patients vs 12/29 (41%) non-randomized controls, the exact dichotomy the authors' own chi-square test evaluated ('significantly more...were dry or improved'), though no chi-square value was printed (only a p-value notation, itself internally ambiguous — see PVal rationale). Per instruction to compute an odds ratio from two-group proportions where the counts support one: OR = (46x17)/(16x12) = 782/192 = 4.07, oriented toward the LSD-favorable direction (no sign flip needed). Confidence is 'approx' rather than 'clean' because this is a proportion-to-odds-ratio conversion from a non-randomized (bed-availability) comparator, which the framework explicitly flags as an approx-triggering assumption.

## Jensen 2024

*Alcohol · 2024 · eligible=Y · config=pending*

### Article
- **Citation/Title**: This is a Research Square preprint (posted August 23, 2024, DOI 10.21203/rs.3.rs-4947184/v1), not peer reviewed at the time of the version extracted; the article's own header states 'Preprints are preliminary reports that have not undergone peer review.' Coded exactly what the preprint reports, per instruction, with preprint status flagged here rather than altering the citation string.
- **Outcome**: No single sentence in Methods designates one named 'primary outcome,' but the Abstract's Results paragraph leads with the SUD-behavioral finding ('Alcohol consumption significantly decreased... Heavy drinking days were reduced by 37.5 percentage points'), and the Results section 'Clinical outcomes' reports this identical contrast with an author-computed standardized effect size: 'The percentage of heavy drinking days decreased from baseline to week 12 by -37.5% (95% CI, -61.1, -13.9, p = .005, Cohen's d = 1.02).' Per field precedence (SUD-behavioral outcome the abstract leads with) and the instruction to prefer a numeric effect size, used the author-reported Cohen's d = 1.02 for this baseline-to-week-12 contrast rather than the raw -37.5 percentage-point mean difference, since Cohen's d is directly stated in the same sentence and is the field's preferred numeric form.
- **SD**: No separate t/F/chi-squared statistic is reported for this contrast (the linear mixed model's effect size is reported directly as Cohen's d, not as a t-statistic). The only paired dispersion measure given in the same sentence is the 95% CI on the raw mean difference (-61.1, -13.9 percentage points); reported this CI and flagged that it is on the raw-difference scale, not a standard error on the d value itself, since no SE/CI for d was separately reported.
- **PVal**: p = .005, same sentence as Outcome/SD (Results, 'Clinical outcomes').
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: All ten enrolled participants completed the psilocybin therapy course (Results, 'Feasibility...': 'All ten participants completed the psilocybin therapy course'). Methods, 'Statistical analysis' states missing clinical-outcome data (due to relapse) were imputed via worst-observed-value carried forward rather than excluded, so the week-12 linear-mixed-model analysis retained all n=10; Results also references 'the 10 participants' as the denominator for individual-level response counts at week 4/week 12.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 13/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power. 13 of 14 elements are explicitly described. Comparator, randomization and blinding are each described as absent ('open-label, single-group' design stated in Methods; Discussion names 'lack of a control group... and an open-label design' as a limitation), satisfying the described-including-as-absent rule. Setting is detailed (Copenhagen University Clinic for Psychedelic Research, recruitment, session structure). sample_size_power is the sole unmet criterion: no a priori power calculation or formal sample-size justification is reported anywhere; the only sample-size-related comment is a Discussion aside that the study could not assess a sex effect 'due to sample size limitations,' which addresses a secondary/exploratory subgroup question rather than describing how the overall sample size or power for the primary analysis was determined, so it was judged insufficient to satisfy this specific criterion.
- **RandomizationYN**: Investigator-initiated 'open-label, single-group' design (Methods, 'Study design and overview'); no randomization was performed or discussed as absent-by-design. Per the v1.2 single-arm boundary rule, coded 0 (absent), not 3.
- **BlindingYN**: Open-label study; Discussion explicitly lists 'an open-label design' among key limitations. Coded 0 per the single-arm boundary rule, not 3.
- **Double+Blind**: Consistent with BlindingYN = 0; the study is open-label throughout, not double-blind.
- **Comparator**: No control or comparison arm; Discussion states 'lack of a control group' as a key limitation. Only one fixed dose (25 mg) was administered to all participants, so there is no high-vs-low dose comparator design either. Coded 0, not 3, per the single-arm boundary rule.
- **PrimaryCare**: No mention of primary-care settings or providers; all sessions took place at the Copenhagen University Clinic for Psychedelic Research, a specialised psychiatric research clinic. The concept is not inapplicable to a clinical AUD trial in general, so coded 0 (absent) rather than 3.
- **Power**: No a priori or post hoc power calculation is reported for the study's primary analyses anywhere in Methods, Results, or Discussion. The Discussion's 'Due to sample size limitations, we were unable to ascertain if sex influenced the effects of psilocybin' pertains specifically to an exploratory sex-subgroup question, not a general statement that the primary analysis was underpowered, so it was judged not to meet the 'bare statement of underpowered' bar for this field. Coded 0; flagged as a borderline call given the Discussion's sample-size comment.
- **Retention (%)**: Study is not randomized, so denominator is the enrolled sample. Participants section: 10 were 'ultimately included' (enrolled); Results, 'Feasibility...': 'All ten participants completed the psilocybin therapy course.' Retention = 10/10 x 100 = 100.
- **ConfoundYN**: Coded 1 on both statistical-adjustment and substantive-discussion grounds: the linear mixed model explored whether Cmax, AUCPPC, AUCSDI and MEQ30 score were associated with the clinical outcome as covariates (Statistical analysis), Spearman correlations tested baseline factors (age, weight, BMI, liver/kidney function) against PK/PD parameters (Table S4), and the Discussion explicitly names candidate confounds not otherwise controlled: 'selection bias due to self-referral and high expectations from positive widespread media coverage may have influenced, at least in part, the observed positive effects.'
- **BaseStatus**: Results, 'Participant demographics': 'Participants met 8 (7-10) of the AUD criteria, indicating severe AUD (defined as >= 6 symptoms out of 11), supported by an AUDIT score of 23.5 (20.2-27).' Author-assigned severity label used directly.
- **Age_Median**: Median age 44 years (41-49) (Abstract; Participants section) falls within the 30-50 bracket.
- **PercentFem**: Abstract and Participants: 'ten treatment-seeking adults (eight men and two women...)'. 2/10 = 20%.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: No race or ethnicity data appear in the provided main text; the article refers detailed participant characteristics to 'Table S1 in the supplementary,' which is not part of the converted text supplied for extraction. Coded '.' rather than fabricating or inferring from the Danish recruitment setting.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: No income, employment, insurance status, or low-income eligibility criterion is reported anywhere in the article. Coded 0.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: All procedures, recruitment (via www.alkoholforskning.dk/psilocybin, 'all regions of Denmark'), ethics approval (Danish Ethics Committee of the Capital Region, Copenhagen) and clinical site (Copenhagen University Clinic for Psychedelic Research) are in Denmark. Emitted 'NonUS' per the closed-enum rule; actual country (Denmark) recorded here.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 0. Participants section funnel: 37 individuals expressed interest, 15 underwent screening, 10 were included (a narrowly screened, self-referred, treatment-seeking volunteer sample recruited via a dedicated study website). Discussion explicitly names 'selection bias due to self-referral and high expectations from positive widespread media coverage' as a limitation affecting generalisability.
- **PhysicalChangeMeasured**: Coded 1: blood pressure and heart rate were repeatedly measured as a study outcome (Methods, 'Feasibility, cardiovascular effects, and adverse effects': 'Blood pressure and heart rate were repeatedly measured in parallel with plasma psilocin concentrations'; Results reports psilocybin 'moderately increased systolic blood pressure' with reference to Figure S2), and plasma psilocin concentration is itself a biomarker outcome reported as a primary pharmacokinetic endpoint (Table 1).
- **Followup in months**: This is a single-dose intervention (Day 0 dosing, with integration sessions at +1 and +7 days only), so there is no meaningfully separate 'end of treatment' horizon distinct from the from-baseline (Day 0) follow-up clock; all outcome time points (week 1, week 4, week 12) are measured from the dosing day. Longest reported clinical follow-up is week 12 (Table 2; Abstract: 'Alcohol consumption significantly decreased over the 12 weeks'). Converted 12 weeks / 4 weeks-per-month = 3, consistent with the codebook's 2-weeks-=-0.5 convention.
- **CessationMeasured**: Coded 0. Unlike studies reporting a discrete abstinence/cessation rate, this article reports only continuous/percentage measures -- percentage of heavy drinking days, percentage of drinking days, and drinks per day (Table 2) -- with no distinct binary or rate-based 'cessation' or 'abstinence' outcome named anywhere in the text.
- **RelapseMeasured**: Coded 0. The word 'relapse' appears only once, incidentally, in the missing-data-handling description ('Missing data in the clinical outcomes were due to relapse and were consequently replaced with the participants' worst value observed'; Statistical analysis). This is not presented as a formally tracked or reported relapse outcome with its own metric, so RelapseMeasured is coded 0; flagged as a borderline call given the explicit mention of relapse as a cause of missingness.
- **Implementation Outcome**: Coded 1: feasibility is an explicitly named study aim and outcome (Abstract, 'Aims'; Results, 'Feasibility, cardiovascular effects, and adverse effects': 'The feasibility... [was] determined by the proportion of participants who completed all sessions of the psilocybin therapy'), and Results reports 100% completion as the feasibility finding.
- **NumDistinctAEs**: Results, 'Feasibility...': 'A total of 46 adverse events were recorded, all categorised as mild, transient, and self-limiting... The most frequent adverse events were anxiety, headache, and fatigue.' Only these three named types are identifiable from the provided text; the full per-type breakdown is in Table S2, which is referenced but not included in the converted text supplied for this extraction. Because 'most frequent' implies additional, less-common AE types exist among the 46 total events that cannot be named from the available text, coded 'Not numbered' rather than fabricating a count of 3.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: The article reports a total event count (46) and names the three most frequent types but never states how many distinct participants experienced >=1 AE (overlap across types/participants is not addressed and Table S2, which likely contains this, is not in the provided text). Coded '.' rather than inferring an unsupported total.
- **AE_denominator**: All ten participants received the psilocybin dosing session and were monitored under the same feasibility/safety assessment (Results: 'All ten participants completed the psilocybin therapy course'; AEs assessed as part of this same monitoring). AE_denominator = 10.

### Adverse events
- **Jensen 2024_AE01**: Severity 'Mild' is author-assigned, not rubric-derived: the article states all 46 recorded AEs were 'categorised as mild, transient, and self-limiting, without requiring intervention,' and separately that 'No participants required rescue medication during the study period,' which directly supports InterventionRequired = 'N' and MeetsFDASerious = 'N' for this type. No per-type participant count (NAffected) is given for anxiety specifically -- only that it was among the three most frequent of 46 total events -- so NAffected = '.'. Onset coded 'post-session-acute' per the article's statement that the most frequent AEs 'occurred within the first 24 hours of dosing.' Considered whether this should be coded AE_type = 'challenging_experience' given anxiety's overlap with expected in-session phenomenology described in the Dosing session narrative, but the authors report this anxiety within their own formal 46-event AE tally (distinct from the separate subjective-drug-intensity/quality-of-experience assessments), so it was coded as a clinical 'AE' following the article's own classification.
- **Jensen 2024_AE02**: Same author-assigned Mild severity and same 24-hour post-dosing onset window as AE01, drawn from the identical sentence in Results, 'Feasibility...'. NAffected = '.' because no per-type count is given, only that headache was among the three most frequent of 46 total AEs.
- **Jensen 2024_AE03**: Same author-assigned Mild severity and same 24-hour post-dosing onset window as AE01/AE02, drawn from the identical sentence in Results, 'Feasibility...'. NAffected = '.' because no per-type count is given, only that fatigue was among the three most frequent of 46 total AEs.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: pending
- **metric / method / confidence**: "Cohen's d" / 'd' / 'approx'
- **note**: Author-reported Cohen's d = 1.02 (baseline to week 12, percentage heavy drinking days, the primary SUD-behavioral contrast this study's Results and Abstract lead with), explicitly stated as 'corrected for correlation between the repeated measurements' (Methods, 'Statistical analysis'). Although the value is directly reported rather than derived, it is a within-subjects pre/post effect size from a single-arm design being fed into a between-groups Tier-1 forest-plot pipeline -- the same structural approximation flagged in this corpus's other single-arm psilocybin-AUD studies -- so confidence is set to 'approx' rather than 'clean', consistent with the instruction to prefer 'approx' when in doubt.

## Johnson 1969

*Alcohol · 1969 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Table 4 gives two columns per criterion: pre/post significance 'taken across all treatment groups' and 'differences between treatment groups.' The primary hypothesis under test is whether LSD outperformed the other three arms, so the relevant comparison is the between-groups column, not the pooled pre/post column. Abstinence is the first-listed and most directly SUD-specific criterion in Table 4 and is treated as the primary outcome per the abstract's lead ('drinking'). No F-value, mean difference, or other magnitude statistic is given for the between-groups comparison anywhere in the article -- only the label 'NS' -- so no numeric or labelled-descriptor value exists to report and Outcome is coded '.'.
- **SD**: No test statistic (F, t, chi-square) or dispersion value is reported for the between-groups Abstinence comparison; Table 4 shows only the qualitative tag 'NS' for that cell with no accompanying number or df. Coded '.'.
- **PVal**: Table 4, 'Differences between treatment groups' row for Abstinence (and every other criterion) is printed literally as 'NS', with no numeric p-value given anywhere for the between-groups test. Recorded verbatim per the non-negotiable rule against converting NS to a number or threshold.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: Results state '[e]ighty-seven percent of the subjects in groups 1 to 4 were successfully followed up' (of 95 total), and this followed-up group is the one whose pre/post responses were compared via repeated-measures ANOVA. 87% of 95 = 82.65, which is not a clean integer, and the article never states the exact analyzed N or how non-integer attrition was handled. Rather than guess between 82 and 83, this is coded '.' per the instruction that an honest '.' is preferable to a fabricated number.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 11/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power, harms, funding_coi. Objective, design (single-blind 4-arm design), setting (London Clinic), eligibility criteria, intervention (dosing/procedure), comparator arms, outcomes, randomization ('randomly assigned'), blinding rationale, statistical methods (chi-square, repeated-measures ANOVA), and results are all explicitly described. No sample-size/power justification is given anywhere. No adverse-event, safety, or harms section or statement appears in the article. No funding source or conflict-of-interest statement appears (only a personal-thanks acknowledgment to colleagues). n_met=11, below the 13-of-14 threshold, so MethodologyDescribedYN=0.
- **BaseStatus**: The article reports 'years of problem drinking' (mean 8.15) and describes patients as clinic-referred alcoholics, some of 'skid row' background, but gives no explicit severity classification (e.g., no AUD severity score, no clinician-rated severity). Coded '.' rather than guessing a band.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Article reports no race or ethnicity data anywhere. Coded '.'.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Study conducted at the London Clinic, Alcoholism and Drug Addiction Research Foundation, London, Ontario, Canada. Coded 'NonUS'.
- **ConfoundYN**: Authors report a chi-square comparison of the four final groups on age, sex, referral source, severity of drinking, marital status, education, employment, and accommodation to establish baseline homogeneity -- a substantive assessment of potential confounders across arms. Coded 1.
- **PrimaryCare**: No aspect of recruitment, intervention delivery, or follow-up is described as occurring through primary-care settings or providers; the study is entirely clinic/hospital-based. Coded 0.
- **ValidatedScaleMeasYN**: Table 3 lists baseline psychometrics (Differential Personality Inventory, Quick/IQ Test, Hidden Figures Test, Straus-Bacon Social Stability Index) used to characterize the sample at intake, not as pre/post outcome measures. The actual outcome criteria in Table 4 (abstinence, drinking practices, consequences, attitudes, employment, accommodation, social relations, marital status) are drawn from the clinic's own intake/follow-up questionnaire, not a named validated instrument. Coded 0.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: Text explicitly describes the clinic population as drawn 'largely from a lower- to middle-class population' and Table 3 reports a quantified 'Social class' variable (mean 3.53, SD 0.93). Coded 1.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Patients were drawn from routine clinic contacts (self-referred or physician/agency-referred, ~220 intakes/year), not recruited via advertisement for a research volunteer pool, so recruitment itself reflects the clinic's real-world caseload. Note, however, that the final 95-patient scheme was reached after a large sequential exclusion cascade (337 initial contacts -> 95 final, Table 1/2), so the analyzed sample is more selected than the recruitment source alone suggests.
- **InterventionTherapy**: Groups 2 and 3 (n=25, n=22) received the drug with an active co-therapist present for structured interaction; group 4 (n=25) received routine individual/group/milieu therapy. Group 1 (n=23) received LSD with only a supportive nurse present, no therapist. Coded 1 at the study level because therapy/counseling beyond drug administration alone was part of the design for 3 of 4 arms, but this does not apply uniformly to Group 1.
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: The Discussion section narratively reports that some patients described 'lessening of tension, depression, irritability, and isolation' after the drug session, but this is an unstructured post-session subjective account, not a formal pre/post mental-health outcome measure tracked with statistics in Table 4 (which covers only drinking, employment, accommodation, social relations, and marital status). Coded 0.
- **HarmsAssessed**: The article contains no adverse-event, safety, or side-effects section, table, or explicit statement anywhere -- not in Methods, Results, or Discussion. Mentions of a Posey restraint belt and post-session chlorpromazine/Tuinal sedation are described as routine session-management procedure, not as treatment of, or assessment for, adverse reactions. Per the cardinal AE rule, this reads as the authors never looking rather than as a 'no AEs occurred' finding, so SafetyReported='N' and HarmsAssessed=0 (not 1).
- **AnyAEReported / NumDistinctAEs / NParticipantsWithAE / ArmScope / WorstSeverity**: All coded 'NA' as the mandatory cascade from SafetyReported='N' (article silent on AEs/safety entirely); AE_denominator is coded '.' per the rule that this field is never 'NA'.
- **Comparator**: Two comparator conditions exist: Group 3, an active-drug control (IV sodium amobarbital + methamphetamine with therapist), and Group 4, a no-drug routine-care control (individual/group/milieu therapy, no hospitalization). Coded 1.
- **Pos Result Y/N**: All four groups, including the LSD arms, improved significantly pre-to-post on most drinking indices, but the ANOVA between-groups comparison was NS on every criterion (Table 4) -- the study's own conclusion is that LSD conferred 'no evident advantages over more conventional modes of therapy' and 'lends no support to the claims made for the efficacy of LSD treatment.' Coded 0 (null/negative) because the relevant test is the LSD-vs-comparator difference, which was null.
- **PercentFem**: Table 3: Male 87, Female 8, total 95. 8/95 x 100 = 8.42.
- **Followup in months**: Text states a 'one-year follow-up' made 'as close to the 12-month mark as possible' from the patient's entry into the scheme. Treatment (a single hospital session) occurred shortly after intake, so the entry-based and end-of-treatment horizons do not differ materially here. Coded 12.
- **EligibleYN**: (a) Prospectively planned trial: 95 patients enrolled under a defined protocol, randomly assigned to 4 arms, with prospective 12-month outcome assessment -- passes. (b) SUD outcome reported: abstinence, drinking practices, consequences of drinking, and attitudes toward drinking are all explicitly measured pre/post -- passes. Both criteria met, EligibleYN='Y'.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Non-significant (no statistic)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: The primary between-groups comparison (LSD vs. active-drug and no-drug controls) on the primary drinking/abstinence outcome is reported only as the qualitative tag 'NS' in Table 4, with no F-value, t-value, mean difference, or other convertible statistic anywhere in the article. No defensible d/g/eta_p/F/chi2/OR/RR conversion is possible.

## Johnson 2014

*Smoking · 2014 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: No single field-labeled 'primary outcome' appears in Methods. The Abstract leads with the SUD-behavioral finding: 'Biomarkers assessing smoking status, and self-report measures of smoking behavior demonstrated that 12 of 15 participants (80%) showed seven-day point prevalence abstinence at 6-month follow-up,' repeated verbatim in Results ('Based on the Timeline Follow-back and verified by CO and cotinine measures, 12 of 15 (80%) participants showed seven-day point prevalence abstinence at 6-month follow-up'). Selected per precedence rule (2) - the SUD-behavioral outcome the abstract leads with - over the paired t-tests reported later in Results for continuous cigarettes/day (t14=11.1, P<.001), breath CO (t14=3.8, P<.01), and urine cotinine (t14=2.3, P=.04), which are supporting analyses of the same underlying abstinence construct rather than the headline finding.
- **SD**: No dispersion or test statistic accompanies the 12/15 (80%) abstinence rate; it is reported as a raw single-arm proportion with no comparator. The paired t-test statistics elsewhere in Results (t14=11.1 for TLFB cigarettes/day; t14=3.8 for CO; t14=2.3 for cotinine) are paired with the continuous pre/post smoking-reduction analyses, not with the abstinence-rate Outcome selected above, so they are not repurposed here.
- **PVal**: No p-value is reported for the 80% abstinence rate; it is a descriptive proportion from a single-arm design with no formal hypothesis test against a comparator.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: 'Fifteen participants (10 males) were enrolled in the study. All of these individuals completed the study' (Participants). The 12/15 abstinence figure is computed over this full enrolled/completed sample, so N=15.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 12/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power, randomization. Comparator is Y because its absence is explicitly stated ('this pilot study was conducted without a control condition as a first step...'), which per the prompt counts as described. Blinding is Y because the open-label design is explicitly named throughout (title, Abstract 'we conducted an open-label pilot study', Discussion 'the open-label design does not allow for definitive conclusions'). Randomization is N because no statement anywhere addresses randomization (or its absence) for this study's own design - the text only discusses other trials' 'randomized controlled trials' and calls for a future 'randomized trial'. Sample_size_power is N because no power calculation (a priori or post hoc) or 'underpowered' language appears; the closest statement ('Results should be interpreted with caution given the small N and open-label design') addresses generalizability broadly, not power specifically. n_met=12 (one below the 13 threshold), so the binary is 0.
- **BaseStatus**: FTCD (Fagerstrom Test for Cigarette Dependence) mean 5.3 (SD 1.3, range 3-8) on a 0-10 scale (Table 1); a mid-range mean conventionally denotes moderate nicotine dependence, and the sample also had heavy, long-standing use (mean 19 cigarettes/day for 31 years, mean 6 prior failed quit attempts) - coded 'Moderate'.
- **Age_Median**: Mean age 51 (SD 10.5, range 26-65; Table 1) falls in the '50+' bracket.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Conducted at Johns Hopkins University School of Medicine, Baltimore, MD, USA (author affiliations; IRB approval statement) - coded 'US'.
- **ConfoundYN**: Discussion substantively flags a potential confound without formal statistical adjustment: 'Another issue is the relative racial homogeneity and high education levels of the present study sample, which may have impacted the outcomes.' No stratification or covariate adjustment was performed (single-arm, N=15). Coded 1 on the 'substantively discussed' branch of the rule; a stricter reading limited to formal statistical adjustment would code 0.
- **Implementation Outcome**: Borderline call. The Abstract frames the study aim as 'to determine the safety and feasibility ... of psilocybin as an adjunct to tobacco smoking cessation treatment,' and Discussion states 'results of the present pilot study also support the feasibility of the approach.' However, the Abstract itself glosses feasibility as '(i.e., potential efficacy)', and the feasibility claim is operationalized entirely through the efficacy-style 80% abstinence rate rather than through classic implementation constructs (acceptability ratings, protocol fidelity, adoption, reach) measured with a dedicated instrument. Retention (15/15 completed) and two participants declining a third session are the closest process data but are not framed or analyzed as implementation outcomes. Coded 0; a reader weighting the explicit 'feasibility' aim language more heavily could reasonably code 1.
- **RelapseMeasured**: Explicitly tracked and operationally defined: footnote 1 distinguishes 'lapses' (any smoking, even a puff, post-TQD) from 'relapses' (smoking on >=7 consecutive days post-TQD, per Hughes et al. 2003). Results report three participants' self-corrected lapses (1, 4, and 48 cigarettes) and one participant's relapse after 13 weeks of abstinence (average 5 cigarettes/day for 14 weeks) who resumed abstinence before 6-month follow-up. Coded 1.
