- **Power**: No power calculation (a priori or post hoc) or explicit 'underpowered' language appears anywhere; the caution statement about 'small N and open-label design' addresses sample size/generalizability broadly rather than statistical power specifically, so it does not meet the rule's 'bare statement study was underpowered' bar. Coded 0.
- **CulturalFraming**: Discussion notes 'the relative racial homogeneity ... of the present study sample' as a limitation on generalizability, but this is a demographic/sampling observation, not an explicit engagement with racial/ethnic identity, historical trauma, or culturally specific language/context as intervention content. Coded 0.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: Coded '.' because the article never reports a single consolidated count of participants with >=1 AE across the three AE categories (6/15 challenging experience, 8/10 headache, an unquantified subset with cardiovascular changes) - summing the per-type counts would risk double-counting participants with more than one AE type, so no aggregate is fabricated.
- **AE_denominator**: AE data are assessed over two different denominators depending on type: the full N=15 psilocybin-exposed sample for cardiovascular changes and challenging-experience ratings (assessed across all 42 sessions in all 15 participants), versus a 10-participant subset for headache - the Post-session Headache Interview was added only after the first 5 participants had already completed their sessions ('The first five participants had completed their psilocybin sessions when another study found that psilocybin increased headache occurrence... Therefore, the final ten participants completed a staff-administered scale'). AE_denominator is coded 15 as the primary safety population; the headache-specific subset (n=10, 8 affected) is captured in that AE's own Description/NAffected instead.

### Adverse events
- **Johnson 2014_AE01**: Results (Safety data): 'one participant (7%) reported extreme ratings, and five others (33%) reported strong ratings of fear, fear of insanity, or feeling trapped at some time during a session. These episodes occurred in six participants (40%)... They were readily managed by interpersonal support, and had resolved by the end of the sessions.' Coded AE_type='challenging_experience' per the prompt's guidance that CEQ-sense in-session phenomenology should be flagged rather than counted as a clinical AE. Onset='in-session' because it resolved by end of session. Severity='Mild'/SeveritySource='rubric' because the rubric explicitly treats a high CEQ score as Mild absent intervention need or persistence, both absent here. InterventionRequired='N' because management was purely interpersonal support, not pharmacological rescue (rescue medication was available per protocol but the text states no clinically significant AE required physician or pharmacologic intervention).
- **Johnson 2014_AE02**: Results (Safety data): 'blood pressure (BP) and heart rate (HR) were elevated during drug effects with peak values occurring on average between 1.5 and 2.5 hours post-psilocybin administration' with group mean peak/baseline values given for systolic BP, diastolic BP, and HR. Grouped as one AE type ('acute cardiovascular changes') rather than split into separate BP/HR entries because the article always reports and discusses them together as one physiological phenomenon; Discussion explicitly lists 'modest acute increases in blood pressure, heart rate' among 'adverse effects associated with psilocybin.' NAffected is coded '.' because only session-level group means (SD, range) are given, not a count of participants affected. Severity='Mild' matches the rubric's own example ('transient mild BP/HR elevation'); SeveritySource='rubric' since no CTCAE grade or SAE label is assigned by the authors. InterventionRequired='N' per the blanket statement that no clinically significant AE required physician or pharmacologic intervention.
- **Johnson 2014_AE03**: Results (Safety data): 'Of the ten participants assessed for headache, eight reported at least one post-psilocybin headache with a mean (SD; range) duration of 5.8 (2.4; 2.0-9.5) hours, onset at a mean of 6.2 (2.1; 3.5-11.5) hours after psilocybin administration and mean severity rating of mild, or 2.6 (1.3; 1-5) on a scale from 1=minimal, to 6=excruciating. Five reported use of over-thecounter headache medication.' SeveritySource='author' because the authors themselves characterize the mean rating as 'mild' using their own study-specific severity scale, rather than the extractor applying the rubric de novo. Onset='post-session-acute' (hours after dosing, resolving within about a day) rather than 'in-session' or 'delayed'. InterventionRequired='N': the OTC medication was self-administered by participants after the monitored session window to alleviate symptoms ('approved medications for use during the study'), not a study-administered pharmacologic rescue, and is consistent with the blanket statement that no AE required physician or pharmacologic intervention.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Abstinence rate (%)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: Single-arm, open-label pilot with no control group; the study's headline SUD outcome (80% biologically verified abstinence, selected per abstract-leads-with precedence) is a raw proportion with no accompanying test statistic or comparator, so none of the seven supported conversions (d, g, eta_p, F, chi2, OR, RR) apply. The paired t-tests reported for continuous smoking/biomarker measures (t14=11.1 TLFB, t14=3.8 CO, t14=2.3 cotinine) are within-sample pre/post contrasts on the same single arm, not treatment-vs-control effects, and are deliberately not repurposed as a Tier-1 conversion input.

## Johnson 2016

*Smoking · 2016 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Citation/Title**: This is a long-term follow-up report of a parent open-label pilot trial: Johnson MW, Garcia-Romeu A, Cosimano MP, Griffiths RR. 'Pilot study of the 5-HT2AR agonist psilocybin in the treatment of tobacco addiction.' J Psychopharmacol 2014;28(11):983-992 (ref. 4 in this article, cited repeatedly in Introduction/Methods as the source of the original sample, intervention description, and 6-month outcome data). The present article (Johnson 2016) reports previously unpublished 12-month and long-term (mean 30-month) follow-up data on the same 15 participants.
- **Outcome**: Two abstinence timepoints are reported: 'At 12-month follow-up, 10 participants (67%) were confirmed as smoking abstinent. At long-term follow-up, nine participants (60%) were confirmed as smoking abstinent' (Abstract; Results). No statement anywhere designates one as the formal 'primary' outcome. I selected the long-term (mean 30-month, range 16-57 months post-TQD) rate of 9/15 (60%) as Outcome because it is the article's namesake/headline finding: the title is 'Long-term follow-up...', the Discussion's key comparative claim explicitly foregrounds it ('the present study found 60% abstinence more than a year after psilocybin administration', contrasted with <31% typical 12-month abstinence for standard medications), and the Conclusion cites 'long-term smoking abstinence' as the study's contribution. This is a judgment call — the abstract's Results sentence order technically states the 12-month figure (67%) first — and the alternative (12-month, 10/15) is a defensible substitute. Did not use the raw Figure 1 OCR chart-text block (garbled/interleaved axis labels) for any numeric value; only prose/table-reported numbers were used.
- **SD**: No dispersion or test statistic is paired with either abstinence proportion; both are reported as raw counts/percentages with no accompanying inferential test (the reported ANOVA F and paired t-tests in Results/Fig.1 caption belong to the continuous TLFB cigarettes-per-day analysis, a different, non-primary measure, not to the abstinence-rate Outcome).
- **PVal**: No p-value is reported for the long-term (or 12-month) abstinence proportion itself; this is a single-arm descriptive rate with no comparator or hypothesis test attached. The p<0.001 values in Results/Figure 1 belong to the TLFB CPD repeated-measures ANOVA and paired t-tests, a separate continuous outcome, and are not repurposed here.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: Methods/Data analysis: 'Participants who did not report to a follow-up visit were considered to have smoked. The three participants who did not complete a long-term follow-up... had been confirmed as daily smokers at the 12-month follow-up. Thus, for these three individuals, carbon monoxide, urine cotinine, and TLFB self-reported daily smoking data for the long-term follow-up were imputed using their individual 12-month follow-up values.' All 15 enrolled participants are therefore analyzed (with imputation for the 3 non-completers), so N=15 is the correct denominator for the 9/15 (60%) long-term Outcome, not the 12 who physically returned.
- **Followup in months**: The long-term follow-up is reported as 'mean 30 months (range = 16-57 months)' post-target-quit-date (TQD, i.e., first psilocybin session); coded as 30. Two nuances: (1) the range shows some individuals were followed to 57 months, but this reflects variable retrospective-interview scheduling around one nominal 'long-term follow-up' visit rather than a distinct longer protocol timepoint, so the mean (30) rather than the max (57) was used. (2) The rule specifies measuring from end of treatment when both an end-of-treatment and from-baseline horizon are given; Methods explicitly defines 'end of treatment (10 weeks post-TQD)' as a separate reference point, so the 30-month figure (measured from TQD, i.e., first dosing, not from the 10-week end of treatment) is very slightly longer (~2 months) than a strict end-of-treatment measurement would give; this difference does not change month-level reporting materially and was not subtracted out.
- **Retention (%)**: Not randomized, so denominator is enrolled N=15. At the article's longest/primary follow-up (long-term, mean 30 months), 12/15 (80%) returned and provided data; the earlier 12-month follow-up had 100% (15/15) completion. Retention (%) is coded 80, matching the long-term timepoint used for Outcome. This value sits exactly at the near-80 threshold flagged as flipping a downstream cell, so it is called out explicitly here: 12/15=80% at long-term follow-up (Abstract/Results); 15/15=100% at 12-month follow-up.
- **InclusionExclusionCriteria**: This article states only one explicit exclusion criterion of its own ('without histories of severe mental illness') plus baseline descriptive characteristics (age, CPD, years smoking, prior quit attempts); it does not restate a full inclusion/exclusion list and explicitly defers: 'For a detailed description of the study sample and intervention see Johnson et al. (4).' Coded 0 for lack of a clear/complete statement within this article itself; consistent with 'eligibility'=N in the MethodologyDescribedYN criteria below.
- **BaseStatus**: No formal nicotine-dependence severity instrument (e.g., FTND) is reported in this article. Coded 'Moderate' based on the described smoking history — 19 CPD, mean 31 years of smoking, mean 6 prior quit attempts (Methods) — which reflects a substantial but not explicitly 'severe'-labeled dependence profile; this is a judgment call in the absence of a validated severity score in this article.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Race/ethnicity is not reported anywhere in this article (unlike some companion articles on the same cohort); coded '.' as not reported.
- **Power**: No power calculation or explicit 'underpowered' statement appears. Discussion states findings 'are limited by the small sample, open-label design, and lack of control condition' — a general small-sample limitation, not an explicit power discussion — so coded 0 (not counted as a bare underpowered statement).
- **QualityOfLifeMeasured**: The Persisting Effects Questionnaire includes one single global item, 'Did the experience change your sense of well-being or life satisfaction?' (Table 1), which is adjacent to but not a dedicated/validated quality-of-life instrument (e.g., no SF-36/WHOQOL-type measure is used). Coded 0 as a judgment call; this single item could alternately support a 1.
- **RelapseMeasured**: Coded 1 because the article distinguishes 'confirmed as smoking abstinent' from 'reporting continuous abstinence since their TQD' (e.g., '10 participants (67%) were confirmed as smoking abstinent, with 8 of these participants reporting continuous abstinence since their TQD'), implying explicit tracking of relapse-and-requit versus uninterrupted abstinence, and the imputation rule for non-completers treats missed follow-up as presumed relapse to smoking.
- **HarmsAssessed**: Coded 1 / SafetyReported='Y' based on this article's own Results content: 'no clinically significant psychological sequelae were spontaneously reported at the long-term follow-up' and the detailed account of one participant's decreased well-being/counseling referral (Results). The acute physiological AE data recapped in the Introduction (mild headache, BP/HR elevation, 40% challenging experiences) is explicitly attributed to the previously published parent trial ('Pilot results demonstrated safety and feasibility in this sample... (4)') and is background context, not new data collected or re-analyzed in this article; it was therefore not treated as this article's own ae_events to avoid conflating the two articles' safety data, per instructions to extract only this article's evidence.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 11/14 met. Unmet: eligibility, sample_size_power, randomization. objective: clearly stated ('To assess long-term effects of a psilocybin-facilitated smoking cessation program at >=12 months...'). design: open-label pilot follow-up described. setting: Johns Hopkins Medicine / laboratory visits stated. eligibility=N: only one exclusion criterion ('without histories of severe mental illness') plus descriptive characteristics given; full criteria explicitly deferred to ref (4). intervention: dosing/timing/CBT fully described. comparator=Y: its absence is explicitly stated as a limitation ('lack of control condition'), which counts as described-as-absent. sample_size_power=N: N=15 is stated but no power calculation or explicit underpowered statement appears, only a general 'small sample' limitation. randomization=N: the word/concept of randomization for this study's own design is never addressed (only used later regarding the planned future RCT, NCT01943994); its absence is not itself stated the way blinding's absence is. blinding=Y: 'open-label design' is explicitly named as a limitation, describing absence of blinding. statistical_methods, results, harms, funding_coi: all clearly described (Data analysis section; Results; psychological-sequelae statement; Funding/Declaration of interest sections). n_met=11 < 13, so MethodologyDescribedYN=0.

### Adverse events
- **Johnson 2016_AE01**: Results: 'one participant deviated from instrument instructions by endorsing both -3 (decreased very much) and +3 (increased very much) at the 12-month follow-up... The participant endorsing mixed results for well-being or life-satisfaction at the 12-month follow-up attributed the decrease in well-being or life-satisfaction to psilocybin session content in which she reported re-experiencing traumatic childhood memories. This individual was referred to additional counseling, which she reported to be helpful in integrating these experiences and resolving associated difficulty.' AE_type='both' because the precipitating content (re-experiencing trauma) occurred in-session (challenging-experience phenomenology) but the reported effect (decreased well-being/life satisfaction) persisted to the 12-month assessment and required a counseling referral, i.e., both an in-session challenging-experience component and a clinically actionable aftermath. Onset='delayed' because the adverse rating (decreased well-being) is documented as persisting to the 12-month follow-up rather than resolving acutely. Severity='Moderate' (rubric): required active, non-pharmacological intervention (counseling referral) and involved functional/psychological impact persisting well beyond the dosing day, but meets no FDA seriousness criterion (no hospitalization, no life-threatening event, no stated suicidality). SeverityIndeterminate='N' because sufficient detail (intervention required, persistence to 12 months, described resolution via counseling) supports a confident Moderate grade. InterventionRequired='Y' (referred to additional counseling). MeetsFDASerious='N'. A second participant 'sought outside counseling after their psilocybin session experiences, although this was reportedly undertaken with the intention of personal growth and self-improvement' (Results) was considered but NOT coded as a separate AE, since the article explicitly frames it as proactive/non-distress-motivated rather than an adverse reaction. NumDistinctAEs=1, NParticipantsWithAE=1, AE_denominator=15 (all 15 completed the 12-month follow-up at which this item was assessed); ArmScope='psychedelic_arm' (single-arm study, entire sample exposed).

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Abstinence rate (%)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: Single-arm, open-label long-term follow-up report with no control group and no test statistic or p-value paired with the long-term (or 12-month) abstinence rate, so none of the seven supported conversions (d, g, eta_p, F, chi2, OR, RR) apply.

## Johnson MW 2026

*Smoking · 2026 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: A priori primary outcome is 6-month biochemically verified prolonged smoking abstinence; the reported effect size for the primary between-group comparison is OR = 6.12 (95% CI, 1.99-23.26; P = .003) from binary logistic regression, stated in the Abstract and the 'Smoking Cessation Outcomes' section of Results. Not the largest available statistic (7-day point-prevalence OR = 3.30 is secondary; the exploratory zero-inflated mixed model's incidence rate ratio is a tertiary/exploratory analysis) — Outcome reflects the designated primary endpoint per the precedence rule.
- **SD**: 95% CI, 1.99-23.26 is the dispersion measure the article pairs directly with the primary OR of 6.12 (Abstract; Results).
- **PVal**: P = .003 is reported immediately alongside the primary OR of 6.12 in the Abstract and Results.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: Intention-to-treat analysis for the primary 6-month prolonged-abstinence outcome included all N = 82 randomized participants (42 psilocybin, 40 nicotine patch); participants who discontinued or were lost to follow-up were counted as nonabstinent rather than excluded, so 82 (not the 68 who completed the 6-month visit) is the denominator over which the primary OR was computed (Methods, 'Statistical Analysis'; Results).
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 14/14 met. Unmet: none. All 14 elements are explicitly described. Sample-size/power counts as described (not merely present) because the article explicitly states sample size was driven by funding rather than a statistical power calculation. Blinding counts as described because the article explicitly states participants and investigators were unblinded, satisfying the 'described as absent' clause.
- **Power**: Coded 1 because the article explicitly discusses the absence of a formal a priori power calculation ('Sample size was informed by financial rather than statistical considerations because of limited data on effect size from prior research'), which the coding rule treats as 'discussed' even without a completed calculation (Methods).
- **Retention (%)**: 82.9 = 68/82 (completers of the 6-month primary follow-up / total randomized N), matching the article's directly stated figure ('68 [82.9%] completing the 6-month follow-up'). Randomized N used as denominator per the randomized-study convention.
- **ConfoundYN**: Coded 1 because (a) the primary logistic-regression models were run without covariates on the stated basis of 'highly comparable group baseline characteristics' (an explicit baseline-balance justification), (b) an exploratory analysis specifically tested whether prior lifetime psychedelic use — a plausible confounder — was associated with outcome (it was not), and (c) baseline CPD was included as a covariate in the exploratory mixed-effects model of daily cigarette use.
- **BaseStatus**: Coded 'Moderate': mean FTCD (Fagerstrom Test for Cigarette Dependence) was 4.6-4.7 (borderline low-to-moderate on the 0-10 scale) and mean CPD was ~15.7/day with a median of 6 prior failed quit attempts — indicating an established, moderately dependent smoking population rather than a minimally dependent or an extreme/heaviest-use (severe) sample.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: 11.0 = 100 - 89.0% White = 3.7% Black/African American + 4.9% East/Southeast Asian + 2.4% multiracial (Results, total-sample race breakdown, N = 82).
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: Coded 0: the article reports educational attainment (bachelor's degree or higher) but no income, low-income eligibility criterion, or public-insurance data, so low-SES inclusion is not explicitly described.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Country is the United States — Johns Hopkins Bayview Medical Center, Baltimore, Maryland (Methods).
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 0: participants were self-selected volunteers responding to ads seeking interest in a 'novel research approach' to quitting; 64.6% reported prior psychedelic use vs 13.8% nationally, and the sample was more educated/higher-intelligence than the general smoking population. Authors explicitly flag this as a recruitment-bias/generalizability limitation (Discussion, 'Limitations').
- **PrimaryCare**: Coded 0: the trial was conducted entirely within a specialty academic-medical-center research clinic (Johns Hopkins Bayview); no primary-care setting or provider involvement is described.
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: Coded 0: depression and anxiety appear only as adverse-event categories in the AE table (Table 2), not as outcomes assessed via a validated mental-health instrument (e.g., no PHQ-9/GAD-7 or similar scale is reported as a study outcome).
- **Implementation Outcome**: Coded 0: facilitator-assignment and contact-time data are reported descriptively (eTables 1 and 3) but no formal feasibility/acceptability/fidelity/reach/adoption/sustainability outcome is defined or measured.
- **ReductionMeasured**: Coded 1: mean post-target-quit-date cigarettes-per-day, modeled via generalized linear mixed-effects models, is reported as a distinct exploratory outcome separate from the binary abstinence/cessation endpoints (Results, 'Statistical Analysis' and 'Smoking Cessation Outcomes').
- **RelapseMeasured**: Coded 1, non-obvious call: the article does not use the word 'relapse,' but Figure 3 and the accompanying text report biochemically verified abstinence status longitudinally at weeks 2, 4, 6, 8, 13, and 26, which functionally tracks return-to-smoking over time in participants who had achieved abstinence.
- **Followup in months**: 6 months is the longest follow-up horizon with outcome data reported in this article (a priori primary endpoint). The article states 12-month follow-up data exist but 'will be published separately,' so 12-month results are not extractable from this text.
- **NumDistinctAEs**: Coded 'Not numbered' rather than an integer. Table 2 is explicitly restricted to 'adverse events reported in >=2 participants during the trial' and lists 20 named AE types with psilocybin-arm counts, but the article states a full listing (including presumably singleton AEs affecting only 1 participant) is in eTable 2 of Supplement 2, which is not part of the converted text available for this extraction. The 20-type count from Table 2 is therefore a floor, not the true total, so it is coded 'Not numbered' per the non-fabrication rule. Additionally, the extracted Table 2 markdown is itself garbled/column-shifted around the 'Elevated blood pressure' row, reinforcing that a confident integer total cannot be asserted.
- **AE_denominator**: Coded 82 (total randomized N), matching the headline statement '70 participants (85.4%) reported an AE' (70/82 = 85.4%, confirming this denominator). Note: the per-event breakdown in Table 2 uses a narrower population (n = 40 psilocybin-arm and n = 40 nicotine-patch-arm participants who received the study intervention, per footnote b, N = 80 total, excluding 2 psilocybin-randomized participants who did not receive the drug). 82 is used here as the article's overall/primary safety-population denominator; the per-event ae_events NAffected values are drawn from the n = 40 psilocybin-arm column specifically (noted in each event's Description).
- **ArmScope**: Coded 'pooled' at the article level because Table 2 as designed reports AE values for both the psilocybin arm and the nicotine-patch (comparator) arm side by side, mixing exposed and comparator participants; the headline 70/82 AE-prevalence figure likewise spans both arms. Individual ae_events NAffected values in this extraction come from the psilocybin-arm column specifically (the only column reliably parseable from the garbled table), which is flagged in each event's Description.
- **WorstSeverity**: Coded 'Moderate': the article states no serious AEs occurred, but one psilocybin-administration case of elevated blood pressure required active pharmacological intervention (0.4 mg sublingual nitroglycerin), which meets the rubric's Moderate band (intervention required, no FDA seriousness criterion met, no further sequelae). No event in the article meets an FDA seriousness criterion or is author-labeled an SAE, so no event reaches Severe.

### Adverse events
- **Johnson MW 2026_AE01**: Depression is listed in Table 2 with a psilocybin-arm count (12/40) but no detail on severity, duration, or whether intervention was needed; defaulted to Mild per the under-specified-AE rule and flagged indeterminate.
- **Johnson MW 2026_AE04**: Respiratory infection count reported (7/40) with no further descriptive detail; defaulted Mild/indeterminate.
- **Johnson MW 2026_AE05**: Anxiety count reported (9/40); the rubric's core adaptation principle defaults expected in-session anxiety to Mild absent evidence of intervention need or persistence, which is not reported here, so flagged indeterminate.
- **Johnson MW 2026_AE06**: Insomnia count reported (3/40) with no further detail; defaulted Mild/indeterminate.
- **Johnson MW 2026_AE07**: Gastrointestinal illness or diarrhea count reported (8/40) with no further detail; defaulted Mild/indeterminate.
- **Johnson MW 2026_AE08**: Rash appears in the '>=2 participants' AE table with a psilocybin-arm count of 0, implying the qualifying occurrences were in the nicotine-patch arm; that arm's count could not be reliably read from the garbled table text, so NAffected is coded '.' and severity is defaulted Mild/indeterminate for lack of detail.
- **Johnson MW 2026_AE09**: Irritability count reported (4/40) with no further detail; defaulted Mild/indeterminate.
- **Johnson MW 2026_AE10**: Back pain count reported (5/40) with no further detail; defaulted Mild/indeterminate.
- **Johnson MW 2026_AE13**: Vivid dreams appears in the '>=2 participants' AE table with a psilocybin-arm count of 0 (qualifying occurrences presumably in the nicotine-patch arm, unreadable in the garbled table); NAffected coded '.', onset assumed post-session-acute (dreams occur during post-dosing sleep) but severity defaulted Mild/indeterminate given no further detail.
- **Johnson MW 2026_AE14**: Fatigue count reported (3/40) with no further detail; defaulted Mild/indeterminate.
- **Johnson MW 2026_AE15**: Joint pain count reported (1/40) with no further detail; defaulted Mild/indeterminate.
- **Johnson MW 2026_AE16**: Migraine count reported (2/40) with no further detail on severity or intervention; defaulted Mild/indeterminate rather than assuming greater severity from the label alone.
- **Johnson MW 2026_AE18**: Concentration impairment count reported (2/40) with no further detail; defaulted Mild/indeterminate.
- **Johnson MW 2026_AE19**: Fever appears in the '>=2 participants' AE table with a psilocybin-arm count of 0 (qualifying occurrences presumably in the nicotine-patch arm, unreadable in the garbled table); NAffected coded '.', severity defaulted Mild/indeterminate.
- **Johnson MW 2026_AE20**: Ear infection appears in the '>=2 participants' AE table with a psilocybin-arm count of 0 (qualifying occurrences presumably in the nicotine-patch arm, unreadable in the garbled table); NAffected coded '.', severity defaulted Mild/indeterminate.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Odds ratio' / 'OR' / 'clean'
- **note**: Directly reported OR = 6.12 (95% CI, 1.99-23.26; P = .003) from binary logistic regression for the a priori primary outcome (6-month biochemically verified prolonged abstinence), ITT N = 82. Independently recomputed from the reported 2x2 cell counts (17/42 psilocybin abstinent vs 4/40 nicotine-patch abstinent: OR = (17x36)/(25x4) = 6.12), which matches the reported value exactly, supporting a clean conversion with no assumption required.

## Jovaisa T 2006

*Opioid · 2006 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: No explicit 'primary outcome' is labelled anywhere in the article. The study measures cardiovascular/respiratory/renal/GI response, cortisol, OOWS-A withdrawal severity (acute phase), rescue-medication use (early postanesthetic phase), and 4-month abstinence/retention. Per precedence rule (2) (no author-designated primary -> use the SUD-behavioral outcome the abstract leads with), the abstract's Results paragraph opens with 'Ketamine group presented better control of withdrawal symptoms' -- this maps to the Objective Opiate Withdrawal Scale (OOWS-A), the study's dedicated opiate-withdrawal-severity instrument, which per this prompt's boundary-case guidance is itself an SUD outcome. Body text states 'Opiate withdrawal scores on OOWS-A scale were significantly higher in Control group... (Fig. 2 and 3)' and the Fig. 2 caption gives 'difference between groups significant for hourly OOWS-A score, p<0.05.' No group means/SDs for OOWS-A are given in extractable text (only in an OCR-garbled figure image), so Outcome is coded as a directional descriptor rather than a number. Note: the most numerically detailed outcome in the article is actually cardiovascular response (Table 3: MAP/HR means, SDs, 95% CIs, exact p-values by hour) -- this was deliberately NOT chosen as Outcome because it is not the SUD-behavioral outcome the abstract leads with, per the stated precedence order.
- **SD**: No dispersion statistic for OOWS-A is given in extractable text; the underlying data exist only inside an OCR-garbled figure image (Fig. 2), which cannot be read reliably. Coded '.'.
- **PVal**: Reported verbatim as a threshold, not an exact value: 'difference between groups significant for hourly OOWS-A score, p<0.05' (Fig. 2 caption), consistent with the body text statement that OOWS-A was significantly higher (worse) in the Control group.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: OOWS-A/anesthesia-phase data are reported for the same n=50 cohort used in Table 3 (Ketamine n=22, Control n=28; 22+28=50), i.e. the 'complete profiles' cohort after 8 of the original 58 enrolled/randomized patients were excluded for protocol non-compliance/incomplete data.
- **Retention (%)**: Denominator = 58 (all patients enrolled were also randomized on the procedure day; no separate lower randomized N is reported). The study's own three-phase design (anesthetic, 48-hour postanesthetic, 4-month remote) makes the 4-month remote phase part of 'the study,' so completers = 45 (patients with 4-month outcome data). 45/58 = 77.6%, which is within 2 points of the 80% threshold that flips a downstream methodological-quality cell -- flagged per instructions. Note the alternate figure: 50/58 = 86.2% completed only the in-hospital (acute + 48-hour) phases before the 8 protocol-noncompliance exclusions; 77.6% was used because it reflects completion of the full study through its longest planned follow-up.
- **MethodologyDescribadYN_note_placeholder**: see MethodologyDescribedYN entry below
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 12/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power, funding_coi. No a priori or post hoc power/sample-size justification is given anywhere (sample_size_power=N), and the Acknowledgements only thank the Lithuanian AIDS Center for laboratory support -- there is no funding-source statement or conflict-of-interest disclosure for the study itself (funding_coi=N). All other 12 criteria are explicitly described (including harms, via 'There were no complications related to anesthesia phase or opiate antagonist induction' and the remote-phase complications/hospitalizations/death account). 12/14 met, below the 13-of-14 threshold, so MethodologyDescribedYN=0.
- **BaseStatus**: No explicit baseline-severity label or standardized severity score is reported. Inferred 'Severe' from: enrollment required a formal ICD-10/DSM-IV diagnosis of opiate 'dependence' (the more severe diagnostic category, vs. 'abuse'/harmful use), a required dependence duration >1 year, and a mean morphine stabilization dose of ~55-58 mg/day prior to RAI. This is an inference, not an author-stated severity rating.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Study conducted at Vilnius University Emergency Hospital, Vilnius, Lithuania. Coded 'NonUS'; actual country recorded here per instructions.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Race/ethnicity is never reported anywhere in the article (Lithuanian cohort). Coded '.'.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Sample was restricted to age 18-35 and ASA physical-status I-II (no/minor comorbidities), and explicitly excluded polysubstance abuse, current long-acting-opioid use, and acute medical/surgical conditions -- narrower than a typical real-world treatment-seeking opiate-dependent population, which commonly includes polysubstance use and comorbidity. Coded 0 (not representative).
- **InterventionTherapy**: Patients were required to have an aftercare plan (outpatient counseling or residential rehabilitation) before RAI and were later tracked for retention in it, but this aftercare was a post-discharge program the patient chose independently, not a therapy/counseling/integration component delivered as part of the drug-administration protocol itself (which occurred entirely under general anesthesia). Coded 0.
- **ConfoundYN**: Table 2 shows the two groups were comparable at baseline on demographic/clinical variables (p>0.1 for all), which is a randomization-balance check, not a statistical adjustment, stratification, or substantive discussion of confounding. Coded 0.
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: The 4-month follow-up questionnaire (based on the Addiction Severity Index) explicitly screened for 'new onset of psychopathology or compulsive behavior,' which is coded as measurement of a mental-health outcome, even though no named psychometric depression/anxiety scale was used.
- **QualityOfLifeMeasured**: The 4-month ASI-based questionnaire covered health status, social/family life, and legal status, but no dedicated quality-of-life instrument (e.g., SF-36, WHOQOL) is named or used. Coded 0.
- **Followup in months**: Longest reported follow-up is 4 months post-procedure (the study's third, 'remote' phase, per its own stated three-phase design: anesthetic, 48-hour postanesthetic, and 4-month remote). Measured from end of the RAI/anesthesia treatment episode, consistent with the article's own phase framework; only one follow-up horizon (4 months) is reported for remote outcomes, so there is no baseline-vs-end-of-treatment discrepancy to reconcile.
- **MortalityMeasured**: Coded 1 because a death (injection-related sepsis after relapse to opiate use) is reported among the 4-month outcome data, not because the study used a dedicated mortality endpoint/instrument. Per instructions, a death reported only as an adverse event still sets this field to 1 and generates an ae_events row (Jovaisa T 2006_AE04).
- **Pos Result Y/N**: Coded 1 based on the primary withdrawal-severity outcome (OOWS-A) and related acute/early-postanesthetic measures (rescue clonazepam/carbamazepine doses, morning cortisol) all favoring Ketamine with p<0.05 or p<0.001. Note: the 4-month outcomes (abstinence, retention, opiate-free weeks) showed no significant between-group difference ('Differences between groups are statistically insignificant'), so positivity is confined to the acute/short-term withdrawal-suppression endpoints, not sustained long-term recovery outcomes -- consistent with the article's own conclusion.
- **Funding/COI YN**: No funding-source statement or conflict-of-interest disclosure is given for the study. The Acknowledgements section only thanks the Lithuanian AIDS Center for performing laboratory analysis, which is an in-kind lab-support acknowledgement, not a funding/COI statement. Coded 0.
- **EligibleYN**: Criterion (a): met -- prospectively planned RCT (randomized, placebo-controlled, double-blind), defined enrollment (n=58), an assigned regimen (ketamine vs. saline infusion), and prospective outcome assessment across three planned phases. Criterion (b): met -- OOWS-A/SOWS opiate-withdrawal-severity scores are a withdrawal outcome, which this prompt's boundary-case guidance explicitly treats as an SUD outcome; the 4-month follow-up additionally reports abstinence, opiate-free weeks, and relapse to opiate use. Both criteria satisfied -> EligibleYN=Y.

### Adverse events
- **Jovaisa T 2006_AE01**: Article states '4 patients admitted to hospital in a 4-month period for trauma, acute pancreatitis, and ulcerative colitis' without a per-condition participant breakdown, so NAffected is coded '.' for this and the two related entries (AE02, AE03); total across the three conditions is 4 patients. Hospitalization itself meets the FDA 'inpatient hospitalization' seriousness criterion, so graded Severe via the rubric (authors did not assign a formal CTCAE grade or SAE label -- SeveritySource=rubric). SeverityIndeterminate is coded N because the hospitalization signal alone is sufficient for a confident Severe grading regardless of the missing per-condition detail. Authors state these events are unrelated to RAI; captured here regardless of attribution per the instruction that understating harm is the cardinal AE error. Group (Ketamine vs. Control) is not specified for these patients, so the article-level ArmScope is coded 'pooled'.
- **Jovaisa T 2006_AE02**: See Jovaisa T 2006_AE01 -- same source sentence and same reasoning (hospitalization -> Severe via FDA seriousness criterion; NAffected unknown per-condition; arm not specified).
- **Jovaisa T 2006_AE03**: See Jovaisa T 2006_AE01 -- same source sentence and same reasoning (hospitalization -> Severe via FDA seriousness criterion; NAffected unknown per-condition; arm not specified).
- **Jovaisa T 2006_AE04**: Text: 'One more patient returned to regular opiate use, developed injection-related sepsis, and later died in hospital' -- 'one more' confirms this patient is distinct from the four hospitalized above, giving NParticipantsWithAE=5 total. Death is CTCAE Grade 5 / meets the FDA 'death' seriousness criterion by definition, graded Severe via rubric (authors did not assign a formal grade; SeveritySource=rubric). Authors frame this as a consequence of relapse rather than of RAI/ketamine; captured regardless of attribution. Arm not specified in the text.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Objective Opiate Withdrawal Scale (OOWS-A) group difference (narrative only)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: OOWS-A group difference (Ketamine < Control, i.e., less severe withdrawal) is reported only as significant per-hour (p<0.05) in the Fig. 2 caption and body text; no group means, SDs, or a test statistic with degrees of freedom are given in extractable text -- the underlying values exist only inside an OCR-garbled figure image -- so no defensible conversion to a standardized effect size (d/g/eta_p/F/chi2/OR/RR) is possible.

## Krupitsky 2002

*Opioid · 2002 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Section 2.10: 'The rate of abstinence and relapse was considered the primary outcome variable.' Fig. 1 plots monthly abstinence/relapse over 24 months but its picture-text is not legibly recoverable from the converted markdown, and no single summary statistic for the full 24-month comparison is printed in prose. The clearest textual quantification of the primary high-vs-low-dose comparison is the Discussion's statement (Section 4): 'Almost 50% of patients in the high dose group and 60% of subjects in the low dose group relapsed within the first 3 months after KPT.' Coded as this two-group descriptor rather than fabricating a point estimate for the full follow-up.
- **SD**: No dispersion or test statistic (t, F, chi-square) is printed for the abstinence/relapse comparison anywhere in the text; Fig. 1 marks significance with asterisks only (caption: '* p<.05; ** p<.01'), and the figure's underlying values are not recoverable from the garbled picture-text in the converted document. Coded not reported.
- **PVal**: Section 3.2 states the high-vs-low dose difference in abstinence/relapse 'were statistically significant starting from the first month and then for almost all of the 24 months of follow-up.' Fig. 1's caption gives the significance thresholds used ('* p < .05; ** p < .01') but no single exact p-value is printed for the overall primary comparison, so the minimum threshold from the figure legend is used per the printed-threshold convention.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: All 70 randomized subjects (35 high dose, 35 low dose; Section 2.2) were tracked for the abstinence/relapse primary outcome via monthly self/relative report and urine testing through 24 months (Sections 2.7.2, 3.2); no differential loss to the primary-outcome analysis prior to relapse itself is reported, so N=70 is used as the analyzed denominator.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 13/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power. No a priori or post hoc power/sample-size justification is reported anywhere in Methods or Results. All other elements are explicitly described: objective and design (Sections 1, 2.1), setting (Section 2.2, inpatient 300-bed addiction center), eligibility criteria (Section 2.4), intervention and the low-dose active-placebo comparator (Sections 2.1, 2.8-2.9), outcomes and assessment schedule (Section 2.6-2.7), randomization and blinding (Section 2.1, 2.3), statistical methods (Section 2.10), results (Section 3), side effects (Section 3.12), and funding disclosure to MAPS and the Heffter Research Institute (Acknowledgments).
- **Comparator**: Coded 1 per the dose-comparison rule: the low-dose (0.2 mg/kg) ketamine arm is explicitly described as an 'active placebo' condition (Sections 2.1, 2.3) receiving identical psychotherapy but a sub-hallucinogenic dose, contrasted with the high-dose (2.0 mg/kg) psychedelic arm. This is a within-substance dose-comparison design, not a placebo-vs-drug or drug-vs-no-treatment design.
- **BaseStatus**: Coded 'Severe': inclusion required an ICD-10/DSM-IV diagnosis of current Heroin Dependence for at least one year (Section 2.4.1), intravenous use is implied (follow-up examined 'evidence of injection sites over the subject's veins,' Section 2.7.2), and all subjects required inpatient detoxification before enrollment (Section 2.2) — consistent with a severe, physiologically dependent population. No formal severity instrument/score is reported, so this is inferred from diagnostic and clinical-course description rather than a graded scale.
- **Retention (%)**: Coded 100 with denominator 70 (the randomized N, equal to the enrolled/screened N per Section 2.2: 'All 70 subjects were screened, evaluated and randomized'). No dropout prior to or during the single KPT session is reported; all subjects were 'discharged from the hospital after they completed KPT' (Section 2.2). Post-treatment loss to follow-up occurs only after relapse ('We were unable to follow patients after they relapsed to heroin,' Section 2.7.2), which is the outcome event itself (RelapseMeasured), not protocol attrition, so it is not treated as a retention loss.
- **ConfoundYN**: Coded 1: Section 2.2 reports formal statistical comparison of the two dose groups on age, duration of heroin addiction, and duration of pre-treatment abstinence, finding no significant baseline differences — a between-group confound check, though no covariate adjustment is applied in the outcome analyses themselves.
- **ArmScope**: Coded 'pooled': the article's only quantified adverse-event statement ('The only side effect noted in all subjects was an acute increase in...blood pressure,' Section 3.12) is not broken out by dose group, so the AE figures span both the high-dose (psychedelic) and low-dose (active-placebo/comparator) arms rather than the psychedelic arm alone.
- **PhysicalChangeMeasured**: Coded 1: acute blood pressure change during the ketamine session (Section 3.12) and urine drug testing at each follow-up point (Section 2.7.2) are physiological/biochemical measures, distinct from the behavioral and psychometric outcomes.
- **QualityOfLifeMeasured**: Coded 0: the Purpose-in-Life Test and Spirituality Changes Scale (Sections 2.6.2, 3.9-3.10) assess meaning/purpose and spiritual development, not a validated quality-of-life instrument; no QoL-labeled measure appears in the assessment battery.
- **ReductionMeasured**: Coded 0: the study's substance-use outcomes are framed as binary abstinence/relapse (Section 3.2) and craving intensity (VASC, Section 3.3), not as a distinct partial-use-reduction or harm-reduction endpoint separate from cessation/relapse.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 0: eligibility restricted the sample to ages 18-30 with at least a high-school education, a stable address and home phone, a relative willing to assist with follow-up, no current probation, and exclusion of psychiatric comorbidity or polydrug/alcohol dependence (Section 2.4) — a substantially screened, volunteer clinical-trial sample rather than a representative cross-section of heroin-dependent patients.
- **Power**: Coded 0: no a priori or post hoc statistical power or sample-size justification appears anywhere in Methods (Section 2.10) or elsewhere in the article.
- **PercentFem**: 8/35 female in the high-dose group plus 7/35 in the low-dose group (Section 2.2) = 15/70 = 21.4%.
- **MortalityMeasured**: Coded 0: overdose mortality is mentioned only as background context on the Russian heroin-addicted population generally ('Many individuals die because of overdose,' Section 2.2), not tracked or reported as a study outcome or adverse event.

### Adverse events
- **Krupitsky 2002_AE01**: Section 3.12: 'There were no complications, such as protracted psychosis or flashbacks, after KPT... The only side effect noted in all subjects was an acute increase in systolic and particularly diastolic blood pressure of 20-30% during the session.' No CTCAE grade or SAE label is assigned by the authors, so severity was graded via the rubric: a transient, expected physiological effect of ketamine dosing, with no reported pharmacological intervention and explicit authorial statement that 'no such complications occurred' -> Mild, not indeterminate. Reported as affecting 'all subjects' without a per-dose-group breakdown, so NAffected/AE_denominator = 70 and ArmScope is coded pooled rather than psychedelic_arm.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Two-group proportions (%)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: The designated primary outcome (abstinence/relapse, Section 2.10) is reported as a survival-curve-style figure (Fig. 1) with month-by-month significance asterisks rather than a single summary statistic, and the figure's underlying values are not legible in the converted text. The only textual quantification is the Discussion's approximate 3-month relapse percentages ('almost 50%' high dose vs '60%' low dose, Section 4), which are imprecise and not paired with exact counts or a test statistic, so no clean or approx conversion is defensible. Excluded from Tier 1.

## Krupitsky 2007

*Opioid · 2007 · eligible=Y · config=pending*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Primary outcome per Methods ('the rate of abstinence was considered the primary outcome variable') and Results ('Retention in Treatment and Abstinence Rate'): 13/26 (50%) abstinent at 1-year follow-up in the multiple-KPT group vs 6/27 (22.2%) in the single-KPT group. Reported as two-group proportions because no single numeric effect-size statistic accompanies this comparison.
- **SD**: No dispersion or test-statistic value is printed for the abstinence-rate comparison itself; the Kaplan-Meier survival analysis is described narratively only. Figure 1's caption shows 'P<O.Ol' (read as P<0.01) for the overall survival curve while the text reports '(p < 0.05)' for the same endpoint (13/26 vs 6/27) — an internal inconsistency, flagged rather than silently reconciled per the non-fabrication rule. Coded '.' since no statistic (e.g., a log-rank value with df) accompanies either p-value.
- **PVal**: Text states '(p < 0.05)' immediately after reporting 13/26 (50%) vs 6/27 (22.2%) abstinence (Results). Figure 1's caption instead prints a discrepant 'P<0.01' for what appears to be the same survival comparison. Recorded the text's threshold, '<0.05', since it is directly attached to the proportions used for Outcome; the figure's discrepant value is noted here, not resolved or averaged.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: 53 = 26 (multiple-KPT) + 27 (single-KPT), the full randomized cohort analyzed for the 1-year abstinence comparison (Results). The 6 participants who relapsed and dropped out before randomization (after the first, pre-randomization KPT session) are excluded from this N; participants who later relapsed/dropped out within the randomized groups remain in the 26/27 denominators as treatment failures, consistent with the survival-analysis framing.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 13/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power. 13 of 14 criteria met; only sample_size_power is absent — no a priori or post hoc power calculation or sample-size justification appears anywhere in Methods or Results.
- **Comparator**: Both arms received active ketamine-assisted psychotherapy; the comparison is a session-frequency (dose-analogous) design — one KPT session vs. three monthly KPT sessions — not a placebo or no-treatment control (Design). Per codebook, a dose-comparison design counts as Comparator = 1.
- **BaseStatus**: No explicit mild/moderate/severe label is given. Coded 'Severe' as a clearly inferable judgment: participants were hospitalized inpatients undergoing detoxification for ICD-10/DSM-IV heroin dependence present ≥1 year, with mean dependence duration 38.8±30.4 months (Participants) — a chronic, hospitalization-requiring presentation.
- **ConfoundYN**: Authors explicitly compared the randomized groups on age, duration of heroin abuse, and gender and reported 'no statistically significant differences' (Results) — a substantive discussion/check of potential confounders across arms, though not a statistical adjustment within the outcome model.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Recruited from a real inpatient addiction-treatment hospital (Participants: 'Leningrad Regional Center of Addictions, a 300-bed hospital'), but extensive exclusion criteria (psychiatric comorbidity, other SUDs, significant medical illness, pending legal charges, concurrent treatment elsewhere) screened out a substantial share of the eligible clinical population (73 screened, 59 enrolled), so coded 0.
- **Retention (%)**: 42/53 completed the assigned treatment protocol: 4/26 (15.4%) dropped from the multiple-KPT group after session 2, and 7/27 (25.9%) dropped from the single-KPT group after the first counseling session (Results). 42/53 x 100 = 79.2%. Denominator is the randomized N (53) per rule. This value falls within 2 points of 80, per the prompt's explicit flag requirement.
- **PercentFem**: 10 females of 59 enrolled (Participants: '49 males and 10 females') = 16.9%.
- **QualityOfLifeMeasured**: The Purpose-in-Life Test (PLT) assesses existential meaning/purpose (Frankl-based), not a standard quality-of-life instrument (e.g., no SF-36/WHOQOL analog is used); coded 0.
- **ReductionMeasured**: Substance-use outcomes are reported as binary abstinent/relapsed via physical exam, urine screens and TLFB (Treatment Assessment; Results); no distinct partial-reduction or harm-reduction endpoint separate from abstinence/relapse/cessation is reported.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: St. Petersburg / Leningrad Region, Russia (Participants; author affiliations).
- **NParticipantsWithAE / AE_denominator**: Side Effects section states the blood-pressure increase was 'noted in all participants' without giving a separate numeric safety-population count. Interpreted as the full enrolled cohort who received at least one ketamine session (n=59), since this side effect was observed before the post-first-session randomization split. Flagged as an inference from 'all participants' rather than an explicitly printed count.
- **ArmScope**: All 59 enrolled participants received at least one ketamine session before randomization, and the reported blood-pressure effect is described for 'all participants' with no non-exposed comparator arm anywhere in the study (both randomized groups are ketamine-exposed); coded psychedelic_arm.

### Adverse events
- **Krupitsky 2007_AE01**: Acute systolic/diastolic BP elevation (20-30%) during ketamine dosing is described as transient and occurring only during the session, with no mention of pharmacological intervention or persistence beyond the dosing period (Side Effects: 'An anesthesiologist was available throughout all ketamine sessions to treat any possible complications' — standby only, not used for this event). Graded Mild per the rubric's explicit 'transient mild BP/HR elevation' example; SeverityIndeterminate = N because the magnitude, timing, and absence of intervention are all stated. SeveritySource = rubric because the authors did not assign a CTCAE grade or SAE label to this event. NAffected = 59 reflects the article's 'noted in all participants' language, interpreted as the full ketamine-exposed enrolled cohort (see AE_denominator rationale above); this is an inference, not a printed count.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: pending
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Two-group proportions (%)' / 'OR' / 'approx'
- **note**: 1-year abstinence was 13/26 (50%) multiple-KPT vs 6/27 (22.2%) single-KPT (text p<0.05; Figure 1 caption prints a discrepant P<0.01 for the same comparison, unresolved). OR computed from the 2x2 abstinent/relapsed table as (13x21)/(13x6) = 3.5, favoring the multiple-session arm. The study's actual reported test was a Kaplan-Meier survival analysis with no printed log-rank statistic, so this OR is an approximation of the final-timepoint proportions rather than a direct conversion of an author-reported effect size.

## Kurland 1967

*Alcohol · 1967 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: The paper's narrative emphasis (abstract, MMPI figures 1-5) is mental-health change, but the MMPI results are only ever reported as 'all... statistically significant' with no values, N, or test statistics printed anywhere, so they cannot supply a quantitative Outcome. The only fully quantified, SUD-related result in the article is in the Conclusion: 'only 23 patients, or exactly one-third, had maintained abstinence up to the six-month follow-up' (23/69 = 33%). This is a single-arm pre/post rate with no comparator; selected per the SUD-behavioral precedence rule as the sole outcome carrying a usable number, consistent with the task instruction that a no-comparator study yields a single-arm-rate Outcome.
- **SD**: No dispersion or test statistic accompanies the 23/69 abstinence figure anywhere in the text. The MMPI section states clinical-scale changes are 'statistically significant' but never prints a test name, statistic, df, or SD, so SD is coded '.' for the chosen Outcome.
- **PVal**: No p-value is given for the abstinence rate. The MMPI section asserts significance ('all are statistically significant') but prints no p-value or threshold anywhere in the article, so PVal is coded '.' for the chosen Outcome.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: 69 is the full pilot cohort ('69 male inpatients were treated as described'; 'in the 69 cases under discussion'), and the abstinence fraction is explicitly computed over this same cohort ('23 patients, or exactly one-third' = 23/69), so 69 is used as the denominator for Outcome. The article does not separately report how many of the 69 were actually reachable/assessed at 6 months, so this denominator assumes the stated fraction applies to the full enrolled cohort.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 8/14 met. Unmet: eligibility, comparator, sample_size_power, randomization, blinding, statistical_methods. Objective, setting, intervention detail, outcomes measured, results, harms, and funding source are all explicitly described. Not described: formal inclusion/exclusion criteria (only 'male inpatients' is given), any statistical methods or test names, sample-size/power justification, and no explicit statement that the study was non-randomized/unblinded/uncontrolled (the contrast with 'a controlled study' initiated later implies it, but nothing states directly that this cohort had no randomization, blinding, or comparator). n_met=8 of 14, below the 13-of-14 threshold, so MethodologyDescribedYN=0.
- **Pos Result Y/N**: Single-arm study; judged on the study's own pre/post direction per the boundary rule. MMPI clinical-scale changes were reported as significant and in a positive direction (except Hypomania), and the authors' own framing ('psychedelic therapy does add significantly to presently available alcoholic rehabilitation resources'; 'the extent of benefit in some cases has seemed considerable') and the 33% abstinence figure support a positive-direction verdict (1), not a null/negative one.
- **RandomizationYN / BlindingYN / Double+Blind / Comparator**: Single-arm, open-label pilot: all 69 patients received the same one-session LSD procedure with no control or comparison group, no randomization, and no blinding described anywhere. Per the v1.2 single-arm boundary rule these are coded 0 (absent design elements), not 3.
- **Clincial Trial YN**: Coded 1 to match the eligibility screen: a prospectively planned intervention (structured LSD session within a defined ~3-week psychotherapy course) administered to a defined enrolled cohort (69 male inpatients) for research purposes, with prospective outcome assessment (MMPI, EEG, cognitive testing, 6-month abstinence follow-up). Single-arm/open-label does not disqualify it as a trial.
- **Retention (%)**: Not calculable. The article gives only the numerator/denominator for abstinence (23/69) at 6 months; it never separately reports how many of the 69 enrolled/treated patients were retained in follow-up versus lost to follow-up, so no completers/denominator arithmetic is possible. Coded '.'.
- **InclusionExclusionCriteria**: Only a minimal population description is given ('69 male inpatients' hospitalized in the Alcoholic Rehabilitation Unit); no explicit inclusion or exclusion criteria (diagnostic threshold, age range, psychiatric or medical exclusions) are stated, so coded 0 per the project's strict not-reported-is-not-met convention.
- **ConfoundYN**: No statistical adjustment, stratification, or substantive discussion of confounding/moderating/mediating factors appears anywhere in the article. Coded 0.
- **Power**: No a priori or post hoc power discussion, or any statement of the study being under/over-powered, appears anywhere. Coded 0.
- **PrimaryCare**: Treatment was delivered entirely within a state psychiatric hospital's specialized Alcoholic Rehabilitation Unit and its own outpatient follow-up team; no primary-care setting or provider interface is described. Coded 0.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Patients are described only as 'hospitalized in the Alcoholic Rehabilitation Unit' with no stated recruitment method (consecutive admissions vs. selective volunteer enrollment, screening steps). Because this is a real hospital inpatient population it is plausibly representative, but since the recruitment method itself is never described, coded 0 per the project's strict not-reported-is-not-met convention.
- **BaseStatus**: Coded 'Severe' based on multiple convergent textual anchors: the sample is chronic, hospitalized alcoholics ('the prodromal phase... average of ten to 12 years of heavy to excessive drinking'; one illustrative patient 'continuously intoxicated for nearly 18 months'), and the discussant explicitly characterizes the population as having 'chronic and severe alcoholism'.
- **Age_Median**: No age data (mean, median, or range) for the 69-patient sample is reported anywhere in the text. Coded '.'.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: No race/ethnicity data for the sample is reported. Coded '.'.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: One illustrative individual case (Figure 5 patient) is described as 'an unskilled laborer, intellectually dull, poorly educated, and culturally deprived,' but this is a single case vignette, not a description of SES for the sample as a whole or an inclusion criterion. Coded 0.
- **Implementation Outcome**: The text notes anecdotally that 'the introduction of this procedure met with rapid and lasting patient, staff, and administrative acceptance,' but this is a narrative aside, not a measured feasibility/acceptability/fidelity outcome with any instrument or systematic assessment. Coded 0.
- **AnyAEReported**: SafetyReported=Y because the authors explicitly assessed safety (independent EEG ratings pre/post in 20 consecutive patients, a battery of intellectual-functioning tests, ongoing clinical evaluation) and state a direct conclusion: 'no patient has been harmed.' The Treatment Procedure section mentions the therapist 'aborting anxiety or other turbulent or disruptive episodes' and reviewing possible reactions (fear, depression, paranoia, confusion) with patients beforehand, but this is generic description of the therapist's standing role/preparatory counseling, not a reported incidence of any specific AE with a count or named case in this cohort. Given the article's explicit 'no patient has been harmed' claim and the absence of any counted or named adverse event, this is coded as the 'no AEs occurred' case: AnyAEReported=N, NumDistinctAEs=0, WorstSeverity=None, ae_events=[].
- **InterventionDescription**: Source text prints the LSD dose as '450 pg.' — almost certainly an OCR/ligature misread of '450 μg' (micrograms), consistent with the well-documented Spring Grove/Kurland program's standard 300-450 mcg LSD dosing. The number 450 itself is unambiguous; only the unit abbreviation is a legibility artifact, so it is read through per the ligature-artifact allowance and written as '450 mcg'.
- **EligibleYN**: Both criteria met. (a) Clinical trial: a prospectively planned intervention (structured ~3-week psychotherapy course incorporating one high-dose LSD session) delivered to a defined enrolled cohort (69 male inpatients) for research purposes, with prospective outcome assessment (MMPI, EEG, cognitive testing, 6-month follow-up) — single-arm/open-label per the boundary rule does not disqualify it. (b) SUD outcome: the article reports a substance-use endpoint, six-month abstinence (23/69 maintained abstinence).

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Abstinence rate (%), single-arm' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: Single-arm open-label pilot (N=69) with no control or comparison group; the only quantified SUD outcome is a pre/post proportion (23/69, 33% abstinent at 6 months). A single-arm rate has no defensible conversion under any of the seven supported Tier-1 methods (d, g, eta_p, F, chi2, OR, RR), so this is routed to Tier 2 rather than presented as a treatment effect.

## Luquiens 2025

*Alcohol · 2025 · eligible=Y · config=pending*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Primary outcome is explicitly feasibility ('The primary outcome was feasibility, assessed as the number of patients completing both psilocybin sessions, and screening and inclusion rates' — Abstract Measurements, Methods 'Measures'). The Statistical analysis section names the primary-endpoint test explicitly ('The rate of patients completing both dosing sessions (primary endpoint) was compared between groups via a Fischer test'). Results 'Feasibility' section reports this comparison: 95% (19/20) in the 25 mg group vs 60% (6/10) in the 1 mg group, P=0.031. This is a feasibility endpoint, not a substance-use outcome; the key SUD-related secondary outcome was 12-week abstinence (55% vs 11%, P=0.043; Table 2, Results 'Preliminary efficacy'), reported here for transparency but not used as Outcome per the boundary-case rule that Outcome must be the stated primary even when the SUD outcome is only secondary.
- **SD**: Fisher's exact test was used for the primary feasibility comparison (2nd-dose completion); the article reports only the resulting P-value (0.031, Results 'Feasibility'), with no separate test statistic (e.g., chi-square value) printed for this comparison. Coded '.'.
- **PVal**: P=0.031 for 2nd-dose completion (95% vs 60%), reported verbatim in Results 'Feasibility' section ('P-value = 0.031').
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: The feasibility/primary-endpoint comparison (2nd-dose completion) was computed over the full randomized, as-treated per-protocol sample: n=20 (25 mg) + n=10 (1 mg) = 30, consistent with the reported percentages (19/20=95%, 6/10=60%).
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 14/14 met. Unmet: none. All 14 elements are explicitly described: objective (Abstract/Introduction aims), design (Methods 'Design' — prospective, single-center, double-blind, 2:1 parallel RCT pilot), setting (French inpatient addiction unit, CHU Nîmes), eligibility (detailed inclusion/exclusion lists), intervention (dosing/preparation/integration protocol described in detail), comparator (1 mg psilocybin arm described), outcomes (Measures section defines primary and secondary outcomes), sample_size_power (dedicated 'Sample size' subsection citing Hertzog's pilot-study sizing method; no formal power calculation but the determination is explicitly described and justified), randomization (SAS-generated sequence, stratified by gender, permuted block sizes 3/6, implemented via Inclusio software), blinding (double-blind design described, plus a formal blinding-integrity assessment), statistical_methods (detailed 'Statistical analysis' section), results (comprehensive Results with tables/figures), harms (dedicated 'Safety' section and AE Table 4), funding_coi (Funding information section names the grant; 'Declaration of interests: None').
- **Pos Result Y/N**: Coded 1: the stated primary (feasibility) endpoint succeeded (95% vs 60% 2nd-dose completion, P=0.031) and the study concluded psilocybin therapy was feasible, acceptable and safe; the SUD-related secondary outcomes also favored the 25 mg group (12-week abstinence 55% vs 11%, P=0.043; % change in drinking days P=0.038; craving frequency P=0.045), giving a consistent positive direction across primary and key secondary endpoints.
- **PrimaryCare**: Study conducted in a specialized inpatient addiction/rehabilitation unit (CHU Nîmes), not a primary-care setting; no primary-care setting or provider involvement is described anywhere in the article. Coded 0.
- **Power**: No formal a priori power calculation is reported; the 20:10 sample size was determined per Hertzog's pilot-study sample-size recommendations (Statistical analysis, 'Sample size'), not a power analysis. However, the Discussion explicitly states a possible 'lack of power' for the antidepressant-subgroup comparison, which per the rubric's rule ('a bare statement that the study was underpowered counts as discussed') is coded 1.
- **Retention (%)**: 30 participants randomized and analyzed as-treated (20:10, per-protocol). The Statistical analysis section states 'only 1 week 12 endpoint was missing' (one 1 mg-group participant lost to follow-up before week 12); no other losses to the analyzed population are reported. 29/30 = 96.7%. Denominator is the randomized N (30) per rule, since randomized and as-treated N coincide here.
- **ConfoundYN**: Coded 1: the study explicitly assessed subgroup/moderator effects — antidepressant use on drinking/depression outcomes (Table 3, dedicated subsection), and baseline PCL-5 as a factor associated with abstinence response.
- **Age_Median**: Abstract reports mean age 49 ± 10; Table 1 reports median age 50 (IQR 42-57). The two values straddle the '30-50'/'50+' bracket boundary. Coded '30-50' based on the explicitly-labeled mean (49), noting the median (50) sits exactly at the bracket boundary.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Race/ethnicity data are not reported anywhere in the article (French single-center cohort). Coded '.'.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Single-center study at CHU Nîmes, France; approved by the French Ethics Committee (Comité de Protection des Personnes) and French national medicines agency. Coded 'NonUS'.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 0: highly selected volunteer sample — 350 screened, 30 enrolled (8.6%) after extensive psychiatric/medical exclusion criteria (psychosis/bipolar history, severe personality disorder, prior hallucinogen use, numerous medical contraindications, etc.); recruitment was substantially driven by media exposure and self-referral ('The study received high media exposure, leading to proactive outreach'; 98% of requests were spontaneous), not reflective of the general severe-AUD treatment-seeking population.
- **Comparator**: Comparator is a very-low-dose (1 mg) psilocybin arm used as an active/blinding comparator rather than an inert placebo or no-treatment control (a dose-comparison design); coded 1 per the rule that dose-comparison designs count as a comparator.
- **InterventionTherapy**: Coded 1: structured preparation (day before) and integration (day after) psychotherapy sessions accompany both dosing sessions, plus non-directive supportive psychotherapy during dosing itself, per a study therapeutic manual.
- **Followup in months**: Longest follow-up assessment was at week 12 post-inclusion (12 weeks / 4 = 3 months). This horizon is measured from inclusion/baseline (not end of treatment) and is used consistently for the week-3/6/12 assessment schedule throughout the article (2nd dose occurs at week 3), so there is no separate end-of-treatment horizon to reconcile against.
- **MortalityMeasured**: No deaths occurred or were reported anywhere in the article; the complete AE listing (Table 4) contains no fatal event. Mortality was not established as a formal outcome measure. Coded 0 (rather than '.') since the absence of any death is confirmable from the exhaustive AE table and narrative.
- **ArmScope**: Both study arms (25 mg and 1 mg) received psilocybin — there is no non-exposed comparator; the 'control' arm is a low, sub-active dose, not a placebo. Coded 'psychedelic_arm' because the entire per-protocol sample (n=30) is psilocybin-exposed by design, even though Table 4 breaks AE counts out by dose group; NumDistinctAEs (19), NParticipantsWithAE (16, = 10 in 25 mg + 6 in 1 mg) and AE_denominator (30) aggregate across both dose groups accordingly.

### Adverse events
- **Luquiens 2025_AE01**: Onset coded 'delayed' (event occurred about 3 weeks after the first dose, days before the scheduled second); severity Severe as a life-threatening cardiovascular event (FDA seriousness criterion), though the article states it was unrelated to treatment. InterventionRequired coded '.' since the article does not describe the clinical management given.
- **Luquiens 2025_AE02**: NAffected coded 2 to match Table 4's '2 (10%)' attributable count; the Safety narrative describes 'two episodes... in the same participant', which may mean only 1 patient experienced both episodes rather than 2 separate patients each having one — flagged as a minor internal inconsistency between narrative and table.
- **Luquiens 2025_AE03**: Graded Moderate per rubric because the nightmares persisted 9 days (sleep disturbance lasting days-to-weeks, no FDA seriousness criterion met), rather than Mild; no explicit intervention is mentioned.
- **Luquiens 2025_AE04**: Graded Moderate because resolution required an active (if minimal/noninvasive) intervention — a snack — analogous to a CTCAE Grade 2 event, rather than a no-intervention Mild event.
- **Luquiens 2025_AE05**: SeverityIndeterminate=Y: the article does not describe plan, intent, or clinical intervention for any of the three ideation events, only that they occurred; per rubric, underspecified AEs default to Mild with this flag set, given the safety-sensitive nature of suicidal ideation this is flagged for human review. NAffected=3 sums the 1 mg-group case (1, attributable) and the 25 mg-group cases (2, not attributable), all described in the Safety 'Suicidal risk' narrative.
- **Luquiens 2025_AE06**: Graded Severe per the rubric's explicit PAT-specific list ('suicidal behavior/attempt' is automatically Severe); the article states this event was not attributable to treatment.
- **Luquiens 2025_AE07**: SeverityIndeterminate=Y: Table 4 gives only the AE label, count, and attributability column, with no severity or intervention detail; defaulted to Mild per rubric.
- **Luquiens 2025_AE08**: SeverityIndeterminate=Y: label-only Table 4 entry with no further detail; defaulted to Mild per rubric.
- **Luquiens 2025_AE09**: SeverityIndeterminate=Y: label-only Table 4 entry with no further detail; defaulted to Mild per rubric.
- **Luquiens 2025_AE10**: SeverityIndeterminate=Y: label-only Table 4 entry with no further detail; defaulted to Mild per rubric.
- **Luquiens 2025_AE11**: SeverityIndeterminate=Y: label-only Table 4 entry with no further detail; defaulted to Mild per rubric.
- **Luquiens 2025_AE12**: SeverityIndeterminate=Y: label-only Table 4 entry; attributability itself is marked 'NA' in the table and no severity detail is given; defaulted to Mild per rubric.
- **Luquiens 2025_AE13**: Graded Severe because inpatient hospitalization directly meets an FDA seriousness criterion; onset coded 'delayed' since the AUD relapse/hospitalization occurred after discharge from the index admission.
- **Luquiens 2025_AE14**: SeverityIndeterminate=Y: label-only Table 4 entry with no further detail; defaulted to Mild per rubric.
- **Luquiens 2025_AE15**: SeverityIndeterminate=Y: label-only Table 4 entry with no further detail; defaulted to Mild per rubric despite ankle sprains typically being minor, since no explicit severity/intervention detail is given in this article.
- **Luquiens 2025_AE16**: SeverityIndeterminate=Y: no follow-up information (e.g., biopsy result, referral outcome) is given for the breast lump; defaulted to Mild per rubric pending further detail. Onset coded 'unknown' because the finding predates any dosing (discovered on entry examination before treatment).
- **Luquiens 2025_AE17**: SeverityIndeterminate=Y: label-only Table 4 entry with no further detail; defaulted to Mild per rubric.
- **Luquiens 2025_AE18**: SeverityIndeterminate=Y: label-only Table 4 entry with no further detail; defaulted to Mild per rubric.
- **Luquiens 2025_AE19**: SeverityIndeterminate=Y: label-only Table 4 entry with no further detail; defaulted to Mild per rubric. NAffected=2 sums the 1 mg-group case (1) and 25 mg-group case (1).

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: pending
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Two-group proportions (%)' / 'OR' / 'approx'
- **note**: Primary endpoint is feasibility (2nd-dose completion: 95% [19/20] vs 60% [6/10], Fisher's exact P=0.031), not a substance-use outcome; computed as an odds ratio from the two-group proportions per codebook precedence. Coded approx (not clean) both because a proportion-to-OR conversion is an assumption a methodologist should see, and because this is a feasibility rather than SUD-behavioral effect size — a human reviewer may prefer substituting the 12-week abstinence secondary outcome (55% vs 11%, P=0.043, OR computable similarly) for the SUD-specific forest plot.

## MacLean 1961

*Alcohol · 1961 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Primary and only reported outcome is the composite improvement rating (much improved / improved / unchanged) for the alcoholic subgroup, which for alcoholics is explicitly anchored to abstinence or reduced drinking versus the prior 12 months (Evaluation section; Table 1-A 'Totals' row). 30 much improved + 16 improved = 46/61 (75.4%) vs 15/61 (24.6%) unchanged. No other primary endpoint is designated anywhere in the article. Note: the 'Composite Totals' row at the foot of Table 1 (68 M / 34 F) is arithmetically inconsistent with the sum of the A and B subgroup totals (50+16=66 M), likely an OCR/print artifact in the source table; it was not used because the alcoholic-only subgroup total (61) is internally self-consistent (50 M + 11 F = 61; 30+16+15 = 61) and is the correct population for this Alcohol-condition extraction.
- **SD**: No dispersion or test statistic is reported anywhere in the article for the improvement-rate outcome; results are purely descriptive counts with no significance testing performed. Coded '.'.
- **PVal**: No p-value is reported for the treatment outcome anywhere in the article (Results, Summary, or Discussion). Coded '.'.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: 61 — the alcoholic subgroup (Table 1-A Totals: 50 M + 11 F = 61), not the full 100-patient series (61 alcoholics + 39 non-alcoholics with other psychiatric disabilities). Outcome is drawn from the alcoholic subgroup's own results table since Condition = Alcohol. All 61 are accounted for across the three outcome categories (30+16+15=61), so no further attrition adjustment was needed.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 7/14 met. Unmet: eligibility, comparator, sample_size_power, randomization, blinding, statistical_methods, funding_coi. Objective, design, setting, intervention, outcome criteria, results, and harms (Side Effects paragraph) are all described. Not described: formal a priori eligibility/exclusion criteria (sample characteristics are given post hoc instead), the absence of a comparator/control group is never explicitly acknowledged, sample size is given but no power rationale, randomization and blinding are never mentioned (not even as absent), no statistical methods/tests are described (purely descriptive counts), and no funding source or conflict-of-interest statement is given (only acknowledgment of clinical/AA staff assistance). n_met=7 < 13, so MethodologyDescribedYN=0.
- **RandomizationYN**: Coded 0, not 3. Single-arm, open-label case series (100 patients, all received LSD-25; no control/comparison group). Per the single-arm boundary-case rule, the absent design element is coded 0.
- **BlindingYN**: Coded 0, not 3, for the same single-arm/open-label reason.
- **Double+Blind**: Coded 0, consistent with BlindingYN=0.
- **Comparator**: Coded 0, not 3. No control or comparison arm exists; all 100 patients received the same LSD-25 protocol.
