- **Retention (%)**: Coded 100. Study is not randomized, so the denominator is the enrolled/treated alcoholic subgroup (61). All 61 are accounted for within the three outcome categories in Table 1-A (30+16+15=61), implying no attrition from the reported analysis. Not stated explicitly as a retention percentage by the authors — inferred from the completeness of Table 1-A.
- **ConfoundYN**: Coded 1. Outcomes are stratified by diagnostic subgroup throughout (Table 1, Results, Discussion — e.g., differential response noted for 'sociopathic disturbance' vs 'personality trait disturbance' vs 'addiction with chronic brain damage'), substantively addressing diagnosis as a factor related to outcome, though no formal statistical adjustment for other covariates (age, sex, dose) is reported.
- **InclusionExclusionCriteria**: Coded 0. The Sample section describes characteristics of the 61 selected alcoholics post hoc (delirium tremens history, prior AA failure, years of uncontrolled drinking) but never states a priori inclusion or exclusion criteria used to select the 61 from the 492 total admissions.
- **ValidatedScaleMeasYN**: Coded 0. The Blewett Psychedelic Scales (A and B) assess the character of the psychedelic experience itself, not a validated clinical or substance-use outcome measure; the composite improvement rating (much improved/improved/unchanged) is an author-devised clinical rating, not a published/validated instrument.
- **PhysicalChangeMeasured**: Coded 0. 'Clinical examination, including certain blood chemistry studies' was collected as pretreatment/baseline data (Collection of Data, section A.3), but the authors explicitly defer these findings 'to be discussed in another paper' and report no physical/biological change outcome within this article.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 0. The 61 alcoholics are a severity-selected subset (~12%) of 492 total hospital admissions for alcohol intoxication over 18 months, specifically chosen as 'difficult cases' (59/61 with prior delirium tremens, 36/61 having failed AA) rather than a representative cross-section of the admitted population.
- **PercentFem**: 18.0 (11/61), computed from Table 1-A Totals row (50 M, 11 F) for the alcoholic subgroup, consistent with the SampleSize_NFinal denominator used for Outcome.
- **BaseStatus**: Coded 'Severe'. Sample section reports an average of 14.36 years of uncontrolled drinking, average 8.07 hospital admissions for alcoholism in the preceding 3 years, 59/61 with prior delirium tremens, and 36/61 having failed Alcoholics Anonymous; authors state the group's prognosis 'would be unfavorable.'
- **Age_Median**: Coded '.'. No aggregate mean or median age is reported for the alcoholic subgroup or the full 100-patient sample; only individual case-example ages (44-64) are given, which cannot be generalized to a sample-level bracket.
- **Followup in months**: 18, the maximum of the reported range ('follow-up has been carried on for periods ranging from 3 to 18 months, the median being 9.09 months'). The protocol also describes a planned 1-year (12-month) interview, but 18 months is the longest duration explicitly reported as achieved in the Results.
- **EligibilityNote**: NA — EligibleYN = 'Y'.
- **EligibilityFailedCriterion**: NA — EligibleYN = 'Y'.
- **AE_denominator**: Coded 'Not numbered'. No explicit safety-population size is stated in the Side Effects paragraph; the implied denominator is the full treated series (N=100), but this figure is never restated numerically alongside the side-effect descriptions.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: Coded '.'. No aggregate count of patients experiencing at least one side effect is given; only a general 'typical side effects' statement and four illustrative case narratives are provided.

### Adverse events
- **MacLean 1961_AE01**: Onset 'in-session': occurs 1/2-2 hours after 8:20 AM dosing, within the session day (session runs through the afternoon). Severity 'Mild' via rubric (transient, expected, no intervention described); authors gave no severity adjective for nausea specifically, unlike 'mild headache' and 'mild gastric distress'.
- **MacLean 1961_AE02**: SeveritySource='author': text explicitly states 'mild headache.'
- **MacLean 1961_AE03**: SeveritySource='author': text explicitly states 'mild gastric distress.'
- **MacLean 1961_AE04**: Aggregates in-session anxiety/tension described in the general Procedure section and in multiple case examples (Case 43 'moderate degree of tension'; Case 2 explicitly labeled 'adverse effects in the form of increasing tension'; Case 5 'feelings of apprehension and oppression'). Graded 'Moderate' using Case 43's own author-assigned qualifier ('moderate') as the representative severity marker for this recurring AE type; per the rubric, expected in-session anxiety defaults to Mild absent intervention need or persistence, but the authors' own 'moderate' language for this type is treated as author-sourced grading. InterventionRequired coded '.' because the protocol's response to persistent anxiety ('more LSD-25 is needed') is a dosing decision, not a clearly-described rescue intervention.
- **MacLean 1961_AE05**: Perceptual distortions (Case 43, visual field, ~4 hours post-dose) graded 'Mild' via rubric — expected acute drug-effect phenomenology, no intervention or functional consequence described.
- **MacLean 1961_AE06**: SeverityIndeterminate='Y'. Case 29 shows confused, disorganized speech during the session and 'no improvement in her mental condition on the day of discharge, 6 days after' the session — persistence beyond the dosing day would indicate Moderate per the rubric's operational band definitions. However, this same patient was independently documented as disoriented, with poor memory and slurred/disconnected speech, 3-5 days BEFORE treatment (pre-existing cognitive impairment, plausibly alcohol-related). It cannot be determined from the text whether the persisting confusion at discharge reflects a drug-emergent AE or her unresolved pre-existing baseline state. Graded 'Moderate' on the persistence signal but flagged indeterminate due to this baseline confound; recommend human review. InterventionRequired coded '.' — no explicit intervention for her confusion is described.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Single-arm improvement rate (%)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: Single-arm, uncontrolled case series with no comparator arm; only a pre/post composite improvement rate is available for the alcoholic subgroup (46/61 much improved or improved vs 15/61 unchanged, anchored to abstinence/reduced drinking for the alcoholics), which has no defensible conversion to a comparative Tier-1 effect size (d, g, eta_p, F, chi2, OR, or RR).

## Marvania 2024

*Alcohol · 2024 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Abstract and Table 1: the abstract's headline result is 'a final assessment showing a 51.98% increase in confidence from baseline,' matching Table 1's row 'Pre versus finalpost 2 week' (change 51.98%). AASE confidence-to-abstain is the study's stated primary aim ('to evaluate the efficacy of ketamine in enhancing confidence levels to abstain from alcohol'), and the 2-week final assessment is the outcome the abstract and conclusion close on, so it was chosen over the intermediate post-session-1/2/3 comparisons in the same table, which reflect cumulative in-treatment change rather than the sustained/primary endpoint.
- **SD**: Table 1, 'Pre versus finalpost 2 week' row, 'T statistics' column = -4.982. No degrees of freedom were printed for this paired t-test in Table 1 (N=18 would imply df=17, but this is not stated in the article, so it is not appended).
- **PVal**: Table 1, same row, 'P' column = '<0.05'; the article states this threshold applies 'across all comparisons.'
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: Results: 'The study included 18 participants.' No dropout or loss to follow-up is reported anywhere in the text; abstinence status ('seven participants had maintained abstinence... the rest gave a history of consuming alcohol') is reported for what appears to be the full enrolled sample, so N=18 is used as the analyzed sample for the final-assessment comparison.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 11/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power, randomization, blinding. Setting (GMERS Medical College psychiatric OPD, Ahmedabad), eligibility, intervention protocol, outcome measures (AASE/AUDIT), statistics (paired t-test/ANOVA), results, harms ('no report of any adverse effects'), and funding/COI ('Nil'; 'no conflicts of interest') are all explicitly described. Comparator absence is counted as described because the Discussion explicitly flags the lack of an 'adequate control' arm as a needed addition for future work. No sample-size/power justification is given, and randomization and blinding are never mentioned or characterized as absent anywhere in the text (the single-arm, unblinded design must be inferred from the Methods rather than stated), so those three criteria are coded N. n_met=11 < 13, so the binary is 0.
- **Pos Result Y/N**: Coded 1 on the primary AASE confidence outcome, which showed a statistically significant increase sustained to 2-week follow-up (p<0.05). Flagging a tension: the Discussion itself questions the clinical meaningfulness of this positive result, since only 7/18 (38.8%) maintained abstinence at follow-up, a lower rate than comparator ketamine studies cited (53.9-70%) and than psychotherapy-alone benchmarks (~28%) cited in the same paragraph. Pos Result Y/N reflects the primary designated outcome (AASE), not the abstinence rate.
- **RandomizationYN**: No random allocation is described anywhere; all 18 enrolled patients received the same ketamine protocol. Per PromptVersion v1.2, a single-arm design codes this element's absence as 0, not 3.
- **BlindingYN**: No blinding of any kind (patient, rater, assessor) is mentioned; open-label single-arm design. Coded 0, not 3, per the v1.2 single-arm boundary rule.
- **Double+Blind**: 0 because BlindingYN=0; open-label study, no blinding of any kind was implemented.
- **Comparator**: No control or comparison arm; ketamine was given to all 18 participants as an add-on to standard detoxification. Coded 0 (design element absent), not 3, per the v1.2 single-arm boundary rule. The Discussion explicitly frames this absence as a limitation, calling for 'a longer study with adequate control... in a controlled setting' in future work.
- **PrimaryCare**: Study was conducted entirely within a psychiatric outpatient de-addiction department (specialist mental-health care), with no interface to primary-care providers or settings described.
- **Power**: No a priori or post hoc power calculation, and no discussion of the study being underpowered, appears anywhere in the text (this is an 18-patient pilot with no such discussion).
- **Retention (%)**: Study was not randomized, so the denominator is the enrolled N (18). No dropout, loss to follow-up, or incomplete final assessment is reported; abstinence/relapse status at the final assessment is given for what reads as the full sample of 18, so retention = 18/18 = 100%.
- **BaseStatus**: Results: 'AUDIT score for all the participants was above 20.' AUDIT >=20 conventionally corresponds to likely alcohol dependence (the most severe AUDIT band), so baseline severity is coded 'Severe.'
- **Age_Median**: Results: 'mean age of 34.5 years' falls in the 30-50 bracket.
- **PercentFem**: Results: '18 participants, 17 males and 1 female' = 1/18 = 5.56%.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Article reports no race or ethnicity data for the sample at all (only sex and age are given), so coded '.' rather than inferring a value from the study's India location.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Study conducted at GMERS Medical College and Hospital, Ahmedabad, Gujarat, India; coded 'NonUS' with the country name recorded here rather than in the field itself.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Participants were drawn from routine psychiatric OPD de-addiction visits (consecutive treatment-seekers aged 18+), with exclusions only for other substance use, non-depression/anxiety comorbid psychiatric disorders, and physical unfitness for the procedure -- a fairly representative clinical de-addiction population rather than a narrowly screened volunteer/advertisement sample.
- **InterventionTherapy**: The protocol consists of standard benzodiazepine detoxification followed by ketamine infusions plus informed-consent counseling about the procedure; no structured psychotherapy, integration, or motivational-enhancement component is described, unlike the comparator studies discussed (e.g., Dakwar et al. and Grabski et al. paired ketamine with MET/psychological therapy). Coded 0.
- **HarmsAssessed**: Discussion: 'There was no report of any adverse effects to ketamine in the dose of 0.5 mg/kg,' which is an explicit (if brief) safety assessment, consistent with SafetyReported='Y'.
- **ReductionMeasured**: The study reports only a binary abstinence/relapse status ('maintained abstinence' vs. 'gave a history of consuming alcohol') rather than a distinct partial-reduction/harm-reduction endpoint (e.g., drinks per week, heavy-drinking days), so coded 0.
- **RelapseMeasured**: Coded 1 because return-to-use status was explicitly tracked at the final follow-up ('the rest gave a history of consuming alcohol'), even though no drinking-days or quantity detail is given beyond this binary status.
- **AE_denominator**: Not explicitly stated as a separate 'safety population'; taken as the full enrolled/treated sample of 18, since the article's single blanket statement of no adverse effects appears to cover all ketamine-treated participants.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: Inferred as 0 from AnyAEReported='N' (article states no adverse effects were reported for the ketamine dose used); not itself given as an explicit count in the text.
- **EligibleYN**: Passes (a): prospectively planned add-on intervention (ketamine, fixed dose/schedule) delivered under an IRB-approved protocol (IECHRPGR reference number given) with defined enrollment (Nov 2023-Jan 2024), explicit inclusion/exclusion criteria, and prospective outcome assessment (AASE at baseline, after each session, and at 2-week follow-up) -- this is a clinical trial despite lacking randomization/blinding/comparator (single-arm trials are eligible per the v1.2 boundary case). Passes (b): AASE (a validated alcohol self-efficacy/craving instrument), AUDIT scores, and abstinence/relapse status are all substance-use outcomes.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Paired t-test (within-subjects, no comparator arm)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: This is a single-arm pre/post design: the reported paired t-test (T=-4.982, p<0.05, 51.98% AASE increase) is a within-subject change over time, not a between-groups effect size, so none of the seven supported Tier-1 conversion methods apply defensibly.

## Nicholas 2022

*Alcohol · 2022 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Citation/Title**: This article is a secondary/exploratory analysis of alcohol and substance-use outcomes drawn from the MAPS-sponsored Phase 3 MDMA-AT trial for severe PTSD. The parent trial, whose primary endpoint was CAPS-5 PTSD severity (not alcohol/substance use), is Mitchell, J.M. et al. (2021), 'MDMA-assisted therapy for severe PTSD: a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled phase 3 study,' Nat. Med. 27(6), 1025-1033, cited throughout Methods (§2.1) as the source of full study methods/protocol.
- **Outcome**: No outcome is explicitly labeled 'primary' anywhere in the article; both AUDIT (alcohol) and DUDIT (drug) change scores are examined. Per the abstract-leads precedence rule, AUDIT is treated as this article's primary SUD outcome because the Abstract Results paragraph and §3.2 both lead with the AUDIT group difference before mentioning DUDIT, and AUDIT is the only one of the two that reached statistical significance. Value taken as the directly reported Hedges' g=.45 for the MDMA-AT vs Placebo+Therapy AUDIT change-score comparison (Abstract; Results §3.2).
- **SD**: Paired with the Outcome value: the F-test reported alongside Hedge's g=.45 for the same AUDIT group difference, printed as 'F(80, 1)=4.20' in both the Abstract and Results §3.2. Reported exactly as printed (df order appears reversed from the conventional F(df_effect, df_error) form) rather than corrected, per the instruction not to construct a decoding theory for a number that looks off.
- **PVal**: p=0.0436, printed alongside the same AUDIT Hedge's g/F-test result in the Abstract and Results §3.2.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: N=82 (42 MDMA-AT, 40 Placebo+Therapy) is the number of participants with complete baseline and study-termination AUDIT/DUDIT data used in the reported AUDIT group comparison (§3.1: 'leaving a total of 82 participants that completed both baseline and study termination assessments...Participants with complete data were used in the present analysis').
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 12/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power, harms. 12 of 14 criteria met. sample_size_power=N: no a priori sample-size or power calculation is reported anywhere in this article; the Discussion only offers a post hoc, informal statement that the narrow AUDIT score distribution 'limited the potential to detect a statistically significant measurable change,' which is not a described power analysis. harms=N: this article contains no dedicated adverse-event/harms/safety section, table, or count; safety data for the parent trial were published separately in Mitchell et al. (2021). n_met=12 < 13, so MethodologyDescribedYN=0.
- **Retention (%)**: 82/90x100=91.11%, using randomized N (90) as the denominator since only a single randomized-N figure is given (§3.1: '90 participants were randomized'). Note: the article states 3 MDMA and 4 Placebo participants withdrew (7 total), which arithmetically implies 83 completers (90-7=83), but the article explicitly states 82 participants completed both assessments. This is an internal numeric inconsistency in the source text; the printed completer count (82) was used as given rather than resolved by inference, per the instruction to report inconsistent numbers as printed and flag them.
- **BaseStatus**: Coded '.' as a judgment call. The analyzed sample had 0 current alcohol use disorder in both arms per the MINI (Table 1); only a minority (21/82, 25.6%) had past AUD in remission, and mean baseline AUDIT was subclinical (3.56/40 possible, Table 1). The Discussion states explicitly that 'no participant with even mild AUD was recruited into the study, despite being permitted.' This population does not cleanly map to Severe/Moderate/Mild AUD severity at baseline, so '.' was used rather than guessing a band.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: 'Both' — the parent trial (and this analysis's sample) was conducted across 15 sites in the US, Canada, and Israel (§2.1).
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: 19.75% = sum of non-White race categories in Table 1 (American Indian/Alaska Native 3.70% + Asian 6.17% + Black or African American 2.47% + More than one race 7.41%), consistent with 100% - White 80.25% = 19.75%.
- **PercentFem**: 64.63% per Table 1 (Female 53/82); the Abstract/§3.1 text rounds this to 64.6%.
- **Power**: Coded 1 (discussed informally, not a priori calculated). No formal power calculation is reported anywhere in the article, but the Discussion states the narrow AUDIT score distribution 'limited the potential to detect a statistically significant measurable change from baseline,' which is treated as a bare discussion of the study being underpowered for this purpose, per the rule that a bare underpowered statement counts as Power=1.
- **SafetyReported**: Coded 'N'. This article has no dedicated adverse-event/safety section, table, or AE counts anywhere in the text. The only safety-adjacent statement is that MDMA-AT 'did not demonstrate increased risk of substance use, abuse, or dependence, or increased MDMA use during and 2 months following treatment' (Discussion) — this is a substance-use-risk finding of this analysis, not a general harms/AE assessment. Full safety data for the parent trial were reported separately in Mitchell et al. (2021), which this article does not reproduce.
- **HarmsAssessed**: Coded 0 to satisfy the required concordance with SafetyReported='N' (SafetyReported='N' iff HarmsAssessed in {0,3,'.'}).
- **AnyAEReported**: 'NA' because SafetyReported='N' per the three-state AE presence table (article silent on AEs/safety entirely).
- **NumDistinctAEs**: 'NA' because SafetyReported='N'.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: 'NA' because SafetyReported='N'.
- **AE_denominator**: Coded '.' rather than 'NA' per the explicit rule that AE_denominator is never 'NA'; use '.' when no safety assessment was reported (SafetyReported='N').
- **ArmScope**: 'NA' because SafetyReported='N' and there is no AE data to scope to an arm.
- **WorstSeverity**: 'NA' because SafetyReported='N', per the three-state AE presence table.
- **QualityOfLifeMeasured**: Coded 0. The only functioning-adjacent instrument used is the modified Sheehan Disability Scale (SDS), which measures functional impairment/disability in work, social, and family life, not an explicit quality-of-life construct. No other QoL instrument is reported.
- **Followup in months**: Coded '.'. The article does not state a precise numeric interval between end of treatment and the 'study termination' AUDIT/DUDIT assessment point. The Discussion's phrase 'did not demonstrate increased risk...during and 2 months following treatment' describes the observation window for a safety-adjacent substance-use-risk claim, not an explicitly stated follow-up duration tied to the AUDIT/DUDIT primary comparison, so it was not used to avoid asserting an unstated number.
- **Pos Result Y/N**: Coded 1, based on the unadjusted, statistically significant AUDIT group difference favoring MDMA-AT (Hedge's g=.45, F(80,1)=4.20, p=.0436; §3.2). Flagging that this effect was no longer statistically significant after adjusting for baseline AUDIT (p=.52), and that DUDIT (drug use) showed no significant group difference at any timepoint — this is a qualified/partial positive result on the article's own primary (AUDIT) outcome.
- **InterventionTherapy**: Coded 1: each of the 3 MDMA/placebo dosing sessions was preceded by 3 90-minute preparatory therapy sessions and followed by 3 90-minute integration therapy sessions (§2.3), i.e., structured therapy well beyond drug administration alone.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 0. Participants underwent extensive clinical screening (CAPS-5 threshold ≥35, MINI-assessed psychiatric/SUD exclusions, exclusion of any current SUD other than mild/early-remission-moderate alcohol or cannabis use disorder) typical of a highly screened trial-eligible volunteer sample rather than a real-world clinical or community population.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: "Hedges' g" / 'g' / 'clean'
- **note**: Authors directly report Hedge's g=.45 for the AUDIT (alcohol) change-score group difference, MDMA-AT vs Placebo+Therapy (Abstract; §3.2); this is a direct pass-through of an author-computed Hedges' g requiring no conversion, and the reported positive value already favors MDMA-AT (greater alcohol-use reduction), so direction=1.

## O'Donnell 2022

*Alcohol · 2022 · eligible=N · config=pending*

### Article
- **EligibleYN**: Applied the screen honestly per instructions: this article's Methods/Discussion describe a fully-conducted RCT (96 randomized, 93 dosed at least once) but the abstract and 'Trial status' section state explicitly that primary outcomes 'will be published elsewhere' / 'have been published elsewhere [9]' -- no outcome data, effect size, or substance-use result of any kind appears anywhere in this text. Per task guidance, a rationale-and-design paper describes a trial rather than reporting one, so it fails (a); and because no substance-use outcome is reported anywhere in the text, it also fails (b). Coded EligibleYN='N', EligibilityFailedCriterion='both'.
- **Citation/Title**: This is the design/rationale paper for the trial whose primary results are reported in the companion publication: Bogenschutz, M.P., et al. (2022). Percentage of heavy drinking days following psilocybin-assisted psychotherapy vs placebo... JAMA Psychiatry 79(10), 953-962 (ref [9] in this article). Extracted citation elements from the title page/header of the supplied text.
- **Outcome**: No effect size is reported anywhere in this text. The article states its primary outcome is 'the proportion of heavy drinking days during the 32 weeks after the first dosing session' (Sec 2.1.1), but explicitly defers all results to the companion publication [9] ('The primary outcomes have been published elsewhere'; 'Trial status' section). Coded '.'.
- **SD**: No dispersion or test statistic for any outcome is reported in this text; only the statistical *methods* (MMRM, RM MANOVA) are described (Sec 4.2), with no computed values. Coded '.'.
- **PVal**: No p-value is reported anywhere in this text; results are deferred to ref [9]. Coded '.'.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: No analysis is reported in this article, so there is no analyzed-N to record. The text gives only enrollment/randomization counts: 96 participants randomized, 93 of whom received at least one dose of study medication (Sec 3.1) -- these are not an analysis denominator. Coded '.'.
- **Pos Result Y/N**: Coded 3 (cannot be determined): no outcome data of any kind is reported in this protocol/design article; directionality of the treatment effect is not stated or determinable from this text.
- **Clincial Trial YN**: Coded 0 to avoid the required internal-consistency contradiction with EligibilityFailedCriterion including a failure of criterion (a) (per the prompt: 'Clincial Trial YN = 1 with a failed criterion (a)... is an internal contradiction and will be rejected'). This does not mean no trial was conducted -- NCT02061293 was in fact completed with 96 randomized participants -- only that this particular article does not itself report the trial's conduct or outcomes, so the field is aligned with the eligibility verdict as the schema requires.
- **RandomizationYN**: Coded 1 from the described design (randomization was in fact performed: stratified by site, permuted blocks of varied sizes, assigned sequentially by the study pharmacist per Sec 4.1.1), per the boundary-case instruction that design features the protocol specifies may still be coded from the described design despite ineligibility.
- **BlindingYN**: Coded 1: the trial is described as double-blind, with study staff (except the pharmacist) blind to treatment arm (Sec 4.1.2), coded from the described design per the boundary-case instruction.
- **Double+Blind**: Coded 1, consistent with BlindingYN=1: the article's title, abstract, and Sec 2.1 all describe the trial as 'double-blind.'
- **Comparator**: Coded 1: active placebo (diphenhydramine 50-100mg) was used as comparator (Sec 4.1.4, 5.4), coded from the described design per the boundary-case instruction.
- **PrimaryCare**: Coded 1 on a narrow basis: Table 1's blood-pressure footnote states participants with mildly elevated BP at screening 'may be provisionally included and referred to their primary care provider for management of hypertension.' This is a single incidental safety-referral touchpoint, not integration of care delivery within a primary-care setting -- a borderline call, flagged here.
- **Power**: Coded 0. The article describes a planned enrollment target (100-180) with a planned sample-size re-estimation after 100 randomizations (Sec 3.1), but does not describe a formal a priori or post hoc power calculation, nor state the study was underpowered. No explicit 'power' discussion found in the text.
- **Retention (%)**: Not calculable: the article reports only randomized N (96) and number who received >=1 dose (93); no study-completion or attrition counts are given (results deferred to ref [9]). Coded '.'.
- **ConfoundYN**: Coded 1: the statistical analysis plan (Sec 4.2) explicitly includes fixed covariates, treatment-by-assessment interactions, and planned tests of statistical mediation/moderation of the treatment effect (Aims 3-4) using a product-of-coefficients bootstrapping approach. This reflects planned methodology, not applied/reported adjusted results, since no results appear in this text.
- **BaseStatus**: Sample is defined by DSM-IV alcohol dependence diagnosis plus a heavy-drinking-days threshold (Table 1), but no explicit severity grading (e.g., an AUDIT score or stated mild/moderate/severe classification) is given in this text. Coded '.' rather than inferring a severity band from the diagnosis alone.
- **Age_Median**: Inclusion criteria specify an eligible age range of 25-65 (Table 1), but no sample mean/median age is reported in this design paper (would appear in the companion results paper). Coded '.'.
- **PercentFem**: Not reported in this text; no sample demographic breakdown is given (design paper only).
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Not reported in this text; no sample demographic breakdown is given (design paper only).
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Both trial sites are in the US: NYU Grossman School of Medicine (New York, NY) and University of New Mexico (Albuquerque, NM), per author affiliations and Sec 2.1.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 0: recruitment relied substantially on self-referral after participants learned of the study via ClinicalTrials.gov and psychedelic-research-related media/books (Sec 3.2), and the Discussion explicitly notes that the trial's 'rigorous inclusion/exclusion criteria' screened out many prospective participants, 'which may limit generalizability of the results.'
- **Followup in months**: Computed as 42 weeks / 4.345 = 9.7 months, measuring from the end of the core 12-week double-blind treatment phase to the final double-blind assessment window culminating in the week-54 visit. This is ambiguous because the design has two treatment endpoints: the double-blind treatment ends at week 12 (with primary-endpoint assessment at week 36), and an optional open-label extension adds further treatment through week 42 before final assessment at week 54. An alternative reading (total protocol duration, week 0 to week 54 = 54 weeks) would give ~12.4 months. Flagged as a judgment call.
- **RelapseMeasured**: Coded 0: the article tracks percent heavy drinking days, drinking days, and abstinence longitudinally (Table 2, Sec 4.2), but does not use the term 'relapse' or frame any outcome specifically as relapse/return-to-use tracking.
- **SafetyReported**: Coded 'Y': Sec 5.1 ('Safety considerations') and Sec 4.2 explicitly describe a dedicated safety-assessment methodology -- an Adverse Events Case Report Form at nearly every visit (Table 2), vital signs, ECG, and a planned statistical comparison of treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) between arms. This satisfies the 'explicit statement/section exists' test, even though no numeric AE results appear in this text.
- **AnyAEReported**: Coded 'N': although safety assessment is described in detail as a methodology, no specific adverse event (type, count, or occurrence) for this trial is reported anywhere in this text -- results are explicitly deferred to the companion publication [9]. This is a schema-forced value; it reflects that no AE data is reported IN THIS ARTICLE, not a claim that the trial recorded zero adverse events. Flagged per the prompt's cardinal AE-understatement concern.
- **NumDistinctAEs**: Forced to 0 by the rule that NumDistinctAEs=0 only when AnyAEReported='N'. No AE count is actually stated in this text; see the AnyAEReported rationale for the underlying ambiguity.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: Not reported in this text (no AE data given). Coded '.'.
- **AE_denominator**: Not reported in this text; no safety-population AE tabulation appears (only the plan to tabulate TEAEs is described). Coded '.', not 'NA', per the rule that '.' applies even when the article discusses safety but gives no analyzable AE denominator.
- **ArmScope**: Coded 'NA': no AE values of any kind are reported in this text, so arm scope (psychedelic_arm vs pooled) genuinely does not apply -- there is nothing to scope. Not coded merely because the study has a comparator arm; coded because there is zero AE data.
- **WorstSeverity**: Coded 'None' as the mechanical consequence of AnyAEReported='N' per the AE-gate table. Flagged explicitly: this reflects the absence of AE data in this specific article's text, not a clinical finding that no adverse events occurred in the trial -- harms were in fact assessed via a detailed AE-CRF protocol, with results appearing only in the companion publication [9].
- **HarmsAssessed**: Coded 1: the study protocol assesses harms as an explicit Aim (Aim 1, safety), with AE-CRF administered at nearly every visit (Table 2) and a planned TEAE tabulation and between-group comparison (Sec 4.2). This is a design/methods fact, true regardless of whether numeric AE results appear in this specific article. Paired with SafetyReported='Y' per the required equivalence.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 12/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power, results. Objective (Sec 2.1.1), design (Sec 2.1), setting (NYU/UNM), eligibility (Table 1), intervention (Sec 4.1.2-4.1.4), comparator (Sec 5.4), outcomes (primary/secondary listed in Sec 2.1.1), randomization procedure (Sec 4.1.1), blinding (Sec 4.1.2), statistical methods (Sec 4.2), harms-assessment plan (Sec 5.1, Table 2), and funding/COI (dedicated sections) are all explicitly described. Fails 'sample_size_power' (enrollment target and re-estimation plan given, but no formal power calculation stated) and 'results' (this is a protocol/design paper; all outcome results are explicitly deferred to the companion publication, ref [9]). 12 of 14 met, below the 13-of-14 threshold, so MethodologyDescribedYN=0.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: pending
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'No statistic reported (protocol/design paper; no outcome data)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: This article reports no outcome data of its own for the study's primary or secondary aims -- results are explicitly deferred to a companion publication (Bogenschutz et al., JAMA Psychiatry 2022, ref [9] in this article). No statistic exists anywhere in this text to convert to an effect size.

## O'Reilly 1964

*Alcohol · 1964 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Findings section: 'Twenty-six patients, or 38% of the total group were found to be abstaining from alcohol in the two months preceding follow-up' (26/68). This is the article's sole quantitative treatment-outcome result (no comparator group exists), consistent with the Summary. The secondary correlate finding (46% of abstainers vs 6% of non-abstainers showed the depression/transcendental-experience pattern) is a predictor analysis, not the primary treatment outcome, so it was not chosen as Outcome.
- **SD**: No dispersion or test statistic is reported alongside the 38% abstinence proportion itself; the only significance testing reported ('.01 level of significance') attaches to the depression/transcendental-experience correlate analysis, a different (secondary) analysis, so it cannot be paired with Outcome. Coded '.'.
- **PVal**: No p-value is reported for the primary abstinence proportion (26/68 = 38%) itself; the article states only that 'there was no significant change in abstinence... between the two periods' (a different, within-subject comparison), without a printed p-value or threshold. Coded '.'.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: 68 is the denominator the authors themselves used for the headline rate: 26/68 = 38% ('Twenty-six patients, or 38% of the total group'). This is despite the separate note that 'complete information was available on all but seven patients' (61/68) — that 61/68 figure describes data-completeness, not the denominator used for Outcome.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 9/14 met. Unmet: comparator, sample_size_power, randomization, blinding, funding_coi. Objective, design (three-source follow-up methodology), setting (Union Hospital, Moose Jaw), eligibility (no psychosis, voluntary), intervention (200 gamma LSD protocol), outcomes, statistical methods (.01 threshold stated), results, and harms are all described. Comparator, sample-size/power, randomization, and blinding are never discussed even as absent (single-arm design is never explicitly framed as a limitation), and no funding/COI statement appears anywhere. 9 of 14 met, below the 13-of-14 threshold for binary=1.
- **EligibleYN**: Coded Y under the single-arm/open-label boundary case: the Introduction frames the program as 'a study of the effectiveness of this treatment' with a standardized, previously-published protocol, defined enrollment criteria (no psychosis, voluntary acceptance), an assigned regimen (200 gamma LSD, day 6-7), and a structured, pre-specified follow-up methodology (three independent information sources, three defined sampling windows) — satisfying criterion (a) despite the follow-up itself being executed via retrospective/cross-sectional questionnaire rather than fixed-interval prospective visits. Criterion (b) is clearly met (abstinence rate as the reported outcome).
- **Pos Result Y/N**: No comparator exists, so judged on the study's own pre/post framing: the Discussion states the treatment 'produced sobriety in 38% of patients without any disastrous side effects,' which the authors themselves present as a positive result. Coded 1.
- **PrimaryCare**: 26 of 68 referrals came from the Bureau of Alcoholism and 42 from private physicians, but this describes only the referral source, not an interface between the treatment/study itself and primary-care settings or providers — treatment was entirely psychiatric-ward-based. Coded 0.
- **Retention (%)**: Study was not randomized, so denominator is the enrolled N=68. 'Complete information was available on all but seven patients' → 61/68 = 89.7%, used as Retention (%). This reflects follow-up data completeness rather than treatment dropout, since nearly all patients completed the single LSD session before discharge.
- **ValidatedScaleMeasYN**: No validated psychometric scale or SUD instrument (e.g., AUDIT, ASI) is named anywhere; abstinence status was determined via questionnaire and corroborating-source report, not a standardized instrument. Coded 0.
- **ConfoundYN**: Findings section statistically tested age, marital status, educational level, AA/church membership, years of uncontrolled drinking, psychiatric diagnosis, number of prior psychiatric treatments, and treating psychiatrist as potential correlates of abstinence (all non-significant except the depression/transcendental-experience factor). Coded 1.
- **BaseStatus**: 62% of the cohort was diagnosed 'Chronic Alcoholism,' ~60% had been drinking uncontrolledly >10 years, and all were admitted to an inpatient psychiatric ward for treatment, indicating a severe, treatment-resistant baseline population. Coded 'Severe'.
- **Age_Median**: Mean age 37 (range 20-62) falls in the 30-50 bracket.
- **PercentFem**: 8 females of 68 total = 11.8%.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Race/ethnicity of the sample is not reported anywhere in the article. Coded '.'.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Study conducted at Union Hospital, Moose Jaw, Saskatchewan, Canada. Coded 'NonUS'.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Sample was drawn from routine referrals (Bureau of Alcoholism and private physicians) to a hospital psychiatric ward — a real-world clinical alcoholism population — with the only exclusion being active psychosis, not a specially recruited or highly screened research-volunteer sample. Coded 1.
- **Followup in months**: Article reports 'a range of two to 34 months had elapsed since treatment, with a mean of 14 months' at the time of the cross-sectional follow-up survey; 34 months (the maximum) is used as the longest reported follow-up duration. Note this is the maximum elapsed time across a cross-sectional cohort survey, not a fixed protocol endpoint.
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: Presence/absence of 'depression' and 'transcendental experience' was recorded from psychiatric/nursing notes and used as a correlate of abstinence, so a mental-health-related dimension was assessed, though not via a validated scale (see ValidatedScaleMeasYN=0). Coded 1.
- **Implementation Outcome**: Article reports that 'only two patients... indicated that, given free choice, they would not receive LSD again' — an acceptability outcome. Coded 1.
- **ReductionMeasured**: Article explicitly states non-abstainers were classed as such 'whether or not they showed some improvement,' i.e., partial reduction was not separately quantified or reported as a distinct outcome. Coded 0.
- **RelapseMeasured**: Abstinence was compared between the 2-month post-treatment window and the last 2 months of the follow-up period specifically to detect change/relapse over time ('no significant change in abstinence or indulgence between the two periods'), and patients with repeat LSD treatments were analyzed on their most recent episode. Coded 1.
- **SafetyReported / HarmsAssessed**: The article devotes discussion to adverse effects (citing Cohen & Ditman 1963) and reports its own safety findings (1/68 hospitalized for a psychotic reaction, plus physical-distress/post-treatment-disturbance correlates), so harms were assessed. SafetyReported='Y' and HarmsAssessed=1 per the required agreement rule.
- **NParticipantsWithAE / AE_denominator**: AE_denominator=68, the full treated cohort ('out of 68 patients only one patient...'). NParticipantsWithAE is coded '.' because only the psychotic-reaction count (n=1) is explicit; the physical-distress and post-treatment-disturbance categories carry no reported counts, so a reliable aggregate participant count cannot be given.

### Adverse events
- **O'Reilly 1964_AE01**: Findings section states the abstinence-correlated (better-outcome) group showed 'no signs of physical distress' at follow-up, implying some patients did display physical distress after LSD, but no count, description, or duration is given anywhere in the article. Graded Mild per the rubric's default for under-specified AEs; SeverityIndeterminate=Y because no intervention/impairment/duration detail exists to grade further. SeveritySource='rubric' since the authors (pre-1980, pre-CTCAE) never assigned this event a severity label themselves.
- **O'Reilly 1964_AE02**: Same Findings passage references 'post-treatment disturbance' as a negative correlate of abstinence, and the Discussion separately mentions 'confusion or depression' as possible post-treatment reactions; treated as one AE type (post-treatment psychological disturbance) since the article gives no distinct counts or descriptions separating confusion from depression. No count or duration given, so graded Mild by rubric default with SeverityIndeterminate=Y. AE_type='both' because depression/confusion in this context plausibly reflects unresolved challenging-experience integration (Discussion's own framing) as well as a clinical adverse reaction.
- **O'Reilly 1964_AE03**: Findings/Discussion state 1 of 68 patients developed a paranoid psychotic reaction requiring hospitalization; the authors explicitly note no evidence linking it to the LSD experience, but it occurred within the treated cohort and is reported in the article's own safety discussion, so it is retained here per the instruction not to understate harm. Hospitalization meets an FDA seriousness criterion, so it is graded Severe with confidence (SeverityIndeterminate=N). Onset timing relative to treatment is not stated (discovered via the follow-up survey, elapsed time up to 34 months), so Onset='unknown'. SeveritySource='rubric' because the authors reported only the fact of hospitalization, not a severity/SAE label, and this is a pre-1980 article predating CTCAE.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Abstinence rate (%)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: Single-arm, no-comparator follow-up survey; the reported 38% (26/68) abstinence proportion has no counterpart group, so none of the seven supported conversion methods (d, g, eta_p, F, chi2, OR, RR) apply. No comparator exists to compute an odds ratio from two-group proportions.

## P. Glue 2016

*Opioid · 2016 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **EligibleYN**: Y. Criterion (a): prospectively planned, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled ascending single-dose trial with defined enrollment (27 patients established on methadone OST), an assigned regimen (60/120/180 mg noribogaine or placebo), and prospective outcome assessment (Methods) -- clearly passes. Criterion (b), per instruction to judge on whether any opioid-use or withdrawal outcome is reported: this is not a pure PK/safety endpoint study -- Methods explicitly states 'Opioid withdrawal was evaluated' via time to resumption of OST and the validated SOWS/OOWS/COWS withdrawal scales (Results, 'Pharmacodynamics'), so a withdrawal outcome is reported and (b) passes. Both criteria met, so EligibleYN='Y' despite the study's title framing it as a 'Safety Study' and its primary designated endpoint (QTc) being a cardiac-safety measure, not an SUD outcome -- the withdrawal/OST-resumption data are secondary but sufficient for (b) per the boundary rule that a trial's only SUD outcome may be secondary.
- **Outcome**: The article explicitly designates a 'primary end point': Results/Electrocardiographic Effects states 'QTcI was chosen as the primary end point because the average sum of squared slopes was somewhat lower for this correction than for QTcF.' Per the precedence rule (author-designated primary outcome beats the abstract-led SUD outcome or the largest of co-equal outcomes), Outcome is drawn from the QTc exposure-response analysis rather than the opioid-withdrawal secondary outcome. The single quantitative model parameter reported for this primary end point is the concentration-QTcI slope: 'The mean slope of the noribogaine/plasma concentration relationship was 0.17 milliseconds per ng/mL' (Discussion; also given in the Abstract). This is a discrepancy worth flagging: the study's only SUD-relevant secondary outcome (opioid withdrawal ratings/time to OST resumption) showed 'a nonstatistically significant trend toward decreased total score...most notably at the 120-mg dose' with the authors noting the design 'may have confounded' this analysis -- consistent with the codebook's depression-vs-drinking-days example, the primary-outcome rule routes Outcome to the non-SUD QTc finding even though this article is included in an SUD evidence review on the strength of its secondary outcome. Alternative candidate numbers considered and rejected as the 'Outcome' value: the by-dose-group mean DeltaDeltaQTcI effects (16/28/42 ms at 60/120/180 mg, Abstract/Results) and the model-projected effects at geometric mean Cmax (30 ms and 42 ms with 90%CI, Discussion) are all downstream restatements of the same concentration-response relationship rather than the single model parameter itself.
- **SD**: 90%CI, 0.14 to 0.20 ms/ng/mL, printed in the same sentence as the 0.17 ms/ng/mL slope used for Outcome (Discussion, 'The mean slope of the noribogaine/plasma concentration relationship was 0.17 milliseconds per ng/mL (90%CI, 0.14 to 0.20 milliseconds)'). No SD/SE/F/t is reported for this specific regression slope; the CI is the only paired dispersion statistic given.
- **PVal**: '.' -- no exact p-value or thresholded p-value is printed anywhere for the QTc concentration-response slope or the by-dose-group QTc effect. The article only characterizes the finding qualitatively as 'a clear, statistically significant dose- and concentration-dependent effect on the QTc interval' (Results) without ever printing a number or threshold alongside that claim, so per the rules this is coded not-reported rather than inferred as '<0.05'.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: 27, the full analyzed cohort (Results: 'Thirty-four subjects were screened, and 27...were enrolled in and completed the study'; 6 active + 3 placebo per dose level x 3 levels = 27). The exposure-response model for DeltaDeltaQTcI vs concentration uses time-matched concentration as a covariate with placebo-corrected DeltaQTcI as the outcome ('Subjects dosed with placebo were analyzed as a pooled group'), so both actively-dosed and placebo subjects contribute to the fitted model, but the article never states a single explicit analysis-population N for this specific model. The number of subjects contributing nonzero noribogaine concentrations is 18 (6 per active dose level); 27 is used here as the best-supported whole-study denominator, and this ambiguity is flagged rather than resolved by assumption.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 14/14 met. Unmet: none. All 14 elements are explicitly described in this industry-sponsored Phase 1 report. Objective is stated in the paragraph preceding Methods; design ('ascending single-dose, placebo-controlled, randomized, double-blind, parallel-group study'), setting ('Zentech Clinical Trials Unit in Dunedin, New Zealand'), eligibility (methadone dose/duration, age, medical-clearance criteria), intervention (dose levels, timing, dosing procedure), comparator (matching placebo capsules), and outcomes (PK, PD, safety measures) are all in Methods. Sample_size_power is coded Y because, although no a priori power calculation is reported, the Discussion explicitly discusses the small-sample limitation ('likely to have been underpowered to identify uncommon or subtle safety findings'), which the codebook treats as sufficient description. Randomization ('computer-generated random code') and blinding ('double-blind') are explicit. Statistical methods, results, harms (dedicated Safety section and AE table), and funding/COI (separate Funding and Declaration of Conflicting Interests sections) are all fully reported.
- **Pos Result Y/N**: 0. Judged against the study's own designated primary end point (QTc), the result is an adverse safety signal, not a positive treatment effect: noribogaine produced a dose- and concentration-dependent QTc prolongation reaching 'clinically concerning levels in the higher-dose groups' (Discussion) and prompted a DSMB-mandated reduction of the top planned dose from 240 mg to 180 mg. The study's only SUD-relevant outcome (opioid withdrawal/time to OST resumption) was likewise non-significant ('nonstatistically significant trend...however, the study design may have confounded evaluations'). Neither the primary nor the secondary outcome supports a positive-result code; 0 (null/negative) rather than 3, since directionality is not genuinely ambiguous -- QTc worsened and withdrawal was unchanged.
- **Ibogaine**: 1. Per explicit task instruction, noribogaine is ibogaine's active metabolite (Introduction: 'ibogaine as being rapidly converted oxidatively to an active metabolite, noribogaine') and is the sole study drug administered in this trial. No other named psychedelic compound (psilocybin, ayahuasca, LSD, mescaline, MDMA, ketamine, cannabis) appears anywhere in the article, so only the Ibogaine flag is set and OtherPsychedelic/MultiplePsychedelic remain 0.
- **BaseStatus**: '.'. Patients were 'established on methadone OST at 25-80 mg/day for at least 30 days' -- i.e., in stable maintenance treatment -- but no standardized severity classification (ASI composite, DSM severity specifier, or comparable measure) for opioid dependence is reported anywhere in the article, so severity cannot be coded without fabricating a threshold.
- **Age_Median**: '30-50'. Results: 'Mean age was 41.2 years,' which falls within the 30-50 bracket.
- **PercentFem**: 22.2. Results: '27 (21 male, 6 female) were enrolled.' 6/27 x 100 = 22.2%.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: 25.9. Results: 'Twenty subjects were white, 5 were Maori, and 2 were other.' Non-white = 5 + 2 = 7 of 27 = 25.9%. Arithmetic shown per the sum-non-white-groups rule.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: 0. No income, employment, insurance status, or other SES indicator is reported anywhere in the article.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: 'NonUS'. All sites and author affiliations are in New Zealand (Zentech Clinical Trials Unit, Dunedin; University of Otago; Southern District Health Board; University of Auckland) -- see Country field note; actual country is New Zealand.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: 0. Participants were a screened Phase 1 volunteer population: eligibility explicitly excluded anyone with 'acute or serious chronic medical or surgical disorders,' and the protocol required confinement to the study site from 24 hours predose to 72 hours postdose plus outpatient follow-up -- a highly monitored, medically pre-screened inpatient sample atypical of routine community OST care.
- **InterventionTherapy**: 0. No counseling, psychotherapy, or preparation/integration component is described; the intervention is a single pharmacological dose administered under inpatient PK/safety monitoring only.
- **CulturalFraming**: 0. The Introduction's account of ibogaine's Bwiti religious/ritual origins ('Ritual eating of root bark is sacramental in the Bwiti religion...where it has psychedelic effects') is background on the substance, not an explicit framing of this trial's participants, methods, or findings around racial/ethnic identity or historical trauma. The demographic report that 5/27 participants were Maori (Results) is population reporting captured under RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent, not cultural framing of the study itself.
- **IndigenousFramework**: 0, per the codebook's explicit instruction that describing a plant medicine's traditional origin in passing is not integration. The Bwiti/West African traditional-use background (Introduction) is mentioned only as context for ibogaine's discovery and is not referenced, incorporated, or drawn on anywhere in this trial's design, consent process, or interpretation.
- **ConfoundYN**: 1. Discussion substantively discusses a specific confound: because active and placebo subjects within a cohort were housed together and observed continuously, 'restarting of OST would have been obvious to other participants, and this may have confounded any evaluation of dose-response for time to restarting OST' -- supported by the near-identical placebo-vs-active resumption times within cohorts (Pearson r=0.993, Results).
- **Followup in months**: 1.25. Methods: 'there were subsequent outpatient and telephone assessments until 35 days postdose.' Since this is a single-dose study, end-of-treatment and dosing day are the same, so no baseline-vs-end-of-treatment discrepancy applies. 35 days = 5 weeks; converting at 4 weeks/month (matching the codebook's 2 weeks = 0.5 month convention) gives 5 x 0.25 = 1.25 months.
- **BehaviorChangeMeasured**: 1. Time to resumption of OST and the SOWS/OOWS/COWS withdrawal-behavior ratings are behavioral/craving-adjacent outcome measures (Methods, 'Pharmacodynamic assessments...included...Opioid withdrawal was evaluated in several ways').
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: 0. COWS/OOWS/SOWS are physiological/subjective opioid-withdrawal symptom scales, not depression, anxiety, PTSD, or well-being instruments; no such mental-health scale is reported anywhere in the article.
- **RelapseMeasured**: 1. 'Time to resumption of OST' is explicitly measured from the last morphine dose until participants resumed opioid agonist treatment (Methods) -- functionally a return-to-opioid-use/relapse-timing outcome, tracked as one of the study's core pharmacodynamic end points.
- **MortalityMeasured**: 1. Safety section states explicitly 'There were no deaths or serious AEs,' confirming mortality was actively monitored and reported as zero rather than left unassessed, consistent with the rule that a reported death count (including zero, via AE surveillance) counts as measured.
- **SafetyReported**: 'Y'. Dedicated Methods subsection ('Safety evaluations included...') and Results 'Safety' section with an AE table (Table 3) and explicit AE-severity narrative.
- **AnyAEReported**: 'Y'. 'A total of 78 adverse events (AEs) were reported by 22 participants' (Results, Safety).
- **NumDistinctAEs**: 'Not numbered'. The article names only 4 specific AE terms (visual impairment, headache, nausea, and vomiting, the last named only as one of the three AEs rated severe) out of 78 total reported AE occurrences across 22 participants; Table 3 is explicitly headed 'Most Commonly Reported Adverse Events,' implying additional, unnamed AE types make up the remainder of the 78 events. No full AE-type list or count of distinct types is given anywhere, so the total distinct-type count cannot be determined from the text.
- **ArmScope**: 'pooled'. Table 3 reports AE-by-individual counts separately for Placebo (n=9), 60 mg, 120 mg, and 180 mg, and the headline '78 AEs...22 participants' figure sums across all four arms including placebo -- reported AE values are not restricted to noribogaine-exposed participants alone.
- **WorstSeverity**: 'Severe', driven by the QTcI-prolongation event (ae_events AE04): see that event's rationale for why it is graded Severe under the rubric (FDA 'medically important event requiring intervention to prevent' a more serious cardiac outcome) despite the article's blanket statement that 'there were no deaths or serious AEs.' Absent that reinterpretation, the highest article-labeled AE severity would be the investigator 'severe'-intensity ratings for one instance each of headache/nausea/vomiting, which are graded Moderate here per rubric (see ae_events rationale) because they do not independently meet an FDA seriousness criterion.
- **Retention (%)**: 100. Study was randomized; Results states 27 patients 'were enrolled in and completed the study,' with no reported loss between randomization (27) and completion (27). Randomized N = completed N = 27, so retention = 27/27 x 100 = 100%.
- **Power**: 1. No a priori power calculation is reported, but Discussion explicitly states the small sample size is 'likely to have been underpowered to identify uncommon or subtle safety findings,' which the codebook treats as sufficient to count power as discussed.
- **PrimaryCare**: 0. The trial was conducted at a dedicated clinical trials unit with inpatient confinement; participants requesting OST resumption were 'released to the care of the local OST clinical service' post-discharge, which is specialty addiction treatment rather than a primary-care interface.

### Adverse events
- **P. Glue 2016_AE01**: Onset coded 'post-session-acute' rather than 'in-session': there is no discrete psychotherapy-style 'session' in this PK/safety trial, but the effect's reported timing (onset within 1 hour postdose, resolved by 2.5-3 hours -- Safety section) fits the hours-scale definition of post-session-acute more literally than 'in-session'. Severity 'Mild' and SeveritySource 'author' both taken directly from the text: 'Visual impairment was the term used to describe noneuphoric changes in light perception of mild intensity.' NAffected=13 summed from Table 3 across all four arms (2 placebo + 2 at 60 mg + 5 at 120 mg + 4 at 180 mg).
- **P. Glue 2016_AE02**: Severity judgment call: the Safety section states one headache instance was among 'Three AEs...rated as severe (nausea, vomiting, and headache); all others were rated as mild-moderate in intensity,' but the same paragraph also states 'There were no deaths or serious AEs.' Because the article's own mild/moderate/severe wording is an informal intensity scale rather than a CTCAE grade or an SAE flag, and the authors explicitly disclaim any serious AE, this instance is graded Moderate (not Severe) under the rubric's Grade-3-equivalent default ('Moderate...unless it meets an FDA seriousness criterion'), rather than accepting the literal word 'severe' at face value -- SeveritySource is therefore 'rubric', not 'author'. No specific onset timing is given for headache individually (only visual impairment has an explicit timing description), so Onset is coded 'unknown'. NAffected=13 summed from Table 3 (5 placebo + 4 at 60 mg + 2 at 120 mg + 2 at 180 mg).
- **P. Glue 2016_AE03**: Same severity-mapping judgment as AE02 applies (one nausea instance was investigator-labeled 'severe' but the article also states no serious AEs occurred; graded Moderate, SeveritySource='rubric'). NAffected=5 summed from Table 3 (1 placebo + 0 at 60 mg + 2 at 120 mg + 2 at 180 mg). Onset coded 'unknown' -- no per-AE timing given for nausea.
- **P. Glue 2016_AE04**: This event is not tabulated in Table 3 ('Most Commonly Reported Adverse Events') alongside headache/visual impairment/nausea/vomiting, and the article's blanket Safety-section statement is 'There were no deaths or serious AEs' -- which would argue for a non-severe grade. However, Results (Electrocardiographic Effects) reports that QT prolongation observed after the first two dose cohorts led the independent DSMB to recommend reducing the planned top dose from 240 mg to 180 mg, and that in the 180-mg group '1 subject had a QTcI value exceeding 480 milliseconds at 1 time, and 1 subject had QTcI > 500 milliseconds at 4 points,' with one subject each in the 120- and 180-mg groups also crossing the pre-specified ΔQTcI > 60 ms safety threshold (Methods). This is graded Severe here (SeveritySource='rubric', overriding the article's blanket 'no serious AEs' summary) because a DSMB-mandated dose de-escalation specifically to avert a more serious cardiac outcome meets the FDA seriousness catch-all ('a medically important event requiring intervention to prevent one of the above') and QTc > 500 ms is a recognized torsades-risk threshold; MeetsFDASerious and InterventionRequired are both coded 'Y' on this basis. This is flagged as the most consequential and least obvious severity call in this record and should go to human review: it directly contradicts the article's own summary sentence, and the cardinal principle here is that understating this signal would be the worse error given it is explicitly the article's 'most notable safety finding' (Discussion) and drove a protocol-level dose change. NAffected is coded '.' because the specific subject counts given (>480 ms once; >500 ms x4 timepoints; ΔQTcI>60 ms once each in 120-/180-mg groups) may refer to overlapping or distinct individuals and cannot be safely summed into one unique-participant count from the text.
- **P. Glue 2016_AE05**: Vomiting is named only once, as one of the three AEs 'rated as severe (nausea, vomiting, and headache)' (Safety section); no dose-group breakdown or affected-participant count is given for vomiting specifically (it does not appear as a row in Table 3, which lists only headache/visual impairment/nausea). NAffected is coded '.' accordingly. Same severity-mapping logic as AE02/AE03 applies: graded Moderate rather than Severe given the article's explicit 'no...serious AEs' statement and the informal (non-CTCAE) nature of the authors' intensity scale; SeveritySource='rubric'. Onset coded 'unknown' -- no timing is given for vomiting.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Exposure-response linear mixed-effects regression slope (change from baseline, placebo-corrected QTcI vs. plasma noribogaine concentration)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: The article's designated primary end point is a concentration-QTc regression slope from a linear mixed-effects exposure-response model, not one of the seven supported conversion methods (d, g, eta_p, F, chi2, OR, RR); no defensible conversion to Hedges' g exists. Direction is set to -1 because the slope's natural sign (QTc lengthening with increasing noribogaine concentration) is an adverse cardiac-safety signal, opposite to a favored SUD-treatment-benefit direction, even though the statistic itself is not being converted.

## P.L. Prior 2014

*Opioid · 2014 · eligible=N · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Primary outcome is explicitly designated by the authors: 'the reduction in percentage of positive urine samples was considered the primary outcome' (Methods). The only reported statistic for this outcome describes a lower positive-urine (relapse) rate in the ibogaine group, 'corresponding to 10% of the sample,' vs a higher, unquantified rate in placebo (Results/Discussion, urine-sample paragraph). No paired absolute percentages for both arms are given, so Outcome is coded as a labelled descriptor rather than a number.
- **SD**: Test statistic paired with the urine-positivity primary outcome, taken from the same sentence: 'F=2,450; df=3,12; p=0,023; ANOVA repeated measures.' Numerator df=3 is unusual for what otherwise reads as a 2-group comparison; the article never explains the 3 numerator df. This is flagged as an internal-consistency oddity rather than resolved or reinterpreted, per the no-decoding-theory rule.
- **PVal**: p=0,023, reported directly alongside the F statistic for the urine-positivity primary outcome (same sentence as SD).
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: N=20 (10 ibogaine, 10 placebo) enrolled. The Results open with 'There were no defects from either group during the 6 month evaluation period,' read here as no dropouts/attrition given its context alongside baseline-comparability reporting; no participant losses are described anywhere else, and the urine analysis is stated as using 'the total samples collected from the 24 week period.' 'Defects' is an apparent OCR/translation artifact (the authors are Brazilian); 'dropouts' is the most contextually consistent reading, but this is not fully unambiguous and is flagged per the non-fabrication rule.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 12/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power, randomization. No a priori/post hoc power calculation or sample-size justification is given (N=20 not justified). The method of group allocation is never described as random anywhere in the article (only 'sorted' into groups; the word 'random/randomized' never appears in the study's own methods text, despite the double-blind, placebo-controlled framing). All other elements -- objective, design, setting, eligibility, intervention, comparator, outcomes, blinding, statistics, results, harms, funding -- are described. 12/14 met, below the 13-of-14 threshold, so MethodologyDescribedYN=0.
- **Condition**: This article is filed under Condition='Opioid' in the request manifest and SourcePDF path ('opioids/...'), but the trial is entirely about cocaine dependence (DSM-IV cocaine dependence diagnosis, cocaine urine screening, Minnesota Cocaine Craving Scale); ibogaine for opioid use is never mentioned in the text. Condition is echoed as instructed; this mismatch is a corpus-labeling finding, not something corrected here. Eligibility criterion (b) is judged met because cocaine craving/use is itself a substance-use outcome, independent of the Opioid filing label.
- **RandomizationYN**: Coded '.' -- the study is called 'double blind, placebo controlled' throughout, including the title, but the word 'random'/'randomized' never appears anywhere in the article body describing this study's own methods; participants are only described as 'sorted' into 2 groups. Because the study has two arms (the concept clearly applies) but the allocation method is never stated, this is not-reported rather than not-applicable, and is not treated as the single-arm/open-label '0' boundary case.
- **Retention (%)**: Coded 100, based on no reported attrition anywhere in the article (see SampleSize_NFinal rationale), with denominator N=20 (enrolled total, used because randomization is not confirmed). Flagged because it rests on interpreting the garbled 'no defects' phrase as 'no dropouts.'
- **BaseStatus**: Coded '.' -- inclusion criteria require DSM-IV cocaine dependence with self-reported use at least weekly for one month, but no severity instrument or score (e.g., ASI) is reported to place baseline severity on the Mild/Moderate/Severe scale.
- **Age_Median**: Coded '.' -- inclusion criterion specifies ages 18-64 and the text states there were no significant between-group age differences, but no actual mean/median age value for the sample is reported anywhere.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Coded '.' -- inclusion criteria allow 'any race or ethnic origin' and the text states no significant between-group differences in ethnicity, but no percentage or breakdown is ever reported.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: Coded 0 -- patients were recruited from NAPS, a municipal (public/SUS-system) primary-care mental-health institution in Brazil, suggestive of a lower-income community population, but the article gives no explicit income data, low-income eligibility criterion, or public-insurance enrollment statement, so this is coded as not explicitly described.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Coded 'NonUS' -- study conducted in Santos/Sao Paulo, Brazil (Universidade Federal de Sao Paulo; NAPS Santos).
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 0 -- despite community-clinic recruitment (NAPS), exclusion criteria are extensive (other substance dependence, major cardiac/renal/hepatic/endocrine disease, any psychotic or bipolar disorder history, active suicidality, current psychotropic medication), narrowing the sample well below a representative real-world cocaine-dependent population, many of whom have exactly these comorbidities.
- **PrimaryCare**: Coded 1 -- participants were recruited from NAPS, explicitly described as 'a municipal institution... specialized in primary care in mental health,' with outpatient follow-up delivered through that same referral pathway.
- **Power**: Coded 0 -- no a priori or post hoc power calculation or explicit statement that the study was underpowered appears anywhere; the conclusion's call for 'studies with larger samples... in order to establish its efficacy and validity' is generic future-research language, not treated as a power discussion.
- **ConfoundYN**: Coded 1 -- authors explicitly report checking baseline comparability between arms ('no statistically significant differences from both groups regarding age, demographic, ethnicity or physical comorbidities at first evaluation'), treated as a substantive assessment of potential confounders.
- **InterventionTherapy**: Coded 0 -- post-dose care is described only as biweekly outpatient psychiatric consultations for craving/BIS-11 assessment and urine collection; no structured psychotherapy, preparation, or integration sessions are described.
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: Coded 0 -- the BIS-11 measures trait impulsiveness, not a depression/anxiety/PTSD/well-being construct; judged not to meet this field's intent, though it is a borderline call.
- **MortalityMeasured**: Coded 0 -- mortality was not a designated outcome. The ambiguous 'no defects from either group' sentence (see SampleSize_NFinal) could be read as 'no deaths,' but mortality is not tracked as a defined outcome measure anywhere in the Methods.
- **Followup in months**: Coded 6 -- the study's own framework runs from 'time zero' (baseline, at dosing) to '24 weeks,' with the 72-hour timepoint as an intermediate acute-phase assessment rather than a separate 'end of treatment' horizon distinct from baseline, since treatment was a single dose. 24 weeks ~= 6 months.
- **IndigenousFramework**: Coded 0 -- ibogaine's origin as a plant used in West Central African initiatory rituals is mentioned once in the Introduction as background, not integrated into the study's design, consent process, or framework, per the prompt's 'origin mentioned in passing is not integration' guidance.
- **CulturalFraming**: Coded 0 -- no reference to participants' racial/ethnic identity, historical/intergenerational trauma, or culturally specific framing of the intervention; the single mention of ibogaine's African ritual origin is background pharmacology, not cultural framing of this study's population or design.
- **ArmScope**: Coded 'psychedelic_arm' with AE_denominator=10 -- the only reported AE (visual hallucinations) is stated as occurring in 'patients from the ibogaine group' specifically; the placebo group is not reported to have experienced hallucinations, so the value is not pooled across arms.
- **HarmsAssessed**: Coded 1, consistent with SafetyReported='Y' -- baseline and serial (every 6h during the 72h admission) ECGs, clinical evaluation of somatic symptoms, and explicit reporting of hallucinations and absence of cardiovascular events are all described.

### Adverse events
- **P.L. Prior 2014_AE01**: Visual hallucinations are the only AE explicitly reported as having occurred in this study ('Patients from the ibogaine group experienced several visual hallucinations during the 72 hour period after exposure, mainly in the first 24 hours'). Classified as challenging_experience rather than a standard clinical AE because it reads as expected ibogaine-induced perceptual phenomenology with varied content, no reported distress, and no documented intervention. Onset coded post-session-acute (24-72h inpatient window) rather than in-session because there is no discrete guided-dosing 'session' described, only an inpatient observation period following capsule administration. NAffected is coded '.' because the text says only 'several' patients without a number. Severity graded Mild via rubric (no author CTCAE/SAE grading; expected effect, no intervention documented, no reported persistence past the 72h inpatient period), SeverityIndeterminate='N' given the reasonably specific description of timing and content. A separate sentence -- 'No other medications (expect for symptomatic for general symptoms such as nausea) were provided' -- was considered as a possible second AE (nausea) but was NOT added as an ae_events row: it describes the protocol's allowed rescue medications, not a confirmed report that any patient actually experienced nausea, and adding an AE row from it would risk fabricating an event the article never clearly asserts occurred. Likewise, ibogaine's known side effects (nausea, ataxia, xerostomia, QT prolongation) listed earlier in Methods as background literature justifying ECG monitoring are not this study's own observed events and are excluded from ae_events on the same basis.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'F (repeated-measures ANOVA, urine-positivity primary outcome)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: The primary outcome (reduction in percent positive urine samples) is reported only as F=2.45, df=3,12, p=0.023 from a repeated-measures ANOVA. The pipeline's supported F-conversion method requires 1 numerator df; this F has 3 numerator df (likely a multi-timepoint within-subjects term) and cannot be cleanly converted to Hedges' g. No group-wise proportions or means for urine positivity are reported for both arms (only 'corresponding to 10% of the sample' for ibogaine, placebo unquantified), so no OR/RR can be computed either. The cleanly-reported MCCS craving effect (means+SDs at each timepoint, Table 2) is a secondary/co-outcome, not the authors' designated primary outcome, so it is not substituted in per the primary-outcome-only rule.

## Pagni 2024

*Alcohol · 2024 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Citation/Title**: This is a secondary/ancillary fMRI analysis of participants drawn from the parent phase II RCT: Bogenschutz, M. P. et al. (2022), 'Percentage of Heavy Drinking Days Following Psilocybin-Assisted Psychotherapy vs Placebo in the Treatment of Adult Patients With Alcohol Use Disorder: A Randomized Clinical Trial,' JAMA Psychiatry (NCT02061293, ref. 16 in this article). Fourteen of the parent trial's participants consented to this ancillary fMRI sub-study; 11 provided complete pre/post scan data and are analyzed here. Eligible per the secondary-analysis boundary case.
- **EligibleYN**: (a) Clinical trial: satisfied via the secondary-analysis boundary case — participants were randomized within the parent RCT protocol (psilocybin vs diphenhydramine) with prospectively planned fMRI outcome assessment; this is not a retrospective or naturalistic study. (b) SUD-related outcome: satisfied by the Craving data section, which reports pre-to-post psilocybin-group reduction in cue-elicited craving ratings (t(3)=5.568, p=0.0114) — craving is an explicitly listed qualifying SUD outcome. Both criteria met, so EligibleYN=Y even though the article's own stated primary outcome is neural reactivity, not drinking or craving.
- **Outcome**: Abstract and Results state the study's aim/primary outcome is psilocybin-induced neural reactivity to alcohol (and emotional) cues, not a drinking or craving endpoint — this is a genuine primary-outcome/SUD-outcome discrepancy (craving is secondary here; see EligibleYN and Pos Result Y/N notes). The alcohol-cue task is listed and reported first among the three cue types and matches the article's title, so it is treated as the primary comparison (Results 'Alcohol cue neural reactivity', Table 2). The analysis is a whole-brain voxelwise treatment-by-time interaction yielding many discrete significant clusters (8 activation, 17 deactivation; Table 2) rather than one scalar primary effect size, so a single number would misrepresent the design. Coded as a short labelled descriptor rather than picking one cluster, which would arbitrarily privilege that cluster over dozens of co-equal exploratory findings and bias interpretation.
- **SD**: No single paired dispersion/test statistic exists for a whole-brain cluster map: Table 2 reports per-cluster voxel extents and cluster-/peak-level p-values individually (e.g., L MFG activation cluster: 188 voxels, cluster p-unc=0.002, peak p-unc<0.001). Coded '.' because pairing one cluster's statistic with the Outcome descriptor (which spans all 8+17 clusters) would misrepresent a single-cluster value as representative of the whole finding.
- **PVal**: The uniform whole-brain statistical threshold applied to define significant clusters for the alcohol-cue treatment-by-time interaction was p-uncorrected < 0.005 (k=10 voxel extent), stated in Methods ('fMRI modeling and analyses') and used consistently in Table 2. Reported as the threshold string exactly as used, since no single omnibus p-value applies to the composite multi-cluster Outcome.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: Eleven participants (psilocybin n=5, placebo n=6) comprised the final analyzed fMRI sample after excluding 3 of the original 14 ancillary-study enrollees (2 did not complete both visits; 1 had fMRI malfunction at the pre-intervention visit) — Results, 'Demographics'. This N=11 is the denominator for all cue-reactivity results in Tables 2-4, including the alcohol-cue Outcome.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 13/14 met. Unmet: harms. Boundary call, flagged for review: n_met=13/14, the exact threshold for MethodologyDescribedYN=1. 'harms' is the sole unmet criterion — this article never mentions adverse events, harms, or safety in any form (not even 'not assessed here'), so it cannot be credited as 'described.' 'sample_size_power' was credited Y because the Limitations section explicitly states the small sample size 'restricts generalizations and limits statistical power,' mirroring the Power field's own convention that a bare underpowered statement counts as discussed; no a priori power calculation is reported. If a reviewer instead requires an a priori justification for sample_size_power, n_met=12 and the binary flips to 0 — the single closest call in this record.
