'setting' credited Y on the basis of named research institutions/scanner facility (NYU Langone; CBI Siemens Skyra) rather than an explicit clinical-setting descriptor (e.g., 'outpatient').
- **Pos Result Y/N**: Directionally mixed but net-positive per the authors' own interpretation: increased PFC/caudate engagement and decreased insula/motor/cerebellar activity are framed throughout Discussion/Conclusion as consistent with 'potential therapeutic neural changes' and 'encouraging' findings, and the one clean behavioral statistic reported (psilocybin-group craving reduction, t(3)=5.568, p=0.0114) was significant and positive. Not coded 3 despite some caveated directionality (e.g., mPFC increases framed as possibly inconsistent with simple 'normalization') because the authors do not describe the overall result as ambiguous or mixed — they consistently characterize it as therapeutically promising.
- **Retention (%)**: 14 participants were randomized into the ancillary fMRI sub-study (psilocybin n=6, placebo n=8; Methods, 'Parent trial and fMRI sub-study'); 11 completed both scans and are analyzed (psilocybin n=5, placebo n=6). Retention = 11/14 x 100 = 78.57%, within 2 points of the 80% cutoff — flagged per instructions since this is close enough to flip a downstream quality cell.
- **Comparator**: Comparator is diphenhydramine 50 mg, described as an 'active placebo' matched to maintain blinding (mimics some subjective/somatic effects without psychoactivity), administered under identical session structure to psilocybin. Coded 1 as an active comparator, not an inert placebo.
- **BaseStatus**: No AUD severity classification (e.g., number of DSM criteria met, mild/moderate/severe specifier) is reported anywhere in this article; only baseline drinking quantity/frequency and craving descriptives are given (Table 1). Coded '.' — the concept applies but was not reported in this article.
- **Age_Median**: Weighted sample mean age = (48.80x5 + 44.00x6)/11 = 46.18 years (Table 1, psilocybin and placebo group means), falling in the 30-50 bracket.
- **PercentFem**: Sample size (male/female): psilocybin 5 (4/1), placebo 6 (3/3) — Table 1. Female = 1+3 = 4 of 11 = 36.36%.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Table 1 race/ethnicity: psilocybin White(4), Hispanic(1); placebo White(4), Hispanic(1), Black(1); total N=11 (8+2+1=11, consistent with sample size). As tabulated, 'White' and 'Hispanic' are presented as separate, non-overlapping categories, so non-white/BIPOC = Hispanic(2) + Black(1) = 3/11 = 27.27%. Flagging the standard ambiguity that Hispanic/Latino ethnicity can cross racial categories elsewhere in the literature; here the source table treats it as mutually exclusive from White, and that convention was followed as-reported.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: Annual income is reported by group (psilocybin $97,500; placebo $148,333; Table 1), but the Limitations section explicitly characterizes the sample as 'of middle-to-high socioeconomic status' with no low-income eligibility pathway or public-insurance criterion described. Coded 0 because the field asks whether low-SES participants were explicitly included/described, not merely whether income was reported.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: All sites are in the United States (NYU Langone/NYU Grossman School of Medicine, New York, NY; Columbia University imaging center; Penn State co-author affiliation) — Methods and author affiliations.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Limitations explicitly states the sample was 'nondiverse, homogeneous... primarily Caucasian, of young adult age, and of middle-to-high socioeconomic status,' and participants were drawn from a screened phase II RCT (SCID-confirmed AUD, extensive exclusion criteria) rather than a real-world/community-representative sample.
- **ConfoundYN**: Baseline group differences were formally tested (t-tests/chi-squared across sex, age, weight, craving, PHDD, DPD, PDD, framewise displacement; Table 1), one of which (PDD) differed significantly and is discussed as a caveat. Six realignment (motion) parameters were entered as covariates in first-level fMRI models. Age, sex, and smoking status are explicitly flagged in Limitations as unaddressed potential confounds. Coded 1 for substantive assessment/discussion, even though full statistical adjustment for demographic confounds was not performed (small-sample degrees-of-freedom limitation, noted in Limitations).
- **Followup in months**: This article's own post-treatment assessment window is the second fMRI scan, obtained a mean of 1.45 days (SD=0.68, range 1-3) after the first drug administration (Results, 'Demographics') — there is no longer-horizon follow-up reported in this article itself. Converted 1.45 days / 30 = 0.048, rounded to 0.05 months. Note this is far shorter than the parent trial's own primary-outcome follow-up (8 months post-treatment, ref. 16), which is not part of this article's reported analyses.
- **SafetyReported**: This article contains no Adverse Events, Safety, or Side-effects section, table, or sentence of any kind — it is a neuroimaging mechanism paper; harms/safety reporting for this cohort appears to live in the parent trial publication (ref. 16), not here. Coded SafetyReported=N per the 'article silent on AEs/safety entirely' convention, cascading AnyAEReported/NumDistinctAEs/NParticipantsWithAE=NA, AE_denominator='.', ArmScope=NA, WorstSeverity=NA, and ae_events=[].
- **HarmsAssessed**: Coded 0 to agree with SafetyReported=N per the required HarmsAssessed/SafetyReported concordance rule; this article does not state whether harms were or were not assessed for this fMRI sub-sample.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Whole-brain fMRI cluster-level statistics (multiple discrete clusters comprising the primary outcome; no single scalar effect size)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: The primary outcome is a whole-brain voxelwise neuroimaging analysis reporting many discrete significant clusters (8 activation + 17 deactivation for the alcohol-cue interaction alone; more for the emotional-cue tasks) rather than one pooled between-group scalar statistic. No defensible single-number Hedges' g (or other supported-method) conversion exists without arbitrarily privileging one cluster over dozens of co-equal exploratory findings, so this is excluded from Tier 1 and routed to Tier 2 qualitative classification instead.

## Pagni 2025

*Alcohol · 2025 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Citation/Title**: This is a secondary analysis of personality-trait data collected within the double-blind, placebo-controlled RCT of psilocybin-assisted therapy for AUD whose primary drinking outcomes were reported in the parent trial: Bogenschutz MP, Ross S, Bhatt S, et al. 'Percentage of heavy drinking days following psilocybin-assisted psychotherapy vs placebo in the treatment of adult patients with alcohol use disorder: a randomized clinical trial.' JAMA Psychiatry 2022;79:953-962 (ClinicalTrials.gov NCT02061293), cited as ref. 18 throughout this article.
- **EligibleYN**: Criterion (a): eligible as a secondary analysis of trial data per the boundary case rule -- the parent RCT (Bogenschutz et al. 2022) randomized 95 adults with AUD to psilocybin vs. active placebo with a prospectively planned intervention; this paper reuses that trial's design and prospectively collected personality/drinking measures. Criterion (b): satisfied because substance-use outcomes (drinks-per-day change/posttreatment DpD via TLFB, WHO drinking-risk categories) are reported throughout Results, even though they are secondary to this article's own stated primary personality outcome.
- **Outcome**: This article's stated primary outcome is personality-trait change (five preplanned between-group ANCOVAs on NEO-PI-R factors: neuroticism, extraversion, openness, agreeableness, conscientiousness), NOT a drinking outcome -- flagged per instructions as a discrepancy from the AUD-review's usual SUD-outcome focus. Among the four traits explicitly hypothesized a priori (neuroticism, extraversion, openness, conscientiousness), reported between-group partial eta-squared values are numerically tied at 0.07 for extraversion/openness/conscientiousness vs. 0.06 for neuroticism (Table 1, 'Effect of Treatment' columns), so no single trait is unambiguously 'largest.' I selected neuroticism as the representative co-equal primary because it is listed first in both the Abstract's Results sentence and the Introduction's hypothesis ordering, and because it is the trait mechanistically tied to this article's central AUD-specific hypothesis (the neuroticism facet impulsiveness is separately correlated with drinking outcomes) -- unlike openness, whose increase paradoxically moved further from normative levels and is not cleanly 'favorable.' This is a judgment call; extraversion or openness (both eta_p=0.07) would be equally defensible alternate picks and are recorded here for transparency. Value taken from Table 1, first data row (Neuroticism), 'Effect of Treatment' block.
- **SD**: Table 1 prints the between-group ANCOVA treatment-effect statistic for neuroticism as 'F (df=1,18) = 5.20.' This df is internally inconsistent with the ANCOVA covariate-term df reported elsewhere in the Results text for the same model family ('F=2.29, df=1, 81, p=0.13' for the openness covariate), which implies an error df near 81 (consistent with N=84). The printed '18' may be a transposition/OCR artifact of '81,' but per protocol I report the value exactly as printed rather than constructing a decoding theory. Downstream users should verify the correct df before relying on this statistic.
- **PVal**: Uncorrected p=0.03 for the neuroticism between-group treatment effect, paired with F(1,18)=5.20 in the same Table 1 row. The FDR-corrected value is pFDR=0.05 (also printed in the same row); both survive the study's own significance threshold, but the uncorrected value is reported per the field rule to pair exactly with the SD statistic.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: N=84 (psilocybin N=44, placebo N=40) is the full analyzed sample for all reported ANCOVA/t-test results, per Methods: 'Of the original 95 participants who were randomized to treatment, two were not treated with study medication and nine did not complete pre- and posttreatment NEO questionnaires. Thus, 84 participants... were included in the analyses.'
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 12/14 met. Unmet: eligibility, harms. Setting (NYU/UNM sites), intervention, comparator, outcomes, randomization, blinding, statistics, results, and funding/COI are all clearly described in this article. Eligibility criteria are only gestured at (comorbid psychiatric conditions and other SUDs were excluded, per Limitations) with full inclusion/exclusion detail deferred to companion papers (refs 18, 27, 28), so coded N. Harms/AEs are not assessed or described anywhere in this article's own text (it is a personality-focused secondary analysis with no safety section), so coded N. Sample_size_power coded Y on the bare 'it was a pilot study, and larger clinical trials are needed to confirm clinical significance' statement in Limitations, per the rule that a bare underpowered statement counts as discussed -- a close call, noted here.
- **Power**: Coded 1 based solely on the Limitations statement 'it was a pilot study, and larger clinical trials are needed to confirm clinical significance,' treated as a bare acknowledgment of limited power per the coding rule. No explicit a priori or post hoc power calculation is given anywhere in this article's text; this is a close call.
- **Retention (%)**: Denominator is the randomized N (95, all randomized at once with no separate enrolled-but-not-randomized figure). Completers = 84 (final analytic sample). 84/95 x 100 = 88.42%, rounded to 88.4.
- **InclusionExclusionCriteria**: Coded 0: this article states only that 'the present study excluded many patients with comorbid psychiatric conditions and other substance use disorders' (Discussion) without restating a full inclusion/exclusion list; the complete criteria are deferred to companion design papers (refs 18, 27, 28), so they are not clearly stated within this article itself.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: Coded 0: income was reported (mean annual income psilocybin $266.7K, placebo $147.7K; Limitations note mean incomes >$140,000), but it describes an affluent, homogeneous sample rather than explicit inclusion or description of low-SES participants (no low-income eligibility or public-insurance enrollment mentioned). Judgment call given the field's illustrative examples skew toward low-SES inclusion.
- **BaseStatus**: Coded 'Severe' based on Results: mean 31.56 years (SD=11.47) alcohol dependent, mean 5.25 of 7 DSM-IV dependence criteria met, mean SIP total score 20.42 (SD=9.03), and mean 52.57% (SD=30.32) heavy drinking days in the past month -- a long-duration, high-criteria-count AUD sample.
- **Age_Median**: Not reported as a numeric value anywhere in the main article text; baseline age is referenced only as a variable examined for group differences ('Baseline treatment group differences were examined with respect to... age...'), with actual figures deferred to online supplemental Table S1, which was not provided in the converted text.
- **PercentFem**: Not reported as a percentage in the main article text; sex/gender is referenced only as a baseline-comparison variable, with figures deferred to online supplemental Table S1, not present in the converted text.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Article states participants were 'predominantly white (~77%)' (the semicolon before 77% in the converted text appears to be a rendering artifact of a tilde/approx symbol, not a numeric error). Non-white percent computed as 100 - 77 = 23.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 0: Limitations explicitly describe the sample as 'relatively small, homogeneous... (i.e., mean annual incomes >$140,000, and 77% white)' and speculate this may reflect 'recruiting from highly urban geographical areas' -- indicating a highly selected, non-representative volunteer sample rather than a real-world clinical/community population.
- **InterventionDescription**: The article prints the psilocybin dose as '25 mg and 30-40 mg/kg,' which is almost certainly a garbled/erroneous figure (30-40 mg/kg total-body-weight psilocybin dosing would be far outside any plausible clinical range; standard PAT dosing is typically expressed as mg/70kg or a fixed mg/kg on the order of 0.3-0.4 mg/kg). Reported exactly as printed per protocol rather than silently corrected; flagged here as an internally implausible number.
- **HarmsAssessed**: Coded 0 to agree with SafetyReported='N': this article's own text contains no Adverse Events/Safety section, table, or explicit statement about AE assessment. Harms were certainly assessed in the parent trial (Bogenschutz et al. 2022, ref 18), but that assessment is not reported or described within this secondary personality-analysis article.
- **Double+Blind**: Coded 1 for the nominal double-blind, active-placebo design, but flagging a functional-blinding limitation the authors themselves report: 'approximately 94% of participants in the parent trial accurately guessed their treatment assignment,' raising concern about functional unblinding despite the double-blind protocol.
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: Coded 0: no validated clinical mental-health instrument (e.g., depression/anxiety scale) was administered in this article; the NEO-PI-R 'depression' item is a personality facet, not a clinical mental-health outcome measure. Judgment call given the facet's name overlaps with a mental-health construct.
- **CessationMeasured**: Coded 0: the drinking outcome used (drinks-per-day, TLFB) and WHO risk categories track quantity/frequency and risk level, not a distinct binary cessation/abstinence outcome.
- **RelapseMeasured**: Coded 0: drinking is tracked longitudinally (weeks 0-36) but is not framed by the authors as a 'relapse' or 'return to use' outcome distinct from ongoing consumption level.
- **Followup in months**: The trial comprises 12 weeks of treatment (medication sessions + psychotherapy, weeks 0-12) followed by an explicitly stated 'additional 24 weeks' of follow-up (Abstract Methods), reaching the week-36 personality assessment. Measuring from end of treatment (week 12) per the field rule: 24 weeks / ~4.33 weeks per month = 5.5 months. Note the article itself repeatedly frames week 36 differently, as '7 months after the second medication session' / '7-8 months posttreatment' (measured from the second dosing session or from baseline, respectively, both ~8 months) -- these framings differ materially from the end-of-treatment figure used here.
- **Pos Result Y/N**: Coded 1: the primary personality-trait analysis (neuroticism, extraversion, openness) showed significant treatment effects favoring psilocybin, all surviving FDR correction. Secondary correlational analyses also linked reduced impulsiveness to lower posttreatment drinking, reinforcing a positive overall result, though this article's own primary outcome is personality change rather than drinking.
- **SafetyReported**: Coded 'N': this article contains no Adverse Events, Safety, or Side-effects section, table, or explicit statement assessing harms. It is a secondary personality-outcomes analysis; the single general remark that 'close clinical supervision... is necessary for ensuring safety' (Discussion) is a general observation, not a report of an actual safety assessment conducted in this study.
- **AnyAEReported**: Coded 'NA' because SafetyReported='N' -- the article is silent on AEs/safety entirely, per the three-state coding table.
- **NumDistinctAEs**: Coded 'NA' because SafetyReported='N'; no AE data of any kind appear in this article's text.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: Coded 'NA' because SafetyReported='N'; no AE participant counts appear in this article's text.
- **AE_denominator**: Coded '.' (never 'NA' per field rule) because no safety assessment was reported in this article, so no AE-assessed population denominator exists to report.
- **ArmScope**: Coded 'NA': with no AE data reported at all in this article, arm scope for AE reporting genuinely does not apply.
- **WorstSeverity**: Coded 'NA' because SafetyReported='N', per the three-state coding table.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Partial eta squared' / 'eta_p' / 'approx'
- **note**: Outcome is the between-group partial eta-squared (eta_p^2=0.06) for the ANCOVA treatment effect on neuroticism (Table 1), selected as the representative co-equal primary among four a priori-hypothesized personality traits (see rationale.article.Outcome for the tie and the judgment call). Proposing method='eta_p' per instruction. Confidence is 'approx' rather than 'clean' because: (a) this Tier-1 effect size is a personality outcome, not the substance-use outcome this AUD review otherwise targets, so its inclusion on a SUD-focused forest plot is itself an interpretive assumption; and (b) the paired F-statistic's printed df (1,18) is inconsistent with the df=1,81 implied elsewhere in the same ANCOVA model family, so conversion precision cannot be fully verified from the article as printed.

## Pahnke 1970

*Alcohol · 1970 · eligible=Y · config=pending*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Article contains no structured abstract or explicit primary-outcome statement; per precedence rule 2 (SUD-behavioral outcome), 'Drinking Behavior' is used over 'Global Adjustment'. Within Drinking Behavior, Table 2 gives the authors' own dichotomized 'rigorous criterion' (0-10 scale score of 8-10, i.e., near-total abstinence) as a two-group high-vs-low dose comparison for the N=104 single-LSD-session cohort: High dose 53% (34/64) vs Low dose 33% (13/40). Chosen over the continuous Table 1 ANCOVA means (F=4.43, p<0.025) because the task instructions specifically direct that a high-vs-low-dose two-group proportion be captured as 'X% vs Y%' with OR computed from the counts; see config_proposal.
- **SD**: Table 2 has a companion 'Results For Above Data' F/p row for this exact percentage comparison, but the OCR/column conversion interleaves it with unrelated case-report prose ('...low dose 005 344 O05...Psychedelic reactivity groups 7.74 0.025 3.07 significant...'), and the row/column alignment cannot be reconstructed without inferring a substitution rule, which is prohibited. Coded '.' rather than guessed. Table 1's clean, unambiguous ANCOVA F=4.43 (p<0.025) is for the continuous drinking-behavior score, a related but distinct analysis from the % criterion used in Outcome, so it is not substituted in per the 'not a different analysis's statistic' rule.
- **PVal**: Same garbling issue as SD: the Table 2 significance test paired with the 53% vs 33% comparison is not reliably legible. Coded '.' rather than reusing Table 1's p<0.025 (continuous-score analysis, not the % criterion).
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: N=104 is the sum of the two dosage-arm N's in Tables 1 and 2 (High N=64 + Low N=40), the subset of the 117 single-LSD-session patients who were personally located for the 6-month follow-up interview (13 of 117 were lost to follow-up).
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 12/14 met. Unmet: eligibility, sample_size_power. Objective, setting (Spring Grove/Maryland Psychiatric Research Center), intervention doses, active comparator, outcome scales, randomization (2/3 allotted high dose), double-blind design, ANCOVA statistics, results tables, a dedicated safety section, and NIMH funding/Sandoz LSD-supply disclosure are all described. No inclusion/exclusion criteria beyond 'alcoholic patients' are stated, and no power calculation or sample-size justification is discussed. 12 of 14 met, below the 13-criterion threshold, so MethodologyDescribedYN = 0.
- **Retention (%)**: Denominator is 117 (patients with a single LSD session, the analytic subgroup used for Tables 1-2; the 18 patients with >1 session were excluded from this subgroup for uniformity of treatment amount, not attrition). Of these 117, 104 were personally located for the 6-month follow-up interview (13 lost). 104/117 = 88.9%.
- **Comparator**: The 'control' group received an active low dose of LSD (50ug) with identical therapeutic preparation to the high-dose (350-450ug) group -- an active dose-comparison, not a placebo control. Coded Comparator=1 per the dose-comparison rule.
- **InclusionExclusionCriteria**: No explicit inclusion/exclusion criteria are enumerated for the 135-patient alcoholism trial beyond the diagnosis label 'alcoholic patients'; coded 0.
- **ValidatedScaleMeasYN**: Global Adjustment and Drinking Behavior were rated on a 'predetermined 0 to 10 behavior rating scale' devised for this research program by an independent evaluation team; no citation to an established/validated psychometric instrument (e.g., AUDIT, ASI) is given, so coded 0.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: No description of how the 135-patient cohort was recruited (screened volunteers vs. broader referral base) is given; a separate, smaller substudy elsewhere in the article describes '82 consecutive alcoholic patients' but that group is not clearly the same as the 135/117/104 cohort analyzed in Tables 1-2, so representativeness could not be established and this was coded 0.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Spring Grove State Hospital and the Maryland Psychiatric Research Center, Baltimore, Maryland, United States.
- **Followup in months**: The independent rating team's protocol specifies assessment at 6, 12, and 18 months, but the article states 'Results are now completed at the six-month checkpoint... Full and final analysis and evaluation of these data are still in progress' -- only 6-month outcome data are actually reported in Tables 1-2, so Followup in months reflects the reported horizon (6), not the full planned protocol (18).
- **MortalityMeasured**: One death is reported in the article (an illustrative case-report patient who died of surgical complications), but that patient was enrolled in a separate cancer-pain pilot project described elsewhere in the same article, not in the 135-patient alcoholism trial that is the subject of this Alcohol-condition extraction. No mortality outcome is reported for the alcoholism cohort itself, so coded 0.
- **PhysicalChangeMeasured**: Chromosomal aberration rate (a biomarker) was measured before/after LSD administration in 32 patients treated at Spring Grove State Hospital (Table 3); the article does not explicitly confirm these 32 are drawn from the 135-patient alcoholism cohort, but Spring Grove was the site of the alcoholism trial, so this is treated as a physical/biological outcome measured within the study program.

### Adverse events
- **Pahnke 1970_AE01**: The article's safety statement ('over 300 patients have been treated with LSD... although there have been two cases in which disturbances persisted in the days following the actual LSD session. Both patients subsequently responded to conventional psychiatric treatment') is a pooled statement spanning the whole Spring Grove/Maryland Psychiatric Research Center LSD program (alcoholic, neurotic, and narcotic-addict patients), not broken out for the 135-alcoholic-patient trial specifically -- hence AE_denominator = 'Not numbered' rather than 104 or 300, and ArmScope = psychedelic_arm since both the high- and low-dose arms received active LSD (no placebo-only comparator exists to pool against). Graded Moderate because the described consequence (persisting disturbance requiring subsequent psychiatric treatment) meets the rubric's active-intervention/persistence-beyond-dosing-day threshold but no hospitalization, life-threatening course, or author-assigned SAE/serious label is stated. SeveritySource=rubric because the authors used only descriptive language ('disturbances persisted'), not a formal severity or seriousness label of their own. Excluded from this article's AE data: the transient in-session apprehension/confusion/paranoia and post-treatment death described in the illustrative case report, because that patient belonged to a separate cancer-pain pilot project, not the alcoholism trial.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: pending
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Two-group proportions (%)' / 'OR' / 'approx'
- **note**: High-dose LSD (350-450ug) vs low-dose LSD (50ug) comparison of the percentage of alcoholic patients meeting the authors' 'rehabilitated' drinking-behavior criterion (0-10 scale score of 8-10) at 6-month follow-up (Table 2, N=104). OR computed directly from the reported 2x2 counts; coded 'approx' rather than 'clean' because converting a dichotomized-scale proportion to an odds ratio involves an assumption a methodologist would want to review, and because the article's own paired significance test for this specific comparison is illegibly garbled in the source conversion.

## Rieser 2025

*Alcohol · 2025 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Co-primary endpoints were 'abstinence and mean alcohol use at 4-week follow-up' (Abstract/Methods), with no single explicit primary-of-the-two designation, so precedence rule (2) applies: the Abstract's Findings paragraph and the Results section both lead with the abstinence comparison before mean alcohol use. Abstinence duration itself was analyzed two ways in the text: (i) a Kaplan-Meier survival/Cox-regression analysis (Results: 'no significant difference in duration of abstinence (p = 0.55, ...) utilizing the Kaplan-Meier survival analysis'; Cox HR = 0.78, 95% CI 0.34-1.76, no p printed for the HR itself) and (ii) a group-mean comparison reported as 'duration of abstinence (p = 0.37, Cohen's d = 0.151)' later in the same Results paragraph (tied to the Fig. 2B boxplot). The Abstract states both together in one sentence ('p = 0.55, ... Cohen's d = 0.151'), pairing the KM p-value with the d-statistic from the separate group-mean test. This is an internal inconsistency in the article (the same d = 0.151 is paired with p = 0.55 in the Abstract but p = 0.37 in the Results narrative) that could not be resolved without inventing a decoding theory, so it is reported as printed and flagged here rather than silently reconciled. Outcome = 0.151 (Cohen's d for abstinence duration, psilocybin vs. placebo, 4-week follow-up) is taken verbatim from the Abstract/Results, chosen as the standardized effect size for the primary abstinence comparison per Tier-1 conversion priority (Cohen's d over an unconvertible HR).
- **SD**: No t- or U-test statistic value is printed alongside Cohen's d = 0.151 anywhere in the text; only the 95% CIs for each group's mean abstinence duration are given in the same sentence as the d-statistic in the Abstract ('psilocybin mean = 16.80 days, 95% CI: 14.31-19.29; placebo mean = 13.80 days, 95% CI: 10.97-16.63'). These CIs are reported here as the paired dispersion measure. Fig. 2B's caption separately gives slightly different SDs (psilocybin SD = 10.71, placebo SD = 12.68) tied to slightly different means (16.61/13.79 vs. 16.80/13.80) for what appears to be the same analysis - a minor additional numeric inconsistency, noted but not used, since it is one step further removed from the Outcome value's own sentence.
- **PVal**: 0.55 is used because it is the value printed in the same sentence as Cohen's d = 0.151 in the Abstract (the article's own headline pairing of this effect size). The Results section later re-uses the identical d = 0.151 with p = 0.37 instead (Mann-Whitney/t-test group comparison, Fig. 2B) - see the Outcome rationale for this unresolved internal inconsistency, which should go to human review.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: N = 37 is the 4-week-follow-up analysis population stated in the Abstract ('We included 37 participants who completed the 4-week follow-up ... psilocybin = 18, placebo = 19') and confirmed by Table 1 ('Sample size: Total 37, Psilocybin 18, Placebo 19') and the CONSORT flow diagram (Fig. 1: placebo arm reaches 19 at the 4-week/6th visit, psilocybon arm reaches 18). The Results-section sentence 'resulting in a final sample size of 37 participants (psilocybin: 19, placebo: 18)' reverses these two arm counts relative to the Abstract, Table 1, and Fig. 1 - an apparent typo in the source article. The majority-supported 18 psilocybin/19 placebo split (matching 3 of 4 places the numbers appear) was used; the discrepancy is flagged for human review rather than silently picked.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 14/14 met. Unmet: none. All 14 elements are explicitly described: objective (Abstract/Introduction), design and setting (single-center, double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled, parallel-group trial at Psychiatric University Hospital Zurich), eligibility (age, DSM-5 AUD diagnosis, recent withdrawal treatment, detailed exclusion criteria), intervention/comparator (25 mg psilocybin vs. 100% mannitol placebo, identical capsules, BRENDA-based psychotherapy), outcomes (primary: abstinence and mean SU at 4-week follow-up; secondary endpoints listed), sample_size_power (G-Power 3.1 a priori calculation, alpha = 0.05, power = 0.95, n = 24/arm target, with underpowering from early termination discussed), randomization (stratified 1:1 by hospital pharmacy, stratification factors named), blinding (double-blind with identical capsules; blinding integrity explicitly assessed via allocation-guess question), statistical_methods (detailed Statistics section: Kaplan-Meier, Cox regression, Mann-Whitney/t-tests, linear mixed models, Wilcoxon tests), results (fully reported with effect sizes and CIs throughout), harms (dedicated Safety assessment section and AE/SAE reporting), funding_coi (Funding and Declaration of interests sections with per-author detail).
- **PrimaryCare**: Coded 0: the entire study (recruitment, dosing, psychotherapy, follow-up) was conducted through the Psychiatric University Hospital Zurich; the text does not mention any primary-care referral pathway, provider, or setting (contrast with trials that explicitly recruit via primary care).
- **Retention (%)**: Denominator = 40, the randomized sample (Fig. 1 CONSORT diagram: 21 randomized to placebo + 19 randomized to psilocybin, both arms fully proceeding to the dosing visit with no pre-dosing dropout). Numerator = 37, participants completing the 4-week primary endpoint (Abstract/Table 1). Retention = 37/40*100 = 92.5%. Note: the CONSORT flow-diagram text (Fig. 1) is OCR-garbled in the converted markdown (overlapping columns, stray symbols), so the 21/19 randomized split was cross-checked against the narrative dropout counts described in the same figure caption and is consistent with them.
- **AE_denominator**: Coded 40 (all randomized/dosed participants: 19 psilocybin + 21 placebo per Fig. 1), since Safety assessment (vital signs, (S)AE case report forms) was applied 'at every visit' from the dosing visit onward across both arms, and the reported AE/SAE counts are explicitly broken out by arm (13/7 AEs, 1/4 SAEs) rather than restricted to completers only, supporting a full-randomized-sample denominator rather than the N=37 completer analysis population.
- **BaseStatus**: Coded 'Severe' from Table 1's DSM-5 AUD severity breakdown for the full analyzed sample: 'AUD severity (mild:moderate:severe, DSM5) 3:6:28' - 28/37 (75.7%) severe, the modal and majority category.
- **PercentFem**: Table 1 / Abstract: 'female:male = 14:23' of the N=37 analysis sample. 14/37*100 = 37.8%.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Table 1, 'Race and ethnicity': Caucasian = 37, African = 1, with a footnote that 'the total exceeds 100% because one participant selected multiple categories' (i.e., the 1 African-identifying participant also selected Caucasian). Non-white/BIPOC = 1 of 37 = 2.7%.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Trial conducted at the Psychiatric University Hospital Zurich, Switzerland (Methods/Study design; ethics approvals from Swiss cantonal and federal bodies). Coded 'NonUS'.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 0: participants were required to have completed withdrawal treatment within 6 weeks of enrollment and passed extensive exclusion criteria (other major psychiatric or substance use disorders, suicidal-behavior history, family history of schizophrenia/schizoaffective/bipolar disorder, unstable physical health, limited hallucinogen-use history). Discussion also notes the sample was 'most Caucasian, reflecting the population distribution in Switzerland,' with the authors themselves calling for 'more diverse populations' in future trials. This is a highly screened, help-seeking volunteer sample, not reflective of the broader real-world AUD population.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: No income, employment, insurance, or other SES-related inclusion criterion or description is reported anywhere in the text. Coded 0.
- **ConfoundYN**: Coded 1: randomization was stratified by age, sex, and AUD severity to balance these factors (Randomization and blinding); an exploratory subgroup analysis examined prior psychedelic experience as a moderator of outcome; a sensitivity analysis standardized alcohol-use outcomes by body weight/BMI.
- **Implementation Outcome**: Coded 0: no feasibility, acceptability, fidelity, reach, adoption, or sustainability outcome is reported. The allocation-guess check assesses blinding integrity, not implementation/fidelity, and is not treated as an implementation outcome here.
- **MortalityMeasured**: Coded 0: mortality is not named as a measured or reported outcome anywhere in the text, and no deaths are reported among the AE/SAE counts (the SAEs are explicitly described as inpatient withdrawal treatments only).

### Adverse events
- **Rieser 2025_AE01**: Text gives only aggregate non-serious AE counts by arm ('we observed a total of 13 AEs in the psilocybin and 7 in the placebo group'), with a pointer to 'Safety outcomes are reported in Supplementary Table S11' for the itemized breakdown, which is not part of the converted text provided. No individual event types, per-event counts, severity grades, or onset timing are available in-text beyond the summary statement 'The psilocybin dose (25 mg) administered in our study was well-tolerated.' Defaulted to Mild per the rubric's under-specified-AE rule (no intervention-need or persistence signal), SeverityIndeterminate=Y, SeveritySource=rubric (no author grading visible in this text), and NAffected='.' since the 13/7 figures are event counts for an unspecified mix of types, not a single AE type's participant count.
- **Rieser 2025_AE02**: Text states explicitly: 'One serious adverse event occurred in the psilocybin and four in the placebo group, all related to inpatient withdrawal treatments' (Abstract), and 'Additionally, 1 SAE in the psilocybin and 4 in the placebo group occurred, all of which were inpatient withdrawal treatments' (Results). Graded Severe because inpatient hospitalization is an explicit FDA seriousness criterion and the authors themselves use the SAE designation via the study's '(S)AE case report form' (Safety assessment), so SeveritySource='author'. NAffected=5 (1+4) reflects the combined arm counts as printed; the per-arm split is preserved in Description. Onset coded 'unknown' rather than inferred, since the text does not state when during the 4-week-to-6-month follow-up period these relapses/hospitalizations occurred relative to the dosing visit.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: "Cohen's d" / 'd' / 'clean'
- **note**: Cohen's d = 0.151 for abstinence duration (psilocybin vs. placebo, 4-week follow-up) is directly reported by the authors and requires no derivation, so it is a clean d-to-Hedges'-g pass-through. Flagging for human awareness: this same d-value is paired with two different p-values in the article (p=0.55 in the Abstract vs. p=0.37 in the Results narrative) - an unresolved internal inconsistency in the source article that does not affect the d-value itself but affects interpretation of its significance.

## Rothberg 2021

*Alcohol · 2021 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Citation/Title**: This is a secondary mediation analysis of the RCT reported in Dakwar E, Levin F, Hart CL, et al. (2020) 'A single ketamine infusion combined with motivational enhancement therapy for alcohol use disorder: A randomized midazolam-controlled pilot trial.' Am J Psychiatry 177: 125-133 (same N=40 sample, NCT02539511). Eligible as a secondary analysis per the boundary-case rule; parent trial named here per instructions.
- **Year**: Masthead shows 'Journal of Psychopharmacology 2021, Vol. 35(2) 150-158 © The Author(s) 2020', i.e. an online-first 2020 copyright with a 2021 print-issue year. Coded Year=2021 (issue year) per the request's Year field and the field rule to prefer issue year, with the 2020 online-first copyright noted here as the discrepancy.
- **Outcome**: Primary outcome is explicitly named in Methods/'Drinking outcome measures': '"At-risk" drinking post-infusion was used as the primary outcome measure' (NIAAA-defined heavy-use threshold over the 3-week post-infusion window). Its reported effect, in Results/'Infusion effects': 'Ketamine decreased the odds of engaging in at-risk drinking by approximately five times (p<0.05)' via binary logistic robust regression — no exact OR/CI is printed for this specific model. Table 2 gives the underlying raw proportions (ketamine 4/17 = 23.5% vs midazolam 11/23 = 47.8% engaged in at-risk drinking), which is the numeric pairing recorded here alongside the article's own 'approximately five times' language. This is the study's own primary comparison, distinct from the mediation analysis (HMS/CADSS) that is the paper's stated aim — per the prompt's precedence rule, Outcome is the stated primary outcome/comparison, not the mediation result the abstract emphasizes.
- **SD**: No test statistic, standard error, or confidence interval is printed for the binary logistic robust regression that produced the 'approximately five times' odds estimate — only 'p<0.05' is given (Results/'Infusion effects'). Coded '.' rather than fabricating a value.
- **PVal**: Printed exactly as 'p<0.05' immediately following the odds-ratio statement in Results/'Infusion effects', paired with the same at-risk-drinking primary outcome as Outcome and SD.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: Table 2 shows the primary at-risk-drinking analysis was run on all N=40 randomized participants (23 midazolam + 17 ketamine). Results/'Post-infusion drinking' confirms: 'Post-infusion drinking behavior was recorded for all 40 participants.' This N differs from the N=35 used in the separate mediation analysis (3 missing HMS, 2 missing CADSS, per Results/'Participants'), which is not the analysis that produced Outcome.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 13/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power. All elements are described except a priori sample-size/power justification for the primary hypothesis: the only power-adjacent statement concerns power for a normality test ('our small sample size (n=40) would preclude an adequately powered statistical test for normality'), not a sample-size/power rationale for the N=40 design itself. Harms is met because the article explicitly states no AEs occurred, which counts as 'described' per the described-vs-present distinction.
- **BaseStatus**: Participants met DSM-IV alcohol dependence criteria with defined heavy-use thresholds (>4 drinks/day males / >3 females, or weekly minimums) and averaged 6.6 drinks/day (Table 1), but the article reports no formal severity grading instrument (e.g., DSM-5 mild/moderate/severe symptom count). Coded '.' rather than inferring a severity band from drinks/day alone.
- **Age_Median**: Table 1: total sample mean age 53.0 (SD 9.8) -> bracket '50+'.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Table 1 race percentages (Asian 5.4%, Black/African American 13.5%, White 70.3%, Multiracial 10.8%) sum to 100% but are computed over n=37 (2+5+26+4=37), not the full n=40 sample — implying 3 participants had unreported/missing race data. Non-white sum = Asian + Black + Multiracial = 5.4+13.5+10.8 = 29.7%, reported as-is from the article's own percentage base rather than recalculated against n=40.
- **Retention (%)**: Results/'Post-infusion drinking': 'Post-infusion drinking behavior was recorded for all 40 participants,' and Table 2 totals 23+17=40, matching the randomized N with no separately reported dropout for the primary analysis. Denominator is the randomized N (40) per the field rule; completers=40, giving 100%.
- **Comparator**: Comparator=1 for the active-control arm, midazolam (0.025 mg/kg IV). Note: the Methods opening sentence describes the parent trial as '...randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled trial...' while the Abstract and Infusion-procedures sections both correctly identify midazolam as an 'active control' — an internal labeling inconsistency in the article itself. Coded based on the actual design (active comparator), not the inconsistent 'placebo-controlled' phrase.
- **Power**: No a priori power calculation for the primary hypothesis is reported. Coded 1 because the article repeatedly discusses the small-sample limitation in terms that function as a power discussion: it explicitly ties n=40 to inadequate power for a normality test (Statistical analyses section) and flags 'a relatively small sample size' as a limitation warranting larger future studies (Limitations) — treated as the 'bare statement that the study was underpowered' the rule says counts as 1.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 0: sample was a treatment-seeking volunteer cohort recruited under fairly narrow exclusion criteria (excluded any other substance dependence, active depressive disorder, and past/current bipolar or psychotic disorders), i.e. a screened research sample rather than a broadly representative clinical population, as the Limitations section itself acknowledges ('unknown whether these results would generalize to a broader population of alcohol-dependent individuals with psychiatric comorbidity').
- **Followup in months**: Study design combines a 5-week (~1.15-month) MET/monitoring program with phone follow-up 'at 1 and 3 months following the completion of the study' (Study design and procedures). Measuring from end of treatment, the longest reported horizon is the 3-month post-completion phone follow-up; the primary at-risk-drinking outcome itself is assessed over a shorter 3-week (21-day) post-infusion window nested within the 5-week program.
- **CessationMeasured**: Coded 1 because '% Days abstinent' is reported as a distinct outcome (Table 3), which functions as a cessation-oriented measure alongside the partial-reduction measures (heavy drinking days, % heavy drinking days) that justify ReductionMeasured=1.
- **ArmScope**: Coded 'pooled' because the no-AE statements cover both arms jointly: 'All participants tolerated study procedures without adverse events' (Participants) and 'No adverse events were reported as a result of either the ketamine or midazolam infusions' (Infusion effects) — values are not reported separately for the ketamine-exposed arm alone.
- **SafetyReported**: Coded 'Y' (not silence) because the article makes an explicit, affirmative safety statement in two places (Participants section and Results/'Infusion effects') rather than simply omitting an AE discussion — this distinguishes 'assessed, none occurred' from 'never looked.'

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Odds ratio (approximate)' / 'OR' / 'approx'
- **note**: Article states ketamine 'decreased the odds of engaging in at-risk drinking by approximately five times (p<0.05)' via binary logistic robust regression but prints no exact OR, CI, or test statistic for that model. Table 2's raw 2x2 proportions give a directly computable OR of (11/12)/(4/13) ≈ 2.98 (odds of at-risk drinking ~3x higher for midazolam than ketamine) — lower than the model-based 'approximately five times' language, likely reflecting the difference between the unadjusted crosstab and the robust-regression estimate; this discrepancy is not resolved, only flagged. 'At-risk drinking' is an adverse/undesired outcome, so OR<1 favors ketamine; direction=-1 flips the natural sign so a positive converted effect size favors the psychedelic arm, consistent with other studies' effect-direction convention. Approx (not clean) because the exact model OR is never printed; measure and computed_or instead use the raw table proportions.

## Savage 1973

*Opioid · 1973 · eligible=Y · config=pending*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Results, first paragraph: 'nine (25%) of the 36 subjects in the treatment group maintained total abstinence from narcotic drugs for at least one year as opposed to two (5%) of 37 in the control group (P<.05; see Table 3).' This is the article's headline, abstract-led outcome ('Comparative verified abstinence data throughout the first posttreatment year were significantly in favor of the treatment group.'). Chose the strict-abstinence dichotomy over the article's secondary recomputation that includes briefly-relapsed patients (12/37 = 33.3%), since the strict figure is the one carried into Table 3's chi-square test and is not itself a subgroup redefinition.
- **SD**: Table 3 (x2 Analysis of Differences in Abstinence...) is badly OCR-garbled: the (0-6 mo) row prints 5 bare numbers (12, 25, 34, 5.35, .03), the (7-12 mo) row prints 6 (13, 3, 23, 33, 6.51, .02), and the (0-12 mo) row prints only 4 (27, 34, 3.86, .05), with no consistent column structure and no row reconciling to the stated N=36/37 or 9/2 counts. Per the anti-fabrication rule, declined to guess which figure (e.g. the isolated 3.86) is the chi-square paired with the 12-month abstinence result rather than inventing a decoding rule; coded '.' and flagging the table as unusable in its current OCR state.
- **PVal**: Same Results-section sentence gives the threshold directly as printed: '(P<.05; see Table 3)'. Used the printed threshold verbatim rather than any Table 3 p-value, since Table 3 itself is unreliable (see SD).
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: 36 treatment-group subjects (37 originally, minus 1 who died of aortic stenosis before the 12-month point and was therefore excluded from the 12-month abstinence analysis) + 37 control-group subjects = 73, the denominator underlying the 9/36 vs 2/37 comparison in Outcome.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 11/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power, blinding, harms. 11/14 met. No sample-size or power justification anywhere. Blinding is never mentioned in either direction (not implemented and not stated as absent), so it does not meet the 'described, including as absent' bar. No dedicated harms/safety assessment or section is described (harms information that does exist is incidental, in Results and the case illustrations, not a described methodology element). Funding is disclosed (grant MH-15555, Friends Medical Science Research Center) with no explicit COI statement, which is sufficient per the field rule for funding_coi=Y.
- **Retention (%)**: 78 volunteers were randomly assigned; 74 comprised the final study sample (the abstract states '37 completers in each group'), because 4 subjects originally assigned to the treatment condition transferred to the regular program before receiving LSD and were excluded from analysis. 74/78 x 100 = 94.9%, using randomized N (78) as denominator per the rule (both randomized and enrolled/completer counts are reported).
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Table 1 reports only one non-white race category, 'Black (%)' = 76 for the total sample (78% treatment, 73% control); no other non-white groups are reported, so no summation was needed.
- **PercentFem**: Explicitly 'All male narcotic addicts in Maryland correctional institutions' were eligible/enrolled; coded 0 as an explicitly reported fact, not a missing value.
- **ConfoundYN**: Tables 1-2 compare treatment vs control on demographic (age, race, marital status, religion, education, occupation, years institutionalized) and pre-treatment criminal/drug-history variables (arrests, age of first arrest, age of first narcotics use) and state 'None of the differences between treatment and control groups were statistically significant.' This is a baseline-comparability check used as evidence randomization worked, not a statistical adjustment of the outcome model; coded 1 because confounders were substantively assessed/discussed, per the field's broader definition.
- **ValidatedScaleMeasYN**: MMPI and IQ tests (Raven, Otis) are validated instruments but are reported only as baseline characterization within the two individual case illustrations (Theodore W., Wally B.), not as a trial-level outcome compared between treatment and control. The trial's actual outcomes are urine-verified abstinence (not a scale) and an unpublished, non-validated 'Global Adjustment Rating Scale' (McCabe and Shock, unpublished data, 1972). Coded 0.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Sample is restricted to volunteer parolees aged 21-50 with >=18 months remaining before parole, medically/psychologically screened (excluding organic brain damage, active liver disease, cardiovascular disease, florid psychosis), and self-selected under an acknowledged prison-coercion caveat ('it is questionable that anyone in prison can be considered a true volunteer'; candidates were shown a film emphasizing LSD's dangers before consenting). Coded 0 as a highly screened, volunteer, incarcerated sample rather than an unselected real-world clinical population.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: Table 1 reports 54% unskilled laborers and mean 8.6 years education for the sample; the clinic is described as being 'on the fringe of the Baltimore ghetto area.' No explicit income/public-insurance criterion is given, but occupational, educational, and setting descriptors substantively describe a low-SES population; coded 1 as a judgment call.
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: MMPI is administered and referenced only within the two individual case illustrations (Theodore W., Wally B.), not analyzed or reported as a treatment-vs-control group outcome anywhere in Results; coded 0.
- **QualityOfLifeMeasured**: The 'Global Adjustment Rating Scale' assesses residential, occupational, and interpersonal functioning tied to parole/rehabilitation status (Fig 2), which is treated here as a rehabilitation/adjustment outcome distinct from a formal quality-of-life instrument; coded 0 as a judgment call.
- **CulturalFraming**: Comment section explicitly names the racial dynamic as a study limitation: 'a wide sociocultural gulf existed between the white therapist and the predominantly black patient group. Black therapists could be expected to have a less difficult time establishing with the patients a relationship of trust, a crucial determinant of positive therapeutic outcome.' Table 1 also reports % Black by group. Coded 1.
- **BlindingYN**: No blinding procedure of any kind (patient, therapist, or outcome rater) is described in Methods; the independent evaluation team rated adjustment from parole-agent interviews with no stated blinding to group assignment. Coded 0 as an absent, undescribed design element (this is why 'blinding' = N rather than Y in MethodologyDescribedYN: the absence itself was never stated by the authors, only inferable from silence).
- **MortalityMeasured**: One treatment-group death (aortic stenosis, unrelated to LSD per the authors) is reported in Results as an incidental fact affecting the 12-month abstinence denominator, not as a designed mortality endpoint. Per the field rule, a death reported anywhere counts as MortalityMeasured=1 and generates an ae_events row (AE01), even though causally unrelated to the intervention.
- **SafetyReported**: Borderline call. There is no dedicated Adverse Events/Safety section or table. However the article does report specific, patient-level safety-relevant events: the treatment-group death (with cause given), and case-illustration content describing in-session fear/mistrust/panic and nausea, plus an authors' summary sentence that 'the partial regression and loosening of defenses during the LSD experience tend to precipitate panic, somatic complaints, and abreaction' in patients like Theodore. Coded 'Y' (safety-relevant information is genuinely reported, even if informally and not systematically across the full N) rather than 'N', per the cardinal rule against understating harm reporting when real signal is present in the text.
- **NumDistinctAEs**: Three distinct types identified from the text: (1) death/aortic stenosis, (2) in-session fear/panic/abreaction (challenging-experience phenomenology), (3) transient nausea. Types (2) and (3) are illustrated via a single named case (Theodore) rather than counted across the cohort, so this is a much softer count than a systematic AE table; flagged here rather than defaulted to 'Not numbered' because the three qualitatively distinct event types are each clearly nameable even though their per-type N is not.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: No statement anywhere gives a count of how many of the 36-41 treatment-group patients experienced fear/panic, nausea, or any other adverse effect; only the death has a stated N (1). Coded '.' for the article as a whole.
- **AE_denominator**: No explicit statement of a safety-assessed population size; the panic/nausea events are drawn from one illustrative case narrative, not a systematically monitored-N cohort. Coded 'Not numbered' rather than '.' because some safety information was in fact reported (SafetyReported=Y).
- **WorstSeverity**: Driven entirely by AE01 (death = CTCAE grade 5 / FDA seriousness criterion = Severe per rubric). This is flagged explicitly because the authors themselves attribute the death to a pre-existing, unrelated cardiac condition (aortic stenosis), not to LSD or the study intervention; WorstSeverity nonetheless reflects the mortality-reporting rule rather than any causal judgment about the intervention's safety.

### Adverse events
- **Savage 1973_AE01**: Results section: 'One of the original 37 patients in the treatment group died before reaching the 12 months follow-up point; he had maintained five posttreatment months of complete abstinence before dying of aortic stenosis.' Onset coded 'delayed' because death occurred post-discharge, well beyond the acute dosing period (~5 months out). Severity graded Severe per rubric (death = Grade 5) using SeveritySource='rubric' because the authors report the fact and cause of death but assign no CTCAE grade or SAE label and use no severity language of their own; this is a pre-1980 article and the 'author' source was not used because no author severity language exists for this event.
- **Savage 1973_AE02**: Drawn from the Theodore W. case illustration: his own session-report quote ('I was making myself afraid by self-imposed mistrust... I was looking for a demon to jump out at me any moment') and the authors' following summary ('the partial regression and loosening of defenses during the LSD experience tend to precipitate panic, somatic complaints, and abreaction'). Graded Mild per the rubric's default for expected, in-session, non-pharmacologically-managed challenging-experience phenomenology (the patient was reassured/held the therapist's hand, no rescue medication, remained in and completed the session). SeverityIndeterminate='Y' because it is unclear from a single illustrative case how many of the cohort this generalizes to, and whether severity varied for other patients the authors' summary sentence may be describing collectively rather than just Theodore.
- **Savage 1973_AE03**: Also from Theodore W.'s quoted session report: 'My stomach began to feel nauseated somewhat and I put emphasis on this rather than something of importance.' Treated as a distinct physical symptom (AE_type='AE') separate from the psychological fear/panic content (AE02). Graded Mild as a transient, expected, self-limiting acute drug effect with no intervention described; SeverityIndeterminate='N' since the description, though from one case, is specific enough to grade confidently.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: pending
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Two-group proportions (%)' / 'OR' / 'approx'
- **note**: Primary outcome is a 2x2 verified-abstinence comparison at 12 months (25% vs 5%, P<.05); converting proportions to an odds ratio is a defensible but assumption-carrying step (a methodologist would want to see it), and Table 3's chi-square figures are OCR-garbled and could not be used to corroborate a chi2-based conversion instead, so this is proposed as 'approx' rather than 'clean'.

## Sessa 2019

*Alcohol · 2019 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: This paper's stated aim is a 'safety and tolerability proof of concept study' (Summary); the authors explicitly defer the drinking-behaviour outcome ('We are not reporting detailed outcome data in respect of drinking behaviour... as the study is still ongoing'). Per precedence rule, the authors' designated primary outcome (tolerability) governs, not the largest or most complete statistic. No comparator exists, so Outcome is coded as the single-arm rate: 4/4 tolerated with no SAEs, plus the secondary drinking pattern reported in the Outcome-and-follow-up section (Case Presentations / Outcome and follow-up sections).
- **SD**: No dispersion statistic or test statistic is reported anywhere in the article for the tolerability or drinking outcomes; this is a purely narrative case series with no inferential statistics computed.
- **PVal**: No p-value is reported anywhere in the article for any outcome; figures show only descriptive means with SEM error bars (figure 1), not significance tests.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: N=4, the four case-series participants who completed the 8-week course and are the subject of this report ('this paper focuses on... the first four participants'); the parent study has screened 16 and 7 have completed overall, but only these 4 are analyzed/reported here.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 10/14 met. Unmet: comparator, sample_size_power, randomization, statistical_methods. 10 of 14 elements described (objective, design, setting, eligibility, intervention, outcomes, blinding [explicit 'open-label'], results, harms, funding/COI). No explicit discussion of a comparator's absence, no sample-size/power justification, no explicit statement on randomization, and no statistical-methods section, consistent with a purely descriptive open-label case series with no inferential statistics.

### Adverse events
- **Sessa 2019_AE01**: Onset coded post-session-acute per 'in the days after the MDMA sessions.' Severity graded Mild using the authors' own descriptor 'minor tinnitus,' which the authors themselves attribute to chronic postwithdrawal phenomena rather than MDMA; no intervention was reported, and no FDA seriousness criterion is met, so SeverityIndeterminate = N despite the absence of a formal CTCAE grade.
- **Sessa 2019_AE02**: Onset coded post-session-acute per 'in the days after the MDMA sessions.' Severity graded Mild using the authors' own descriptor 'mild memory disturbance,' likewise attributed by the authors to postwithdrawal phenomena rather than MDMA; no intervention reported, no seriousness criterion met.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Single-arm rate (no inferential statistic)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: Open-label single-arm case series (n=4) with no comparator arm and no inferential statistic for either the primary tolerability outcome or the secondary drinking-behaviour outcome; none of the seven supported Tier-1 conversion methods applies, so this is excluded and routed to Tier 2.

## Sessa 2021

*Alcohol · 2021 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: The article's stated main/primary outcome measures are safety and tolerability endpoints (Methods: 'number of patients completing the 8-week psychotherapy course, the number accepting the second booster dose of MDMA... and adverse events'), not the drinking-behaviour data, which the Methods explicitly label a secondary outcome. Per the primary-vs-secondary boundary rule, Outcome is coded from the primary endpoint: 12/14 (85.7%) participants received both MDMA sessions (Results, 'Physiological and Tolerability Effects' section) and the Adverse Events section states no unexpected AEs occurred. No formal completion-rate statistic for the full 8-week course is given as a single number. This is a discrepancy worth flagging: the article's only SUD-related outcome (drinking units/week, TLFB) is secondary, which is why the article passes eligibility criterion (b), but Outcome itself reflects the primary safety/tolerability endpoint, not the drinking data.
- **SD**: No dispersion or test statistic accompanies the primary completion/AE outcome; the study explicitly performed no hypothesis testing ('As this is a non-RTC, open label study, no hypothesis testing was performed').
- **PVal**: No p-value is reported for the primary outcome; consistent with the stated absence of hypothesis testing in this open-label feasibility study.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: 14 is the full enrolled/dosed cohort ('14 were enrolled'; 'n=14 eligible participants who underwent the course of MDMA-assisted psychotherapy') and is the denominator for the primary completion and AE reporting.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 13/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power. Design, setting (Addaction substance misuse service, Clinical Research and Imaging Centre), full inclusion/exclusion lists, intervention dosing, absence of comparator/randomization/blinding, outcomes, results, AEs, and funding/COI ('Alexander Mosely Charitable Foundation'; 'Declaration of Interests: None') are all explicitly described. Only sample_size_power fails: the article states no formal statistical/power justification for the N=14 sample beyond noting 'a relatively small sample size' as a post-hoc limitation, with no a priori or post hoc power discussion.
- **Retention (%)**: Study is not randomized, so the enrolled N (14) is the denominator per the non-randomized rule. Numerator is ambiguous: the clearest single completion figure reported is '12 of the fourteen participants received both sessions of MDMA-assisted psychotherapy' (85.7%). An alternative reading from the Figure 3 caption ('One participant dropped out of the study at 3-months and two patients failed to provide data at 9-month follow-up') would suggest 13/14 (92.9%) if only the formal drop-out counts as attrition. The 12/14 figure was chosen because it ties directly to completion of the core MDMA-assisted intervention, which is the study's stated primary retention-relevant outcome.
- **ConfoundYN**: Coded 1 based on the Discussion's substantive treatment of non-randomization as a confound when comparing BIMA outcomes to the historical 'Outcomes Study' comparator cohort ('it is not appropriate to statistically compare these two studies – as patients were not randomised into the studies').
- **Power**: No a priori or post hoc power calculation or discussion is present anywhere in the text. The Limitations section states only 'a relatively small sample size', which does not explicitly invoke statistical power, so this was not treated as meeting the 'bare statement of underpowered' threshold.
- **PrimaryCare**: Recruitment was from a specialist community substance-misuse service (Addaction) and academic/NHS mental-health/neuropsychopharmacology centres; no interface with primary-care (GP) settings or providers is described.
- **BaseStatus**: The article reports AUD severity as spanning both bands ('most eligible participants in the moderate to severe range', Figure 1/SADQ/SIP). Given the closed enum forces a single value, 'Severe' was chosen as best judgment, supported by the population's detoxification requirement and high rates of alcohol-related blackouts (64%), withdrawal seizures (14%), risky incidents (86%) and forensic history (75%). This is a genuine judgment call given the mixed 'moderate to severe' description.
- **Age_Median**: Reported mean age 48 years falls in the 30-50 bracket.
- **PercentFem**: 6 of 14 enrolled were female (8 males, 6 females) = 42.9%.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Article states 'All were White British', so BIPOC percent = 0.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: Coded 1 based on explicit employment-status reporting (4 employed, 9 unemployed, 1 retired), i.e. 64% unemployed, describing a socioeconomically disadvantaged sample; no direct income or public-insurance figures were given.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Study conducted in the United Kingdom (Bristol/Imperial College London, North Somerset Substance Misuse Service); coded 'NonUS' per the closed-enum rule (never a literal country name in the data field).
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: 36 participants attended screening and only 14 (39%) were enrolled, reflecting extensive medical/psychiatric exclusion criteria typical of an early-phase safety trial; coded 0 as a highly screened rather than real-world representative sample.
- **Comparator/RandomizationYN/BlindingYN/Double+Blind**: Open-label, single-arm, non-placebo-controlled feasibility study ('this trial... it was an open-label, non-placebo-controlled study. Therefore all patients knew they would be getting MDMA'). Per PromptVersion v1.2, these four design elements are coded 0 (absent), not 3, since the concept clearly applies to this design and was simply not implemented.
- **QualityOfLifeMeasured**: Quality of life was listed as a secondary outcome and data were collected ('changes... quality of life measures... will be published in forthcoming papers'), but detailed QoL results are deferred to a future paper and not reported quantitatively here.
- **Implementation Outcome**: The study's stated main outcome measures include completion of the 8-week course and acceptance of the second booster MDMA dose, which are feasibility/acceptability implementation outcomes.
- **EligibilityNote / EligibilityFailedCriterion**: Both coded 'NA' because EligibleYN = 'Y': the article reports a prospectively planned open-label clinical trial (criterion a) with a secondary SUD outcome (drinking units/week via TLFB, criterion b).

### Adverse events
- **Sessa 2021_AE01**: Included despite being framed by the authors as an expected, benign drug effect, per the instruction not to silently absorb reported safety-monitoring findings; graded Mild because the article states values stayed within normal limits and no intervention was required for any participant except the one captured separately in AE02.
- **Sessa 2021_AE02**: The peak BP value (183/118) is objectively high, but the article explicitly states the participant was 'asymptomatic' and 'no medical intervention was required', and the event resolved spontaneously within two hours; graded Mild per the rubric's instruction to grade by consequence/intervention need rather than raw intensity. Withholding the scheduled booster dose was treated as a protocol/dosing decision, not a medical intervention, consistent with the authors' own framing.
- **Sessa 2021_AE03**: Coded as a distinct challenging_experience-type event per the instruction to flag CEQ-sense phenomenology (elevated anticipatory SUDS before dosing) rather than omit it; graded Mild as it is described as expected and self-resolving without pharmacological intervention (lorazepam was available but not stated as administered).

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Descriptive completion/safety rate (no inferential statistic)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: This is a single-arm, open-label safety/tolerability study with no comparator and no hypothesis testing performed; the primary outcome (completion/AE rates) is purely descriptive, so no standard effect-size conversion (d/g/eta_p/F/chi2/OR/RR) is defensible. The secondary drinking-behaviour data (130.6 to 18.7 units/week at 9 months) is a within-subject pre/post change with no between-groups comparator and cannot be treated as a between-groups effect size either.

## Smart 1964

*Alcohol · 1964 · eligible=N · config=pending*

### Article
- **Outcome**: No primary outcome belongs to this article. Smart & Storm ran no trial; the numeric findings in the text (e.g., 12/24, 15/16, 46/61 alcoholics 'improved'/'much improved') are the reviewed studies' own reported outcomes (refs 5-7), not this article's. Coded '.'.
- **SD**: No dispersion/test statistic of the authors' own exists to pair with an Outcome; none was computed for a study that does not exist. Coded '.'.
- **PVal**: No p-value is reported for an analysis conducted by these authors; none was run. Coded '.'.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: No analytic sample belongs to this article. The article discusses other studies' samples (e.g., 24, 16, 61, 17, 3 patients) but has no N of its own. Coded '.'.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 3/14 met. Unmet: setting, eligibility, intervention, comparator, outcomes, sample_size_power, randomization, blinding, statistical_methods, harms, funding_coi. This is a narrative commentary, not an empirical study. 'objective' is met (stated aim: 'to examine closely the studies purporting to show that LSD is a useful adjunct to therapy'). 'design' is met because the review's own evaluative framework is described — three requirements: control groups, blind administration/scoring, and pre/post measures — applied to the five reviewed studies. 'results' is met via the SUMMARY section stating the review's findings (none of the five studies used control groups or blinding; only one reported comparable pre/post measures, and even that one used different pre- and post-treatment instruments). Setting, eligibility, intervention, comparator, outcomes, sample-size/power, randomization, blinding, statistical methods, harms and funding/COI are all N because no trial of the authors' own exists for any of these elements to describe.
- **Pos Result Y/N**: Coded 3 (cannot be determined) — there is no intervention or trial conducted by these authors whose direction of effect could be judged; the article's own conclusion is a critique of methodology, not a treatment-effect finding.
- **RandomizationYN**: Coded 3 — the concept is genuinely inapplicable: no trial or participants belong to this article. The text discusses the absence of randomization in the five reviewed (other) studies, not in a design of its own.
- **BlindingYN**: Coded 3 for the same reason as RandomizationYN — no trial of the authors' own exists in which blinding could have been implemented.
- **Double+Blind**: Coded 3, consistent with BlindingYN=3 (not 1, per the 'must not be 1 when BlindingYN=0' rule and the broader inapplicability of the concept here).
- **Comparator**: Coded 3 — no comparator or control group exists because no trial was conducted by these authors.
- **PrimaryCare**: Coded 3 — the article is a methodological commentary, not a clinical-care or intervention study that could interface with primary-care settings.
- **Power**: Coded 3 — no statistical power analysis, a priori or post hoc, exists for a study the authors did not conduct. (The article does discuss general research standards but performs no power analysis of its own.)
- **Retention (%)**: Coded '.' — not calculable; there is no completer/denominator pair belonging to this article's own sample, because there is no sample.
- **ConfoundYN**: Coded 3 — no confound assessment is possible without a study or data of the authors' own.
- **InclusionExclusionCriteria**: Coded 3 — no participants were enrolled by these authors, so no inclusion/exclusion criteria of their own exist.
- **ValidatedScaleMeasYN**: Coded 3 — no outcome measure, validated or otherwise, was administered by these authors.
- **Funding/COI YN**: Coded 0 — no funding source or conflict-of-interest statement is given; only an acknowledgment of two colleagues' critical reading and the authors' institutional affiliation (Alcoholism and Drug Addiction Research Foundation, Toronto) are noted, which is not a funding/COI disclosure.
- **BaseStatus**: Coded '.' — no participant sample of the authors' own exists over which baseline severity could be assessed.
- **Age_Median**: Coded '.' — no participant sample of the authors' own exists.
- **PercentFem**: Coded '.' — no participant sample of the authors' own exists.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Coded '.' — no participant sample of the authors' own exists.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: Coded 3 — concept inapplicable; no participants of the authors' own.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Coded '.' — no study population of the authors' own to locate. (The authors' institutional affiliation is Toronto, Canada, per the byline, but this field concerns a study sample, not author affiliation.)
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 3 — no recruitment was conducted by these authors.
- **InterventionDescription**: Coded '.' — no intervention was administered by these authors; the article describes LSD dosing/therapy protocols used in the five reviewed (other) studies, which is not this article's own intervention.
- **InterventionTherapy**: Coded 3 — no intervention, and therefore no adjunct therapy, was delivered by these authors.
- **HarmsAssessed**: Coded 3, consistent with SafetyReported='N' (permitted values 0/3/'.' ). No trial exists in which harms could have been assessed; the article's discussion of 'placebo reactors' is a citation of others' literature, not an AE assessment of its own.
- **Followup in months**: Coded '.' — no follow-up was conducted by these authors. The article critiques the wide, inconsistent follow-up intervals used across the five reviewed studies (ranging roughly 2 months to 3 years) but performed no follow-up of its own.
- **BehaviorChangeMeasured**: Coded 0, not 1 — no measurement was performed by these authors; they synthesize behavior-change findings (drinking histories, 'improved'/'much improved' ratings) reported in five OTHER trials. The same reasoning (no measurement performed by these authors) applies to PhysicalChangeMeasured, MentalHealthChangeMeasured, QualityOfLifeMeasured, Implementation Outcome, ReductionMeasured, CessationMeasured, RelapseMeasured and MortalityMeasured, each coded 0.
- **LSD**: Coded 0, not 1 — substance flags are intervention flags, not exposure/topic flags. Smart & Storm administered no LSD; they critique LSD's use in five OTHER trials (Smith 1958; Chwelos et al. 1959; MacLean et al. 1961; Chandler & Hartman 1960; Eisner & Cohen 1958). No substance flag is set on this record for the same reason.
- **SafetyReported**: Coded 'N' — the article performs no AE/safety assessment of its own study (there is no study); its discussion of 'placebo reactors' and symptom overlap is drawn from the cited psychopharmacology literature (refs 11-14), not from an AE assessment conducted here.
- **AnyAEReported**: 'NA' because SafetyReported='N'.
- **NumDistinctAEs**: 'NA' because SafetyReported='N'.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: 'NA' because SafetyReported='N'.
- **AE_denominator**: Coded '.' (per spec, never 'NA') — no safety-assessed population exists because no trial was conducted by these authors.
- **ArmScope**: 'NA' — no arms exist; the article is not a trial and has no exposed population.
- **WorstSeverity**: 'NA' because SafetyReported='N'; no severity scale applies.
- **EligibleYN**: Coded 'N'. Under the eligibility screen's criterion (a), a clinical trial requires 'a prospectively planned intervention administered to participants for research purposes, with defined enrollment, an assigned regimen, and prospective outcome assessment.' This article is explicitly a critique/commentary (section header 'NOTES and COMMENT') that evaluates five OTHER published trials against methodological standards (control groups, blinding, pre/post measurement); the authors enrolled no participants and administered no intervention. Criterion (a) fails; EligibilityFailedCriterion='not_clinical_trial'.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: pending
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Not applicable (narrative review, not a trial)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: Smart & Storm 1964 is a methodological critique of five prior LSD-alcoholism trials, not a clinical trial of its own; it reports no effect size, test statistic, or sample of its own to convert. No defensible Tier-1 conversion exists.

## Smart 1966

*Alcohol · 1966 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Primary outcome per abstract-leading precedence: the article gives no single authors-designated primary endpoint, so the SUD-behavioral outcome the abstract leads with was used — percentage gain in abstinence, reported in the abstract as 'the gain in abstinence time in the lysergide, ephedrine and control groups, respectively, was 34%; 32%; and 20%.' Note a discrepancy: the Results section (p.476) gives slightly different values for the same comparison — 'average gains of 33.7% (lysergide), 31.5% (ephedrine) and 19.6% (control)' — the abstract figures appear rounded; both are internally consistent with each other (not contradictory, just rounded differently), so no anti-fabrication flag beyond noting it. This is a 3-group (not 2-group) comparison, so it is reported as a labelled 3-way descriptor rather than a single numeric effect size.
- **SD**: The paired inferential statistic for the same abstinence-gain comparison, from p.476: 'Simple analyses of variance...showed no differences between the lysergide, ephedrine and control groups in percentage gain in total abstinence (F = 0.323, p>.05).'
