No error/numerator degrees of freedom are printed for this specific F-test (unlike Table 2, whose df apply to the separate drinking-occasions/drunkenness-occasions ANOVAs, not to this abstinence comparison), so df is omitted rather than inferred.
- **PVal**: Printed verbatim as '>.05' immediately after the F-statistic on p.476; not converted to an exact value or to 'NS' since a threshold was explicitly printed.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: N=30 (10 lysergide, 10 ephedrine, 10 control) is the analyzed sample for all drinking-behavior comparisons, confirmed in the abstract ('30 alcoholics...were randomly selected... divided into 3 groups') and in Results ('all 30 patients were seen' at follow-up). See Retention (%) rationale for the separate question of the pre-completion enrolled/randomized total.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 13/14 met. Unmet: harms. 13/14 met; only 'harms' unmet — the article never describes a methodology for assessing or monitoring adverse events/safety (only incidental precautionary details such as bed restraint 'for security' and PRN chlorpromazine, plus an unattributed mortality mention with no cause or arm given). 'sample_size_power' credited on a close call: the Discussion explicitly reflects on whether N=10/group was adequate to detect the effect sizes claimed by prior uncontrolled studies ('the small number of patients used—only 10 in each group. But in previous reports the effectiveness of lysergide was so striking that 10 subjects should have demonstrated them'), which meets the field-level rule that a bare underpowered-study statement counts as 'discussed.'

### Adverse events
- **Smart 1966_AE01**: Article states in the Follow-Up section (p.474): 'Two patients died during the period covered by the study but both died after their follow-up had been completed.' No cause of death, treatment-arm assignment, or temporal proximity to the drug session is given, and the authors do not label it an SAE or discuss it further anywhere in the paper. Graded Severe under the rubric (death meets the FDA-seriousness criterion by definition) with SeveritySource='rubric' rather than 'author' because no author severity/seriousness language accompanies the mention (consistent with this being a pre-1980 article, predating CTCAE). SeverityIndeterminate='Y' because causal relationship to lysergide/ephedrine, timing relative to drug exposure, and arm membership are all unreported. ArmScope is coded 'pooled' rather than 'psychedelic_arm' because arm membership of the two deceased patients is not stated and should not be assumed to be lysergide-arm-only. Coding this event at all — and consequently SafetyReported='Y', HarmsAssessed=1, MortalityMeasured=1 — is a judgment call: the study has no dedicated adverse-event/safety section, and this note could be read as routine study-accounting rather than a safety report. It is coded conservatively per the cardinal 'never understate harm' rule and the field-level instruction that any reported death generates an ae_events row, and is flagged here for human review rather than silently omitted.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Non-significant 3-group ANOVA (multi-df F) — percentage gain in abstinence' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: The primary comparison is a one-way ANOVA across three arms (df=2 numerator; error df not printed for this specific test), F=0.323, p>.05. The supported conversion methods include only single-df-numerator F; a 3-group omnibus F is not reducible to a single pairwise effect size without additional pairwise data the article does not report, so this is excluded rather than approximated.

## Smith 1958

*Alcohol · 1958 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: No test statistic is reported anywhere in the article; treatment response is reported only as counts in three ordered clinical categories (Table 1/Table 2 and Summary): 6 much improved, 6 improved, 12 unchanged, 0 worse, out of 24. This is the article's sole, stated primary outcome (drinking response) with no comparator, so it is coded as a single-arm improvement rate: 12/24 (50%) improved or better.
- **SD**: No dispersion measure or test statistic (SD, SE, F, t, chi-squared) is reported anywhere for the treatment-response outcome; the article is purely descriptive/tabular.
- **PVal**: No p-value or significance threshold is reported anywhere in the article; results are presented as raw counts by diagnostic category with no inferential test.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: All 24 enrolled patients were followed up ('A follow-up investigation was carried out on all the patients') and all 24 appear in the Table 1/Table 2 results, so N analyzed = N enrolled = 24.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 9/14 met. Unmet: comparator, sample_size_power, randomization, blinding, statistical_methods. Objective, design (single-dose LSD/mescaline adjunct pilot), setting (University Hospital, Saskatoon), sample selection ('most difficult cases', A.A. failures), intervention (dose/route/session structure), outcome categories, results (Tables 1-2, case vignettes), side effects, and funding (National Health Grants, Rockefeller Foundation) are all explicitly described. There is no comparator group, no sample-size or power justification, and randomization/blinding/statistical methods are never mentioned at all (not even as absent) because the design is single-arm and purely descriptive with no significance testing. 9/14 met, below the 13-of-14 threshold for MethodologyDescribedYN = 1.
- **EligibleYN**: Criterion (a): 24 patients received a prospectively assigned single-dose LSD/mescaline regimen under a defined study protocol (Methods section) with prospective follow-up (2 months-3 years) — a single-arm open-label clinical trial per the eligibility boundary case. Criterion (b): the stated outcome is drinking behavior (abstinence/reduction/unchanged), a direct substance-use endpoint. Both criteria met.
- **Clincial Trial YN**: Coded 1 to match the EligibleYN=Y criterion-(a) judgment: a prospectively planned single-dose intervention with defined enrollment and prospective outcome assessment.
- **Pos Result Y/N**: No comparator exists, so directionality is judged from the study's own pre/post framing: 12/24 (50%) rated much improved or improved, none worsened, and the authors describe the results as 'sufficiently encouraging to merit more extensive... trials.' Coded 1 (positive) rather than 3, since the direction is stated, not ambiguous, even though the effect is modest and confined mainly to the character-disorder subgroup.
- **RandomizationYN**: Single-arm, open-label pilot with no randomization procedure described anywhere; coded 0 per the v1.2 single-arm rule, not 3.
- **BlindingYN**: No blinding of any kind is described (open interview, unblinded drug administration); coded 0 per the v1.2 single-arm rule.
- **Double+Blind**: Coded 0 to remain consistent with BlindingYN = 0 (cannot be 1 when BlindingYN = 0).
- **Comparator**: No control, placebo, or active-comparator group; all 24 patients received LSD or mescaline. Coded 0 per the v1.2 single-arm rule, not 3.
- **PrimaryCare**: All care occurred in an inpatient psychiatric/university hospital research setting with follow-up through Alcoholics Anonymous and a provincial alcoholism bureau; no interface with primary-care providers or settings is described.
- **Power**: No a priori or post hoc power discussion of any kind appears; the article is explicitly framed as a preliminary/pilot study without any sample-size justification.
- **Retention (%)**: Not randomized, so the denominator is the enrolled N (24). The article states follow-up was obtained on 'all the patients,' i.e., 24/24 completers = 100%.
- **ConfoundYN**: The authors substantively discuss confounding by diagnostic subgroup (Table 2 breaks results out by character disorder/psychopathy/psychosis) and explicitly flag a confound in the one psychotic patient who also received ECT ('difficult to know whether the mescaline produced any real benefit'), so coded 1.
- **InclusionExclusionCriteria**: The sample-selection basis is explicitly stated ('Only the most difficult cases were taken into this study. All but four... had tried A.A. and were considered to have failed') rather than a formal numbered list; coded 1 as a borderline but genuine statement of selection criteria.
- **ValidatedScaleMeasYN**: Drinking outcome was rated by clinician judgment into three ordinal categories (much improved/improved/unchanged), not a named validated instrument; liver function tests and glucose tolerance are biochemical, not scale-based. Coded 0.
- **BaseStatus**: Sample is explicitly the most refractory subgroup: mean 12.1 years of uncontrolled drinking, 8/24 with prior delirium tremens, complications in all but 2 cases, and prior A.A. failure in all but 4 — coded 'Severe'.
- **Age_Median**: Ages of the 24 patients (Table 1) range 20-63; computed mean = 38.6, computed median = 36 (average of the 12th/13th sorted values), both falling in the 30-50 bracket.
- **PercentFem**: 23 of 24 patients are listed 'M' and 1 (case 16) is listed 'F' in Table 1: 1/24 = 4.17%.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: No race/ethnicity breakdown is reported for the cohort; only Case 17 is described incidentally as 'of Italian origin,' which is not a cohort-level race/ethnicity figure.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: No explicit statement that low-SES participants were included or described at the cohort level (income, insurance status, eligibility criteria); coded 0.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Study conducted at University Hospital, Saskatoon, Saskatchewan, Canada — coded 'NonUS'; actual location given here per instructions.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Sample was deliberately restricted to 'the most difficult cases' who had already failed A.A. — a highly selected, non-representative refractory subgroup, not a real-world community/clinical sample; coded 0.
- **InterventionTherapy**: Drug administration was explicitly combined with a prolonged psychotherapeutic interview during the session, prior rapport-building psychotherapy (2-4 weeks), and post-session follow-up psychotherapy plus occupational/recreational therapy; coded 1.
- **HarmsAssessed**: A dedicated 'Side Effects' section reports nausea, vomiting, and liver-function monitoring, and reaction intensity was systematically graded per patient; SafetyReported = 'Y' accordingly, so HarmsAssessed = 1.
- **Followup in months**: Article states follow-up 'ranging from 2 months to 3 years' with an average of 1 year; longest reported duration (3 years) converts to 36 months.
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: Anxiety/depression are described qualitatively within individual case narratives and as acute drug-session phenomena, but no formal mental-health outcome or scale is reported as a study endpoint distinct from the drinking-outcome classification; coded 0.
- **MortalityMeasured**: No deaths are mentioned anywhere in the article ('None of the patients followed up became worse'); mortality was not assessed or reported as an outcome, coded 0.
- **LSD**: LSD-25 (200-400 gamma) was administered as one of the two study interventions (Table 1, Method section).
- **Mescaline**: Mescaline (0.5 g) was administered as the other study intervention (Table 1, Method section); some patients (e.g., case 22) received both LSD and mescaline across sessions.
- **MultiplePsychedelic**: Both LSD and mescaline were used as interventions across the cohort (and within-patient in at least case 22), so both substance flags are 1 and MultiplePsychedelic = 1 per the rule that it must be 1 whenever two or more substance flags are 1.
- **SafetyReported**: A dedicated 'Side Effects' section plus per-patient reaction-intensity grading (Table 1) and liver-function/glucose monitoring constitute an explicit safety assessment; coded 'Y'.
- **AnyAEReported**: Nausea, vomiting, variable-intensity psychological/perceptual reactions, one case requiring sedative intervention, and mild liver-function abnormalities are all reported; coded 'Y'.
- **NumDistinctAEs**: Five distinct AE types were identified: (1) general in-session psychological/perceptual disturbance, (2) nausea, (3) vomiting, (4) Case 17's severe anxiety/depression requiring sodium seconal, (5) mild liver-function-test abnormalities.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: No single combined count of participants with >=1 AE is given anywhere; the article reports overlapping subsets (22/24 with a graded reaction, 3 vomited, 'many' with nausea, 'some' with LFT abnormalities) that cannot be summed into one union count, so coded '.'.
- **AE_denominator**: All 24 patients received a psychedelic dose and were assessed under the Side Effects section and Table 1's reaction-intensity column, so the safety population equals the full enrolled sample of 24.
- **ArmScope**: Single-arm study; every reported AE value derives solely from the psychedelic-exposed cohort, so 'psychedelic_arm' per the rule that this applies to single-arm studies by definition.
- **WorstSeverity**: Maximum severity across the five ae_events entries is Moderate (Case 17's anxiety/depression requiring sodium seconal, AE04); no event met an FDA seriousness criterion or CTCAE Grade 4-5 equivalent.

### Adverse events
- **Smith 1958_AE01**: Table 1 grades each patient's reaction intensity mild/moderate/intense; cases 9 and 11 are explicitly stated to have shown 'no reaction beyond some slight increase in tension,' leaving 22/24 with a graded reaction. The narrative states these effects 'persisted for an average of 8 or 9 hours' and 'in all cases, the patients felt well by the following day,' supporting a default Mild grading per the rubric's expected-acute-drug-effect principle, since no general intervention or lasting impairment is described for the cohort as a whole (Case 17's exception is captured separately as AE04).
- **Smith 1958_AE03**: Authors explicitly qualify vomiting as 'not severely,' which is treated as an author-assigned severity descriptor (SeveritySource = author) rather than rubric inference.
- **Smith 1958_AE04**: Case 17 narrative: 2 hours after LSD, 'tension and depression were so marked that sodium seconal... was administered,' with 'some relief of the tension but the depression persisted' afterward, complicating the therapeutic relationship. Graded Moderate per the rubric (active pharmacological intervention required, functional/therapeutic impairment beyond the dosing day) but not Severe, since no FDA seriousness criterion (hospitalization, life-threatening event, persistent disability) is described.
- **Smith 1958_AE05**: Text states 'Some of the patients showed mild abnormalities of the liver function tests' without naming how many, and discusses cases 8 and 10's pre-existing hepatic conditions (cirrhosis, prior hepatitis) in the same passage without clarifying whether the LFT abnormalities were drug-induced or pre-existing baseline findings. Severity is graded Mild per the authors' own word ('mild', SeveritySource = author), but SeverityIndeterminate = Y because the affected count and drug-attributability are both unclear.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Single-arm improvement rate (%)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: Single-arm, open-label pilot with no comparator group and no dispersion or test statistic reported anywhere; only a categorical pre/post improvement rate (much improved/improved/unchanged) is available, which has no defensible standardized-effect-size conversion.

## Terasaki 2022

*Alcohol · 2022 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Methods/Outcomes and the Abstract both explicitly designate 'the primary clinical outcome was 30-day, all-cause hospital readmission rate.' Of the two pairwise comparisons reported against the linkage-alone (LA) control (Results/'Primary clinical outcome'; Table 2), this record uses the ketamine (KET) vs LA comparison — RR = 0.37 (p = 0.17) — rather than the naltrexone (NTX) vs LA comparison (RR = 0.52), because naltrexone is not a psychedelic and sets no substance flag in this database; ketamine is the article's only psychedelic intervention, and the substance flags on this record (Ketamine=1 only) are scoped to it. Note: 30-day readmission is a healthcare-utilization outcome, not itself a classic SUD-behavior measure. The article's substance-use-specific data — baseline/follow-up TLFB drinking quantity and the post-hoc 'alcohol-related readmission' subclassification (Results, 'When analyzing alcohol-related readmissions post-hoc...') — are secondary/exploratory and underpowered (TLFB follow-up obtained from only 6/13 KET participants). Per the prompt's boundary-case rule ('trials whose only SUD outcome is secondary are eligible... Outcome is still the stated primary outcome's effect size'), EligibleYN=Y rests on these secondary SUD elements while Outcome is coded to the stated primary (readmission) effect size.
- **SD**: No confidence interval, standard error, or chi-squared value is printed alongside the KET-vs-LA RR of 0.37; Table 2 and the Results text give only the RR point estimate and 'p=0.17'. The Statistical analysis section states relative risks were derived from chi-squared tests of independence, but no chi^2 statistic is printed for this specific 2-arm comparison. Coded '.' rather than back-computing an unreported statistic.
- **PVal**: Results/'Primary clinical outcome': 'Comparing the KET arm to the LA arm, the relative risk of readmission was 0.37 (p = 0.17)'; matches Table 2 ('RR to LA arm 0.3736' / 'p=0.17').
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: Table 2 column headers give KET n=13 and LA n=17 for the readmission comparison (13+17=30). All 44 randomized participants had readmission ascertainable via EHR query with no loss to follow-up for this outcome (Methods/Statistical analysis: primary and secondary clinical outcomes 'did not require participant responses, so all participants were included in intent-to-treat analysis'), but the specific KET-vs-LA effect size excludes the 14 NTX-arm participants, giving a denominator of 30, not 44.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 13/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power. All elements described except a priori sample-size/power justification: N=44 reflects a one-year pragmatic pilot enrollment window, not a calculated target, and the only power-adjacent statement is a post hoc admission in Limitations that the study 'was under-powered for detecting statistically significant differences.' Blinding is met as 'described' because the article states explicitly there was no blinding ('open-label'; 'There was no blinding in this study'), which satisfies the described-vs-present distinction even though BlindingYN=0.
- **Pos Result Y/N**: Coded 0 (null), not 1. The ketamine arm trended favorably on the primary outcome (15.4% vs 41.2% readmission, RR=0.37) but this was explicitly nonsignificant (p=0.17), and the article itself frames the result as promising but inconclusive ('differences were non-significant'; Conclusions: 'showed promise ... though differences were non-significant'). Per USPSTF-strict interpretation, a favorable but nonsignificant primary-outcome trend is coded as a null result, not a positive one.
- **Comparator**: Comparator=1. The primary comparator is the linkage-alone (LA) arm (n=17): standard enhanced-linkage-to-care with no pharmacologic intervention. A second active-drug arm (IM naltrexone, not a psychedelic) was also run in parallel but is not itself the comparator used for this record's Outcome (see Outcome rationale).
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 1: participants were drawn from the actual consecutive census of the hospital's addiction consultation service (real inpatient clinical population), not recruited via advertisement/volunteer solicitation, and the authors describe the design as 'very pragmatic' (Strengths). Flagged as non-obvious because screening was still substantial — of 205 assessed, 96 (47%) met exclusion criteria, mostly medical comorbidities specific to ketamine/naltrexone safety (cardiovascular, hepatic, renal, opioid use) — so the enrolled sample, while drawn from a real clinical population, was meaningfully narrowed by drug-safety exclusions.
- **Retention (%)**: Coded 50 = 22/44 (14-day addiction-clinic attendance across all arms: KET 8/13, NTX 7/14, LA 7/17; Table 2). This study reports several different 'completion' metrics with different denominators: the primary readmission outcome was ascertained for all 44/44 (100%) via EHR regardless of patient contact, while only 18/44 (40.9%) provided re-assessment data (PHQ9/TLFB/acceptability) at follow-up. 14-day clinic attendance is used here as the best single 'retention' figure because it is the study's own explicitly reported follow-up engagement metric applied uniformly across arms; the alternative denominators are noted here rather than silently discarded.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Table 1, All Arms (n=44) race breakdown: White/Caucasian 25 (56.8%), Black/African American 3 (6.8%), American Indian/Alaska Native 7 (15.9%), Multiple/Other 9 (20.5%). Non-white sum = 3+7+9 = 19/44 = 43.2%. Hispanic/Latinx ethnicity (15/44, 34.1%) is reported as a separate crosscutting category per the article's own table structure and is not added to the race-based BIPOC figure, consistent with the field rule that ethnicity crosses racial categories.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Single US site: 'urban, academic, safety-net hospital' — Denver Health & Hospital Association, Denver, Colorado, USA (Author details).
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: Coded 1: eligibility required 'public (e.g., Medicaid) or private insurance,' the site is explicitly a 'safety-net' hospital, and Table 1 reports housing status including 'not stably housed / unknown' for 38.6% of the sample — an explicit low-SES-relevant description.
- **ConfoundYN**: Coded 1: Discussion explicitly identifies and discusses baseline imbalances across arms as possible confounders despite randomization ('the linkage alone arm appeared to be more male, have higher baseline hospital utilization, and be less stably housed... these factors may have biased in favor of pharmacologic intervention'), and reports a post-hoc per-protocol sensitivity analysis adjusting for protocol deviations.
- **InterventionTherapy**: Coded 1: the KET arm received brief structured pre-infusion counseling (advice to focus on deep breathing and approach difficult experiences with curiosity, plus a standardized relaxation soundtrack) — preparatory support beyond bare drug administration. All arms also received 'enhanced linkage' to outpatient addiction care, which is a care-coordination component rather than psychotherapy itself; the pre-infusion counseling is the basis for coding 1.
- **ReductionMeasured**: Coded 1: self-reported daily drinking quantity/frequency was captured at baseline via a modified Timeline Follow Back and re-assessed at the follow-up clinic visit (Methods/Outcomes; Results: 'Some data elements were re-assessed at the addiction clinic follow-up visit (including PHQ9, TLFB...)'), functioning as a partial-reduction-type drinking measure even though the low follow-up rate precluded robust analysis.
- **CessationMeasured**: Coded 0: no abstinence/complete-cessation rate is reported as a distinct outcome; TLFB tracked drinking quantity/frequency (reduction-type), not an abstinence endpoint.
- **RelapseMeasured**: Coded 0: the study does not track relapse/return-to-drinking as its own outcome. Hospital readmission and ED visits are healthcare-utilization proxies, not an explicit relapse-to-use measure, and are coded as the primary/secondary clinical outcomes instead.
- **Followup in months**: Longest reported outcome horizon is the 30-day all-cause readmission window (Methods/Outcomes), measured from hospital discharge (end of the single-dose intervention) = 1 month. No longer horizon is reported for this study's own data (the 3-month naltrexone retention figure cited in Discussion belongs to a different study, Busch et al.).
- **ArmScope**: Coded 'psychedelic_arm.' Tables 5 and 6 report AE counts separately by arm (KET/NTX/LA); because naltrexone is not a psychedelic and this database's substance flags are scoped to the psychedelic intervention only (Ketamine=1), all ae_events and AE summary fields report KET-arm-only values, excluding NTX- and LA-only rows (e.g., an NTX-only injection-administration error with 'no ill effect' is not included in ae_events under this scope).
- **AE_denominator**: Coded 13 = the full randomized/exposed KET arm, the broadest ketamine safety population. Sub-tables use smaller denominators for specific timepoints (Table 5 immediate symptom inquiry: n=12 of 13, one participant did not receive the intervention; Table 6 since-discharge PRISE: n=6 of 13, reflecting low follow-up attendance) — these are recorded in each ae_event's Description rather than collapsed into a single misleading denominator.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: Coded '.': the article reports per-symptom counts at two different, partially overlapping KET sub-samples (n=12 immediate, n=6 follow-up) without a deduplicated count of unique KET participants experiencing at least one AE across both assessments, so this cannot be computed without fabricating a number.

### Adverse events
- **Terasaki 2022_AE01**: Table 5 (treatment-emergent AEs immediately post-intervention, KET n=12): lightheadedness rated 'distressing' in 1/12 (8.3%) participants. No CTCAE grade, medical intervention, or duration is reported, so graded Mild by the rubric's under-specified-AE default.
- **Terasaki 2022_AE02**: Table 5 (treatment-emergent AEs immediately post-intervention, KET n=12): arm pain rated 'tolerable' in 1/12 (8.3%) participants. No CTCAE grade, medical intervention, or duration is reported, so graded Mild by the rubric's under-specified-AE default.

- **Terasaki 2022_AE08**: Table 5 (treatment-emergent AEs immediately post-intervention, KET n=12): tingling rated 'tolerable' in 1/12 (8.3%) participants. No CTCAE grade, medical intervention, or duration is reported, so graded Mild by the rubric's under-specified-AE default.
- **Terasaki 2022_AE09**: Results/'Acceptability and safety': CADSS mean 20.31 (SD 16.62) in the KET arm (n=12), described as 'nearly identical to that seen in a similar trial.' Per rubric core adaptation principle, in-session dissociative/CEQ-type phenomenology is expected with ketamine and is graded by consequence, not intensity; no intervention or persistence beyond the session is reported, so graded Mild with sufficient information (not indeterminate).
- **Terasaki 2022_AE10**: Results/'Acceptability and safety' and Table 4: KET arm mean SBP +12.66 mmHg (p=0.006) and DBP +10.34 mmHg (p=0.05) at 40 min, both attenuated at 160 min; article states 'no instances of treatment-emergent symptoms attributed to these vital sign changes.' Graded Mild with sufficient detail (explicit values, timepoints, resolution, no attributed symptoms or intervention) — not indeterminate.
- **Terasaki 2022_AE11**: Table 6 (adverse events since discharge reported at follow-up visit, KET n=6 of 13 randomized who provided follow-up data): diarrhea — 1/6 tolerable and 1/6 distressing. PRISE self-report gives only a tolerable/distressing rating, no CTCAE grade, intervention, or precise timing since discharge, so graded Mild by rubric default and flagged indeterminate. Onset banded 'delayed' because this was assessed at the outpatient follow-up visit after hospital discharge; the exact interval is not reported precisely (clinic intake was 'targeted within 7 days post-discharge' but attendance was often later or did not occur for most of the arm).

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Risk ratio' / 'RR' / 'approx'
- **note**: RR = 0.37 for 30-day all-cause readmission, ketamine arm vs linkage-alone arm (2/13 vs 7/17, Table 2), the article's stated primary clinical outcome. Direction flipped to -1 because RR<1 favors ketamine (fewer readmissions is the desired direction) — the raw RR scale is opposite the 'higher = more favorable' convention used for d/g. Confidence is 'approx' rather than 'clean': the comparison is nonsignificant (p=0.17) in a 13-vs-17 pilot subset of a 3-arm unblinded trial, excludes the parallel naltrexone arm entirely, and Discussion itself flags likely baseline imbalance biasing in favor of the drug arms — a methodologist would want to see these caveats before treating the RR as a clean point estimate.

## Thurgur 2025

*Alcohol · 2025 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Citation/Title**: This is a secondary/Bayesian re-analysis of data from the parent open-label feasibility trial Sessa B, Higbed L, O'Brien S, et al. 'First study of safety and tolerability of 3,4-methylenedioxymethamphetamine-assisted psychotherapy in patients with alcohol use disorder.' J Psychopharmacol (Oxf). 2021;35:375-83. https://doi.org/10.1177/0269881121991792. Same 14 participants, same trial (NCT04158778); this paper reanalyzes drinking-risk and secondary PROM data at the 3-month timepoint. Eligible under the secondary-analysis boundary case in the prompt.
- **Outcome**: Abstract/Results/Table 1: primary analysis of this paper is the Bayesian posterior probability of a 2-level reduction in WHO drinking risk level at 3 months, with 9/14 participants meeting the success threshold. Chose the flat-prior mean posterior (0.63) over the skeptical-prior estimate (0.55) as the primary value since Table 1 lists it first and it is the uninformative/reference prior; the skeptical-prior result is the sensitivity analysis. Note the Abstract's phrasing ('55%-63%...flat or skeptical prior respectively') orders the two priors oppositely to the Results/Table 1 text (flat=63%, skeptical=55%) -- the numeric values themselves are consistent across Abstract, Results text, and Table 1, only the prose ordering in the Abstract is inconsistent, so this is flagged as a wording issue, not a numeric discrepancy per Non-negotiable #2.
- **SD**: Table 1 / Results paragraph: flat-prior 95% credible interval 0.40-0.85, paired with the flat-prior mean posterior (0.63) chosen for Outcome.
- **PVal**: This paper's primary analysis is a Bayesian posterior probability, which the authors do not accompany with a p-value. A separate, preliminary frequentist binomial test against a 50% null was also reported (k=.64, P=.423, Results section) but that is a distinct test object (a classical hypothesis test), not the p-value paired with the Bayesian Outcome value, per the SD/PVal pairing rule -- coded '.' rather than substituting a p-value from a different analysis.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: Table 1: WHO drinking-risk Bayesian analysis sample size = 14 (all enrolled participants; Demographics section confirms 14 enrolled, 8 male/6 female).
- **Pos Result Y/N**: Judged on the study's own pre/post direction (single-arm, no comparator). 9/14 (64%) participants achieved the 2-level WHO risk reduction success threshold, and the Bayesian posterior probability of success was 55-63%, which the Discussion frames as comparable to a 68% two-level reduction rate reported for established AUD treatments (COMBINE/Project MATCH secondary analysis). Multiple secondary PROMs also showed significant improvement (PACS P=.031, OCDS obsessive P=.006 and compulsive P=.021, PSQI sleep P=.041, self-compassion P<.001, IRI personal-distress P=.009). Coded 1 (positive) despite the preliminary binomial test against a 50% null being non-significant (P=.423), since that frequentist test is not the primary analysis and direction is not ambiguous or mixed.
- **Clincial Trial YN**: Coded 1, consistent with EligibleYN=Y via the secondary-analysis boundary case: this article reanalyzes data from a prospectively conducted open-label clinical trial (Sessa et al. 2021, same cohort/protocol), so criterion (a) of the eligibility screen is satisfied through that boundary case rather than through this paper itself being a new prospective trial.
- **Retention (%)**: Denominator is enrolled N since the study was not randomized (single-arm). Abstract/Demographics: 14 enrolled, and Table 1's primary WHO-risk Bayesian analysis includes all 14 (14/14 = 100%). Smaller per-instrument analysis Ns reported in Figures 2-3 (PACS/OCDS n=13, PSQI n=14, SCS n=13, IRI n=12, SF-36 n=11) reflect instrument-specific missing PROM data at 3 months, not overall study attrition from the enrolled cohort, so they were not used as the retention numerator.
- **BaseStatus**: Coded '.': the article states participants had an AUD diagnosis and had 'recently undergone detoxification' but gives no explicit severity classification (e.g., DSM-5 criterion count or a Mild/Moderate/Severe descriptor) for this cohort within this text.
- **PercentFem**: Demographics: 'The study enrolled 14 participants (8 males and 6 females...)'. 6/14 = 42.86%.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Demographics: 'All were white British.' BIPOC percent = 0.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Trial sponsored by Imperial College London, approved by the South West-Central Bristol Research Ethics Committee (UK) and MHRA; corresponding author affiliation London, UK. Coded 'NonUS'; country is United Kingdom.
- **SocioeconomicStatusYN**: No income, insurance status, or SES-related eligibility criteria described anywhere in the article.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 0: the Limitations section lists substantial exclusion criteria (antidepressant use, serious suicide risk, comorbid psychotic/personality disorders, cardiovascular/severe liver disease) and explicitly states the study 'lacked ethnic diversity' (all-white-British sample), indicating a highly screened, non-representative sample rather than a real-world clinical population.
- **ConfoundYN**: Coded 1: Limitations section explicitly discusses inability to statistically account for comorbidity as a potential confound ('due to the small sample size and lack of systematically collected comorbidity data, we were unable to account for these potential confounds'), which counts as substantive discussion of a confounding factor.
- **Power**: No a priori or post hoc power calculation or discussion is present anywhere in the article; only sample size (n=14) and 'small population'/'small sample size' limitations are mentioned, which is distinct from a power discussion per the field rule.
- **SafetyReported**: Judgment call, flagged per Non-negotiable #5 (understating harm is the cardinal AE error). This paper's own Methods/Results explicitly defer AE reporting to the parent publication: 'adverse events, all of which have been previously reported (Sessa et al. 2021)'. However, the Introduction also states, describing this same 14-participant trial, 'MDMA was well-tolerated and no unexpected adverse events were observed.' Because the article affirmatively states harms were assessed (even though full detail lives in the parent paper), coding SafetyReported='N' would incorrectly imply 'the authors never looked', which the prompt identifies as the worse error. Coded 'Y'.
- **AnyAEReported**: 'No unexpected adverse events were observed' is standard trial-safety phrasing implying expected/anticipated AEs did occur but nothing outside the anticipated safety profile -- it is not equivalent to 'no AEs occurred'. Per the cardinal rule against understating harm, coded 'Y' rather than collapsing this into a zero-AE reading.
- **NumDistinctAEs**: This article names no individual AE types, counts, or a table -- it only carries forward the single summary clause above from the parent trial. Coded 'Not numbered' rather than fabricating a count or a specific AE type.
- **WorstSeverity**: No severity-relevant detail (intervention required, functional impairment, seriousness criteria) is present in this article's text for any AE. Defaulted to 'Mild' per the rubric's under-specified-AE default (Severity rubric, 'Under-specified AEs' section), since no signal of escalation is present. This default could not be attached to a distinct ae_events row because no AE type is nameable from this text without consulting the parent Sessa et al. 2021 article, which is out of scope for this extraction; ae_events is therefore empty despite AnyAEReported='Y'. This whole AE block is a genuinely non-obvious judgment call and would benefit from human review against the parent article.
- **HarmsAssessed**: Set to 1 to agree with SafetyReported='Y' per the required HarmsAssessed/SafetyReported concordance rule.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 13/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power. sample_size_power is the only unmet criterion: sample size (n=14) is stated but no a priori or post hoc power calculation is discussed anywhere. comparator, randomization and blinding are each counted as met because their absence is explicitly described ('open-label' stated repeatedly; Limitations calls for 'larger and randomized placebo-controlled trials'; Discussion explicitly addresses the challenge of blinding MDMA), consistent with the rule that describing an element as absent satisfies the criterion. setting is minimally but genuinely described (Imperial College London sponsor, UK REC/MHRA approval, overnight stay at the treatment center after dosing sessions, per Future Directions). harms is met because the article states AEs were assessed and reported (in the parent publication), even though it does not re-tabulate them here. n_met=13 meets the >=13 threshold, so MethodologyDescribedYN=1.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Bayesian posterior probability (single-arm binomial success rate)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: The study's primary statistic is a Bayesian posterior probability of a binary treatment-success outcome (2-level WHO drinking-risk reduction) derived from a single-arm 9/14 success rate with flat and skeptical priors. A Bayesian posterior probability is not one of the seven supported conversion methods (d, g, eta_p, F, chi2, OR, RR), and there is no comparator arm from which to compute an OR/RR; no defensible conversion to Hedges' g exists.

## Tomsovic 1970

*Alcohol · 1970 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Primary reported comparison is the nonschizophrenic lysergide-treated group vs. nonschizophrenic control group I on the two-part (abstinence/nonabstinence) Drinking Adjustment classification at 1 year, the comparison the article's own discussion foregrounds ('Table 7 shows that the nonschizophrenic lysergide-treated alcoholics were superior to all the other groups... significant beyond the .01 level'). Table 5, 12-month row: Lysergide N=32, complete abstinence (Category A) N=14 (44%); Control group I N=26, Category A N=3 (11%). Coded as '44% vs 11%'.
- **SD**: Table 7, cell for Group 1 (nonschizophrenic lysergide, N=32) vs Group 2 (nonschizophrenic control I, N=26) = 7.19, footnoted as significant at the .01 level. The table's own footnote states 'Chi square for .05 level of confidence with df 1 = 3.84', confirming this is a 1-df chi-square, so coded 'χ²(1) = 7.19'.
- **PVal**: Table 7 footnote marks the 7.19 cell with the symbol defined as 'Significant at .01 level of confidence'; no exact p printed, so coded as the threshold '<.01'.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: Sum of the two compared groups' 12-month reported-case counts in Table 5 (nonschizophrenic lysergide N=32 + nonschizophrenic control group I N=26 = 58), the denominator underlying the Table 7 chi-square that produced Outcome/SD.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 12/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power, blinding. No a priori or post hoc power/sample-size justification is given (sample_size_power=N), and blinding is never discussed in either direction — the open-label, unblindable coin-toss design is described, but the article never states whether raters or assessors were blinded (blinding=N). All other elements are explicitly described: objective (Methods intro), setting (VA Hospital, Sheridan, Wyo.), eligibility/recruitment (volunteers from the 90-day Program, non-acutely-psychotic per admission criteria), intervention (500 µg dose, room, procedure), comparator groups I and II, outcome measures (Drinking Adjustment Scale), randomization (coin toss pairing), statistical methods (chi-square, significance thresholds), results (Tables 3-7), harms (recrudescence study, Schwarz review citation), and funding (VA research funds, footnote 1).
- **Pos Result Y/N**: Coded 1 because the designated primary statistic (chi²(1)=7.19, p<.01, nonschizophrenic lysergide vs. control I) was significant favoring lysergide. Flagging strong authorial caveats: the Discussion calls this comparison 'a questionable basis for assuming lysergide benefits' because control group I appears to have suffered a 'deprivation effect' (volunteers who wanted but did not receive the drug did unusually poorly), and separately states that vs. control group II (the more representative comparison, 'what was already being achieved') the abstinence difference 'was not statistically significant and we could not conclude that lysergide was beneficial.' The overall picture is genuinely mixed; 1 reflects the statistic selected as Outcome/SD per the primary-comparison precedence rule, not an unqualified positive-study verdict.
- **RandomizationYN**: Coded 1 for the lysergide vs. control group I comparison: paired volunteers were assigned to lysergide or control I by coin toss ('Selection of Subjects and Controls'). Control group II was a nonrandomized historical/naturalistic comparison cohort (prior Program completers or non-volunteers), not randomized. The single binary reflects that randomization was used for part of the design.
- **InclusionExclusionCriteria**: Coded 1: participants were drawn from the 90-day Alcoholic Rehabilitation Program and had to volunteer; the article also states an explicit exclusion basis — 'None of these patients were acutely psychotic at the time of the study or they would not have been admitted to the Alcoholic Rehabilitation Program.' No further formal medical inclusion/exclusion list (age, comorbidities) is given.
- **Retention (%)**: Lysergide-treated arm: 75 patients received the drug (Table 1 totals, 52 nonschizophrenic + 23 schizophrenic), treated as the randomized/allocated N since 75 already reflects post-coin-toss allocation to lysergide. At 12 months, reported cases were 32 (nonschizophrenic, Table 5) + 12 (schizophrenic, Table 6) = 44. 44/75 × 100 = 58.7%.
- **BaseStatus**: Coded 'Severe': inpatient VA 90-day Alcoholic Rehabilitation Program admission, mean years of problem drinking ~10-13 years across groups (Table 2), and substantial psychiatric comorbidity (23/75 lysergide-treated diagnosed schizophrenic; Table 1 subtypes include antisocial personality, chronic brain syndrome, drug addiction).
- **Age_Median**: Table 2 reports mean ages of 43-45 across the nonschizophrenic lysergide and control subgroups at both the 3-month and 1-year timepoints; falls in the '30-50' bracket. Age was not separately tabulated for the schizophrenic subgroups ('number was too small for meaningful analysis').
- **PercentFem**: Not reported anywhere in the article. This is a Veterans Administration hospital inpatient program (1965-1968 era), which strongly implies a near-entirely male sample, but since no explicit figure or statement is given, coded '.' rather than inferred.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Not reported anywhere in the article.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Veterans Administration Hospital, Sheridan, Wyoming, USA.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 0: sample was self-selected volunteers from a single VA inpatient program, and the authors themselves note this volunteer self-selection likely biased control group I ('a self-selected group of patients who have a poor prognosis because of their strong propensity to value a drug cure').
- **ValidatedScaleMeasYN**: Coded 1 for the Blewett and Chwelos Scales for the Assessment of Psychedelic Reactions (Scales 1 and 2), a named psychometric instrument used to assess the drug experience and post-treatment attitude change. The Drinking Adjustment Scale used for the primary outcome was study-specific, not a named validated instrument.
- **MentalHealthChangeMeasured**: Coded 0: the Blewett and Chwelos scales assess psychedelic-experience phenomenology and personality/attitude change ('intellectual', 'emotional', 'spiritual' insight), not clinical depression/anxiety/PTSD/well-being constructs, so distinguished from a mental-health outcome measure.
- **RelapseMeasured**: Coded 1: drinking control was tracked longitudinally at 3, 6 and 12 months per patient, explicitly showing 'a typical erosion of drinking control in the course of a year' — i.e., return-to-use over time was tracked, not just a single endpoint status.
- **MortalityMeasured**: Coded 0. The only mention of death is in the attrition discussion — 'A few patients died and about one-third had to be removed for methodological reasons' — given as a reason for loss of contact across all six study groups (including the two non-lysergide control groups), with no count, no attribution to lysergide, and no linkage to the article's dedicated harms discussion (which addresses only recrudescence and states 'None of our lysergide-treated patients... suffered any of these serious aftereffects'). Judged not to constitute a reported mortality outcome or AE of the intervention; flagged here as a non-obvious call rather than silently coding 1 with a fabricated AE entry.
- **ArmScope**: All reported AE information (recrudescence, altered dreaming, in-session dissociative episodes) is drawn exclusively from the 75 lysergide-treated patients; no AE data are reported for control groups I or II.
- **AE_denominator**: No single safety-population denominator is stated. Recrudescence was assessed over '61 patients who gave information on the absence or presence of recrudescence' (out of 75 lysergide-treated); the 2-patient in-session dissociation report and the 7-patient altered-dreaming report do not state their own denominators. Coded 'Not numbered' because denominators differ by AE type and no unified figure is given.
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: Not calculable as a single unduplicated count: the recrudescence (19), altered-dreaming (7) and in-session dissociation (2) groups may overlap, and the article gives no combined tally of unique patients with ≥1 AE.

### Adverse events
- **Tomsovic 1970_AE01**: NAffected=19 and onset timing from 'Of the 19 patients who reported single or multiple recrudescences, 3 had the first within a few weeks... 12 (65%) reported the first on the 3-month follow-up questionnaire, 3 (15%) on the 6-month, and 1 on the 12-month' — first occurrence generally weeks to months out, coded 'delayed'. Severity graded Mild per rubric (pre-1980 article, no CTCAE existed): described as 'relatively brief experiences that varied from very uncomfortable to very pleasant,' with no intervention or lasting impairment reported, so SeveritySource='rubric', not 'author' (authors used no formal severity/SAE language for this event).
- **Tomsovic 1970_AE02**: Reported as 'An additional group of 7 patients (11%) reported that they experienced alterations in dreaming,' distinct from the 19-patient recrudescence group, and explicitly called 'questionable examples of recrudescence' by the authors rather than merged into that count — kept as its own AE type. No onset timing is given for this subgroup specifically (unlike the 3/6/12-month breakdown given for recrudescence), so Onset is coded 'unknown' rather than inferred. Graded Mild per rubric: transient, non-intervention-requiring sleep-related phenomenon.
- **Tomsovic 1970_AE03**: SeverityIndeterminate='Y' because the article gives no information on whether any pharmacological or clinical intervention was used for the two patients who 'lost contact with reality... and acted bizarrely' and 'could not completely recall what had happened,' nor how long the disorientation/amnesia persisted beyond the session. Graded Moderate rather than the rubric's under-specified-AE default of Mild because the authors themselves flagged it as a concerning departure from the expected reaction ('gave us concern'), which exceeds ordinary transient anxiety/perceptual distortion, but nothing in the text meets an FDA seriousness criterion (no hospitalization, life-threat, or persistent disability documented), so MeetsFDASerious='N'. AE_type coded 'both': the event occurred in-session as acute drug-effect phenomenology but was also treated by the investigators as a clinically notable adverse occurrence.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Chi-squared' / 'chi2' / 'clean'
- **note**: χ²(1)=7.19 (Table 7) comparing nonschizophrenic lysergide-treated (n=32) vs. nonschizophrenic control group I (n=26) on 1-year abstinence/nonabstinence classification. The article explicitly states the applicable critical value is for df=1 ('Chi square for .05 level of confidence with df 1 = 3.84'), confirming a genuine 1-df 2x2 comparison, so convert_to_d()'s 1-df assumption holds cleanly here. Direction=1 because the result favors lysergide (higher abstinence). Note the authors themselves question the validity of this specific comparison (possible 'deprivation effect' inflating the gap via control group I's unusually poor outcomes), which is a substantive caveat for methodologist review even though the statistic itself converts cleanly.

## Vanderijst 2024

*Alcohol · 2024 · eligible=N · config=pending*

### Article
- **Outcome**: This is a study protocol (BMC Psychiatry, 2024, DOI s12888-024-05502-y). At the time of publication no participants had been enrolled or dosed; the article describes the planned primary clinical outcome (between-group change in % heavy drinking days, baseline to 4 weeks posthospital discharge, Methods 'Primary clinical outcome') but reports no observed effect size anywhere in the text. Coded '.'.
- **SD**: No dispersion or test statistic is reported; the Data analysis section names the planned statistical models (mixed-model ANOVA with Helmert contrasts, GLMM, mediation analyses) but presents no computed values. Coded '.'.
- **PVal**: No p-value is reported anywhere in the article; the trial has not been run. Coded '.'.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: The protocol specifies a planned total N=62 (31 high-dose/31 active-placebo, powered for 46 completers assuming 25% attrition; Methods 'Sample size'), but this is a target enrollment figure, not a final analyzed N from a completed analysis of Outcome. No analysis has occurred, so coded '.' rather than substituting the planned N.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 13/14 met. Unmet: results. All fourteen elements are addressed except 'results': as a protocol, the article has no results section and reports no outcome data (not even 'no results yet' language qualifies as describing results). Every other element is described in detail: objective/hypotheses (Background/Proposed study), design (double-blind RCT, 7-month parallel-group phase II), setting (Brugmann University Hospital Psychiatry Dept, inpatient detox unit), eligibility (explicit inclusion/exclusion lists), intervention (30mg psilocybin + ACT-based psychotherapy), comparator (5mg active placebo, rationale given), outcomes (primary/secondary/exploratory all itemized with instruments, Table 1), sample size/power (a priori power calc, Cohen's f=0.25, power=0.9), randomization (computer-generated, 1:1), blinding (double-blind, blinding-efficacy assessment plan), statistical methods (Data analysis section), harms (AE classification by severity/causality/expectedness, safety as a co-primary outcome), and funding/COI (Funding and Competing interests sections). n_met=13/14 meets the >=13 threshold, so the binary is 1.
- **Pos Result Y/N**: Coded 3 (cannot be determined) because no outcome data of any kind are reported; directionality of any treatment effect is genuinely unknown/unassessed, not merely absent as in a null single-arm result.
- **Clincial Trial YN**: Coded 0 to avoid contradicting the eligibility screen. Criterion (a) failed because the article does not report a conducted clinical trial: no participant has been enrolled, randomized, or dosed, and no prospective outcome assessment has occurred. The protocol describes a planned RCT design, but per the prompt's contradiction rule, Clincial Trial YN=1 alongside a failed criterion (a) is disallowed.
- **RandomizationYN**: Coded 1 from the described design ('computer-generated randomization', 1:1 allocation, Methods) even though Clincial Trial YN=0, per instruction that design features the protocol specifies may still be coded from the described design despite the trial not having been executed/reported.
- **BlindingYN**: Coded 1: the protocol specifies a double-blind design with systematic blinding-efficacy assessment (participants and therapists asked to guess allocation) — a specified design feature, not an executed/reported result.
- **Double+Blind**: Coded 1, matching BlindingYN=1: the title and Methods explicitly specify 'double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled' design.
- **Comparator**: Coded 1: active placebo condition (5mg psilocybin, a low-dose comparator) is specified; this is a dose-comparison design, which counts as 1 per the field rule. Comparator's nature: low (5mg) vs high (30mg) dose of the same substance, chosen to maintain blinding while remaining sub-therapeutic (Explanation for choice of comparator section).
- **Retention (%)**: Not calculable: zero participants have been enrolled or completed the study at the time of this protocol's publication, so no completers/denominator counts exist. Coded '.' (distinct from the design-specified fields above, since retention is an empirical outcome, not a design parameter).
- **Age_Median**: The article gives only the eligibility age range (21-64 years) as an inclusion criterion, not the median/mean age of an actual enrolled sample (none exists yet). Since the field requires the bracket containing the sample's actual median/mean age, coded '.' rather than inferring a bracket from the eligibility bound.
- **PercentFem**: Not reported; no participants have been enrolled. Coded '.'.
- **RaceEthnicity_BIPOCPercent**: Not reported; no participants have been enrolled and no demographic data exist yet. Coded '.'.
- **Country_USvsNonUS**: Trial conducted at Brugmann University Hospital, Brussels, Belgium (monocentric). Coded 'NonUS'.
- **Followup in months**: Coded 6, the longest follow-up timepoint specified in the protocol's schedule (Table 1: 6-month posthospital-discharge follow-up for heavy drinking, drinking days, abstinence, mood, anxiety, trauma, global functioning). This is the protocol-specified follow-up horizon, not an observed/reported follow-up outcome, since no participant has yet reached this timepoint.
- **HarmsAssessed**: Coded 1: the protocol specifies safety as a co-primary outcome, with AEs 'systematically recorded at each study visit and follow-up' and classified by severity, causality, and expectedness (Primary feasibility and safety outcomes section; Table 1 tracks 'Adverse events' at nearly every visit). This reflects the specified assessment plan, consistent with SafetyReported='Y' below.
- **SafetyReported**: Coded 'Y' on the basis that an explicit Safety/Adverse Events assessment plan and section exist in the article (per the field's judging criterion: 'whether an Adverse Events/Safety section... exists'), even though the trial has not yet been conducted and so no AE data exist. This is a genuinely ambiguous case not covered by the three-state table in the prompt (which assumes a completed study); flagged here rather than buried. The alternative reading (SafetyReported='N' because nothing has actually been assessed yet) was considered but rejected because the article unambiguously commits to and describes a systematic AE-assessment methodology, unlike a true 'authors never looked' case.
- **AnyAEReported**: Coded 'N': zero AEs have occurred or been reported because the trial has not yet dosed any participant. This differs from the classic 'no AEs occurred' scenario (which implies a completed safety assessment with a true negative finding) but produces the same downstream coding; flagged as a judgment call.
- **NumDistinctAEs**: Coded 0, consistent with AnyAEReported='N' per the three-state convention (0 only permitted when AnyAEReported='N').
- **NParticipantsWithAE**: Coded 0: zero participants have been dosed, so zero participants can have an AE to date.
- **AE_denominator**: Coded '.': no safety population has been assessed yet (zero participants dosed), so no denominator exists. Distinguished from the 'SafetyReported=N' case, where '.' is also used but for a different reason (no assessment planned at all); here the plan exists but has not yet been executed on any participant.
- **ArmScope**: Coded 'NA': with zero participants dosed in either arm, arm scope for AE reporting genuinely does not apply yet (not merely because the study has one arm — it currently has zero exposed participants of any kind).
- **WorstSeverity**: Coded 'None', consistent with AnyAEReported='N' per the three-state convention.
- **BaseStatus**: Coded 'Severe': inclusion criteria require a DSM-5-TR diagnosis of severe alcohol use disorder (6 or more criteria), explicitly stated in both Background ('sAUD') and inclusion criteria.
- **QualityOfLifeMeasured**: Coded 1: the secondary hypotheses explicitly state the high-dose group is expected to show 'a greater improvement in health-related quality of life compared to the active placebo group' (Background/Proposed study, secondary hypotheses), and Table 1 tracks 'Global functioning' as a planned outcome.
- **RecruitmentRepresentYN**: Coded 1: recruitment is planned from the routine inpatient alcohol-detoxification pathway at a public university hospital (real-world clinical population), explicitly framed by the authors as 'pragmatic' (Background: 'trials that are more pragmatic in nature'; Recruitment section). Flagged as non-obvious because the Discussion itself notes this pragmatic aim is constrained by 'strict and rather restrictive inclusion and exclusion criteria', which somewhat limits representativeness.
- **RelapseMeasured**: Coded 1: the primary and secondary clinical outcomes (percentage of heavy drinking days, percentage of days of abstinence, drinks per day, tracked from baseline through 4 weeks and 6 months posthospital discharge) function as a return-to-use/relapse measure, though the article does not use the word 'relapse' as a named instrument; treated as functionally equivalent tracking of return to problematic drinking after detoxification.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: pending
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'No statistic reported (trial protocol; no participants dosed, no outcome data collected)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: This is a trial protocol (registered EudraCT 2022-002369-14 / NCT06160232, recruitment starting winter 2024, expected completion autumn 2026). No participants have been dosed and no outcome data of any kind exist, so no effect-size conversion is defensible. Excluded from Tier 1 pending publication of the trial's actual results.

## Yoon 2019

*Alcohol · 2019 · eligible=Y · config=carried-forward*

### Article
- **Outcome**: Methods states the primary outcome measure is clinical response (≥50% MADRS improvement from baseline at 4 hours postinfusion). Results reports two time points for this measure: 60% (3/5) responded after the initial dose and 100% (5/5) responded by the fourth dose. The cumulative, endpoint figure (100%, 5/5) was chosen as the primary-comparison value since it represents the study's full-course result on the designated primary outcome; the earlier 60% figure is the interim assessment after dose 1.
- **SD**: No dispersion measure, test statistic, or df is reported anywhere in the article for the MADRS response-rate outcome; this is a research letter reporting only raw counts/percentages, so SD is coded not reported.
- **PVal**: No p-value is reported for the clinical-response outcome (or for any outcome in the article); coded not reported.
- **SampleSize_NFinal**: The 100% (5 of 5) response-by-fourth-dose figure explicitly states N=5 as its denominator (Results paragraph), so SampleSize_NFinal=5. Note an apparent internal tension: the same sentence also states '1 patient left the trial after receiving 2 ketamine infusions,' which is not decoded or reconciled here per the never-construct-a-decoding-theory rule — the article's own stated denominator (5 of 5) is reported as printed rather than inferred down to 4.
- **MethodologyDescribedYN**: 12/14 met. Unmet: sample_size_power, statistical_methods. Brief research-letter format. sample_size_power=N: no sample-size justification or power calculation is discussed anywhere. statistical_methods=N: results are reported as raw percentages/counts with no statistical-testing methodology described. comparator=Y and randomization=Y are coded as 'described as absent': the title and Methods explicitly frame the design as a 'case series' and 'open-label pilot study,' which is an explicit (if terse) statement that no control/comparator arm and no randomization were used, satisfying the described-as-absent standard rather than mere silence. n_met=12 < 13, so MethodologyDescribedYN=0.

### Config proposal
- **disposition**: carried-forward
- **metric / method / confidence**: 'Single-arm response rate (%)' / None / 'excluded'
- **note**: Single-arm, no-comparator case series (N=5); the primary outcome is a pre/post response-rate proportion with no dispersion, test statistic, or comparison group, so none of the seven supported conversion methods (d, g, eta_p, F, chi2, OR, RR) apply. Excluded from Tier 1, routed to Tier 2.
